An archive member or XBC1 payload that is an NDSR pack (the upscaler's networks, `/monolib/shader/ndsr.pck`) goes to the NDSR reader (`ndsr_shaders`) from `monolith_shaders`: Xenoblade Chronicles 2's Switch 2 Edition (`0400E95004038001`) keeps it as an XBC1 member of `bf2.ard`, which only the HCPS and GLSLC scans saw, and its title screen drew no scene, with 9 `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map` keys and `the dispatched program has no compute shader`; the split finds 28 more shaders with it (22,533 → 22,561), the same 9 keys Xenoblade DE's Switch 2 Edition takes from its loose copy.
- **ARH2 and NVN2 HCPS programs:** an `arh2` index (Xenoblade Chronicles X: Definitive Edition and its Switch 2 Edition: `sts.arh`, `stso.arh`) holds hashed members, named `/#<hash>`: a header of `arh2`, a u32 count and a u32 alignment, then 16-byte entries of u64 hash, u32 disk size and u32 expanded size. Offsets accumulate the aligned disk sizes, which include any XBC1 wrapper, rather than ARH1's path trie and explicit offsets. The reader checks the header, the alignment (a power of two) and every member's ARD bounds, then uses the same recursive decoder. An HCPS program's controls can be Maxwell or NVN2, and one asset can hold both: each control's own version (0xc9 at +4 is NVN2) picks its lengths, Maxwell's section length at +0x20 and code length at +0x704 (at least 0x760 bytes), NVN2's at +0x30 and +0x44 (at least 0x48 bytes), and its stage reader (the code header, or NVN2's SPH at the code area's start). Assuming Maxwell made the 0xd8-byte NVN2 controls read as truncated: the Switch 2 Edition's split stopped at its first SLCT in `main`, and an archive job with one such program lost its Maxwell shaders too. A control shorter than its layout's minimum still stops the split. Maxwell programs read exactly as before (Xenoblade Chronicles 2, 3 and Definitive Edition). Ported from NXNative `b32d0bf3b` (MIT) without its shader map header label, which would change the other Monolith games' tables; synthetic tests in `dm/gfx/tests/test_monolith.py` cover NVN2 programs, an NVN2 SLCT in `main`, a mixed asset, truncated controls of both layouts, aligned ARH2 members and bad ARH2 bounds. Found on [Xenoblade Chronicles X: Definitive Edition – Nintendo Switch 2 Edition](../../dm/games/0400453019AA8001/README.md#on-nx-18); format from [XbxDeTool's ARH2 reader](https://github.com/Nenkai/XbxDeTool/blob/master/XbxDeTool/ArchiveHeaderFile.cs).
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 3](../../dm/games/010074F013262000/README.md#files-and-shaders), whose note records the census and runtime key checks; [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md#files-and-shaders), with the same archive and standalone SLCT layouts on SDK 3.5.0; [Xenoblade Chronicles: Definitive Edition](../../dm/games/0100FF500E34A000/README.md#files-and-shaders), with loose containers.
- **Format references:** [ARH1 layout](https://github.com/roccodev/ard-tools/blob/master/docs/arh1.md), [xc3_lib shader structures](https://github.com/ScanMountGoat/xc3_lib/blob/main/xc3_lib/src/spch.rs). The reader's lengths and keys were checked against the supplied game files.

### Needle shader containers in main and GFD archives

- **Symptom:** the split finds no shaders, although `main` contains NVN control sections; it reports that no data record or code-address pair identifies their code areas.
- **Cause:** Needle keeps independent shader programs in `HNSHV002` containers, embedded in `main` and in GFD's GSC files inside CRI CPK archives. These are neither GLSLC outputs nor BNSH files. A container can hold many variants separated by reflection metadata.
- **Fix:** `shader_split.py` `needle_shaders` uses the container's big-endian whole length at +8, then the little-endian NVN section descriptors: 0x90 bytes immediately before each control section, with control offset, code offset, code length and control length at +0x30. Both regions must fit within that container, and the control/code headers and lengths must agree. Graphics stages come from SPH; headerless compute stages from the control's thread-group dimensions. Each program is paired with the single-stage cover, since its draw-time partner is not recorded by this wrapper. Synthetic fixtures check embedded variants, compute, length mismatches and container boundaries.
- **Found on:** [Persona 5 Royal](../../dm/games/01005CA01580E000/README.md#shaders), with 0x850-byte control sections. The game note records counts and runtime coverage.

### Shaders in a zlib stream in main (YEBIS .tfxo)

- **Symptom:** shaders the game draws are missing from the map (`DM_NVN_SHDUMP`), in no RomFS file and no module, stored or LZ4, and not compiled at run time.
- **Cause:** Silicon Studio's YEBIS post-effect library (bloom, glare, depth of field, vignette; `C:/workspace/YEBIS_draft/src/GPU/…` in `main`) keeps its shaders in a zlib stream in `main`: YEBIS's `.tfxo file`, a text header (the shader count, `hlsl`, `TexUtil_NVN_NX`, the technique list) and the shaders, each a control section (its own length 0x850 on SDK 7) directly followed by its code area.
- **Fix:** `shader_split.py` inflates it and reads the shaders as single-stage programs (`tfxo_streams`, `tfxo_shaders`).
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Sword](../../dm/games/0100ABF008968000/README.md): after the [GFPAK reader](#bnsh-entries-of-gfpak-archives), 38 shaders the opening drew were still missing; none was a patched copy of a split shader (the nearest ones of the same length differ in hundreds of bytes). `main` imports `glslcCompile` from `subsdk1` but never calls it and holds no GLSL source. The only zlib stream in `main` that inflates (`main+0x1c98ed9`, 39.5 MB) holds 3,155 shaders (3,015 distinct, 3,112 pixel and 43 vertex); they covered all 38.
- **Check:** a temporary trace in `nvnProgramSetShaders` (the key's code and control addresses, a backtrace and the heap around them) showed control sections packed back to back with technique names such as `ps_BrightPassT1_THRESHOLD_LUMINANCE_S1` beside them; the names are in `main`, and so is the data.

### Loose control sections and code areas in main (Bink 2)

- **Symptom:** two `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map` lines and `program has no vertex shader` as the opening movie starts; every movie drew white.
- **Cause:** the movie player (Bink 2's Unity plugin) builds its programs from 32 control sections and code areas in `main`'s read-only segment that are in no GLSLC output and no BNSH file. 26 are 0x20-byte records in two tables in `.data` (`{code area pointer, control section pointer, u32 code length, u32 control length}`, both pointers relative relocations); the code takes the other 6 by address (`adrp` + `add` to the control section and, a few instructions away, to the code area it copies with `memcpy`), the code area right after the control section's own length (+0x20, 16-byte aligned) or right before it, and two control sections share one code area. The 8 compute shaders' code areas have no header (SDK 10).
- **Fix:** the reader (`raw_shaders`) takes them. Both neighbours can pass the header and length checks, so it takes only a code area the code builds the address of, the nearest one to the control section's.
- **Found on:** [Advance Wars 1+2: Re-Boot Camp](../../dm/games/0100300012F2A000/README.md) (Unity 2020.2 with Bink 2's Unity plugin). Of 25 `main`s on hand no other has such control sections.

### SNVN program records in Retro Studios' RFRM forms

- **Symptom:** the provisional split finds only `main`'s 294 GLSLC outputs; the 3D scene and the title logo are magenta, with `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map` lines; or the NSP script stops at the shader split with `ADIR of 13 entries in 0x30c bytes, neither 36 nor 52 bytes each` for every pack and `the shader split found no shaders in main or the RomFS` (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond).
- **How it is stored:** Retro's files are RFRM forms (`RFRM`, big-endian u64 data length, u64, then the form type and two u32 versions; chunks inside are a fourcc, u64 length, two u32 versions, then the data). `MaterialArchive.arc` is one `MTRL` form holding an `SDX ` chunk (the same materials' D3D bytecode, 3,468 DXBC programs) and an `SNVN` chunk (the NVN ones). The `.pak` files are `PACK` forms whose `ADIR` chunk lists the resources (u32 count, then 36-byte entries: fourcc type, 16-byte GUID, big-endian u64 offset and length); each `MTRL` resource is an RFRM form whose data is one zlib stream holding another `MTRL` form with an `SNVN` chunk. An SNVN program record is a big-endian u32 code length, u32 control length, a byte, u32 stream length, then a zlib stream that inflates to the program's code areas back to back followed by their control sections in the same order (0x840 bytes each on SDK 4). The chunk's tables differ between files, so the reader finds the records by their own lengths: a stream whose header ends where it says and that inflates to exactly code + control bytes.
- **Startup archive in `main`:** the first programs the game builds (the `CGraphicsUtilCache` quads that draw the title logo) come from a startup material archive compressed into `main` (`NMaterialStartup::get_startup_archive_compressed_buffer`, 5,870 bytes; `NMaterialArchive::ImportStartupArchive` inflates it at boot). Its `CBufferedCompression` header is a type byte (1: LZSS on 8-bit units, `CLZSSDecompressor::DecompressBuffer8`) and three zero bytes; the data inflates to an `MTRL` form like `MaterialArchive.arc`'s. Its programs share code areas with `MaterialArchive.arc`'s but differ in control +0x7d0 (a per-program value), so their keys are their own.
- **Little-endian forms (Metroid Prime Remastered):** the same forms with every length, count and record header little-endian (`rfrm_order` tells the order from the u64 length: the reading that is a length is the small one). A pack's first chunk is a `TOCC` form holding the `ADIR` chunk, whose entries are 52 bytes: fourcc type, GUID, two u32 versions, u64 offset, u64 length and u64 stored length (shorter for a compressed resource; none of the 778 `MTRL` resources is). An `MTRL` resource's zlib stream inflates to an `EXPT` chunk and then the `MTRL` form. Control sections are SDK 12's 0x880 bytes, and four vertex stages of `MaterialArchive.arc` carry 0xc0 bytes more than their own length (+0x20) says: GLSLC's extra data at +0x7e8 (offset 0x880) / +0x7ec (length) / +0x7f0 (16 entries of 12 bytes), which the reader steps over to reach the next stage's section.
- **zstd records and 60-byte entries (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond):** the split stops with `ADIR of 13 entries in 0x30c bytes, neither 36 nor 52 bytes each` for every pack and finds no shaders. The `ADIR` entries are 60 bytes, the 52 and a u64 0; a resource stored compressed (stored length shorter) has the `CBufferedCompression` header of the startup archive (none of the `MTRL` resources is). An `MTRL` resource's data, and each program record's stream, is zstd where the older games have zlib: the record keeps its 13 bytes (code length, control length, a byte, stream length), the stream is the program's frame and then an empty one, and 44 bytes the lengths do not count follow it. `MaterialArchive.arc`'s `SNVN` chunk starts with a table of (offset, length) per record and a directory of the materials by name. Control sections are still 0x880 bytes, most with the 12-byte entries of +0x7e8 after them, and the next section starts on 16 bytes (0x890 after a stage with one entry), so the control length is only a multiple of 4. The archive has an `NVN2` chunk of the same layout beside `SNVN`, which the reader leaves. The packs reach 3.9 GB, so the reader maps them.
- **Records the reader skipped (found on Metroid Prime 4: Beyond, wrong since Metroid Prime Remastered):** the reader took a record only when its control length was a multiple of 0x40 and stepped from one control section straight to the next. A stage's 12-byte entries (+0x7e8) make the length 0x88c for one stage with one entry, and the next section starts on 16 bytes (0x890), so two stages give 0x1110 or 0x11d0. Such records were passed over silently, as if they were other data: 16 of the 583 programs of Metroid Prime Remastered's `MaterialArchive.arc` (control lengths 0x1110, 0x11d0, 0x88c) and 28,479 of its 68,962 shaders in all, which its map lacked from its bring-up until this fix. The length test is a multiple of 4 now and the step rounds up to 16 bytes when no section follows directly; every key of the old map is in the new one. Tropical Freeze's files were not on hand to split again (its sections are SDK 4's 0x840 bytes; whether its records carry such entries is not known).
- **Reader:** `rfrm_shaders`, `snvn_records`, `retro_main_forms` and `retro_lzss8` in `shader_split.py`; each record is one program, so its stages pair with each other.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Country: Tropical Freeze](../../dm/games/0100C1F0051B6000/README.md) (SDK 4.4.0): 24,098 shaders (11,644 vertex, 11,734 pixel, 720 geometry, which run as compute kernels): 3,460 first seen in `MaterialArchive.arc` (its 3,468 stages), the rest in the 795 `MTRL` resources of the packs, 8 in the startup archive and 294 GLSLC outputs in `main`; about 8 s.
- **Also found on:** [Metroid Prime Remastered](../../dm/games/010012101468C000/README.md) (SDK 12.3.1, little-endian): 68,962 shaders (34,472 vertex, 34,472 pixel, 12 geometry, 6 compute; 40,483 before the skipped records were read): 1,156 first seen in `MaterialArchive.arc`, the rest in the 778 `MTRL` resources of the world, preload and UI packs, and 8 in the startup archive (`main+0x1d0e7a4`, the same `CBufferedCompression` type 1); `main` holds no GLSLC output. Tropical Freeze's split gives the same 80,212 stages from its 76 RFRM files with the byte-order-aware reader.
- **Also found on:** [Metroid Prime 4: Beyond](../../dm/games/010019A01E2F2000/README.md) (SDK 19.3.15): 259,989 shaders (129,992 vertex, 129,972 pixel, 19 geometry, 6 compute) from `MaterialArchive.arc` (3,473 stages in 1,740 programs) and the `MTRL` resources of 444 packs, none in `main`; 2 minutes 19 s. Metroid Prime Remastered's split with the changed reader keeps every key it had and adds the skipped records' (above).

### MGFX effects in MonoGame XNB files

- **How it is stored:** the 13 XNB files in `Content/Effects` (XNB platform `S`, uncompressed, read by `EffectReader`) each hold one MGFX effect, and `main` holds SpriteBatch's built-in one; no other file and no GLSLC output. A compressed XNB (MonoGame's LZX flag 0x80 or LZ4 flag 0x40, as in Stardew Valley) is decompressed first (`xnb_decompress`).
- **Layout:** MGFX version 8 is MonoGame's own layout: `MGFX`, u8 version, u8 profile, u32 effect key, the constant buffers, then each shader as u8 vertex (1) / pixel (0), u32 length, the bytecode (u32 control length, the control section, u32 code length, the code area: GLSLC's output without its header), the samplers, the constant buffer indices and an attribute list (as MonoGame's OpenGL backend writes it), then the parameters and the techniques with their passes (`mgfx_effect`).
- **Reader:** the reader parses every effect to its end, which checks the layout; in `main`, four bytes `MGFX` count only when version 8 and a control section follow.
- **Pairing:** a pass names its vertex and pixel shader by index, 255 for none, and 10 of the 13 effects have no vertex shader: SpriteBatch binds its own and the effect's pass only replaces the pixel shader. Vertex and pixel shaders therefore combine across effects, and they are paired as single-stage programs.
- **Found on:** [Celeste](../../dm/games/01002B30028F6000/README.md) (MonoGame for the Switch, its C# compiled to C++ by Sickhead's BRUTE): 26 shaders (20 pixel, 6 vertex), all with Mario's binding layout; every binding decodes. [Stardew Valley](../../dm/games/0100E65002BB8000/README.md) (compressed XNB).

### MGFX version 5 effects (MonoGame 3.0)

- **Symptom:** the NSP script's split stops with `[RED] 24 file(s) could not be read`, each `an XNB effect without an MGFX version 8 effect of NVN shaders`.
- **Cause:** MonoGame 3.0's EffectReader (`MonoGame.Framework, Version=3.0.0.0` in the XNB) writes MGFX version 5: `MGFX`, u8 version, u8 profile and no effect key; constant buffers without names (i16 size, u8 count, {u8 parameter, u16 offset}); each shader's bytecode is a whole GLSLC output of one stage (magic 0x19866891, its +0x44 length equal to the shader's), not a control section and a code area; and the shaders are in MonoGame's DirectX layout: samplers without names and no attribute list. The parameters and techniques are version 8's. The length in front of `MGFX` in the XNB runs about 0xff0 bytes past the effect, all zeros.
- **Fix:** `mgfx_effect` reads version 5 too, taking each shader through `glslc_shaders`; an XNB effect may end in zeros. The 7 `.dx11.mgfxo` files in `Resources` (MonoGame's stock effects) are version 5 as well: 5 hold GLSLC outputs and are read the same way, `EnvironmentMapEffect` and `SkinnedEffect` hold only DirectX bytecode and give no shader.
- **Found on:** [Bastion](../../dm/games/010038600B27E000/README.md): 72 shaders, 24 pixel shaders in `Content/Effects` (no vertex shader: SpriteBatch's own pairs with them) and 48 in `Resources`.

### Unity 2020.3 Shader objects in UnityFS bundles

- **How it is stored:** Unity 2020.3 keeps each shader's compiled programs in its `Shader` object (class 48), in UnityFS bundles (`StreamingAssets/aa`) and in the player's own serialized files (`data.unity3d`, `Resources/unity default resources`).
- **Reader:** `dm/gfx/tools/unityfs.py` reads them: UnityFS blocks (LZ4 / LZMA), the serialized file, the object through its type tree, then `compressedBlob` per platform (LZ4 segments; each segment starts with a u32 count and 12-byte `{offset, length, segment}` entries).
- **Type trees:** the player's files have no type trees; they borrow the bundles' `Shader` tree with the same type hash (same Unity build, same layout). Type-tree strings with the high bit set index Unity's common string table, which `unityfs.common_strings` reads from the game's own `main` (`AABB\0AnimationClip\0…`).
- **Program code:** on Switch every subprogram is one whole NVN program. Its code is a 0x60-byte header of four `i32 ×6` arrays indexed by NVN stage (0 vertex, 1 fragment, 2 geometry, 3 tess control, 4 tess evaluation, 5 compute): an unused / scratch size, the block offset from +0x60 (−1 = no stage), the control size and the code size. Each block is the control section followed by the code area. The vertex and fragment stages of one program form a pair.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Engage](../../dm/games/0100A6301214E000/README.md): 209 `Shader` objects in 188 bundles plus the engine files, 9,269 stage shaders (4,634 vertex / pixel pairs and one geometry shader), no compute shaders.

### Unity compute kernels with no code

- **Symptom:** `[RED] 1 file(s) could not be read, their shaders are left out (first: Data/resources.assets: error: unpack_from requires a buffer of at least 96 bytes for unpacking 96 bytes at offset 0 (actual buffer size is 0))` from the shader split, and the game's built-in shaders in that file missing at run time.
- **Cause:** a `ComputeShader` kernel can have an empty `code`, with no buffers and no constants, so nothing to translate. `unity_compute` read it as a program, and the exception cost the split every shader in the file, not only that kernel.
- **Fix:** `unity_compute` in `shader_split.py` skips a kernel with no code and reads the rest (`dm/gfx/tests/test_unity_compute.py`).
- **Found on:** [DAVE THE DIVER](../../dm/games/010097F018538000/README.md) (Unity 6000.0.52f1): 11 of the 142 kernels in `Data/resources.assets`, such as `ParticleGrid`'s `BuildContactList` and `ClothRendering`'s `UpdateClothMesh`. The file holds 1,815 programs, of which 1,669 shaders occur nowhere else.

### Legacy Unity stages lose their pass partners

- **Symptom:** `failed to create render pipeline` with a fragment `user(locn1)` input missing from the vertex output, while the split found all shaders but almost no vertex / pixel pairs.
- **Cause:** before Unity 2019.3 each blob entry contains one stage. The vertex and pixel variants of a parsed pass have different blob indices, so pairing by the source's `#var<platform>.<index>` connects none of them. Without its pixel partner, output padding does not know which unwritten vertex attributes the fragment shader reads. Return of the Obra Dinn's `OneBit/Rope` has vertex blob 0 with pixel blob 8 and vertex blob 28 with pixel blob 35; both fragment stages read generic locations 0–4.
- **Fix:** `unity_pass_pairs` in `shader_split.py` reads the stages listed by each parsed pass, separately per platform, and pairs its vertex stages with a deterministic pixel-input cover in the supplemental `pso_pairs.tsv`. This covers every input used by the pass's pixel variants without depending on equal stage keyword lists or generating every combination. Existing evidence-pair tables, combined-stage Unity programs and source labels stay unchanged; profiles without a stored supplemental table take the generated one beside the shader package.
- **Found on:** [Return of the Obra Dinn](../../dm/games/010032E00E6E2000/README.md) (Unity 2017.4.28f1, 1.0.1): the two `OneBit/Rope` pipeline failures reported in PR #427.

### Unity 2022 Shader objects with parameter blobs

- **How it is stored:** the program code keeps the 0x60-byte stage header ([Unity 2020.3 Shader objects in UnityFS bundles](#unity-20203-shader-objects-in-unityfs-bundles)), but two things changed. From Unity 2022 the blob also holds each program's parameter tables as entries of their own (the parsed form's `m_ParameterBlobIndices`; their bytes start `PGlobals`), and the player's programs are listed in `m_PlayerSubPrograms[tier][].m_BlobIndex` (`m_SubPrograms` is empty).
- **Reader:** `unity_code_blobs` keeps only those player programs. UnityPy's type trees for 2020.3 (Engage) and 2021.3 have neither field, so those read every entry as before. Subprogram version 202012090 (from Unity 2021.2) also has no local keyword list, so `unityfs.subprogram` reads no second keyword array (with one it read the code length as a keyword count).
- **Type trees:** the player's serialized files have no type trees and the Addressables bundles hold no `Shader`, except the `unitybuiltinshaders` bundle, whose tree has the same hash and is borrowed in the second pass.
- **Found on:** [Ace Attorney Investigations Collection](../../dm/games/010005501E68C000/README.md) (Unity 2022.3.17f1): 4,951 stage shaders: 2,475 vertex / pixel pairs (4,294 in the player's files, 517 built-ins, 140 in bundles) and one geometry shader, which Metal cannot express (its build fails).
- **Note:** armyja's issue #17 proposed UnityPy and a reader of its own; neither was needed.

### Unity Shader layouts written out for players without bundles

- **Symptom:** the split stopped with `no bundle has a Shader type tree of the same hash to borrow`.
- **Cause:** a Unity player with no bundle to borrow a tree from: its serialized files have no type trees, and no bundle carries a `Shader` tree of the same hash.
- **Fix:** `unityfs.py` carries the layouts written out (`KNOWN_TREES`; `tree_from_layout` builds the tree a file with type trees would have), and the split's second pass gives one to the stripped files when no bundle lent a tree of that hash. Only there: taken in the first pass, Ace Attorney Investigations Collection's player files came before its `unitybuiltinshaders` bundle and the built-ins' source column and package indices moved, while with the second pass its tables come out byte-identical. The structs both the 2020.3 and 2022.3 layouts share are written once (`_SHADER_COMMON`), and the 2022.3 layout comes out byte-identical.
- **Found on:**
  - [Brain Memory Training Academy](../../dm/games/0100F5D01DA3A000/README.md) (Unity 2022.3.5f1: only `Data/*.assets`, `level*` and the two engine files in `Data/Resources`, none with type trees). The `Shader` type hash there (`ea424a5f…`) is the one in Ace Attorney Investigations Collection's bundles (2022.3.17f1): the same layout, about 30 structs. Checked against Ace Attorney's own tree: the same 2,324 nodes with the same alignment flags, and its 40 bundle `Shader` objects decode identically with either. 496 stage shaders: 439 engine built-ins, 46 of the game's, 11 of the player's in `main`, and the built-in VR blit's geometry shader, which does not build.
  - [Patrick's Parabox](../../dm/games/0100E44016C22000/README.md) (Unity 2020.3.30f1, built-in shaders only, no bundles) stopped there with hash `a30e3dcf…`, which one 18 KB shader bundle of Fire Emblem Engage (2020.3.18f1) carries; its tree is now in the table too (`SHADER_2020_3`: per subprogram global and local keyword indices, every subprogram in `m_SubPrograms`, no parameter blobs, keyword names or stage counts), equal to Engage's node for node (2,179 nodes, the alignment flags included), and each of the game's 48 `Shader` objects reads to its exact end.
  - [Raging Loop](../../dm/games/010020400E1C2000/README.md) (Unity 2018.3.10f1, no bundles: 48 `Shader` objects in `Data/*.assets` and `Data/Resources`) stopped there with hash `8db76007…`, and no game of that Unity version with a bundle was at hand, so `SHADER_2018_3` is written from AssetRipper's TypeTreeDumps (`StructsDump/release/2018.3.10f1.dump`), checked node for node against the dump (1,325 nodes, the alignment flags included). It is a layout of its own, older than the common structs: no conservative raster state, `BufferBinding` without `m_ArraySize`, `ConstantBuffer` without `m_IsPartialCB`, the parameters in each subprogram and no `m_CommonParameters`, one segment per platform (the pre-2019.3 form Cuphead's trees gave `shader_programs`). Each of the 48 objects reads to its exact end: 450 subprograms of version 201802150 (older than the local keyword list, which starts at 201806140).
  - [Return of the Obra Dinn](../../dm/games/010032E00E6E2000/README.md) (Unity 2017.4.28f1): hash `266d5311…`. `SHADER_2017_4` derives from the 2018.3 layout by removing `m_HasProceduralInstancingVariant` and `m_NonModifiableTextures`, with the instancing flag retaining the alignment. It matches all 1,313 nodes and alignment flags in [AssetRipper's release type tree](https://github.com/AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps/blob/main/StructsDump/release/2017.4.28f1.dump); the game note records the object census and exact-end checks.
  - [Donkey Kong Country Returns HD](../../dm/games/01009D901BC56000/README.md) (Unity 6000.0.28f1, no bundles): hash `2ec7223d…` matches BALL x PIT's Unity 6000.0.62f1 bundles. `SHADER_6000_0` derives from the 2022.3 layout by removing each pass's `m_EditorDataHash` and `m_Platforms` arrays and appending the shader's `m_AssetGUID`. The generated tree equals the bundle tree in types, field order and alignment flags; the game note records the object census and build results.
  - LEGO Builder's Journey (Unity 2021.2.13f1, provisional profile: every serialized file is stripped and no bundle carries a `Shader` tree): hash `d8236c9b…`. `SHADER_2021_2` is written from [AssetRipper's 2021.2.13f1 release tree](https://github.com/AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps/blob/main/StructsDump/release/2021.2.13f1.dump) and matches it node for node (2,174 nodes, the alignment flags included): its subprograms drop the local keyword list (as Unity 2022 does), keep their parameters and use signed 64-bit requirements; each pass appends `m_SerializedKeywordStateMask`, and the parsed form adds keyword names and flags. All 195 `Shader` objects read to their exact ends, and the split finds 9,410 stage shaders (3,567 vertex, 3,571 pixel, 2,271 compute, one geometry) in the player's files and `main`.
  - Dynablaster (Unity 2021.3.25f1, no bundles, provisional profile; seedds/nxnative#1147): hash `f0c18427…`. `SHADER_2021_3` derives from the 2021.2 layout by adding each program's `m_PlayerSubPrograms` and `m_ParameterBlobIndices` and the shader's `stageCounts`; the keyword state mask stays in the pass. It matches all 2,304 nodes and alignment flags of [AssetRipper's 2021.3.25f1 release tree](https://github.com/AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps/blob/main/StructsDump/release/2021.3.25f1.dump), and it read all 50 Shader objects of the three small stripped files on the reporter's Mac (1,771 shaders split).
  - Castlevania: Belmont's Curse's Digital Sound & Art Gallery (`01005F4027E94000`, on the Midnight Edition cartridge; Unity 6000.3.6f1, `main` build ID `79efa8c369b16c2a8b7993675b1f6ee3`; not the game itself, whose files are 2022.3): hash `57f0dd2d…`. `SHADER_6000_3` is the 6000.0 layout with `SInt64 m_AssetLocalIdentifierInFile` after `m_AssetGUID` (`Shader` version 4, from 6000.3.0f1 on; the 6000.3.0b4 dump still has version 3, which hashes like 6000.0). It matches all 2,306 nodes and alignment flags of AssetRipper's 6000.3.0f1, 6000.3.11f1 and 6000.3.20f1 release trees, and all 97 of the Gallery's stripped `Shader` objects read to their exact ends. The Gallery has not been run.
  - Dodgeball Academia (Unity 2019.4.21f1, provisional profile: every serialized file is stripped and no bundle carries a `Shader` tree): hash `ed2a06ce…`. `SHADER_2019_4` derives from the 2018.3 layout, checked node for node against [AssetRipper's 2019.4.21f1 release tree](https://github.com/AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps/blob/main/StructsDump/release/2019.4.21f1.dump) (1,481 nodes, the alignment flags included) and equal to the tree [Subnautica](../../dm/games/0100429011144000/README.md)'s Unity 2019.4.36f1 bundles carry under the same hash: global and local keyword indices in each subprogram, a ray tracing program in each pass, and 2019.3's segments (one `offsets` / `compressedLengths` / `decompressedLengths` list per platform); the parameters stay in each subprogram, without `m_CommonParameters`. All 62 `Shader` objects read to their exact ends, and the split finds 7,996 stage shaders (4,162 vertex, 3,778 pixel, 55 compute, one geometry) where 11 came from `main` alone.
  - Lovers in a Dangerous Spacetime 1.0.4 (provisional profile, Unity 5.5.0p1): hash `4496e93f2179252104401c8dda0a1751`. `SHADER_5_5` matches all 1,222 nodes (types, names, order and alignment) of [AssetRipper's 5.5.0f3 release tree](https://github.com/AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps/blob/main/StructsDump/release/5.5.0f3.dump). All 69 Shader objects read to their exact ends; the split succeeds with 548 stage shaders (284 vertex, 264 pixel; 544 from assets, four from main). Older arrays mostly do not align, but the channel vector aligns on its parent. No shader requirements, procedural instancing or nonmodifiable textures. Extraction validated only: translation and gameplay remain unchecked.
- **Note:** a Unity version whose `Shader` hash is not in the table still stops with the same message; read its tree from any bundle of that Unity version, or from AssetRipper's TypeTreeDumps for that exact version (compared node for node, the alignment flags included), and add it with its hash to `KNOWN_TREES` and to `dm/gfx/tests/test_unity_known_trees.py`. To take one file out of an NSP without extracting the RomFS, filter `nx_extract`'s `Copy` by path.

### Unity 6 Shader objects

- Read with no change: Hollow Knight: Silksong's 2,068 Addressables bundles carry their type trees (they report the engine version `0.0.0`, Unity's stripped version string), so no layout had to be written out.
- BALL x PIT keeps most of its shaders in the player's loose serialized files (`resources.assets`, `sharedassets1.assets`, the built-in resources), which have no type trees; they borrow the `Shader` tree its 10 shader bundles carry (`shader_split.py` defers a file that raises `NoTree` to a second pass), so it needed no layout either.
- **Found on:** [Hollow Knight: Silksong](../../dm/games/010013C00E930000/README.md) (Unity 6000.0.50f1): 2,450 stage shaders: 1,189 vertex, 1,193 pixel, 67 compute and one geometry shader, which does not build; 11 of them are the player's own in `main`. [BALL x PIT](../../dm/games/010086A022444000/README.md) (6000.0.62f1): 38,099 shaders, most of them in the loose files. [Silver Pines](../../dm/games/0100D5B026844000/README.md) (6000.3.15f1): 56,386 stage shaders in its Addressables bundles, read with no change.

### Unity ComputeShader objects

- **Symptom:** the 3D battle scene was black behind a correct UI, with `[RED] shader 854a8afc9d07f88d is not in the shader map` and `the dispatched program has no compute shader`.
- **Cause:** `DM_NVN_SHDUMP` saved the program; its code bytes were in `Data/sharedassets2.assets`, uncompressed, inside a `ComputeShader` (class 72): the post-processing stack's colour grading LUT baker (`Lut3DBaker#KGenLut3D_AcesTonemap`), so the LUT the scene is graded through stayed black. The split read only `Shader` objects. A `ComputeShader` holds per platform variant a list of kernels, each with its unique variants; a variant's `code` is the program code of a `Shader` subprogram (the 0x60-byte stage header, only the compute block present) and its `threadGroupSize` matches control +0x748.
- **Fix:** `unity_compute` in `shader_split.py` reads them beside the `Shader` objects of every serialized file. The game has no bundle with a class 72 tree, so `unityfs.py` carries Unity 2022.3.54f1's layout (`COMPUTE_2022_3`, written from AssetRipper's TypeTreeDumps for that version; all 12 objects decode, `resourcesResolved` true at each end). 85 compute shaders came in (86 with the player's own).
- **Found on:** [Monster Train 2](../../dm/games/010051701FB46000/README.md) (Unity 2022.3.54f1).
- **Older layouts:** Unity 2019.4 stores each kernel's code directly, without `uniqueVariants` or `variantMap`. `unity_compute` accepts that form too, and `COMPUTE_2019_4` supplies the stripped-file layout. Found on [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md) (2019.4.27f1); its missing runtime shader is the built-in GPU skinning kernel. The layout matches the release dump's 116 nodes and alignment flags, and both built-in compute objects decode to their exact ends.
- **Unity 2021.3:** `COMPUTE_2021_3` covers stripped class 72 hash `abd9135b8ce83d043fef4e9ec7f53366`, matching all 144 nodes and alignment flags in [AssetRipper's 2021.3.18f1 release tree](https://github.com/AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps/blob/main/StructsDump/release/2021.3.18f1.dump). Kernel parents use `variantMap`, global and local keywords; each kernel holds byte-vector code, a `staticvector` thread group size and signed 64-bit requirements. Unlike 2022.3 there are no `uniqueVariants`, `variantIndices` or dynamic keywords; both versions store code as bytes. `resourcesResolved` aligns after every platform variant, including when another platform follows. The synthetic tests cover two platforms, multiple keyword variants, byte-code padding, exact object consumption and shader provenance. Found on Shin chan: Shiro and the Coal Town (Unity 2021.3.18f1): [PR #440](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/440) reports recovered GPU skinning kernels and correctly rendered characters on the iPhone; that gameplay result has not been independently repeated here.
- **Unity 2020.3:** `COMPUTE_2020_3` covers stripped class 72 hash `d0171b849bb064b2424893e7d4809be5`. It is the 2021.3 layout without the kernel's trailing `SInt64 requirements`: kernel parents use `variantMap` (not aligned) with global and local keywords, code is a byte vector and `threadGroupSize` a staticvector (not aligned). All 143 nodes and alignment flags match [AssetRipper's 2020.3.27f1 release tree](https://github.com/AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps/blob/main/StructsDump/release/2020.3.27f1.dump); the synthetic tests cover the 2021.3 fixture without `requirements`. Found on MADiSON (`0100C3F0181C8000`, 1.1, Unity 2020.3.27f1, SDK 13, provisional profile): `unity default resources` and `sharedassets0.assets` skipped their ComputeShader objects, the split had 1 compute shader, and the run logged 6 `shader … is not in the shader map` and one `the dispatched program has no compute shader`. The 3D scene was black on an iPhone 16 while the crosshair, menus and sound worked. With the layout the split takes 91 compute shaders (8,795 in all), the Mac run logs no red line and the scene draws; those results are the author's, and the fixed build has not run on the iPhone. The hash is not MADiSON's alone: Unity's type hash (MD4 over each node's type, name, byte size, array flag, version and align flag) recomputed from the release trees of 2020.3.0f1, 18f1, 27f1, 30f1 and 48f1 gives it each time, and the same formula gives 2021.3.18f1's `abd9135b…`. So the layout can also reach the registered 2020.3 games. [AI: THE SOMNIUM FILES - nirvanA Initiative](../../dm/games/0100713016CCC000/README.md) already has its compute kernels, since its bundles carry type trees. The tables of [Fire Emblem Engage](../../dm/games/0100A6301214E000/README.md) and [Patrick's Parabox](../../dm/games/0100E44016C22000/README.md) have no Unity compute kernels: if their stripped files hold ComputeShader objects, their next NSP pack adds them through the shader-only derived profile (below). Neither game's files were available to check this.
- **Unity 2017.4:** [Return of the Obra Dinn](../../dm/games/010032E00E6E2000/README.md) needs hash `20291e02…` for stripped `internalskinning` objects. `COMPUTE_2017_4` restores the nested buffer-counter fields; all 120 nodes and alignment flags match the release dump. The game note records the object census and exact-end checks.
- **Unity 6000.3:** `COMPUTE_6000_3` covers stripped class 72 hash `43a0948a3f403068c0b31bff90ba0c9a`: the 2022.3 layout with an `int resType` after each resource's `texDimension` and `threadGroupSize` a `staticvector` again. All 159 nodes and alignment flags match [AssetRipper's 6000.3.11f1 release tree](https://github.com/AssetRipper/TypeTreeDumps/blob/main/StructsDump/release/6000.3.11f1.dump) and its 6000.3.0b4, 6000.3.0f1 and 6000.3.20f1 trees; a synthetic test reads a fixture to its exact end and into the split. Found on Hello Kitty Party Land (Unity 6000.3.11f1, provisional profile): its characters drew as huge stretched triangles, with `[RED] … unity default resources: ComputeShader objects that no bundle lends a type tree to and unityfs.py has no layout for` in the split and `the dispatched program has no compute shader` in the run, since the built-in `Internal-Skinning` kernels were left out. [PR #857](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/857) reports that all 11 objects read to their exact ends (`Internal-Skinning` 40 kernels, `Internal-BlendShape` 9), the split finds 92 compute shaders instead of 1, and the Mac app draws the characters right; [PR #791](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/791) reports the same hash on another game. Neither result has been repeated here, since no game with this hash is available. [Silver Pines](../../dm/games/0100D5B026844000/README.md) (6000.3.15f1) already has these kernels in its table without this layout.
- **Registered tables after a reader update:** a newly supported layout can add kernels to an existing game's shader map. For registered module builds, the NSP script permits a shader-only derived profile only when every original shader's package, stage and source, every original fact and every evidence pair remain unchanged (package indices may move), and every added shader is compute with matching key/source evidence from `unity_compute`. Each added kernel must also have its fact row. Registered settings, build rows and cuts are retained; the repository tables are not edited. A cached comparison pins the registered tables, split inputs and comparison code. Deleted or changed original rows, graphics additions and additions without reader evidence still stop the build. The incremental-build tests cover acceptance, rejection and cache invalidation.
- **Note:** a file whose `ComputeShader` has no tree still goes to the second pass; if it has none there either, the split prints a `[RED]` line and leaves its kernels out instead of stopping. The original bring-up omitted stripped objects in [Cuphead](../../dm/games/0100A5C00D162000/README.md) (2), [Fire Emblem Engage](../../dm/games/0100A6301214E000/README.md) (4) and [Raging Loop](../../dm/games/010020400E1C2000/README.md) (2 built-ins; no compute dispatched). Their type hashes were not recorded, so their compatibility with the newer layouts remains unchecked. Unity 2017.4.9f1, 2017.4.17f1 and 2017.4.28f1's published class 72 release dumps are identical, but that does not establish the hashes in Cuphead or AI: THE SOMNIUM FILES.

### GLSLC outputs in a Unity TextAsset

- **Symptom:** black, jagged holes in the ground of a map (whole tiles of the terrain mesh missing while others draw), with about 25 distinct `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map` keys and `program has no vertex shader`, on a Unity game whose `Shader` objects all split cleanly.
- **Cause:** the game draws its maps and effects with programs of its own, not Unity's: `DM_NVN_SHDUMP` saved 24 of them, and none was in any `Shader` subprogram of the 1,606 `Shader` objects in the RomFS (all of them decoded); their code areas were in `Data/resources.assets`, uncompressed, inside the `TextAsset` (class 49) `shader.switch.vfxsb` (11 MB, first word 0x20160817): 1,160 whole GLSLC outputs of a vertex and a pixel stage each. Their control sections carry GLSLC's full header (control +0x20 = 0xa10 and more), unlike the 0x850-byte sections Unity 2018 stores in its subprograms. The split read only `Shader` and `ComputeShader` objects, and the fallback for loose GLSLC outputs skips Unity files.
- **Fix:** `unity_text_glslc` in `shader_split.py` reads the `TextAsset`s of a serialized file that holds the GLSLC magic (`unityfs.text_assets`: name and bytes, the two leading fields every Unity version shares, so no type tree is needed) through `glslc_shaders`, which keeps only outputs that pass its checks. A file without the magic is not decoded, so other games' tables do not change. This one TextAsset added 1,207 distinct shaders (the game has 31,635 in all), with 1,156 vertex / pixel pairs; they covered all 57 keys the runs had reported missing.
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY CRYSTAL CHRONICLES Remastered Edition](../../dm/games/0100CE4010AAC000/README.md) (Unity 2018.4.2f1): the intro's overworld shot.

### Unreal Engine 5 shader code archives (Oodle)

- **How it is stored:** `ShaderArchive-Global-SF_GLSL_SWITCH_MOBILE-SP_GLSL_SWITCH_MOBILE.ushaderbytecode` (1,219 shaders) and `ShaderArchive-Octopath_Traveler0_Chunk0-…` (9,377), both stored in the pak ([01](../agents/01-extract.md)). Version 2 archives of shader map hashes, shader hashes, shader map entries, shader entries `{u64 offset, u32 size, u32 raw size, u8 frequency}` (17 bytes packed; frequency 0 vertex, 3 pixel, 5 compute), preload entries and shader indices, then the code.
- **Compression:** every shader is Oodle-compressed (Mermaid, block header `8C 0A`). There is no Oodle decoder in this repository, so the game's own `OodleLZ_Decompress` (statically linked into `main`; the host finds it as the one function that takes the address of the library's own name string "OodleLZ_Decompress", its profiler scope: Octopath Traveler 0 `main+0x4f77e10`, Pokemon Legends: Z-A `main+0x1a9c9e0`) decodes them in the Mac host: `DM_OODLE_JOB` (`dm/host/hle_oodle.c`), the NSP script's `decode_ue_shaders` for any game with Unreal paks.
- **Record:** a decoded shader is an `LSLG` record: `LSLG`, a stage tag (`SV`, `SP`, `SC`), a header with the source name (`/Engine/Private/…usf:MainPS`), then `u32` code length, the code area, `u32` control length (0x880), the control section and a 24- or 29-byte tail, all at unaligned offsets (`uepak.lslg`).
- **Check:** the host keys came out exactly: 37 of 37 shaders a first run reported missing were in the two archives.
- **Found on:** [Octopath Traveler 0](../../dm/games/01005270232F2000/README.md): 10,324 unique shaders (1,457 vertex, 6,346 pixel, 2,521 compute); vertex and pixel shaders combine per draw, so every vertex shader is paired with the single-stage cover. Bindings: separate textures and samplers, as Engage's (`0x168` / `0x568`); 61 vertex shaders pick a texture slot at run time (profile key `tex_dyn_slots`, [Texture slots picked at run time](#texture-slots-picked-at-run-time)), 2,084 shaders do atomics on buffer images ([Image atomics](#image-atomics)).
- **Note:** 16 compute shaders do atomics on a 3D `r32ui` image, all Unreal's paged global distance field (`GlobalDistanceFieldCompositeObjects.usf:CompositeObjectsIntoPagesCS`), which the mobile renderer does not dispatch (none in any run). They build since image atomics are native ([Image atomics](#image-atomics)).

### Unreal Engine 4.27 shader code archives (LZ4)

- **How it is stored:** the desktop renderer's `ShaderArchive-Global-SF_GLSL_SWITCH.ushaderbytecode` (1,764 shaders) and `ShaderArchive-DOLL_Chunk0-SF_GLSL_SWITCH.ushaderbytecode` (63,185), stored in the pak: the same version 2 archives and the same `LSLG` records as [Unreal Engine 5's](#unreal-engine-5-shader-code-archives-oodle), but every shader is an LZ4 block (the first bytes are LZ4 tokens followed by `LSLG`; LZ4 is Unreal's default shader compression since 4.26).
- **Reader:** `uepak.py` decodes them itself (`lz4_shaders`): every compressed shader must decode to its raw size as an `LSLG` record, else the archive goes to the Oodle job as before.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined](../../dm/games/0100A9D01C446000/README.md) (Unreal Engine 4.27): 62,901 unique shaders (10,114 vertex, 50,756 pixel, 1,968 compute, 63 geometry).
- **Note:** its fragment shaders carry no output types either: one global shader writes integers bit-cast into a float output to an `R8Uint` target, listed in the profile's `shader_facts_manual.tsv` until 2026-11-16, when the [typed-output entry point](#typed-outputs-for-every-pixel-shader) took its place ([Integer fragment outputs declared by hand](#integer-fragment-outputs-declared-by-hand)).

### Unreal Engine 5.6 IoStore shader libraries

- **Symptom:** `[RED] the shader split found no shaders in main or the RomFS`, the split's log ending in `shaders: 0`, and no `UE shader decode` step, on a game whose container holds shader libraries and whose pak names `GlobalShaderCache-`: from 2026-11-04 until [issue #1277](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1277) was fixed, such a pak counted as inline.
- **How it is stored:** the desktop renderer (`SF_GLSL_SWITCH`) with the same `LSLG` records, but no archive in the pak: the two libraries are IoStore shader libraries in `FFRS-Switch.ucas`, whose shader groups are Oodle-compressed as a whole ([01](../agents/01-extract.md)). Each shader entry packs its frequency (bits 0–3), group (4–33) and offset in the group's decoded bytes (from bit 34), and a shader runs to the next one's offset.
- **Reader:** `uepak.py` writes them out as version 2 archives, so the split reads them as any other. The pak beside them can name the global shader cache as [an inline game's does](#unreal-engine-427-shaders-inline-in-iostore-export-bundles), so that name alone does not tell the two apart: `inline_in_containers` does, by the shader libraries.
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md) (Unreal Engine 5.6.1): 73,496 unique shaders (9,253 vertex, 61,305 pixel, 2,934 compute, 4 geometry). The symptom on its [Asian release](../../dm/games/0100B8A026F14000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### IoStore patch containers keep their shader groups in the base container

- **Symptom:** `ValueError: …/Glow-Switch_0_P.utoc: shader group 0 has no chunk` from `uepak.py io_archive` while the NSP script decodes the shaders (`decode_ue_shaders`, after `[4/8] UE shader decode`).
- **Cause:** an update's IoStore container is a patch: its shader libraries have the base game's chunk IDs (so they replace their tables), and an unchanged shader group is listed with the base container's chunk ID. `io_archive` looked every group up in the container the library chunk came from.
- **Fix:** `uepak.py` reads every `.utoc` under the RomFS (`io_tocs`, in mount order: the base game's containers, then `_P` patches and numbered `_0_P`, `_1_P`, … patches, `mount_order`, case-insensitive `_P` marker) and resolves a group's chunk ID in any of them; a library chunk ID two containers share is taken from the later (patch) one, whose tables are the current set. TOC metadata is read without a key; `Toc.read` refuses encrypted data without a key only when the selected library or group needs it, so an encrypted group-only container is refused and a fully superseded encrypted library is never read.
- **Found on:** LEGO Horizon Adventures (update v262144, Unreal Engine 5; provisional profile): the update's `Glow/Content/Paks/Glow-Switch_0_P.utoc` over the base's `Glow-Switch.utoc`; 20,889 shaders in its two libraries, 20,880 unique after the split.

### An Unreal pak key split into two 16-byte literals

- **Symptom:** `[RED] …/Nicola-Switch_0_P.pak: the index is encrypted and no window of main decrypts it` from the NSP script before `[5/8] UE shader decode` (the message now reads `no key in main (a 32-byte window or two 16-byte literals its code stores together) decrypts it`).
- **Cause:** `uepak.py find_key` tried every 32-byte window of `main`'s read-only and data segments. The engine gets the key from a callback that copies a 32-byte array into its argument (`UE_REGISTER_ENCRYPTION_KEY`), and clang compiled the copy as two 16-byte constants loaded from separate places: `adrp`, `adrp`, `ldr q0`, `ldr q1`, `stp q0, q1, [x0]`, `ret`, so the key's halves are not side by side and no window holds it.
- **Fix:** `find_key` first tries the keys the code builds that way (`code_keys`: each `stp qA, qB, [x0]` with both registers loaded by `ldr q` from an `adrp` page within the 8 instructions before it, a register written by anything else in between dropped), then the windows as before. The right key is the one that decrypts the index start to a short mount point, as for a window. Covered by `dm/tools/tests/test_uepak_key.py` (a synthetic `main` and pak, no game files).
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST III HD-2D Remake](../../dm/games/01003E601E324000/README.md) (Unreal Engine 4.27): 8 candidate pairs in `main`, the key found in about 2 s.

### Unreal Engine 4.18 shaders inline in cooked packages

- **How it is stored:** legacy version 4 paks with LZ5-compressed blocks ([01](../agents/01-extract.md)) and no shader code archive. Each material's shader map is serialized in its `.uexp` (38 to 87 shaders in the ones looked at) and the engine's global shaders in `Engine/GlobalShaderCache-GLSL_SWITCH.bin` (945: 597 pixel, 104 vertex, 241 compute, 3 geometry). Each shader's code is a byte array (`i32` length, then the bytes) holding one zlib stream that inflates to the same `LSLG` record as Unreal Engine 5's.
- **Reader:** `shader_split.py` (`ue_legacy_jobs`, `ue_inline_shaders`) decodes every file of the newest copy of each path with the game's own LZ5 and takes each zlib stream that inflates whole from exactly the length in front of it and starts with `LSLG` (every such stream in the files looked at). Records stored uncompressed are taken too, but none were seen.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST XI S](../../dm/games/01006C300E9F0000/README.md): 38,892 unique shaders (4,654 vertex, 33,981 pixel, 234 compute, 23 geometry) from 1,740 files; vertex and pixel shaders are paired as single-stage programs, as for Octopath Traveler 0.

### Unreal Engine 4.27 shaders inline in cooked packages (LZ4)

- **Symptom:** `the RomFS has Unreal paks, but none … holds a shader code archive`, then `the shader split found no shaders in main or the RomFS`.
- **How it is stored:** a version 11 pak (Zlib blocks, index not encrypted) with no `.ushaderbytecode`: the project turned `bShareMaterialShaderCode` off, so each material keeps its shaders in its own `.uexp` and the engine's global shaders are in `Engine/GlobalShaderCache-SF_GLSL_SWITCH.bin`, as in [Unreal Engine 4.18](#unreal-engine-418-shaders-inline-in-cooked-packages). Unreal 4.27 serializes each one as an entry of `FShaderMapResourceCode`: the code (`i32` length, then an LZ4 block, the engine's shader compression since 4.26), the raw size (`i32`) and the frequency (`u8`).
- **Reader:** `uepak.py` `inline_shaders` tells such a pak from one with archives by its directory index (cooked packages and no `.ushaderbytecode`; an encrypted index is read with `main`'s key, [Unreal Engine 5.1 shaders inline in encrypted IoStore containers](#unreal-engine-51-shaders-inline-in-encrypted-iostore-containers)), and `shader_split.py` `ue_legacy_jobs` reads it file by file, Zlib and LZ4 blocks decoded in Python (`Pak.read`), so no host runs. `ue_inline_shaders` finds each LZ4 block by its first literals, the record's `LSLG`, with the token and literal length bytes in front of it, and takes it when it decodes to exactly the raw size after the block.
- **Found on:** [OCTOPATH TRAVELER II](../../dm/games/0100A3501946E000/README.md): 13,980 unique shaders (1,910 vertex, 11,346 pixel, 694 compute, 30 geometry) from the 3.3 GB pak in 13 s.

### Unreal Engine 4.27 shaders inline in IoStore export bundles

- **Symptom:** `the RomFS has Unreal paks, but none … holds a shader code archive` from the NSP script, on a game with a `.pak` beside a `.utoc` and `.ucas`.
- **How it is stored:** the same inline records as [the paks' `.uexp` files](#unreal-engine-427-shaders-inline-in-cooked-packages-lz4), `bShareMaterialShaderCode` being off, with IoStore on as well: the cooked packages are the container's export bundles (chunk type 2 in Unreal 4.27's numbering, TOC version 3), Oodle-compressed block by block, and the pak beside it keeps everything else, the global shader cache (`Engine/GlobalShaderCache-SF_GLSL_SWITCH.bin`) too. The pak's directory has neither `.ushaderbytecode` nor `.uexp`, so it was taken for one with archives.
- **Reader:** `uepak.py` `inline_shaders` also takes the global shader cache with no `.ushaderbytecode` as inline when the containers in the pak's folder hold export bundles and no [shader libraries](#unreal-engine-56-iostore-shader-libraries) (`inline_in_containers`; with neither a `.uexp` nor such a container the NSP script stops rather than read the global shaders alone), and `shader_split.py` `ue_legacy_jobs` then also reads the export bundles of the containers beside such a pak, the newest copy of each chunk ID (`mount_order`), through the game's own Oodle in the host's tool mode. Bulk data chunks are skipped. `Toc.names` reads the container's directory index, so a shader's label names its package, as for a pak, and `shader_groups` works the same way.
- **Found on:** [Mario & Luigi: Brothership](../../dm/games/01006D0017F7A000/README.md): 45,824 unique shaders from 8.8 GB of export bundles in 39 s.

### An entry of a package's export list read as an Oodle shader table

- **Symptom:** the shader split reports `the game's decoder in the Mac host failed on a 8 → 30650 byte stream`, the host's output says `asked to decode Legacy compressor; not supported!`, and a run of the NSP script stops with `file(s) could not be read` (a provisional profile goes on without those shaders).
- **Cause:** `uepak.shader_map_entries` scans every Unreal file for a stream's two first bytes and holds a table by its two counts and its walk. An export of a split package's summary can pass all of that as a one-entry table: name number 1 is the count, flags 8 the stream's length, the 8-byte size `0xb8c` (`8C 0B`, then zeros) the stream, the offset 30650 the raw size. `ue_inline_shaders` then took the decoder's refusal as fatal, where its zlib and LZ4 scans take it as no stream, and the failure cost the whole job, about 1/64 of the pak's bytes, not the one file.
- **Fix:** the decode is the proof. `ue_inline_shaders` passes `shader_map_entries` a check of each table's first entry: a stream the decoder refuses, a stored one that is no record, or a game whose `main` has no decoder at all makes it no table, and it hides nothing inside its span. A stream that decodes to exactly its raw size is a real one, so a record of another layout stays red, as do a later entry that fails and a host that dies. `ue_packages` reads each package of a job on its own, so one that does not read costs itself. Nothing depends on the summary's layout, so this holds for every package version and for `.uexp` files and export bundles too.
- **Found on:** [OCTOPATH TRAVELER II](../../dm/games/0100A3501946E000/README.md), update 1.1.1, by PR #1333: six summary files matched, four jobs were lost, 11,832 of 13,980 shaders were left (the PR's measurement). The scan came with [Unreal Engine 5.4's tables](#unreal-engine-54-shaders-for-the-switch-2), so every game with inline Unreal shaders was exposed.

### Unreal Engine 5.4 shaders for the Switch 2

- **Symptom:** `shaders: 0` from the split, with no red line, on a game whose pak holds `Engine/GlobalShaderCache-SP_GLSL_SAGE.bin` (`Sage` is the engine's name for the platform).
- **How it is stored:** inline as in [the 4.27 export bundles](#unreal-engine-427-shaders-inline-in-iostore-export-bundles), `bShareMaterialShaderCode` being off, in the TOC version 6 container's export bundles, which are chunk type 1 in Unreal Engine 5's numbering. Two things differ. The code of each `FShaderMapResourceCode` entry is one Oodle stream, not LZ4. And the record is NVN2's, with no `LSLG`: a 0x3e-byte header, `u32` name length, the name (`Global/<type>/0:/Engine/Private/<file>.usf|<entry>`), `u32` code length, the code area, `u32` control length, the control section (version 0xc9), a 12-byte tail. The frequency byte after each entry names the stage as in Unreal's `EShaderFrequency`.
- **Reader:** `uepak.shader_map_entries` finds a table by its first entry's stream (`8C 0A` is Mermaid) and takes it only when the count before the first entry, the same count before the 20-byte hashes, and a walk over all its entries agree: those two bytes also turn up inside other data, and decoding such a false start aborts the decoder (85 of 5,341 candidates in the global cache). `uepak.sage_record` splits each decoded stream by its lengths and the control section's own. `Toc.export_bundles` now takes UE5's chunk type as well; a container is read only beside a pak `inline_shaders` accepts, so FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE and Octopath Traveler 0 are unaffected.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): 133,154 unique shaders (45,614 vertex, 60,840 pixel, 21,654 compute, 5,041 mesh, 5 geometry) from 19 GB of container in 76 s.

### Unreal Engine 4.22 to 4.25 paks (version 8)

- **Symptom:** `[RED] the shader split found no shaders in main or the RomFS`; the survey counts the game's Unreal paks, but no `UE shader decode` step runs and the split reads none of them.
- **Cause:** the paks are version 8, which `uepak.py` did not read (only 4 and 11): `footer` raised, and `unreal_paks` skipped both paks as no Unreal paks. Version 8 keeps version 4's per-file index, but its footer is version 11's (key GUID, encrypted flag, magic 0xcc from the end, five 32-byte compression method names after the SHA-1), and an FPakEntry's compression method is an index (from 1) into those names instead of version 4's flags.
- **Fix:** `uepak.py` reads version 8 as a legacy pak: its footer as version 11's, its index as version 4's, each file's method named from the footer (`Zlib`, decoded in Python by `Pak.read`). The shaders are inline in the cooked packages and `Engine/GlobalShaderCache-GLSL_SWITCH.bin`, as in [Unreal Engine 4.18](#unreal-engine-418-shaders-inline-in-cooked-packages), so `shader_split.py` `ue_legacy_jobs` reads them unchanged. `dm/tools/tests/test_uepak_v8.py` builds a version 8 pak with a Zlib file and a stored one.
- **Found on:** DRAGON BALL Z: KAKAROT + A NEW POWER AWAKENS SET (`010051C0134F8000`, 1.00, SDK 9.3.1): 43,113 shaders from 3,776 files in 38 s; it plays from the title through the opening fight.

### An encrypted legacy Unreal pak with the shaders inline

- **Symptom:** the shader split says `[RED] /<pak>: the pak cannot be read (<path>: the index is encrypted and no key was given); its shaders are left out`, then `[RED] the shader split found no shaders in main or the RomFS` when that pak holds all of them.
- **Cause:** the pak's index and its entries are AES-encrypted. `shader_split.py` `ue_legacy_jobs` opened legacy paks without a key, so the index did not parse and the pak was left out. The paks with archives already took their key from `main` ([An Unreal pak key split into two 16-byte literals](#an-unreal-pak-key-split-into-two-16-byte-literals)); the inline path did not.
- **Fix:** `ue_legacy_jobs` takes each pak's key from `uepak.Keys`, the key finder the shader code archives use too (`uepak.job`): for a pak whose footer says the index is encrypted it tries each `main` it is given (base game and update) with `uepak.find_key`, a key found once first. Each job carries the key, so `ue_legacy_shaders` decrypts the entries too. With no key in any main the pak is reported in red and skipped. Covered by `dm/tools/tests/test_uepak_legacy_key.py` (a synthetic encrypted version 4 pak and `main`, no game files).
- **Found on:** Little Nightmares II (`010097100EDD6000`, 1.4, v262144; one version 8 pak): 20,170 shaders from 57 files in 9:38; it reaches the title screen and plays on the Mac.

### Unreal Engine 5.1 shaders inline in encrypted IoStore containers

- **Symptom:** the NSP script stops at `[8/11] UE shader decode` with `[RED] …/pakchunk1-Switch.pak: the index is encrypted and no key in main (a 32-byte window or two 16-byte literals its code stores together) decrypts it`, although `pakchunk0-Switch.pak` opened with `main`'s key. Past that: `the RomFS has Unreal paks, but none … holds a shader code archive`, then `the shader record at … (frequency 5) is not a record of the known layout` for every package.
- **How it is stored:** inline as in [the 4.27 export bundles](#unreal-engine-427-shaders-inline-in-iostore-export-bundles) (`bShareMaterialShaderCode` off), with four differences. Every pak's index is AES-encrypted, with the key of GUID zero that `main` holds. Beside each container (`pakchunk0-17-Switch.utoc`, TOC version 5, flags compressed, encrypted and indexed) is a pak, and all but `pakchunk0`'s are empty: 365 bytes, no file, mount point `/`. The containers are encrypted with the same key, data blocks and directory index alike. And each `FShaderMapResourceCode` entry is one Oodle stream, as in [Unreal Engine 5.4 for the Switch 2](#unreal-engine-54-shaders-for-the-switch-2), but it decodes to the Switch's `LSLG` record (`SP_GLSL_SWITCH`).
- **Cause:** `find_key` took a key only when the index decrypted to `../`, so it refused the right key on the empty paks. `inline_shaders` filed every encrypted index under "archives" without reading it (Octopath Traveler 0's case), so the NSP script ran the archive decode, which found nothing. `ue_legacy_jobs` skipped encrypted containers, and `Toc` dropped an encrypted directory index. Finally, the Oodle table path of `ue_inline_shaders` took only NVN2 records.
- **Fix:** all in `uepak.py` and `shader_split.py`, for every game:
  - `find_key` accepts any short mount point that is a path (it starts with `.` or `/`, printable up to its NUL).
  - `inline_shaders` reads an encrypted index with the key that `unreal_paks(…, mains=)` gets from `uepak.Keys`. Every caller passes the ExeFS `main`: `profile_guess.build_sources`, `uepak.job` and the split. A pak that names no file is neither kind (`None`).
  - `ue_legacy_jobs` reads an encrypted container with the key of the pak beside it, else the first key found, and its jobs carry that key to `ue_io_shaders`.
  - `Toc` decrypts the directory index with the key, so shaders are labelled by their packages.
  - The Oodle table path takes an `LSLG` record through the same `take` as the zlib and LZ4 scans. A stored record is left to the plain `LSLG` scan, so it is not taken twice.
  - Covered by `test_uepak_key.py` (`test_root_mount_point`) and `test_uepak_iostore.py` (`test_an_encrypted_index_is_read_with_mains_key`, `test_split_reads_an_encrypted_container_with_the_key_of_the_pak_beside_it`, `test_lslg_records_in_an_oodle_table`). None needs game files.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON BALL Sparking! ZERO](../../dm/games/010035F022078000/README.md): 14,842 unique shaders (9,927 pixel, 3,698 vertex, 1,215 compute, 2 geometry) from 18 containers in 31 s. All 2,125 entries of its global shader cache are `LSLG` records.

### Unreal Engine 3 shader caches (Oodle packages)

- **Symptom:** `the shader split found no shaders in main or the RomFS`, or `no compressed chunk at … (the chunks do not run to the end of the package)` for zero-padded `.xxx` packages.
- **How it is stored:** the engine's global shaders are in `GlobalShaderCache-Switch.bin` (magic `BMSG`, not compressed), the materials' in `CombinedShaderCache-Switch.xxx`, an Unreal Engine 3 package (tag `0x9E2A83C1`, a positive file version after it: 864; Unreal Engine 4's is a negative legacy version). After the package summary come compressed chunks, each {tag, block size, compressed and raw size of the chunk, then each block's two sizes} and the blocks: 0x20000 bytes of Oodle Mermaid each (`8C 0A`), a block stored whole having its two sizes equal. Each shader is a record {`u32` length, `u32` code area length, `u32` control length (0x880), in the global cache one more `u32`}, then its code area and right after it its control section.
- **Reader:** `shader_split.py` `ue3_package` / `ue3_body` decode a package's chunks (Zlib in Python, Oodle with the game's own decoder through the Mac host's tool mode, `DM_OODLE_SERVE`), and `ue3_shaders` finds each record by its control section's magic, its code area length (+0x704) back to the code area's magic, and the two lengths in front. The chunk walk accepts an all-zero tail as package padding. Any nonzero byte in a tail that cannot form a chunk is still an error. `profile_guess.py` counts packages whose first block is Oodle, so the NSP script gives the split the host.
- **Found on:** [Batman: Arkham Knight](../../dm/games/0100ACD0163D0000/README.md) has 1,847 records in the global cache and 22,874 in the combined one, with 21,923 keys after the profile's [`shader_key_skip`](#control-sections-shared-among-shaders-that-differ-only-in-their-compile-information). Its 4,058 packages decoded in about a minute. None of 68 sampled packages outside the combined cache holds a shader. Borderlands: Game of the Year Edition (an upstream profile; NX 18 has the code, not the profile) pads packages to 32 KiB, which caused all 922 compressed packages to fail the walk.

### Task and mesh shaders in BNSH, and indirect mesh draws

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader <key> is not in the shader map` for shaders whose code is in a `.bfsha` the split reads, `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferDrawMeshTasksIndirect`, and grass and ferns missing.
- **Cause:** a BNSH program's stage pointers went only to compute (+0x30). NVN2's task and mesh stages follow at +0x40 and +0x48, so the split saw only the pixel shaders of such a program. The draw takes a GPU address, whose record the game's culling writes earlier in the frame: zeros when it is recorded.
- **Fix:** `bnsh_shaders` reads the two pointers, taking only one that leads to a control section of that stage, as an older BNSH's program ends before them. Metal's indirect mesh draw takes `{x, y, z}`, not `{count, first}`, so `mdx_run` copies the count on the GPU into `{count, 1, 1}` before the render pass, and the task or object function reads `{count, first}` from the record ([Mesh draws with no task shader above 1024 workgroups](rendering.md#mesh-draws-with-no-task-shader-above-1024-workgroups)). Reading the record on the CPU took 73 % of the submit thread in Kokiri Forest, waiting for the GPU.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 491 mesh and 17 task shaders in `Foliage*.bfsha` and `Model*.bfsha`.

### Float atomics in storage memory

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's picture is far too bright and warm wherever the view opens out: lifted blacks, yellow skin, a pale veil. The build log has `Invalid reduction type: F32FtzRn`, easy to miss among thousands of lines.
- **Cause:** RED.F32.FTZ.RN (0x98e, type 3) adds a float to a word of memory, and the translator dropped it with that log line. agl's scene analysis sums luminance this way (`probe_analyze`, `shadow_analyze`, `sky_analyze`, `cube_map_probe_analyze`, `inner_mask_analyze`, and Foliage and TerrainGround variants). The CPU reads those buffers back into exposure: the sums stayed 0, so it applied its full negative correction. The tone map itself was right.
- **Fix:** `InstEmitMemory.cs` `EmitAtomicAddF32`: a compare-and-swap loop on the word, in `EmitAtomicOp`, so ATOM's float form gets it too. Metal's buffer atomics have no float add in the form the chain uses. A chain change. F16x2FtzRn stays a log line: no shader here uses it.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py FloatRed`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 28 of its seen shaders; the frame's mean brightness at Navi's close-up went from 0.71 to 0.38.

### textureGatherOffsets and a loop over its offsets

- **Symptom:** dotted horizontal rows in blocks of the picture, in smooth and blurred areas, from a Switch 2 game's upscaler: the frame before it is clean and the frame after it is not. The same input gives a different picture each run. Bright scenes show more of them.
- **Cause:** spirv-cross's `spvGatherConstOffsets` calls `gather()` in a loop with `coffsets[i]`. Metal takes that offset only as a constant expression. At the chain's `-O1` the loop stays, the compiler accepts it without a word, and the gathers read texels at offsets that differ between lanes and between runs. agl's NDSR network builds its 28x28 input tile with these gathers, so some of its lanes read zeros for a channel (at Ocarina of Time's first shared-memory barrier, a word's high half was the luma without its green term), and the network drew that as rows.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_rewrite.py` `gather_offsets`: each call of `spvGatherConstOffsets` and `spvGatherCompareConstOffsets` becomes its four gathers with the offsets written out, from the constant array the call names. A call whose array is not such a constant stops the shader. A chain change.
- **Squares:** when the four offsets form a 2x2 square (b, b+(1,0), b+(0,1), b+(1,1), in any order), the call is one gather at b with a swizzle (`gather_quad`). Each offset's texel is the (i0, j0) corner of its own footprint. With integer offsets, those corners are the four texels of b's footprint, each wrapped on its own either way, so the result is the same bits. The test compares the one gather with the four on the GPU at 49 positions per lane, from four texels before the texture to four past it, clamped and repeating. Ocarina of Time: 27 of its 32 shaders with gather offsets have squares, all of them compute (the NDSR networks), with 3 to 15 squares each. On an M6, standing on the Kokiri Forest deck, the frame's 4-dispatch compute encoder that holds the network went from 959–961 µs to 935.5 µs (two runs each, same session; the frame's GPU time shows no difference at that size). A contributor measured the network's largest dispatch on an M4 Pro at 845 → 779 µs (discussion #1789).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_gather_offsets.py`. Offline, one captured dispatch of `b106afba1a18faa9`: about 40 % of the pixels differed between three runs of the old shader; the rewritten one gave the same bytes each time, equal to a version that reads the texels explicitly, and took 3.85 ms against 4.16 ms.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 28 NDSR compute shaders of its seen shaders. Every game with agl's upscaler builds it the same way. Not yet checked: whether this is the open cause of [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md)'s isolated bright dots.

### Runtime per-texel gathers wrap wide offsets on Metal

- **Symptom:** a per-texel gather (`TLD4.PTP`, `textureGatherOffsets` with offsets the shader computes) with a runtime offset of +8 reads as if the offset were -8. A ramp texture makes the wrong texel visible in the shader result.
- **Cause:** the translator passed runtime per-texel offsets to Metal's gather as its offset argument. Metal takes that argument as a signed four-bit value, -8 to 7, and wraps a wider one; `TLD4.PTP` packs six bits per offset. Upstream measured four Ocarina of Time compute shaders with offsets up to 19 on an M4 Pro.
- **Fix:** the translator's `QueryHostSupportsNonConstantTextureGatherOffsets` is false (`dmsh_xlate/Program.cs`, `IGpuAccessor.cs`), so `TexturePass.cs` lowers each runtime per-texel gather to four gathers at shifted coordinates at the base texture size, keeping the gather corner and the sampler's address mode. Depth-compare gathers take the same path (they kept native offsets before), and a NaN coordinate becomes 0 before the shift, as the SPIR-V generator does for native offsets. Constant offsets are unchanged. A chain change. Ported from NXNative `3ec5b785f` (PR #2831, MIT).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py GatherOffsets`. Upstream's M4 Pro probe matched explicit texel reads in 3.58 million samples; its shader regression changed three Ocarina of Time and two Donkey Kong Bananza shaders.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (upstream's review: `55fcda025feae496` and `ce8270569d33a7af` compute offsets in 0 to 19) and [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md).

### F2IP

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 243 at 0x… is not implemented`, or `ISBERD mode b8 in a task or mesh shader`, in the build.
- **Cause:** 0x243 converts two floats to saturated bytes and packs them, a in byte 1 and b in byte 0, the layout of F2FP's halves; bits 78..79 are the rounding, as in F2I. A task shader can read its own payload back with ISBERD's mode 0xb8, the mode of its 0x927 store.
- **Fix:** `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` `AF2ip`, `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs` `AIsberd`. The packing order comes from code that uses the result, not from documentation: the RZ form's byte is shifted to bytes 2 and 3, so b is byte 0.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py F2ip`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): `FoliageShader.sharcb` and 8 foliage mesh shaders.

### `nn::ndve`'s movie enhancement file

- **Symptom:** when an AV1 movie starts, `[RED] shader <key> is not in the shader map` for 11 compute shaders and `[RED] the dispatched program has no compute shader (or it is not mapped)`.
- **Cause:** `Movie/Ndve/Ndve-Nvn2-vid_seq5.dat.zs` holds the compute networks (`roulibr`) that upscale the game's movies, in a wrapper the split did not read: `NDVE`, a header, an NDSR pack, then the networks, each control section with its code area right behind it. The pack's entries are 8 bytes, a shader taking two, so a data entry moves the next shader by 8. The walk went by 16 bytes and lost it.
- **Fix:** `shader_split.py` reads the wrapper (`ndve_shaders`) and walks the pack by entry. On Ocarina of Time the walk also finds 3 more compute shaders in `agl_ndsr.pck`, whose records are the same; a profile gets them at its next split.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_split_ndve.py`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### The NDSR pack's record start

- **Symptom:** `draw skipped: no render pipeline (vertex - fragment -)` in a game's final passes; shaders "not in the shader map", all from `agl_ndsr.pck`. A short synthetic pack found by fuzzing raised `struct.error: iterative unpacking requires a buffer of a multiple of 16 bytes` while choosing the record start.
- **Cause:** the pack's records start at +0x10 in one SDK's version and +0x14 in another's, by the words in its header (Donkey Kong Bananza 0x300, 0x6efeff, 0xc0000; [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md) 0x40100, 0x80300, 0x90, 0x20c00).
- **Fix:** `ndsr_shaders` in `shader_split.py` scores up to 16 complete records at each start and takes the start with more pointers to NVN2 control sections. The two starts can leave different numbers of trailing bytes in a short pack; those partial records are excluded from the score, as from the subsequent record walk.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md).

### RAGE RPF6 archives and `rgxd` effects

- **Symptom:** the NSP script's split finds no shaders in a RomFS made of `.rpf` files; after a first reader, `[RED]` lines name groups of files that could not be read.
- **How it is stored:** Rockstar's RPF6 (big-endian, as on the PS3 and Xbox 360): a 16-byte header {`RPF6`, entry count, name table offset / 8, tag `0xfffffffd` = encrypted}, 20-byte entries, then a name table. The entry and name tables are AES-256 ECB decrypted 16 times over, with a key in `main`'s data segment. Entry 0 is the root directory; a directory's children are consecutive entries; a name hash is Jenkins one-at-a-time of the lower-case name, found by hashing every string in the name table. A plain file's data is raw, one zstd frame, a zlib stream, or raw deflate (`audio_switch.rpf`'s config files). A resource (flags bit 31) keeps its type in the offset word's low byte, so its offset is `(word & 0x7fffff00) × 8`, and its data starts with an `RSC\x05` header (12 bytes) or, when the flags are bit 31 alone, an `RSC\x85` header (16 bytes); `.wsc` scripts are AES encrypted like the tables as well. The shaders are RAGE effects (`.nvn` in `shaders.rpf`, and effects in `main`): `rgxd`, the parameter names, then one record per shader {u32, GLSLC's magic 0x19866891, u32 code length, u32 control length}, the code area, then the control section. A control section may carry bytes past its own length (`+0x20`), so it is compared at that length.
- **Fix:** `dm/gfx/tools/rpf6.py` reads the archives: `find_key` tries `main`'s 4-aligned 32-byte windows with at least 24 distinct bytes on an archive's first entry until it decrypts to the root directory (the key is never printed or written). Update 1.0.4 has the same key but not in one piece: its key setup loads the eight key words, each a big-endian u32 zero-extended to 64 bits, from four 16-byte constants, so `key_from_setup` follows the setup's loads and stores (it starts `cmp w1, #0; mov w8, #-3; csel w8, w8, w1, eq; cmn w8, #3` in both builds). `shader_split.py` reads every archive's files in parallel ranges (`rpf_jobs`, `rpf_shaders`), and `rgxd_shaders` reads effects in files and in `main`. Reading resources as plain files gave 145 groups of unreadable files; with the resource offsets, both headers, zlib, raw deflate and the scripts' encryption, none.
- **Found on:** [Red Dead Redemption](../../dm/games/01007820196A6000/README.md): 134 archives (11 GB), 3,413 shaders from 131 effects in `shaders.rpf` and 168 from `main`.
- **Check:** `rpf6.py <main NSO> <file.rpf>` lists an archive; the fuzz harness has an `rgxd` seed (`dm/tools/fuzz/fuzz_shader_split.py`).

### Double Eleven `.pkdswitch` packs
