- **Symptom:** after the RPF6 archives are read, the first screen's two shaders are still `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map`.
- **How it is stored:** Double Eleven's resource pack: the members one after another in `.pkdswitch`, and an index `.pkiswitch` beside it: u32 version 3, u32 0, u32 member count, then 16-byte entries {length, stored length, offset in the data file, name hash}; a member whose stored length differs from its length is one zlib stream. The shaders are whole GLSLC outputs (`langselect`: members 4 and 5, the screen's pixel and vertex shader); the other members are textures (`DFvN`) and fonts (`BMF`).
- **Fix:** `d11_pack_shaders` in `shader_split.py` reads a `.pkdswitch` through its index and scans each member for shaders.
- **Found on:** [Red Dead Redemption](../../dm/games/01007820196A6000/README.md): `d11extra/langselect.pkdswitch`, the language selection shown before the game.

## Control sections and code

### Code area and control section layout

- **Code area:** graphics = 0x30-byte header (magic `0x12345678`) + 0x50 SPH + instructions (at +0x80); compute = 0x30-byte header (magic `0x12345679`) + **zero padding, instructions at +0x100** (all 71 of Mario's).
- **Control section:** magic `0x98761234`; +0x700 = c1 offset in the code area, +0x704 = code size, +0x748 = compute thread-group size (u32 ×3), +0x754 = compute shared memory bytes.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### Control sections shorter than 0x880 bytes

- **Cause:** the control section stores its own length at +0x20. Mario's, Engage's, Dread's and 13 Sentinels' are all at least 0x880 bytes; 3D World has 2,888 of 0x850 bytes (its ShaderData bfsha and six layouts) and 0x848 in BAHS version 9, each followed directly by other data.
- **Fix:** the key hashes the first `min(0x880, control +0x20)` bytes (`ctl_len` in `shader_split.py`, `keys_hash` in `hle_nvn.c`); the other games' tables came out byte-identical.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md) (SDK 10).

### Program keys computed inside `nvnProgramSetShaders`

- **Symptom:** shaders reported missing at random, and their dumped control sections are garbage (Unity). Also: about 130 `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map` keys a boot, nearly all different from run to run, all single-stage programs, with `program has no vertex shader`, and every frame the same picture of grey noise; with `DM_NVN_SHDUMP` set the game draws correctly.
- **Cause:** NVN lets the caller free a program's control section once `nvnProgramSetShaders` returns; Unity does, and the host used to hash it at the first draw, so keys computed later hashed freed memory. The keys were then computed in `SetShaders` but kept by the NVNprogram's address, and Unity 2018.4 copies an NVNprogram after `SetShaders` and draws with the copy: the copy had no keys at its address, so its stages were hashed at the first draw again, from the freed section. `DM_NVN_SHDUMP` hid it, as it points every stage at a host copy of its section.
- **Fix:** the keys are computed inside `SetShaders` (`hle_nvn.c` `prog_key`) and are found by the program's serial, which a copy carries, as the Metal side finds the program's translation. A serial's record stays as long as the process (a copy can outlive the program it was made from; 64 bytes a `SetShaders`). Mario calls `SetShaders` for some programs with a code address that is not mapped yet, so a stage whose code is not in a registered memory pool at that moment is hashed at its first draw, from a host copy of its control section that `SetShaders` keeps.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Engage](../../dm/games/0100A6301214E000/README.md) and Unity games in general (the source names no other); [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md) (the unmapped code addresses); Ori and the Will of the Wisps (`01008DD013200000`, Unity 2018.4.24f1, 1.2.1: the program copies, 135 stages a boot; no profile yet).

- **Missing control sections (resolved, found by clang-tidy):** `prog_key` must return the hash failure without marking a cached key valid. Previously a serial-less stage returned success with uninitialized output, and a serial-backed stage cached a zero key. Resolution and prefetch now stop with a fatal diagnostic rather than reading an invalid key; `t_nvn` checks both forms and a successful retry after supplying a control section. No game failure was observed.

### Shader bytes the CPU cannot read

- **Symptom:** a crash in `CC_SHA256` under `keys_hash` / `prog_key` (SIGSEGV at a low address such as `0x100`, or SIGBUS on a page the game never mapped). Now `[RED] shader control section … is not readable` or `[RED] shader code area … +… is not readable (control …): no key, the stage is reported missing`, then the stage's usual "not in the shader map" line.
- **Cause:** the host hashes a stage's control section and code area to key it. A game took a buffer's address after its Finalize (S2Arm's host returned 0 for it) and passed 0 + 0x100 as the code address; another game's code area lay on a page it had not mapped; a control section can be freed before a stage keyed at its first use reads it.
- **Fix:** `hle_nvn.c` `shader_bytes_readable`, ported from S2Arm 2ec0fcea (its key part) and aad3c740 (Apache-2.0): before hashing, the control section's first bytes, then its key bytes, then the code area must be outside the lowest 64 KB and readable page by page by `vm_read_overwrite`, which returns an error instead of faulting. Otherwise the stage gets no key and is reported missing. A code area not yet in a pool at `nvnProgramSetShaders` is still keyed at its first use, with no red line. Not ported: 2ec0fcea's finalized-buffer address change (NX 18 keeps a finalized buffer's address as it was) and 2b2ed97e, whose `BindProgram` control read NX 18 does not make.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_nvn`: a stage with readable bytes is keyed; a code area at `0x100`, a code area on a page without access and a control section without access give no key and no fault; readable again, the same key.
- **Found on:** S2Arm's player reports (an iPad and a Switch 2 game; ported, not reproduced here).

### Control sections shared among shaders that differ only in their compile information

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map` for shaders whose control section and code area are both in the game files, but in different records: dumped (`DM_NVN_SHDUMP`), the control section is one record's and the code area another's.
- **Cause:** the engine shares one control section among shaders whose sections differ only in GLSLC's compile information (+0x778..+0x791 and +0x7d0..+0x7e7): `DM_NVN_PROGTRACE` showed the same control pointer set first with its own code area and later with another shader's. The GPU does not read those bytes, so on hardware it makes no difference; the host's key hashes them. Masked, Batman: Arkham Knight's 24,721 control sections are 5,615; the tools read nothing there (`dmsh_build.py` reads up to +0x75f).
- **Fix:** the profile key `shader_key_skip <offset>+<length>` (`0x770+0x78`): the key hashes that range as zeros, in `shader_split.py` (`key_ctl`, and `same_shader` ignores it) and in the host (`hle_nvn.c` `keys_hash`). Without the key, every game's keys are as before. It is a profile input of the shader chain, so the libraries follow it.
- **Found on:** [Batman: Arkham Knight](../../dm/games/0100ACD0163D0000/README.md): 40 shaders missing in the first 45 s before the key. With `0x7d0+0x18` alone the opening cutscene still met one whose section differed at +0x778, as [pull request #340](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/340) had found, so the range starts at +0x770.
- **Also found on:** [EA SPORTS FC 27](../../dm/games/0100C49025D3E000/README.md) (`0x7d0+0x18`): Frostbite's NVN renderer keeps one copy of each control section in a cache keyed by a hash that leaves out the compile information (`fb::ShaderPlatformDataNvn`, `s_controlBlockCacheMutex` in `Engine.Render.Core2.PlatformNvn.nro`); 10 programs were missing in the first menus, and the 10,342 keys are 7,873 shaders with the key. Its sections differed only at +0x7d0..+0x7e7 (+0x778 is zero in all of them).

### Compute code with and without a header

- **Cause:** compute code areas have a header in Mario (`0x12345679`), not in D2R. 3D World's particle compute shaders (SDK 10) start their instructions at code +0 (like D2R), not after a `0x12345679` header.
- **Fix:** `shader_split.py` accepts both; `dmsh_build.py` strips only a header that is there.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md) (header); [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md) (no header, SDK 10).

### Exact program start for the decoder

- **Symptom:** 12 compute shaders (particles, post effects) translated into an endless loop that hung the GPU at the first gameplay scene; SPIRV-Cross failed on 7 shaders instead of 2 ([SPIRV-Cross parse failures](#spirv-cross-parse-failures)).
- **Cause:** Ryujinx's decoder treats every 32-byte boundary *relative to the start* as a scheduling word. Mario's compute code was first fed from +0x30: the groups were misaligned by 0x10, and instructions were dropped or misread.
- **Fix:** the program start must be exact (compute instructions at +0x100). `dmsh_build.py` now refuses compute code whose padding is not zero.
- **Check:** for a new title, the first non-zero word after the header must be a control word, and the trailing `EXIT` (`e30000000007000f`) / `BRA`-to-self (`e2400fffff87000f`) must sit in instruction slots of 32-byte groups counted from your chosen start.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### GLSLC outputs read through their section headers

- **Symptom:** GLSLC outputs fail the reader's check and are dropped (44 of DARK SOULS: REMASTERED's `main`'s 72 outputs and most of its archives' pixel shaders).
- **Cause:** SDK 4's GLSLC outputs (version 0x20.4): pixel shaders' control sections store at +0x20 a value 12 bytes short of their length, so the code area is not right after it.
- **Fix:** an output's own section headers say where everything is: the output's +0x4c is the section count, section i's header is at +0x90 + 0x90 i (`{size, data offset from the output, type}`, type 0 = GPU code), and a GPU code header adds +0x30 control offset, +0x34 code offset (both from the section's data), +0x38 code length, +0x3c control length. `glslc_section` reads them for outputs the old rule rejects.
- **Found on:** [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md) (SDK 4.5.0).
- **Check:** on every output the old rule keeps (1,073 in 13 Sentinels, Three Houses, FINAL FANTASY TACTICS, Engage and Age of Calamity) both give the same key; the new reading also takes 8 outputs of [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md) and 3 of [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md) (GLSLC version 0x1d.0, whose control +0x20 holds odd values) that used to be dropped, so those two profiles' tables gained them.

### VertexA and VertexB parts merged into one vertex shader

- **Symptom:** `no GLSLC output`, a black screen with sound, a missing scope reticle, or `vertex shader of shader type 0 without a VertexB part in the expected place`.
- **Cause:** Maxwell's first vertex slot (type 0, VERTEX_CULL_BEFORE_FETCH) can run before a type-1 VertexB. Running A alone left the generic varyings at (0, 0, 0, 1), so full-screen passes sampled their sources at (0, 0) and 2D draws sampled the first atlas texel. Reading `c1` from after B for every pair also lost the constants in layouts that store them between A and B.
- **Fix:** `sph_stage` accepts type 0 as a vertex shader. `vertex_ab` reads the two SPH offsets and sizes from control +0x748. It redirects A's `EXIT` instructions with CC T to B's first instruction and merges the SPHs as in Ryujinx's `TranslatorContext.Translate(VertexA)`. When control +0x700 points to B's 0x30-byte header, `c1` follows B. Otherwise `c1` starts at the offset and ends at B's SPH. The latter layout requires a zero tail after B because the merged program has one constant bank. A type-0 program with no B is translated as it is.
- **Found on:** [Catherine: Full Body](../../dm/games/0100BF00112C0000/README.md) has 1,244 VertexA/B shaders with B's header at +0x700's offset and zero padding after B. A writes no generic outputs, ends in an `EXIT` predicated on CC FCSM_TR, an unconditional `EXIT` and a `BRA` to itself. Neither part uses absolute or table jumps. Borderlands: Game of the Year Edition (an upstream profile; NX 18 has the code, not the profile) has both layouts, including the reticle shader whose billboard-axis guard reads an embedded constant before B.

## Translation

### else-if chains in the SPIR-V back end

- **Symptom:** with `XLATE_DEBUG=0`, a shader with an `if / else if / else` (or `if / else if`) chain loses its `else if` and trailing `else` branches: their blocks are unreachable in the SPIR-V (which `spirv-val` still accepts) and SPIRV-Cross drops them from the MSL.
- **Cause:** `AstOptimizer.RemoveEmptyBlocks` turns an `Else` that holds only an `If` (and an optional trailing `Else`) into an `ElseIf` block beside it, a transform for GLSL's syntax that SPIR-V has no construct for. `SpirvGenerator` takes only an `Else` as an `If`'s false target, so the false edge went straight to the merge block. The default `XLATE_DEBUG=1` puts a comment node before each decoded instruction, so an `Else` never holds an `If` alone and no `ElseIf` forms: the chain's default build was not affected.
- **Fix:** `StructuredProgram.MakeStructuredProgram` leaves the flattening off for SPIR-V (`flattenElseIf: targetLanguage != TargetLanguage.Spirv`), so the nested `Else { If }` blocks become nested SPIR-V selections; `SpirvGenerator` throws if an `ElseIf` block ever reaches it. The translator is rebuilt: a chain change (upstream NXNative 2f0210dba, PR #3253).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_spirv_else_if.py`: three synthetic sm86 pixel programs (three-way, four-way, and `if / else if` without `else`) in both modes; every block reachable, `spirv-val` passes, the MSL keeps every branch's result. The translator before the fix fails 6 of its subtests.
- **Found on:** synthetic programs; any shader whose chain's predicates are computed before its first branch, built with `XLATE_DEBUG=0`.

### Optimized shader calls and memory snapshots

- **Symptom:** with shader job `debug = false`, SPIR-V generation throws `GetLocalPointer` / `KeyNotFoundException` or casts an `AstOperation` to an `AstOperand`. Other inputs compile but read a call's output before calling it, lose an unused-return call's side effects, or read local memory after an intervening store instead of preserving its earlier value.
- **Cause:** AST expression propagation treated every single-use assignment as a movable value. SPIR-V call arguments require local storage passed by reference; propagating their temporaries destroyed those lvalues. Moving call results or memory loads changed evaluation order, and dead-assignment removal could discard a call or atomic whose result was unused. A snapshot of an output argument could also move past another call that overwrote it.
- **Fix:** mark address-taken call locals and retain their storage. Propagate only pure expression trees with immutable leaves; keep memory reads and call/atomic effects at their original evaluation points, and retain unused assignments with side effects. This is shared translator behavior, without shader, game or build gates. Default shader builds use `DebugMode` and already skip this AST optimizer; their generated source is expected to remain unchanged.
- **Found on:** Nintendo Switch 2 Welcome Tour (upstream's profile `040057C02159A000`, not in NX 18; fixed upstream in `8d62601f3`), while testing original RT bodies in an isolated callable-region prototype. The pointer and effect failures also reproduce in ordinary compute IR without that prototype mode. This does not establish playable RT graphics.
- **Check:** ordinary compute GPU fixtures cover nested calls, constant/expression/formal arguments, output lvalues, return ordering, storage side effects and memory snapshots. An independent aliased Ampere carry fixture fails 7 of 736 state words before the fix and zero afterward; plain register-SSA and debug-mode controls pass both before and after.

### A jump enters a condition whose definition it bypassed

- **Symptom:** Metal warns that a boolean is used uninitialized on a path through a translated shader. A generated `if (!bypass) predicate = ...; if (predicate || bypass)` reads `predicate` before testing the bypass flag; the analogous AND form has the same defect.
- **Cause:** goto elimination widened an existing IF/ELSE condition with the jump flag. The jump can bypass the original condition's definition. Combining those operands does not preserve their original evaluation paths, even when the flag alone determines the combined result.
- **Fix:** materialize the entry condition in a registered boolean temporary, initialized from the jump flag. Evaluate the original condition only on its original entry path. Place that initialization after the enclosure that skips the bypassed statements, so the jump cannot skip the temporary's initialization too. This is shared structured-control-flow lowering, in both debug and optimized translation; it does not initialize arbitrary shader locals.
- **Constant conditions:** a constant condition is defined on every entry path, so `GuardEntryCondition` keeps it as a direct logical condition and adds no temporary (upstream `87d3a47aa`). Upstream found that wrapping a constant `false` label condition in a temporary hid the loop-exit shape its `LoopExitCohort` pass looks for (not in NX 18) and moved subgroup reductions out of their loop again; here it only spares the temporary. Conditions whose definitions a jump may bypass keep the guarded evaluation.
- **Found on:** Nintendo Switch 2 Welcome Tour (upstream's profile `040057C02159A000`, not in NX 18; fixed upstream in `8d62601f3`), six native RT condition sites. Ordinary compute CFGs independently reproduce the same error.
- **Check:** 64 synthetic twelve-block CFGs, including conditional and unconditional jumps, exhaust 2,048 input patterns in debug and optimized modes: 3,145,728 path/output checks, with an eager AST interpreter rejecting every undefined read. The old lowering fails that interpreter and emits 103 conditional-uninitialized warnings per mode; current SPIR-V validates and Metal compiles with `-Werror=sometimes-uninitialized`. The full build's early warnings come from rechecking the previous library for cache prefill: shader `009135d86e77a09e_b` reproduces those exact old diagnostics, while its current source passes the strict compile.

### Tested tools and compiler-specific shader caches

- **Symptom:** a shader build accepts tools that differ from `dmsh_chain.lock`; after an override build, returning to the tested Metal compiler can still restore the override's libraries.
- **Cause:** `own_compilers.py` previously accepted any present Metal compiler or SPIRV-Cross. The shader and fetch-variant caches hashed the recorded Metal version and flags, without the actual compiler or SDK. Prefill checked only a sample's compiled bytes, so a mixed folder's other libraries could be attributed to the wrong compiler. The NSP stage key recorded Xcode's version, which can stay unchanged when its separately downloaded Metal toolchain changes, and did not follow `SPIRV_CROSS` overrides.
- **Fix:** ship a universal SPIRV-Cross built from a pinned commit and require its fingerprint for NVN builds. Require both tested Metal compilers before an NVN game's shader steps; `--allow-other-metal` (`DM_ALLOW_OTHER_METAL=1` for `own_compilers.py`) explicitly permits another present Metal compiler. Shader and fetch-variant keys include the actual Metal compiler and SDK identities. Earlier-library prefill requires that identity on every library's reflection (`chain_compiler`); matching a few sample compiles cannot establish the unsampled libraries' compiler. Unmarked legacy libraries are not imported, and the Metal cache format is bumped to discard entries prefill might have misclassified. NSP and regression keys hash the selected converter's bytes, including overrides; NSP keys also include both SDKs and Metal tools.
- **Found on:** [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#found-on-this-game), [issue #242](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/242). The report used an untested compiler, but whether that caused the corrupted picture remains **open**. The cache failures were reproduced with stand-in libraries; this does not establish the rendering cause.
- **Check:** `test_own_compilers.py` checks enforcement and the explicit Metal override. `test_shader_toolchain_cache.py` first restored an untested library after switching compiler identities, for both main shaders and fetch variants, and now rejects it. It also checks stage invalidation on actual Metal, SDK and selected converter changes. `test_spirv_cross_pin.py` checks the shipped binary, pin, lock, architectures and licences.

### Metal compiler of a newer Xcode

- **Symptom:** after an Xcode update the build stops with `[RED] this Mac's Metal compiler is not the one the shaders were tested with (dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_chain.lock): metal_macosx Apple metal version 32023.922 …` (since the lock lists two compilers: `is not one the shaders were tested with`); `xcodebuild -downloadComponent MetalToolchain`, as the message said, downloads the same untested compiler again.
- **Cause:** since Xcode 26 the Metal toolchain is a system asset that each Xcode downloads in its own build and `xcrun` takes for the selected Xcode (`xcodebuild -showComponent MetalToolchain` names it), whatever the macOS version. When #717 was reported, macOS 27.2 with Xcode 27.1 selected gave the then-pinned 32023.921, with Xcode 27.2 selected 32023.922. The message named the Xcode to install but not that it must be the selected one, and README linked the App Store, which offers only the newest Xcode.
- **Fix:** the stop message names the selected Xcode and its folder, and says to install the tested Xcode from Apple's download page (beside a newer one as `Xcode-<version>.app`), select it (`xcode-select -s` or `DEVELOPER_DIR`), then download its Metal toolchain; docs/building.md says the same. The lock stays at the one tested compiler: another one needs a regression run before it is recorded, and the lock is part of the chain version. Whether two installed toolchain assets each follow their own Xcode is inferred from `-showComponent`, not yet tried with both installed (**open**).
- **Found on:** no game: [GitHub issue #717](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/717), on macOS 27.2 with Xcode 27.2 (Metal Toolchain 27B47).
- **Check:** `test_own_compilers.py` (the selection step before the download), `test_ci_toolchain.py` (the message's, README's and docs/building.md's Xcode are CI's).

### Idle MSBuild workers block regression measurements

- **Symptom:** `regress.py` waits for other jobs to end while the only reported jobs are sleeping `MSBuild.dll /nodemode:1 /nodeReuse:true` workers using 0% CPU.
- **Cause:** the busy-machine check treated every `dotnet` process as an active build. Reusable MSBuild workers can remain asleep after their build exits, and later builds can recreate them after a build-server shutdown.
- **Fix:** exclude only sleeping, zero-CPU MSBuild reuse workers whose parent has exited. Active workers, I/O-blocked workers, workers still attached to a parent, and build commands remain busy jobs.
- **Check:** `test_regress.py` supplies the process census that blocked the measurement, with active, I/O-blocked and parented controls.
- **Found on:** no game: Xcode 27.2 toolchain validation on an M4 Pro.

### MSL 4.1 and Xcode 27.3's Metal compiler for the game shaders

- **Change:** the Mac game shaders are compiled with `-mmacosx-version-min=27.3 -std=metal4.1` (`dmsh_build.py` `MAC_METAL`, the minimum of `dm/mac/min_os.mk`), as the iPhone's already were (`ios_metal.py`: `-mios-version-min=27.3 -std=metal4.1`), and `dmsh_chain.lock` records Xcode 27.3's Metal compiler, 32023.925.6, for both platforms (chain `ac67cb709fa0`). Before, the Mac line named neither: the language and the libraries' minimum followed the SDK, which gives MSL 4.1 and 27.3 with the macOS 27.3 SDK, so a newer SDK would have changed both with no change to the line or the chain version. MSL 4.1 is the newest language (Metal Shading Language Specification 4.1, 1.6.10: OS 27 and later).
- **Measured:** on 32023.925.6 (Xcode 27.3, 27C5020n, M4 Max), the full Mac shader sets of [NieR:Automata](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md) (1,225 shaders, 1,915 compiles) and [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (42,848 shaders, 52,973 compiles, its mesh and image-atomic shaders among them) were built with the old line and the old lock (`DM_ALLOW_OTHER_METAL=1`) and with the new line and lock, each into an empty compile cache. Every shader built in both; all 2,897 and 87,617 metallibs and every MSL source are the same bytes, the reflections differ only in `chain`, `chain_metal` and `chain_compiler`, the compiler printed the same warnings and notes, `shader_regressions.py` finds no regression, and the builds took 43 / 43 s and 2,390 / 2,403 s. NieR's iPhone libraries (the same build with `IOSOUT`) are the same bytes too. In MSL 4.1 a call to `atomic_*_explicit` on a non-volatile atomic lowers as a non-volatile atomic (it lowered as a volatile one in 4.0, which declared only the volatile overloads); the libraries above were already MSL 4.1 on both lines, so this is no change here.
- **Not measured:** Xcode 27.2's compiler (32023.922) against 32023.925 byte for byte: only 32023.925 is installed on the Mac where this was measured. CI moved to Xcode 27.3 in the same change (`.github/workflows/tests.yml`, [Continuous integration](../development.md#continuous-integration)).
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_shader_msl_version.py`: both lines name `-std=metal4.1` and their platform's minimum (`MACOS_MIN`; `MSL_MIN_iphoneos`, which is `project.yml`'s), and a kernel compiled with each records Metal 4.1 and that OS in its AIR. `test_shader_pch.py`, `test_dmsh_side.py` and `test_image_atomic_bounds.py` compile with the chain's own lines.

### Targets 27.2 and two tested Metal compilers

- **Change:** NX 18 runs on macOS 27.2 and iOS 27.2 and builds with Xcode 27.2 or 27.3. The game shaders' lines name `-mmacosx-version-min=27.2` and `-mios-version-min=27.2` (`MAC_METAL`, `ios_metal.py`, `MACOS_MIN`, `MSL_MIN_iphoneos`, `project.yml`), still with `-std=metal4.1` (MSL 4.1 compiles for both 27.2 systems). `dmsh_chain.lock` lists one `metal_<sdk>` line per tested Xcode: Xcode 27.2's 32023.922.4 (27B52, Metal Toolchain 27B47) and the Xcode 27.3 beta's 32023.925.10; `tool_lock.py` and `own_compilers.py` take either, and `--update-lock` replaces only the selected Xcode's line. The lower minimum and the lock changed the chain version (`2eb54f86ce5e`): every game's shaders build once more. Which compiler built a shader is in its cache keys (`METAL_FP`, `dmsh_mtlcache.compiler_id`), so the two compilers never share an entry. KK3 stays compiled out of the Mac library: KosmicKrisp and upstream count it fixed from macOS 27 ([`dmsh_metalwa.py`](../../dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_metalwa.py)).
- **Not measured:** the NieR:Automata and Ocarina of Time shader regression pass with Xcode 27.2's compiler against 32023.925.10, and a run on a 27.2 system. The code builds against both SDKs with no 27.3-only API.
- **Check:** `test_own_compilers.py` (both compilers pass, another stops, the lock's Xcodes are `XCODE_VERSIONS`, `--update-lock` keeps the other Xcode's line), `test_ci_toolchain.py`, `own_compilers.py --check-metal` with each Xcode selected through `DEVELOPER_DIR`.

### A pipeline the compiler service crashed on

- **Symptom:** `[RED] render pipeline (vertex … fragment …) not built, the system compiler service crashed (… XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED …)`; that pipeline's draws are missing for 30 frames, or for the rest of the run after `crashed on it 4 times in a row`.
- **Cause:** Metal's compiler runs in a system service. When the service crashes during a build (its XPC connection interrupted or invalid; Metal retries a few times itself first) the build fails, but not because of the pipeline. Before, such a pipeline went on the failed list (`g_pso_fail`) for the whole run like one that does not compile, and its draws were lost for good. The S2Arm project saw both forms on its copy of this layer: one crash in 35 runs of The Witcher 3 that the next build compiled, and on an M1 Max running Ocarina of Time a pair the service crashed on within 4 ms every time, in each of three runs; there, retrying every 30 frames crashed the service all run long, and the builds running beside it failed with it.
- **Fix:** `pso_retry_later` (`nvn_mtl.m`, `nvn_pso_retry.h`; ported from S2Arm 2a7e0bf9): a render or rasterizer-discard pipeline whose build error says the service crashed (`pso_err_service_crash`) is built again after 30, 120 and 480 frames, its draws skipped meanwhile, and the fourth crash in a row puts it on the failed list. Any other error (a compile or link error) puts it on the failed list at once, as before. A timeout is not retried. Compute, transform feedback and geometry capture pipelines are not covered.
- **Check:** `t_pso_retry`: the crash texts against compile and link errors, the 30 / 120 / 480-frame back-off, the fourth failure giving up, and a pair that always crashes built 4 times in 10 minutes. Not seen in a run here; a crash cannot be caused on purpose, so the path in `pso_get_` was not run.
- **Found on:** no game here. Reported by the S2Arm project (The Witcher 3; [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on an M1 Max).

### SPIRV-Cross parse failures

- **Symptom:** SPIRV-Cross can't parse the SPIR-V (`nullptr` exception).
- **Cause:** 2 agl post-effect compute shaders still produce such SPIR-V. Before the compute start-offset fix ([Exact program start for the decoder](#exact-program-start-for-the-decoder)) it was 7: misdecoded code produced the bad SPIR-V.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### Pixel shader inputs the vertex shader does not write

- **Symptom:** `Fragment input(s) user(locnN) mismatching vertex shader output type(s) or not written by vertex shader` and the draw is skipped; the host reports `[RED] failed to create render pipeline (vertex … fragment …)`. Offline packing fails with `pso_offline: metal-tt failed` for the same reason.
- **Cause:** every pixel shader input must be a vertex shader output. Maxwell attributes are untyped slots: a pixel shader that reads a slot the vertex shader never wrote gets a default, but Metal refuses to build the pipeline. Translating one shader at a time cannot know what the partner reads. The offline archive exposed a second omission: prebuild reads both `shader_pairs.tsv` and BAHS program variations in `pso_pairs.tsv`, but padding read only the first table.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` reads both pair tables and gives each vertex shader the outputs its partners read but it doesn't write, as `float4` (0, 0, 0, 1) (`vs_pad`). A profile without `pso_pairs.tsv` takes the split's copy beside the package, as the archive builder does. The union controls pixel-before-vertex build order and shader cache dependencies ([Shader cache key covering the pairs](#shader-cache-key-covering-the-pairs)); the profile input stamp includes its `pso_pairs.tsv`, and the NSP script's shader reuse key includes the generated fallback. Padding precedes the depth, shadow-input, pulling and geometry-capture variants. Rebuild shaders and the host for the new chain. The host caches failed pipelines and reports each once with its vertex / fragment keys; before that, every draw retried the failing compile.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md): 331 of 9,043 pairs failed; the opening course used none of them, later levels did (about 11,500 failed builds in one 11-minute session). The missing BAHS table was reproduced on [Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#files-and-shaders) ([#283](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/283)) and [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md#files-and-shaders) ([#276](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/276)); their notes keep the pair counts and validation.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_pairs.py` covers both table locations, union/deduplication, defaults and cache dependencies; Metal compiles the generated sources for Mac and iOS and builds an archive with regular and pulling vertex entries in both depth modes. `test_nsp_mac.py` checks fallback invalidation and progress ordering; `test_nsp_incremental.py` regenerates a missing fallback. Check all pairs offline after a game build (recipe in [05](../agents/05-debugging.md)).

### Pixel shader inputs the mesh shader does not write

- **Symptom:** `[RED] task / mesh pipeline: task - mesh … fragment …: … is not found in the output from the previous stage`, and the mesh draws of that program are skipped. Ocarina of Time's foliage: the point meshes `3a6340a04bf09797` and `a203347bb7bdbd03` write only a point size and a position, while their pixel shaders (`2a7c48d595feaaeb`, `cd609edc78b467d8`) read generic attribute 5 (pull request #1590).
- **Cause:** the same as [for a vertex shader](#pixel-shader-inputs-the-vertex-shader-does-not-write): a pixel shader input no stage wrote reads a default on the console, and Metal refuses the pipeline. Mesh shaders were left out of that rule twice: `shader_split.py` `var_pairs` paired only vertex keys with pixel keys, so no table held a mesh shader's partners, and `dmsh_meshstage.to_stage` made only the outputs its SPHs enable. It is not point sprite coordinate replacement: the pixel shaders' SPH input maps read generic attribute 5 (not a fixed-function texture coordinate, the only kind the hardware's point sprite select replaces, nor the point coordinate at attribute `0x2e0`), NVN has no point sprite function (only `nvnCommandBufferSetPointSize`), and the same foliage file's triangle meshes leave locations 2, 8 and 9 unwritten that their pixel shaders read.
- **Fix:** `var_pairs` pairs a mesh key with its variation's pixel key as it pairs a vertex key, and `dmsh_build.py` passes `to_stage` what the partners read (`partner_inputs`, which `vs_pad` uses too): the locations neither the vertex nor the per-primitive SPH enables become vertex outputs of (0, 0, 0, 1). A mesh shader's pairs feed its build order and cache key as a vertex shader's do. `pso_offline.py` `plan` and the host's prebuild (`nvn_mtl.m` `pre_desc`) leave mesh pairs out: mesh pipelines are built at the draw (`nvn_geometry.inc` `mtk_pso`), not from archive bases. Ocarina's `shader_pairs.tsv` was split again. Of Ocarina's 1,284 mesh pairs (491 mesh shaders), 31 read a location their mesh shader does not write (19 mesh shaders, in the foliage and model files); with the padding Metal built the mesh pipeline of every pair but one.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): the foliage files' variations; pull request #1590 found the failing pipelines and padded at run time on Metal's error text, which this replaces.
- **Then:** one pair padded past Metal's mesh size: [Mesh outputs as wide as their pixel shaders read](#mesh-outputs-as-wide-as-their-pixel-shaders-read).
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_meshstage.py` (`test_pad_unwritten_pixel_inputs`, and a padded point mesh compiled by Metal), `test_dmsh_pairs.py` (`VariationPairs`, with `DM_GAME` set), `test_pso_offline.py`.

### Programs resolved before a depth mode change

- **Symptom:** `depth mode changed after N vertex shaders were loaded` (before: `[RED] … they keep the old convention`), then whole scenes, characters or terrain missing or clipped, as their depth falls outside the range: the game set `nvnDeviceSetDepthMode` after it had drawn.
- **Cause:** a vertex (or NVN2 mesh) shader's entry point follows the depth mode it was loaded under (`main0` rewrites z for near = -w, `main0_z` leaves it). Programs resolved before the change kept those shaders: the serial table, `prog_get_ptr`'s last result, the combined programs of `bind_stages` and the shader cache (`g_shcache`, `shader_get_layer`'s `#l` entries) all gave the old ones. S2Arm counted the change mid-run in 22 of 728 player diagnoses over 7 titles.
- **Fix:** `nvn_mtl.m`, ported from S2Arm 8df8cbe7 (Apache-2.0). A shader whose entry follows the mode records the mode it was loaded under (`NvShader.depth_moded`, `load_z`). One of the other mode is stale (`shader_depth_stale`): the shader cache loads it again and keeps the old one for the process (`g_sh_stale`), as pipelines may still name it by its address, and the shared preparation records key a mesh shader by the mode as they do a vertex shader. `prog_get_ptr` takes a program holding a stale vertex or mesh shader as not resolved. A combined program is put together again from its stages' programs (`bind_combine`, `bind_rebuild`; its cache is now `g_bind_cache` at file scope), keeping a stage's old shader when that stage's program was finalized or reused since. Any other program is resolved again and replaces its entry in the serial table, which lets the old one go at the fence as before. A changed program gives the draw a new `NvProg`, so the draw fetches its pipeline again. Not ported: S2Arm's `prog_peek` look-ahead, which NX 18 does not have. The pipelines made for the old shaders stay cached.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_depth_reresolve` (CPU only): a program resolves to the same object while the mode stands and to a new one without the old shader after it changes, which the serial table gives and a second look keeps; a combined program is put together again with its fragment program's shader; the shader cache stops giving the old shader and keeps it; back in the first mode, a program of that mode's shader stands.
- **Found on:** S2Arm's player diagnoses (ported; not reproduced on a game here).

### Mesh outputs as wide as their pixel shaders read

- **Symptom:** `[RED] task / mesh pipeline: … Total mesh size (35840) exceeds the maximum mesh size allowed (32768)`, and the program's mesh draws are skipped: Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh `626fbf48041cb82b` with pixel shader `c5eb985e769e317f` (`Foliage.Product.110`, `#BNSH@0x1b1000#var3`), once [padded](#pixel-shader-inputs-the-mesh-shader-does-not-write).
- **Cause:** what Metal counts against a mesh's 32 KB is its declared outputs, the largest vertex count times the vertex struct (35,840 = 160 × 14 `float4`). `to_stage` declared a `float4` for every attribute an SPH names: the mesh writes 26 components a vertex, x alone in four attributes and x, y, z in six, but declared 11 `float4` beside its position, and 3 more padded. The hardware's attribute area holds only the written words.
- **Fix:** with its pixel partners known, `to_stage` declares each output as wide as they can see (`_width`): the components the shader writes, and a w they read but it does not write, which must hold 1. A Metal fragment input wider than the mesh output it links to reads 0 in the missing components (checked on the GPU: `float`, `float2`, `float3` into `float4` read 0 in the rest, `w` too), the hardware's default for x, y and z. `dmsh_build.py` `partner_reads` takes what each partner reads from its SPH input map (`dmsh_meshstage.pixel_reads`, byte 0x18 on, two bits a component); a location its MSL declares that the map does not name counts as all four. Without pairs every output stays a `float4`. The mesh above comes to 160 × (16 + 4 × 4 + 6 × 16 + 3 × 16) = 28,160 bytes, the 3 padded locations kept `float4` as its pixel shader reads their w.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): all 1,284 of its mesh pairs build their Metal mesh pipelines.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_meshstage.py` (`test_outputs_as_wide_as_read`, `test_pixel_reads`, and Metal compiling narrowed outputs).

### Single-stage programs paired with a cover

- **Cause:** a game that builds one NVN program per GLSLC output and combines vertex and pixel programs per draw with `BindProgram` stage bits has its real pairs only at draw time, so the offline build cannot know which pixel shaders a vertex shader must feed.
- **Fix:** the composite-program path ([Programs bound per stage](rendering.md#programs-bound-per-stage)) does all the pairing at replay. When every pixel shader input is a plain `float4` at fixed locations, `shader_split.py` pairs every vertex shader with a *cover*: the fewest pixel shaders whose inputs together are every input any pixel shader reads. `vs_pad` then gives each vertex shader all of them, and any vertex / pixel combination links.
- **Found on:** [13 Sentinels: Aegis Rim](../../dm/games/01003FC01670C000/README.md): 838 `ProgramSetShaders` at startup; every pixel shader input is a plain `float4` at locations 1–9 (the SPH input map, words 6–13, matches the translated MSL inputs for all 375 pixel shaders), so the cover is one shader.

### Unwritten components of a written output start as (0, 0, 0, 1)

- **Symptom:** outdoors dim and nothing casts a sun shadow (every shadow-map lookup fails).
- **Cause:** a vertex shader may write only part of an output (Link's Awakening writes a world position's x, y and z) while the pixel shader reads the rest (it divides by w). On the hardware the unwritten components read as (0, 0, 0, 1); Ryujinx gets the same by initializing the components the next stage reads when it links stages. The translator's MSL starts every vertex shader with `main0_out out = {};`, all zeros, so w was 0, and a fragment shader dividing by it got infinite compare values.
- **Fix:** general: `dmsh_build.py` (`vs_init`) sets every `float4` output to (0, 0, 0, 1) right after it, and the translated body overwrites what it writes.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md) (Mac and iPhone): 12,281 of 18,410 vertex outputs never write w. The sun's shadow map (2048×2048, depth 32-bit float) was correct; the surface shaders compute the lookup from a world position the vertex shader passes in `locn24`, divided by its w. Found with `DM_NVN_TEXRAW` on the shadow map, the light matrices from `DM_NVN_CBDUMP`, and a hand-edited copy of one surface shader that wrote the compare value and the stored depth as its colour. Compared with forcing the compare to pass (`compareFunction` always): the same brightness, plus shadows from the fences, the house and the trees.

### Early fragment tests from the control section

- **Symptom:** rows of dark dots along contour lines of alpha-tested surfaces drawn after a depth pre-pass (Tears of the Kingdom: the stone lattice in front of the Great Sky Island start, as reported by MacNativeGames ae8f4f4; not reproduced here). On Switch 2, GPU time: every covered pixel of a shader that writes memory was shaded (PRAGMATA's cabin on an M4 Pro: GPU 19.0 ms a frame, p95 frame 64 ms, a quarter of the frames over 33 ms).
- **Cause:** byte +0x718 of a pixel shader's NVN control section is its early fragment tests flag: the NVN driver's getter is `ldrb w0, [x8, #0x718]` in the `sdk` of all 21 games on hand, and Ryujinx receives it as the 3D engine's EarlyZForce. The translator always passed `earlyZForce` false. Without the attribute, Metal tests depth after a fragment shader that writes memory. In the lattice's G-buffer pass (EQUAL, no depth write, after a LESS pre-pass) that late depth differed by an ulp from the pre-pass along contour lines, the test failed there and the G-buffer kept its clear value. A Switch 2 (NVN2) control section keeps the flag at byte +0x5b: the NVN2 driver reads the pixel shader's flags one byte each at +0x58..+0x62 (`ldr x8, [x0, #8]; ldrb w0, [x8, #0x5b]; ret`, at the same slot of the `sdk`'s seven vtables, one per control section version), and Ampere's SPH has no such bit (NVK sends it as the 3D method `SET_API_MANDATED_EARLY_Z`). Over 70,763 NVN2 pixel shaders of four games, +0x58 is set exactly when the SPH writes depth (word 19 bit 1), and +0x5b on none of those. `job_for` read the flag in its NVN1 branch only, so no NVN2 shader got the attribute.
- **Fix:** general: `dmsh_build.py` (`early_fragment_tests`) sets `early_fragment_tests` in the translation job of every pixel shader whose control byte +0x718 is set. `dmsh_xlate` passes it to Ryujinx as `earlyZForce`, and the MSL entry point is declared `[[ early_fragment_tests ]]`. A shader whose SPH writes depth (word 19 bit 1) is left as it is, since a test made early would not use the depth it computes. For an NVN2 section the flag is +0x5b (`dmsh_ampere.early_fragment_tests`), with the same depth-writer rule.
- **Found on:** [Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#found-on-this-game): 1,677 of its pixel shaders set the flag, and none of them writes depth. Rebuilding the chain changed exactly those 1,677 shaders' MSL, by the attribute alone. Elsewhere the attribute alone changed 699 of Wonder's 16,268 shaders and 51 of Scarlet's 30,142. Breath of the Wild and ANIMAL WELL set the flag in no shader. Switch 2: [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) 746 of 22,352 pixel shaders, [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#found-on-this-game) 2,855 of 13,546, Orbitals 38 of 16,459 and PRAGMATA 6,175 of 18,406 (the last two have no profile here). On PRAGMATA's cabin route the flag is set on all 81 pixel shaders whose only reason to test late is their own store (the streaming feedback's `atomic_fetch_min`), on 11 of 84 that discard, and on none of its 75 without side effects or its 5 depth writers; with the attribute GPU time fell to 16.6 ms, p95 to 26 ms and frames over 33 ms to 0.2 %, with no flicker and no coarser textures (PR #1846).
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_early_fragment_tests.py` (NVN1 and NVN2).
- **Open:** with the attribute, a fragment shader that writes memory gives a picture that varies from run to run on Apple's GPU: [Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#open)'s `8f470f5ce5864df7`, a per-fragment visibility flag (2026-10-30; the frame's replays: one digest without the attribute, 3 to 7 in 8 with it).

### Dead-code removal dropped the inputs of atomics

- **Cause:** Ryujinx's `Optimizer.RemoveNode` removes a dead node, then follows each source whose last use went away into the node that produced it. It did so even when that producer has side effects, such as an atomic or a call whose result is unused. Such a producer stays in the block, but its sources lost their uses, so the next dead-code pass deleted the chain that feeds it, and the atomic read undefined values.
- **Fix:** general: `RemoveNode` follows only into producers without side effects (`HasSideEffects`). This follows MacNativeGames' translator patch 0002.
- **Found on:** no game on hand so far. Rebuilding the chain changed no shader for this reason in Tears of the Kingdom, Breath of the Wild, Wonder, Scarlet or ANIMAL WELL (94,717 shaders); every MSL change was the early fragment tests attribute.

### Dead-code removal dropped a coordinate of a multi-output instruction

- **Symptom:** a simulation or filter that reads neighbouring texels misbehaves though every constant is right. In the translated MSL a variable is declared with no value and read at once (`int _139; int _141 = _139;`), used as a coordinate. On Ocarina of Time: grass whose blades shift at 10 Hz, with a 6-frame cycle in the depth buffer and a wind velocity texture of strong stripes.
- **Cause:** the same walk as the entry above. `RemoveNode` also followed a producer with another destination still in use. An image read with a dead `.x` and a live `.y` stays in the block, but its coordinates lost their use, and the next pass deleted the chain computing them. So the read took an undefined coordinate. Ocarina of Time's divergence step `04942f42cae10d0f` read its `y - 1` neighbour at a garbage row, so the wind's pressure solve never settled.
- **Fix:** general: `Optimizer.cs` `RemoveNode` follows only a producer all of whose destinations are unused (`DestHasNoUses`). A chain change.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py DeadChannel`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 8 of its 42,845 shaders, among them the wind solver and two NDSR upscaler variants. [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md): 3 compute shaders, noted open earlier.

### Position invariance across shaders

- **Symptom:** the ground kept stale 32×32 blocks where the G-buffer draw failed the depth test: blocks of a stale image where a depth pre-pass and a later pass with other vertex shaders meet (Odyssey: 32×32 blocks on every fourth frame). With mesh shaders: white shapes on plants for single frames, at other places each frame while the leaves move (Ocarina of Time).
- **Cause:** a game may draw the same geometry with two programs and rely on identical depth, for example a depth pre-pass and then a less-or-equal G-buffer pass. Maxwell evaluates identical position math identically; Metal only does for outputs marked `[[invariant]]` (the translation marks `gl_Position`) when compiled with `-fpreserve-invariance`. Without it, Metal compiled two vertex shaders' identical position math to slightly different depths, so fragments of the second pass failed a less-or-equal depth test against the pre-pass and those pixels kept the previous content.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` (Mac) and `ios_metal.py` (iOS) pass `-fpreserve-invariance` to every compile. A mesh shader's position is marked too: the mesh stage wrapper (`dmsh_meshstage.to_stage`) declares `[[position, invariant]]`, as the translation does for `gl_Position`.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) (Mac): the Cap Kingdom ground. Presents 4k were brighter and blocky, 4k+1 and 4k+2 clean, 4k+3 partly blocked, repeating to the decimal across runs. The blocks were already in the HDR target when the G-buffer pass ended, and only with the ground's material (`b6384a85afd3ef94`) drawn. In some frames the game first draws a depth pre-pass (`alRenderDepthOnly`) for objects its occlusion queries report visible (the queries rotate through three slots, hence the cycle), then draws the G-buffer with other vertex shaders and depth test less-or-equal, without clearing the colour targets.
  [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (Mac): the foliage in Kokiri Forest is drawn twice by two different mesh shaders, a depth pre-pass (depth LESS, no colour) and a colour pass with depth EQUAL and no depth write. Pixels where the colour pass missed EQUAL showed white for one frame; how the white reaches the picture was not traced (the scene target's clear value, alpha 0, is the likely source). Without the mark the wrapper's position was a plain `[[position]]`. With it: no flashes in five 121-frame windows on an M4 Max (14 on main), none in 240 frames at a flashing flower on an M4 Pro.
- **Note:** ruled out on the way: the TBDR optimizations, same-pass feedback, the present path. Evaluating the game's conditional rendering on the CPU from earlier frames' results made it worse (the condition belongs to the same frame on the GPU), so the host still ignores the condition there and draws.

### A NaN colour under a zero blend weight

- **Symptom:** white clouds over the ground, coming and going (Ocarina of Time: over the ground mist between Kokiri Forest and Hyrule Field, and over the ferns at the camera in Kokiri Forest while they sway). The scene target holds NaN pixels after a fog or lighting pass and bloom spreads each into a white blot; dropping that pass removes them.
- **Cause:** NVIDIA's float blender has an option, "zero times anything is zero" (`SET_BLEND_FLOAT_OPTION`, method 0x670 of the 3D engine, bit `ZERO_TIMES_ANYTHING_IS_ZERO` in NVIDIA's published class headers), and NVN's driver switches it on in its start-up command list: the word `0x80010670` (an immediate write of 1 to that method) is in the `sdk` module of Ocarina of Time (`sdk+0xbb81ac`, among other immediate writes) and of 12 of 14 other games looked at. A blend term whose weight is 0 is then 0 whatever the colour, so a pixel shader may write a NaN colour with alpha 0 under SRC_ALPHA / ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA blending, its way of adding nothing to that pixel, and the target keeps its value. Apple's blender multiplies as IEEE does and stores NaN (measured on an M6: NaN, +Inf and -Inf at alpha 0 all store NaN; only a factor of ZERO itself leaves its term out), which every later pass reading the target carries on. It is not Direct3D's rule: Direct3D 11.3's specification (17.5) wants NaN kept through blending, 0.0 weights included. The NaN itself is the game's arithmetic, translated exactly: Ocarina of Time's mist shader averages hit positions over a layer whose density is 0 there, a positive hit count with a weight sum of 0 (1/0 × 0), and its FMULs carry no `.FMZ`.
- **Fix:** the rule is the blender's, so it follows the draw's blend state. `dmsh_rewrite.py` `blend_zero` gives every pixel shader with a `float4` colour output a guard before each return of `main0` and a word from the host at fragment buffer `DMSH_BLEND_BUFFER`, two bits per colour output; the build runs it before it makes the other entry points, which call `main0`'s body and take its parameters, so all of them have it. The host (`nvn_mtl.m` `bz_word`, `bz_bind`) sets an output's bits for a floating-point target that blends by a sum or difference with a source colour factor of SRC_ALPHA (zero where alpha is 0) or ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA (zero where alpha is 1), and whose destination factor does not read the source colour. There the guard returns a non-finite rgb as 0, what the zero weight makes of it. With blending off, MIN / MAX, another factor or another target format the bits are 0 and the colour is returned as it is: NVIDIA stores that NaN or Inf too. A finite colour and the alpha are never changed. `DM_NVN_BLEND_ZERO=0` gives 0 for every draw.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_blend_zero.py` (the guard and the word in every entry point through `dmsh_side.unit` and `dmsh_fetch.fold`, what `dmsh_entry.fs_uint` reads of a shader unchanged, the rule, each text compiled by Metal's compiler); `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_blend_zero` draws NaN, ±Inf and 2 at alpha 0 and 1 through the replay into an RGBA16F target under seven blend states and reads it back, with the rule and with `DM_NVN_BLEND_ZERO=0` (Apple's blender as it is).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (Mac, [issue #1839](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1839), PR #2030): the half-resolution mist shader `5ad65c0eefd09c7f` wrote NaN into its RGBA16F target (about 15,000 to 229,000 pixels a frame in three reports), the mist composite `888c1068ac8f7043` carried it into the R11G11B10F scene and bloom made the clouds. Not reproduced on the Mac this was written on (an M6): the village route from the reference save and two passes over the village floor with the camera low and high gave 0 non-finite pixels with `DM_NVN_BLEND_ZERO=0` too (`DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`), so the fix is checked there by the replay test, in the mist's blend state and target format, and by the word being set in play (output 0, on more than 131,000 passes or states of one run). Its cost, standing on the village deck with the resolution fixed (`DM_NVN_GPU_TS=submission`), three runs a side: GPU p50 10.86 ms on main's libraries, 10.95 ms with the guard in every pixel shader (10.91 ms with the word 0 for every draw), submit and present 5.67 against 5.73 ms, 60 fps both; the difference is about the spread between runs (0.06 and 0.11 ms).
- **Open:** a NaN or Inf already in the target under a zero destination weight, and a non-finite weight on a colour of 0, which a shader cannot see; the alpha channel's own weights; the Vulkan and OpenGL shader paths, which this fork does not have (the layers were removed in git 989e75d4; whether those drivers set the option is not known, for the later Vulkan port).
- **Note:** found with `DM_NVN_SNAPRAW` and `DM_NVN_TEXSNAP` (raw floats of every colour target after chosen passes of one frame) and `DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`, then the one shader instrumented beside the library (`DM_MSL_DIR`): the first NaN at `1/w × v` with `w = 0`, every NaN texel stored with alpha 1 (source alpha 0). Ruled out on the way: the fog volumes (finite), FMZ handling (none in the code), stale uniforms (`DM_NVN_UB_DEFER=0`, `DM_NVN_UBP_CHECK`), the depth test, `-fmetal-math-mode=relaxed`. The driver's command word was found by scanning the modules for writes to method 0x670; the code that sends the list was not disassembled, and no console was measured.

### The blend helper's output reference on iOS

- **Symptom:** pixel shader compilation fails with `reference type must have explicit address space qualifier` at `dm_blend0(main0_out& dm_o, ...)`: every iOS build, and a Mac build made on macOS 26. A Mac build made on macOS 27 compiled, so the fault passed where it was written.
- **Cause:** `blend_zero` generated a reference without an address space (the output is a local variable of the fragment function). Metal's language version follows the deployment target: the iOS build names iOS 18, whose language requires the qualifier, and the Mac compile named none, so it took the build Mac's system. Measured with Metal front end 32023.922 on macOS 27.2, a shader with that signature: it compiles with no minimum and with iOS 27.0, and fails with `-mmacosx-version-min=26.0`, `15.0` and `-mios-version-min=18.0`. One chain version therefore gave different results on two Macs.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_rewrite.py` declares the helper's output parameter `thread main0_out&`; the helper's behavior is unchanged. Every Mac compile of a game's shaders names the Mac host's minimum macOS and MSL 4.1 (`dmsh_build.py`'s `MAC_METAL`; in NX 18 since 0.5: [MSL 4.1 and Xcode 27.3's Metal compiler for the game shaders](#msl-41-and-xcode-273s-metal-compiler-for-the-game-shaders)), as the host's own libraries do ([The host's own shaders compiled from source at run time](#the-hosts-own-shaders-compiled-from-source-at-run-time)); `dm/mac/min_os.mk` is a chain input (`dmsh_chain.MAC_MIN`) and the flag is in each shader's cache key. NX 18's iPhone line names iOS 27.2 (`ios_metal.py`), not iOS 18. `test_dmsh_blend_zero.py` compiles the plain, integer-output, side and readable-tile variants with the chain's own Mac and iPhone lines.
- **Found on:** [The Binding of Isaac: Rebirth](../../dm/games/01009BC00C38A000/README.md): all 10 pixel shaders failed on iOS, while its 9 vertex shaders and all 19 Mac shaders compiled (macOS 27). [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 22,296 of 42,848 Mac shaders failed on two Macs with macOS 26 ([GitHub issue #2272](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2272)); 42,848 built there with the qualifier (a reporter's run).

### gl_FragCoord.z against the depth stored for its own surface

- **Symptom:** dark specks flickering in a ground mist, a few pixels each, at different places every frame (Ocarina of Time in Kokiri Forest; the console shows none). Dropping the mist removes them, as does dropping its depth-only pass.
- **Cause:** a pixel shader tests its fragment against a depth image of the same surface, which an earlier pass stored. The mist's fragment shader `5ad65c0eefd09c7f` discards when `gl_FragCoord.z` is above the depth its depth-only pass (fragment `4757b5b373a5406e`, the same geometry) stored, so a fragment should never reject its own surface. NVIDIA stores exactly the z a pixel shader reads. Apple's GPU stores a value at or below the fragment's `[[position]].z`, with any vertex or mesh shader and `-fpreserve-invariance`. On an M4 Pro the stored value is lower at 0.2–3.8% of a triangle's pixels and never higher. An M6 stores exactly the fragment's z, as NVIDIA does (`check_t_zsnap`: 0 of 718,400 pixels, 2026-11-09), so the gap belongs to the GPU generation; other generations are not measured. The gap is at most one ulp of z plus 2^-23 of the depth's step across a pixel (`fwidth(z)`). It happens even for triangles of constant depth. With the stored depth one ulp under, the mist discarded its own surface there. The specks went when the depth-only pass wrote `[[position]].z` as its depth, and the mist's own test was the failing one (a raw dump of its 768x432 target held 41,226 zero pixels).
- **Fix:** general, in the chain: `dmsh_rewrite.py` `frag_z_snap`. Where a `gl_FragCoord.z` read in `main0` meets a value read from a texture in one comparison, through any arithmetic on either side, the read becomes `dm_zsnap(gl_FragCoord.z, value, fwidth(gl_FragCoord.z))`. That returns the texture value when it lies in `[z - ulp(z) - 2^-20 fwidth(z), z)` and z otherwise. A comparison of a fragment with its own surface's stored depth then sees equality, as on NVIDIA, whatever its operator or the scale on each side. A surface outside that window, constants, uniforms, and z read in other functions are unchanged. Not chosen: making the depth-only pass write `[[position]].z` as depth. That moves its depth test after the shader, and a stored depth that differs from the rasterizer's fails an `EQUAL` pass on the same geometry (Metroid Dread's G-buffer after its prepass).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_zsnap.py` (the rewrite on the translator's shapes) and `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_zsnap`. The GPU test draws 2,000 triangles depth-only, then reads the stored depth back in a second draw of the same triangles. Five sets: perspective, w = 1, constant depth, depth < 0.001, depth > 0.999. Through `dm_zsnap` no pixel is above its own stored depth (plain z: 9,511 of 718,400 pixels on an M4 Pro, 0 on an M6, where the rewrite changes no result), and a copy 4e-6 farther stays farther at every pixel.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 39 of its pixel shaders compare z with a texture value, all rewritten. They are the mist's 4 and its foliage's `z * c[188].w > d * c[188].w`. Elsewhere the rewrite changes 158 of OCTOPATH TRAVELER 0's shaders and 122 of Minecraft Dungeons II's, both `z < d`, which the gap left as NVIDIA's. It changes none of OCTOPATH TRAVELER II's 7,005 shaders that read z, nor Three Houses'.

### iOS math mode (relaxed)

- **Cause:** the translated shaders reproduce Maxwell's NaN behaviour with `isnan` selects (8,337 of 12,809 use them), and the default fast mode allows the GPU compiler to assume there is no NaN.
- **Fix:** the iOS libraries are compiled with `-fmetal-math-mode=relaxed` (`dm/ios/tools/ios_metal.py`; the Vulkan and GL libraries too, until 0.5 removed those layers). Relaxed keeps fast math's reassociation but not that assumption.
- **Found on:** listed among the mechanisms first met in [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md); the source gives the 8,337 of 12,809 count without naming the game.

### Front end at -O1: Apple's control-flow lowering

- **Symptom:** making a pipeline archive prints `Max Unstruct ID 758 in function agc.main in shader 13941 exceeds supported limit`, and one pipeline takes many minutes, in `metal-tt` offline or in the host's prebuild on the device (`Preparing pipelines` holding one count for minutes). The line is a note, not an error: `metal-tt` goes on, exits 0 and writes that pipeline into the archive.
- **Cause:** at the front end's default `-O2`, `CorrelatedValuePropagation` and the `SimplifyCFG` after it fold a translated shader's repeated tests of one value into switches whose cases jump past their own join, so nested `if`s become an unstructured graph. Apple's GPU backend lowers unstructured control flow with per-function IDs; past its budget (255 on `applegpu_g13s`, the check in `libapplegpu-nt.dylib` of AIR-TT 32023.921 picks 255 or 65,535 by a capability not yet characterized), it prints the note and lowers the function another way that is far slower and larger. At `-O1` those two passes do not run; the backend still optimizes the AIR itself, so ordinary shaders come out the same.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` (Mac) and `ios_metal.py` (iOS) compile with `-O1`.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md#files-and-shaders), [issue #276](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/276): the float body of `alRenderMaterialUber` (fragment `007d7e411ef14ebd`, 16,227 `if`s in one function) took 24.6 minutes and 17.3 MB of `g13s` code at `-O2`, 31 s and 1.3 MB at `-O1`. Its `-O1` graph still goes past the budget (642), at that small cost.
- **Check:** random samples of archive bases from seven games (Odyssey, Tears of the Kingdom, Breath of the Wild, Kirby, Luigi's Mansion 3, Wonder, Pikmin 4; 22 to 400 each) compile at `-O1` to vertex and fragment code within a few percent of `-O2` (Odyssey 400 bases: −0.9% / +0.01%; TotK: +0.1% / −1.0%; Kirby: −3.1% / +0.07%), with no `Max Unstruct ID` note at either level, and the front end is faster. Odyssey's full `g13s` archive of 32,864 bases builds in 15.6 minutes (exit 0); its Cap Kingdom frames 4,200 to 6,600 match `-O2` except for scattered differences of at most 3 of 255 in three of nine frames, and their GPU time per frame (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF=every:150`, three runs each on an M-series `g18g`) has medians of 4.76 to 5.05 ms at `-O1` and 4.75 to 4.83 ms at `-O2`, within run-to-run noise.

### Tests of one uniform word folded into switches

- **Symptom:** the Mac app's (or the iPhone build's) pipeline archive step runs for many hours: a group of 1,000 bases runs past its time limit, and `compile_split` leaves out single pipelines that each ran past theirs (`[RED] pso_offline: applegpu_<gpu>: left out of the archive, the device builds it`), while `air-nt` grows past the Mac's memory and swap fills. Alone, such a pipeline does not finish in 10 minutes and takes 10 to 13 GB, where the build before took seconds. On the device, the same pipelines would hold `Preparing pipelines` for as long.
- **Cause:** a game's material shader chooses a branch by a uniform word, and the translator writes the GLSL switch as Ryujinx's structurizer gives it: one test of the word for each case, with flags for the fall-throughs and each case nested in the next (`bool _6240 = as_type<int>((*spvDescriptorSet0.fp_c5)._m0[16].w) != 102; if (!_6240) { … }`). The Metal front end's SimplifyCFG (which runs at [-O1](#front-end-at--o1-apples-control-flow-lowering) too) folds a chain of blocks that each hold only such a test of one value into a `switch`, whose cases jump into the nested blocks past their joins, and Apple's GPU compiler (`applegpu-nt`, AIR-TT 32023.922) takes that graph for hours. Turning the switches back into compares (`metal-opt -passes=lowerswitch`) does not help: the graph is the cost, not the instruction. Before the host bounded [reads at fixed offsets](#reads-past-a-uniform-bindings-size) (chain `b7249b1c2b66`), each read's bounds test sat in the test's own block, so no chain was folded; with the reads plain, Nintendo Switch Sports Resort's pixel shader `692008b305ce264f` has 42 switches in its IR (none before) and `7c2becb054a53765` 66 (6 before).
- **Fix:** every game, a chain change. `dmsh_rewrite.uniform_tests`, called by `dmsh_build.py` after the other rewrites (and on the geometry stage and its reader), reads a word of a game uniform slot (`c3` and up; the driver's banks are left as written) that the shader tests for equality with an integer constant more than once through `dm_uword`, a volatile load of that word for each test (`as_type<int>(dm_uword((*spvDescriptorSet0.fp_c5)._m0[16], 3)) != 102`). The front end can neither merge one test's load with the next nor move it out of its block, so it folds no chain. A uniform load per test is less than the bounds test each read had before. A word tested once, a test against a variable, and an ordering test are left as they are.
- **Found on:** Nintendo Switch Sports Resort (`04008E001F098000`, no profile yet): with chain `b7249b1c2b66` the Mac app's archive ran 15 hours in its first group (vertex `1f0649e274faa626`, `1552eae0ea641bcd` and `24ce8042ec8edfff` with pixel `7c2becb054a53765`, vertex `2265ca4db20439bf` with pixel `692008b305ce264f`, each left out after about 30 minutes alone), where chain `592225da745b` had built 1,000 bases in about 3 minutes. Measured alone with `metal-tt` 32023.922 (`applegpu_g14s`, macOS 27.2, M2 Max with 32 GB), `2265ca4db20439bf` + `692008b305ce264f`: chain `592225da745b` 7.4 s and 0.25 GB; chain `b7249b1c2b66` stopped at 10 minutes and 13.4 GB; that chain's vertex library with the old pixel library 4.2 s, the old vertex library with the new pixel library stopped at 5 minutes and 10 GB; the old MSL with the fixed-offset bounds tests taken out stopped at 5 minutes; the same with the tests kept only on the 910 reads that feed an equality test, 3.1 s; with `uniform_tests`, 6.3 s and 0.21 GB. `1f0649e274faa626` + `7c2becb054a53765` with `uniform_tests`: 11.5 s and 0.45 GB.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_uniform_tests.py` (the forms it rewrites and leaves; with Xcode, the plain shader's IR has a switch with `dmsh_build.py`'s and `ios_metal.py`'s commands and the rewritten one none).

### Specialized second functions in the offline pipeline archive

- **Symptom:** the Mac app's (or the iPhone build's) pipeline archive step stops in its first group with `[RED] pso_offline: metal-tt failed for applegpu_<arch> (exit 1): applegpu-nt: error: cannot find private metadata at offset '8' in '…/<key>_bp.metallib'`, and the app is packed without an archive, compute pipelines included. The host's prebuild then makes every base on the first start. Only a game with the near = 0 depth mode (`depth_mode zero`) whose vertex shaders declare the border corrections' constant.
- **Cause:** `pso_offline.py` specializes a function that declares the [border corrections' constant](rendering.md#unused-border-corrections-retain-sample-registers) `dm_border_off` as the host's prebuild does (`HOST_CONSTANTS`, `pre_fn` in `nvn_mtl.m`). `metal-tt` 32023.922 specializes only a library's first function: it does not find a later function's private metadata (offset 8 is the second function's entry), also in a library linked by Apple's own `metallib`. The same function with the library's two functions swapped compiles, and the then-second `main0` fails, so it is the position, not the entry. A near = 0 game asks for `main0_z`, the second function of each pulling library `<key>_bp`; Ocarina of Time, where the specialization was first checked, uses `main0`, and a pixel library's base is its first function. One failing pipeline fails its group, and a failed group of bases ends the step. In NX 18 the same holds for the archive's other pipelines: a near = 0 layout base's vertex function `main0_z` (from `<key>_b` or its shadow-input `<key>_bv`), a near = 0 task / mesh pipeline's mesh function (`entry_z`), and a layout base's integer, mixed or dual-source pixel entry (`entry_u`, `entry_m`, `entry_d`) that is not its library's first function; those groups were refused and halved, or left out, instead of ending the step.
- **Fix:** a workaround for Apple's compiler: a specialized function that is not its library's first is specialized from a library of its own (`needs_cut`, `cut_entries`, over the bases, the compute pipelines, the task / mesh pipelines and the layout bases). `real_libraries` writes `<library>@<entry>.metallib` with that function alone (`dmsh_side.cut`, the bytes `metallib --function-name` gives) beside links to the group's other libraries (a folder's libraries are linked, not copied; one-file libraries are copied as before). The archive serves the host's lookup through the whole library, as a base is found by its function, not by its library file. The script and the archive key do not change for a game whose specialized functions all come first.
- **Found on:** Bayonetta Origins: Cereza and the Lost Demon (`0100CF5010FEC000`, no profile yet; [issue #2337](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2337), `applegpu_g14s`: 492 of 4,772 bases); [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md#found-on-this-game) and [Castlevania: Belmont's Curse](../../dm/games/0100BA4026E94000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (`applegpu_g16s`); Nintendo Switch Sports Resort (`04008E001F098000`, no profile yet; [PR #2371](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2371): 2,401 of 11,075 bases, from 124 such libraries); reproduced on an Apple M6, macOS 27.2, compiling a two-function library built by Xcode for `applegpu_g17p`. With the fix, Switch Sports Resort's 2,401 bases compiled in 96 s, and 11 sampled bases were found through the whole libraries. Still there with `metal-tt` 32023.925 (Xcode 27 beta, macOS 27.3, an M4 Max) for `applegpu_g16s` and `applegpu_g19p`: a two-function library's specialized `main0_z` fails as a pair base, as a layout base with a vertex descriptor and as a task / mesh pipeline's mesh function, and so does a pixel library's second entry `main0_u`; each compiles from its cut library.
- **Check:** `test_pso_offline.py` (`test_a_specialized_function_after_the_first_has_a_library_of_its_own` for bases, `Layout.test_a_specialized_vertex_function_after_the_first_has_a_library_of_its_own` for layout bases and `entry_u`, `Mesh.test_a_specialized_entry_z_has_a_library_of_its_own` for `entry_z`, and with Xcode `test_the_cut_library_holds_the_function_alone`, for a folder and for a one-file image); `t_pso_bundle` (a pulling library whose `main0` and `main0_z` declare the constant, archived for near = 0: `pre_desc` finds its `main0_z` base and no `main0` base; without the fix `pso_offline.py` fails).

### Bracket nesting past the front end's 256

- **Symptom:** the shader build prints `fatal error: bracket nesting level exceeded maximum of 256` and `<key>.metal: does not compile`, and the shader is left out.
- **Cause:** a translated expression nests deeper than the Metal front end's default limit of 256 brackets. Ocarina of Time's compute shaders `1f78730053feb036` and `e172bd7ec218ab8e` (3.5 MB of MSL each) reach 259.
- **Fix:** every library compile passes `-fbracket-depth=1024` (`dmsh_build.py` `METAL_DEPTH`, `ios_metal.py` `DEPTH`). A source within the old limit compiles to the same bytes with the flag, on macOS and iOS. Like the precompiled header, the flag is not in the compile records, so the Metal cache keeps every library already compiled.
- **Found on:** Ocarina of Time (Switch 2, `0400486024DEC000`, PR #1299); both shaders now compile.
- **Check:** `test_shader_pch.py` (`BracketDepth`: 300 nested brackets fail without the flag and compile with it, the test shaders' bytes are unchanged, and both builds take the same depth).

### min, max and clamp with a NaN operand

- **Symptom:** the half of Mario facing the camera's left was white (GitHub issue 72, on an iPhone).
- **Cause:** spirv-cross writes `FMin` / `FMax` / `FClamp` as `fast::min` / `max` / `clamp`, which leave a NaN operand's result to the GPU. Maxwell's `FMNMX` returns the other operand and its saturation gives 0, as Metal's `precise::` functions (IEEE `fmin` / `fmax`) do. Plain `max` / `min` / `clamp` compile to the `fast_` intrinsics in every Metal math mode, relaxed included ([iOS math mode (relaxed)](#ios-math-mode-relaxed)). Galaxy's GX lighting normalizes light directions the game leaves at (0, 0, 0) (the key and fill lights of its characters) and takes `max(0, dot(ldir, NaN))` as the spot term: 0 on the M4 Pro with either function, so the attenuation is 1. The fill light sits at the camera's left in view space, so its terminator runs down the middle of a character facing the camera.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` (`precise`) writes the `precise::` functions instead (every game; the Vulkan and GL shader tools did too, until 0.5 removed those layers).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Galaxy 2](../../dm/games/0100FD8022DAA000/README.md) (GitHub issue 72). The NVN chain had about 124,000 `fast::max`, 98,000 `fast::clamp` and 41,000 `fast::min` in [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md), none guarded by its `isnan` selects.

### Integer fragment outputs declared by hand

- **Superseded (2026-11-16):** every pixel shader has the typed-output entry point ([Typed outputs for every pixel shader](#typed-outputs-for-every-pixel-shader)), and the six profiles' rows below were removed; the pipeline red line prints a row only for a shader whose `main0` has a shape `fs_uint` does not know, where the root-cause fix is teaching `fs_uint` that shape. The text below is how it was.
- **Symptom:** a pipeline red line for a float output meeting an integer attachment (the red line prints the row to add); a BC encoder's pipeline fails.
- **Cause:** with no PC version the translator has no output types and declares every output float, and Metal refuses a pipeline whose float output meets an integer attachment. A scan for outputs built from integer operations was not trusted: the translated code keeps float bits in integer variables (register reuse), so such a scan cannot tell an integer output from raw float bits stored into a float target.
- **Fix:** the profile's `shader_facts_manual.tsv` declares such outputs `uint`. When another one fails, the pipeline red line prints the row to add. Shaders that write an output from integer bits now also get integer entry points ([Integer entry points for integer colour outputs](#integer-entry-points-for-integer-colour-outputs)).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) (agl's BC4 encoder `8e2cc2671cae6020` and its twin for a 2D source `93aa5e22a8202409`, whose failure showed up in an iPhone run); [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md) (the BC4 encoder `0544282c162305db` and the Ultrahand mask `e3fb0bb58a691e10`, R8Uint, found by PR #11; the Mac route does not reach it); [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md) (two system pixel shaders write an ID into an `R8_UI` target on the character creation screen); [DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined](../../dm/games/0100A9D01C446000/README.md) (one global shader writing an `R8Uint` target, [Unreal Engine 4.27 shader code archives](#unreal-engine-427-shader-code-archives-lz4)).
- **Note:** the compressed render target side is in [Compressed textures as render targets through an uncompressed shadow](rendering.md#compressed-textures-as-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow).

### A pixel shader's depth after a partly written target

- **Symptom:** a pixel shader that writes depth and only some components of a colour target writes the wrong depth: in the translated MSL, `gl_FragDepth` takes a value the shader computes a step before its last one, or a colour.
