- **Cause:** the colours are read four registers per render target with any component enabled (component c of the n-th such target in R(4n + c)), then comes the sample mask register, then the depth. `TranslatorContext.GetDepthRegister` counted enabled components instead, which agrees only when each enabled target has all four. With OmapTargets 0x10f (RT0 RGBA, RT2 R) the shader computes its depth into R9, `R6 = 1 / -(z * a + b)` then two constant adds, and R6 was read.
- **Fix:** `GetDepthRegister` returns 4 × the enabled targets + 1. A chain change.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py DepthRegister` (R6 0.25, R9 0.75: the old translator writes 0.25).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 46 of the 126 depth-writing pixel shaders in its shader package (mask 0x10f ×24, 0x100 ×16, 0x3f ×4, 0x30 ×2), 16 of them in its seen shaders, among them agl_common's depth pass `3a4a0270fc35b848`, whose depth now takes the step after R6 too, `(R6 * c3[32].z + 1) * c3[32].w`. Its effect on the picture has not been compared. No other local game has such a shader: the 1,105 depth-writing pixel shaders of seven Switch 1 games enable whole targets. Found by the flaviopz fork.

### Integer entry points for integer colour outputs

- **Symptom:** `failed to create render pipeline … output of type float4 is not compatible with a MTLPixelFormatR32Uint color attachment` (or `MTLPixelFormatRGBA16Uint`); every draw into the target skipped.
- **Cause:** with no PC version the translator types every fragment output float, and the shaders write bit-packed integers through `as_type<float>`. A hand-written row per shader (`shader_facts_manual.tsv`, Breath of the Wild's way, [Integer fragment outputs declared by hand](#integer-fragment-outputs-declared-by-hand)) does not scale.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_entry.py` `fs_uint` gives a pixel shader a second entry point, `main0_u` (since 2026-11-16 every pixel shader, with signed members too: [Typed outputs for every pixel shader](#typed-outputs-for-every-pixel-shader); the text below is how it began). It was given when the shader writes an output from integer bits (directly or through a float local that holds them), nothing but constants (a clear drawn into an R32Uint target) or nothing but flat inputs passed through. Its output struct holds two members on each float colour `n`, the float and the unsigned integer vector of the same bits, each present by a bool function constant (`DMSH_UINT_FC + n`, `dm_ui<n>`), and it returns each colour as whichever is present: the hardware writes the output's bits to an integer target whatever the shader computed, so the copy is exact. The host specializes it with the pipeline's integer targets (`pso_uint_mask`, `uint_fn`: one function per set, kept), whether all of them are integer or some, and the alpha test and logic operation entry points `main0_u_a` and `main0_u_l` the same way (`at_fn`, `lo_fn`). A shader without `main0_u` keeps `main0` and the red line.
- **Found on:** [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md): about 1,400 pixel shaders writing its G-buffer of two integer targets (R32Uint, RGBA8Uint), so every scene draw was skipped. [Pokémon Sword](../../dm/games/0100ABF008968000/README.md): 10 pipelines as the opening's stadium loads; the scene shaders write colour 0 and 2 as floats and colour 1 from integer bits into an RGBA16Uint target (colour 1 in all 336 of Sword's). [Silver Pines](../../dm/games/0100D5B026844000/README.md): URP's rendering-layers mask, an R8Uint target beside float outputs (colour 1 forward, 4 and 5 deferred), in 9,720 pixel shaders; without it only the fog drew. Trails in the Sky 2nd Chapter (upstream's profile `01007A2027548000`, not in NX 18): its G-buffer float at colour 0 and integer at colours 1 to 3.

### Integer outputs told by the pipeline's targets

- **Symptom:** a deferred scene with part of its materials unlit or black: grass, ground and trees dark while wood and characters look right, whole areas of a map black. There may be no red line. A texture dump shows an integer G-buffer target (`fmt=73`, RGBA8Uint) holding only zeros, and the lighting pass reads it. Or `[RED] shader … writes colour target …, an integer format, as a float`.
- **Cause:** a G-buffer of float and integer targets needs a pixel shader to return some outputs as floats and others as integers. Until 2026-11-13 the build chose which from the shader's code and gave such a shader fixed mixed entry points (`main0_m`, then `main0_n`), and the host took one only when the pipeline's integer targets were exactly its set. The translator keeps registers in integer locals, so a plain float colour reaches its output as integer bits too, and the code says less than the pipeline does. A shader with no entry point for its pipeline kept `main0`, whose float output met the integer attachment: a pipeline built on its own is refused for that, but one specialized from a Metal 4 base is not, and the target stayed zero with no error. Kirby Star Allies' G-buffer is RGBA16F, RGBA8 sRGB, RGBA8Uint, RG11B10F: 283 of its pixel shaders got the mask 0b111, 8 got 0b101 and 396 none. Following each write back through the locals (`int_outputs`) fixed Kirby, and Trails in the Sky 2nd Chapter then needed four more rules in turn, each found by its red line in play: a uniform word written straight into an output, outputs of nothing but zeros, a register value every branch overwrites, a texel read with `read()`. Each was beside integer outputs into integer targets, and each rule moved other games' masks.
- **Fix:** the pipeline's formats say which outputs are integers, as they do on the hardware: `main0_u` returns each colour as a float or as integer bits by a function constant, and the host specializes it per pipeline ([Integer entry points for integer colour outputs](#integer-entry-points-for-integer-colour-outputs)). No rule reads which outputs hold integers any more; the build only decides whether a shader gets `main0_u` at all. Over five of the local games' libraries that gives `main0_u` to the shaders that had it before and a few more (PRAGMATA 403 of 15,877 pixel shaders, Pikmin 4 119, Luigi's Mansion 3 5, Fire Emblem Engage 8, none in OCTOPATH TRAVELER II, Diablo II: Resurrected, Hollow Knight: Silksong or Bastion), and with `main0_m` and `main0_n` gone, fewer entry points in all (PRAGMATA's 8,578 `main0_u` and 3,212 `main0_m` became 8,981).
- **Found on:** [Kirby Star Allies](../../dm/games/01007E3006DDA000/README.md): 687 pixel shaders whose integer target stayed zero. Trails in the Sky 2nd Chapter (upstream's profile `01007A2027548000`, not in NX 18): the red line from five shaders in turn (`10cd95a2a310ea8c`, `979fd4d4d4bd7615`, `b09f3ac96129e558`, `96ec090803707686`, `aa682f8e32981529`).
- **What made a shader an integer writer** (until 2026-11-16, when every pixel shader got `main0_u`: [Typed outputs for every pixel shader](#typed-outputs-for-every-pixel-shader)): a float local that holds a register's bits counts: [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md)'s visibility pass writes `float _439 = as_type<float>(int(gl_PrimitiveID))` and the depth to an RG32Uint target, and without it no shader got `main0_u` (`[RED] task / mesh pipeline failed: … output of type float4 is not compatible with a MTLPixelFormatRG32Uint color attachment`). So do writes after an early return: four of those shaders (`a9c1f9ea0c76a54c`, `1282ed0df15021c7`, `a88532b3a2bbc36f`, `bfce48e3c0debea2`) return zeros early, after a discard, and write the primitive id and depth after that; read only up to the first `return out;`, they got none, and the whole 3D scene was black behind the UI.

### Integer entry points for flat inputs passed through beside constants

- **Symptom:** an Unreal landscape drawn from a runtime virtual texture is one flat lit colour on the Mac (green grass with no texture) and a grid of repeating tiles on an iPhone; rocks, characters and everything else right; no red line. A texture dump shows the virtual texture's BC tile caches full and varied, and its page table (R16Uint, `fmt=23`, 512x512 with 10 mips, the texture the landscape's pixel shader reads at `reg130`) holding 255 in every entry: level 15, page address 15, so the sample lands on one texel of the cache. Since this entry, `[RED] shader … writes colour target …, an integer format, as a float, and has no integer entry point`.
- **Cause:** `fs_uint` gave a pixel shader its integer entry point when its outputs were all written from integer bits, all constants, or all flat inputs passed through. Unreal's page table update (global shader `17eda07e4bac39f8`; its vertex shader `478292f0f6d79ae4` reads the update records from a texture buffer and packs the page's level and address into a flat varying) writes the flat input's x and the constant 0 to y, z and w: neither rule held, `main0` kept its `float4` output, a pipeline specialized from a Metal 4 base was built without an error ([Integer outputs told by the pipeline's targets](#integer-outputs-told-by-the-pipelines-targets)), and the R16Uint target took 255 at every pixel drawn. That entry's red line fires only for a shader that has an integer entry point, so nothing named the shader.
- **Fix:** `fs_uint` takes any mix of constants and flat inputs passed through. The reflection now records which colour outputs `main0` declares float (`raster.float_colors`, `raster_facts`), and the pipeline path (`pso_desc`) prints the red line above, with the `shader_facts_manual.tsv` row (`pso_fout_hint`), when such an output meets an unsigned integer attachment and no integer entry point fits, whether the shader has one or none. A chain change.
- **Check:** `python3 -m unittest dm/tools/tests/test_shader_rewrites.py` (`TypedOutputs`: since [Typed outputs for every pixel shader](#typed-outputs-for-every-pixel-shader) every shader gets the entry point, whatever it writes).
- **Found on:** [DRAGON BALL: Sparking! ZERO](../../dm/games/010035F022078000/README.md): the landscape of the story battles' stage on an M1 (base v0), and [#1889](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1889) (an iPhone 17). Rebuilt with the change, the floor shows its dirt, grass and tracks, with no new red line; the iPhone is still to be checked.

### Typed outputs for every pixel shader

- **Symptom:** upstream, on the `MTLDevice` path (NX 18 has none: its pipelines always come from Metal 4 bases, where only the red line below and undefined values show) `[RED] failed to create render pipeline … output of type float4 is not compatible with a MTLPixelFormatR8Uint color attachment` for many fragment shaders, and most of the scene missing (black sky, no characters). With `DM_PSO_MTL4=1` the same pipelines build from Metal 4 bases and the picture looks right, but `[RED] shader … writes colour target 5, an integer format, as a float, and has no integer entry point` stays: the target takes undefined values. A signed integer target (`R32Sint`, …) is refused the same way, by every shader.
- **Cause:** the class of a colour output (float, unsigned, signed) is its target's: Maxwell writes the register's bits whatever the shader computed. `fs_uint` gave a pixel shader its integer entry point `main0_u` only when its code looked like an integer writer (integer bits, constants, flat inputs passed through), rules each found by a red line in play and each missing the next game: Kirby and the Forgotten Land's 76 shaders writing its R8Uint colour 5 matched none. `main0_u` had no signed members, so a signed target had no entry point at all, and the host's red line fired only for unsigned targets.
- **Fix:** every pixel shader of a known shape gets `main0_u`, whatever its code writes. Its output struct holds three members on each colour `n` declared `float`, `uint` or `int` (scalar or vector): the float, unsigned and signed vector of the same width, each present by a bool function constant (`DMSH_UINT_FC + n`, `DMSH_SINT_FC + n`; the float where neither is set), and returns the colour's bits as whichever is present (`as_type`, nothing converted); a colour of another type (`half`) or shape stays as `main0` has it. `main0_u` also holds the fixed-function work Metal lacks, each switched by a constant with its arguments present only then: the alpha test (`DMSH_TYPED_ALPHA_FC`, on colour 0's alpha before the outputs are typed, `dmsh_alpha.test`) and the logic operation (`DMSH_TYPED_LOGIC_FC`, on the typed outputs, reading the attachments `DMSH_LOGIC_FC + n` names, `dmsh_logic.parts`), so it replaces `main0_u_a`, `main0_u_l` and `main0_l`, and one function serves every variant a pipeline asks for. `main0_a` stays, with no constant, so an alpha-tested pipeline of the declared classes needs no specialization at run time. The build cuts `main0_u` into the side library `<key>_bx` (`ps_libraries`, `entry_u_side`, `entry_u_alpha`, `entry_u_logic` in the reflection), so the base library every shader loads, and the pipeline archive compiles, keeps `main0` alone with no function constant. A side library that does not build leaves `main0_u` in `<key>_b`, and one that does not build with it is built without it (`entries_lost`), as before. The reflection records which colours `main0` declares float, unsigned and signed (`raster.float_colors`, `uint_colors`, `sint_colors`); the host (`pso_cls`, `typed_fn`, one function per set of classes and switches, kept) takes `main0_u` when a target's class is not the declared one, in either direction (a `fout` fact that declares an output unsigned meeting a float target too), or when the pipeline has a logic operation, with the alpha test on when the pipeline has one; the side library is loaded once per shader on first need (`bx_lib`). An alpha test with a logic operation, which no library combined, now draws through `main0_u` (`lop_key`). The red line now names a shader whose pipeline needs a class it cannot give: one whose `main0` has a shape `fs_uint` does not know, with the `shader_facts_manual.tsv` row (`pso_fout_hint`, also on a refused pipeline, only for such a shader). The hand-written `fout` rows of six profiles went with it (Breath of the Wild's and Tears of the Kingdom's BC4 encoders and Ultrahand mask, Pokémon Legends: Z-A's, DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined's and FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE's): their shaders take `main0_u` like any other. The BC encoders write bits no float keeps (NaN payloads, denormals) through `main0`'s float output, and `main0_u` gives them to an integer target unchanged (`t_typed_outputs`' colour 3). Replaces PR [#2807](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2807)'s second entry point `main0_t` for the same problem. A chain change.
- **Cost:** measured on 200 of Cuphead's pixel shaders with a cold compile cache (M4 Pro): the iOS libraries 13.55 → 13.92 MB (+2.8%), the Mac's 17.54 → 18.62 MB (+6.1%), build time within noise (13–14 s both once warm). A first version with `main0_u_a` and `main0_u_l` as entry points of their own took +47% and +38%: each is a whole copy of the shader's body. The base library, the pipeline archive, frame time and the draws of pipelines of the declared classes do not change. The first pipeline of a shader with a target of another class or a logic operation loads its side library and specializes `main0_u` (a synchronous `newFunctionWithName:constantValues:`), as `main0_u` and `main0_l` always did.
- **Check:** `python3 -m unittest dm/tools/tests/test_shader_rewrites.py` (`TypedOutputs`), `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_typed.py` (the unit, the cut and the reflection's classes), `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_typed_outputs` (every mix of float, unsigned and signed targets on colours 0 to 2, and the alpha test of a float colour 0 beside integer targets, through `pso_desc` on the Metal 4 path and the `MTLDevice`'s, the bits read back), `check_t_logic` (the logic operation through `main0_u`) and `check_t_alpha`.
- **Found on:** [Kirby and the Forgotten Land](../../dm/games/01004D300C5AE000/README.md#found-on-this-game) on an iPhone ([#2760](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2760)): 76 shaders in Waddle Dee Town writing an R8Uint colour 5. Mortal Kombat 11's signed and unsigned material outputs (PR [#2807](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2807)).
- **NX 18:** ported 2026-11-17 (upstream `c75f0126625`) onto NX 18's older form of `fs_uint` (`main0_u` for all-unsigned targets, `main0_m` for mixed ones, chosen by the shader's code; upstream's `c398ff35b` and `4245f9b4d` in between were not ported, and this one replaces them). NX 18 builds every pipeline through MTL4Compiler: `typed_fn` specializes `main0_u` with `fn_spec` (a `FnSpec` noted for the Metal 4 descriptor, as `lo_fn` does), and `pso_cls` keeps the merged pass's `lmap` mapping NX 18 had. The offline archive (`pso_offline.py fragment_entry`) mirrors `pso_cls`: a row whose targets need `main0_u` is left to the device, since the build puts it in `<key>_bx` with its constants.

### Integer textures and images through float declarations keep their bits

- Integer textures and images read and written through float declarations (an `RGBA8_UINT` texture read by `texture3d<float>` and bit-cast, `R16_UINT` / `R32_UINT` buffers through `texture_buffer<float>`, integer stores into a float-declared `texture3d` image) were checked on an Apple GPU: all keep the integer bits, so they need no facts.
- Metal leaves a mismatch between texture format and shader type undefined, so the host does not rely on it where it can avoid it: 32-bit integer buffer images get matching float views. See [Float views for integer buffer images](rendering.md#float-views-for-integer-buffer-images).
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined](../../dm/games/0100A9D01C446000/README.md) (the volumetric lightmap's indirection texture, the light grid buffers).

### Integer-typed vertex inputs fed normalized formats

- **Symptom:** `failed to create render pipeline … Cannot convert attribute …`; draws missing (a pipeline that fails to build).
- **Cause:** the translation declares a vertex input `uint4` when the shader only moves its bits (x bit-cast to an integer, y copied as a float; [Integer vertex inputs](rendering.md#integer-vertex-inputs)), and a game that feeds it a 16-bit normalized format gets a pipeline Metal will not build (no integer input pairs with a normalized format).
- **Fix:** the profile's `shader_facts_manual.tsv` declares such inputs float (the bits Maxwell would see), which changes only that profile's chain version. The guess is not wrong in general, so overrides are added only for shaders seen failing with float formats. The general replay-side answers are the CPU conversion and the shadow vertex inputs ([CPU conversion of vertex attributes the input type cannot take](rendering.md#cpu-conversion-of-vertex-attributes-the-input-type-cannot-take), [Shadow vertex inputs](rendering.md#shadow-vertex-inputs)).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md): missing palm tree and flowers (the pipeline `147c829e79fb322f` + `0ebba5ff6a44aa52`, `shadowmap_pbr.bfsha` variant 641, vertex inputs 1–5): the foliage's shadow-map and depth pre-pass draws; the main pass shades with depth test EQUAL, so without the pre-pass the plants never appeared. Four outdoor shaders with the same loop (`03c43da3e3f46da8`, `079700f3577e311f`, `15c02683a10f8e9d`, `2344874045b1e758`) failed the same way on the iPhone in Mabe Village (Half2, RG8 / RG16 normalized) and are listed too. 1,437 of the game's vertex shaders have integer-typed inputs, many of them bone indices the shader multiplies as integers.

### Depth mode near = 0 needs the per-mode entry points

- **Symptom:** outdoors the whole screen blurred (depth of field out of focus everywhere).
- **Cause:** a game that sets depth mode near = 0 had every vertex shader translated for GL, so all depths sat in 0.5..1.
- **Fix:** the per-mode entry points of [04, section 5](../agents/04-graphics.md#5-depth-convention).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md) (Mac and iPhone): the depth-of-field composite (`52fcbb4ca2a78907`, `postprocess.bfsha`) blends the sharp frame with two blurred levels by a blur amount computed from the half-size depth copy: `pow(clamp((c5[2].y / (depth + c5[2].x) − c5[3].x) · c5[3].y), c5[3].z)` above a screen line, by screen position below it. Found with a save file from the iPhone (Mabe Village outside the house), `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` / `DM_NVN_TEXDUMP` of that frame, and LASRecomp, which rewrites z only for the GL mode.

### Open: the window Y-direction register `SR_Y_DIRECTION`

- The register is −1 under a lower-left origin. The Ryujinx recompiler answers a constant +1 (a TODO in `InstEmitMove.cs`; LASRecomp, a static recompilation of Link's Awakening, answers −1). Answering it per profile means a translator change, i.e. every game's chain.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md): 2,487 of its pixel shaders read it; in the room shader the value only scales a depth slope under `abs()`; other shaders may use its sign (`dFdy`-based effects).

### Subgroup operations rewritten for a one-lane subgroup

- **Symptom:** `SPIRV-Cross threw an exception: Subgroup builtins are not available in this type of function`.
- **Cause:** Metal has no SIMD-group functions in vertex functions, and SPIRV-Cross's own subgroup emulation covers only `Elect`. RE Engine scalarizes uniform loads: ballot the active lanes, shuffle the first lane's value to all and take a fast path when all agree.
- **Fix:** with a one-lane subgroup that is exact, so `dmsh_build.py` rewrites the vertex and domain shaders' SPIR-V that way before SPIRV-Cross (`spv_single_lane`: the ballot is the lane's own bit, shuffles and broadcasts return its own value, all / any are the predicate, all-equal and elect are true, lane ID 0, size 1). A mask component read through `OpAccessChain` / `OpInBoundsAccessChain` becomes an extraction from the constant mask, preserving the index.
- **Found on:** [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md) (about 4 % of its vertex shaders); [Xenoblade Chronicles 3](../../dm/games/010074F013262000/README.md) (vertex `4b98320a0f512efe`, a prefix ballot that reads `SubgroupLtMask` through a component pointer).
- **Types the module lacks:** the rewrite's constants need `bool` and a `uint` 4-vector; a module without one (a shader that reads only the lane ID) was left as it was, and now gets the type declared with the constants.
- **Note:** domain shaders take the rewrite too: SPIRV-Cross writes them as post-tessellation vertex functions, with the same limit (PRAGMATA's `859225c91a5fe2b2` and `f1ad8f78c6c1551e` did not build; two of Bayonetta 3's failed this way, not rebuilt since). The host draws no tessellated patches yet ([Tessellation stages are not drawn](#tessellation-stages-are-not-drawn)).

### Geometry shaders that only pick the layer

- **Symptom:** the tone mapper read an empty LUT and the screen came out a flat blue.
- **Cause:** Unreal Engine 4.18 builds its 32×32×32 colour-grading LUT with `WriteToSliceMainVS` + `FWriteToSliceGS`, one slice per instance. Metal has no geometry stage.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` recognises such a shader in its SPIR-V (`gs_slice`: triangles in, at most 3 vertices out, positions and other inputs copied, `gl_Layer` taking the bits of one input component). It builds no library for it and records the component in its reflection. Each vertex shader that writes integer bits from the instance index into an output component gets two more entry points (`vs_layer_entry`: `main0_l` / `main0_lz` write that component as `[[render_target_array_index]]`). At bind time `prog_fold_gs` (`nvn_mtl.m`) swaps the vertex shader for its layer variant and drops the geometry stage when the components match. The pass and pipeline side of layer-writing vertex shaders: [Layered passes for vertex shaders that write the layer](rendering.md#layered-passes-for-vertex-shaders-that-write-the-layer), [Layer-writing vertex shaders get the triangle class](#layer-writing-vertex-shaders-get-the-triangle-class).
- **Note:** Unreal binds each stage from a program of its own, so a vertex shader is often paired for a moment with the previous draw's geometry shader. A stub that does not fit stays in the program, and only a draw that uses it prints a red line (`draw_gs_check`). The other 22 geometry shaders in DQ11 (one-pass cube shadows that emit transformed triangles) still do not build ([Geometry shaders with no Metal stage](#geometry-shaders-with-no-metal-stage)).
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST XI S](../../dm/games/01006C300E9F0000/README.md).

### NVN2 task and mesh shaders

- **Symptom:** `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferDrawMeshTasks`, and a build that fails with `Ampere instruction 927 ... is not implemented` in shaders the map calls vertex or domain; what they draw is missing.
- **Cause:** NVN2 has NVIDIA's task and mesh shaders, stages 6 and 7. The control section says so (+0x56), while the SPH reads as vertex or domain (type 1 or 3), so the split filed them there. Their stage memory has instructions of its own: ISBERD reads a workgroup's number (mode 0x78) or a payload word (0x38), and 0x927 writes a task's payload word (0xb8), a mesh attribute word (0xf8), the primitive count byte (0x88, at +3) or a primitive index byte (0x80, from +4). Control +0x58 is the workgroup's threads, +0x5a the payload's bytes, +0x5e whether a mesh shader has a task, +0x5f a primitive's vertices (1 point, 2 line, 3 triangle), +0x60 and +0x62 a mesh shader's most vertices and primitives, and +0x64 the code area offset of its per-primitive SPH where +0x68 bit 7 is set. Attributes come in all the blocks of 32 vertices, then all the blocks of 32 primitives ([Mesh attributes: every vertex block before the primitive blocks](#mesh-attributes-every-vertex-block-before-the-primitive-blocks)), each component 0x80 bytes: a vertex block the components the SPH's OMAP enables (byte 0x35 bit 3 the point size, bits 4..7 the position, bytes 0x36 on a nibble a generic attribute), a primitive block the per-primitive SPH's. A task writes its launch count at payload +4 and its records from +0x10; the hardware puts a mesh workgroup's number among its task's launches at payload +0.
- **Fix:**
  - `shader_split.py` names the stages `ts` and `ms`, as `task` and `mesh` in the shader map.
  - `dmsh_build.py` translates one as compute, its SPH taken off, with its stage memory in shared memory at the offsets `dmsh_meshstage.memory` gives (job `stage_memory`, `AIsberd` and `AStageOut` in `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs`). A byte store goes through the shared memory's byte store, which the build makes one Metal byte store, as neighbouring threads write neighbouring bytes ([Stage memory byte stores through a compare-and-swap loop](#stage-memory-byte-stores-through-a-compare-and-swap-loop)).
  - On the console a workgroup of 32 threads is one warp, so the zero count each thread writes first cannot pass the count one thread writes later. Metal's threads run in no such step, and a late zero would erase the count: that store is left out, and the wrapper starts the stage memory at zero.
  - `dmsh_meshstage.to_stage` wraps the kernel as a Metal `[[object]]` or `[[mesh]]` function: it fills the inputs, calls the kernel's body (whose early returns come back to it) and hands the outputs over. A mesh shader has `main0` and `main0_z` for the two depth conventions, as a vertex shader does.
  - The host keeps these stages in the tessellation stages' places, 3 and 4 (`hle_nvn.c` `st_host`, `nvn_mtl.m` `gfx_stage_bits`): a program has mesh shaders or tessellation, never both. `nvn_geometry.inc` `mesh_tasks_draw` draws through `draw_` with the pixel stage alone, on a mesh pipeline built in the background; the mesh stage shares the vertex stage's argument table, as a geometry mesh draw does, and a task shader has the object stage's. Their storage buffer tables in `c0` are at 0x670 and 0x770: [Task and mesh draws reading the dummy buffer](#task-and-mesh-draws-reading-the-dummy-buffer).
- **Not done:** `DrawMeshTasks3D` and the multi-draw forms have no game yet to show their arguments, so they stay red. A task or mesh shader with shared memory of its own (LDS, STS, ATOMS) is refused at build time: it would lie over the stage memory and the control section does not give its size. No game's does.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md): 7 task and 18 mesh shaders, which draw its bushes and grass. All 11 pipelines its first race makes build, and the race holds 60 fps with them. The facts of pull request #983 were checked against the game's code and hold, except its storage buffer table offsets.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_meshstage.py` (the stages compile for macOS and iOS where the Metal compiler is installed), `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`StageMemory`).

### Task and mesh draws reading the dummy buffer

- **Symptom:** large flat panels in front of the sky, the clouds behind them blinking at times, in some places and not others. In a `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP_ALL` dump every task and mesh shader's storage buffer shows as `st3` / `st4 sbufp reg6` or `reg7` `dummy addr=0 size=0`, though the game binds buffers for those stages.
- **Cause:** the task and mesh storage buffer tables in `c0` start at 0x670 and 0x770, and the host took them to start at 0x610 and 0x710, 0x100 apart as the other stages' are. Task shaders read `c0` 0x670 and 0x680 and mesh shaders 0x770 to 0x7b0, so the build gave them slots 6 and up, while the game binds slots from 0. Each read the shared dummy buffer. Three of those shaders write their storage, so that buffer probably held their data when the others read it (not observed directly): shapes drawn into a 128x128x5 array got values past 1,000 where the rest stay under 1, and the vertex shader that moves its vertices by that array lifted them into the sky.
- **Fix:** `DMSH_C0_SSBO_TS` and `DMSH_C0_SSBO_MS` in `dmsh_abi.h` are 0x670 and 0x770. The tables no longer lie over the graphics stages' single words at 0x610 to 0x647, so the host writes the largest point size for every graphics stage again.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md#sky-panels), [issue #1186](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1186).
- **Check:** `python3 -m unittest dm/tools/tests/test_dmsh_abi.py`.

### Stage memory byte stores through a compare-and-swap loop

- **Symptom:** a game with task and mesh shaders runs at a lower internal resolution than it could, or below its frame rate, and the GPU time sits in the pass that draws its mesh work (a render pass's start stamp is written after its vertex stage, where Metal runs the object and mesh stages, so in a `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` profile the mesh work shows as the gap before that pass or as its length). `DM_NVN_SKIP_MESH=1` (diagnostic: every task and mesh draw dropped, the picture wrong) takes most of that time away.
- **Cause:** the translator stores a byte or half-word of shared memory (`SharedStore8`, `SharedStore16`: STS.U8 / .U16, and a mesh shader's primitive count and index bytes, 0x927 modes 0x88 and 0x80) through a helper from upstream Ryujinx, a compare-and-swap loop on the word, because SPIR-V's shared memory is an array of words. A mesh workgroup's threads write neighbouring index bytes, so four threads contend for each word and each store retries, divergently, about four times. Metal's threadgroup memory takes byte and half-word stores, and C++'s memory model, which MSL follows, makes each byte its own memory location: neighbouring stores need no atomics.
- **Fix:** the helper is named `dmsh_shared_store8_<n>` / `dmsh_shared_store16_<n>` (`HelperFunctionManager.cs`), and `dmsh_rewrite.py` `shared_store_msl` replaces its body in the Metal source with one store through a `uchar` pointer, or an aligned `ushort` one marked `may_alias`, over the array (threadgroup, or device where `tg_spill` moved it). Character types and `may_alias` keep the compiler's type-based alias analysis from reordering the store against the word loads around it. Two threads storing the same byte at once leave one of the two values, as the loop did. A helper of another form stops that shader's build (`shared store rewrite failed`).
- **Not taken:** an atomic AND to clear the field and an atomic OR to set it (pull request #1503) need no retries either, but two threads writing the same byte leave the OR of both values, a value neither wrote; on the GPU that is what 64 threads writing different bits to one byte give.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 515 of 42,848 shaders (500 mesh shaders, the foliage among them, and 15 compute kernels with half-word stores). Pull request #1503 measured the atomic AND / OR form on an M3 Pro in Kokiri Forest: the foliage pass from 8.3-8.6 ms to 3.5-4.0 ms, and the game's internal resolution from 854x480 to 1120x630 (the frame from 25-28 ms to about 21 ms). On an M6 (12 GPU cores), standing on the forest deck, the three forms measure the same: GPU p50 14.0-14.6 ms with each, the two depth passes that hold the mesh work 3.1-3.3 and 2.2-2.4 ms, against 10.3 ms with the mesh draws dropped (three runs of each form, one of them with no pipeline archive). The store has not been measured on an M3. [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md): its 18 mesh shaders with a byte store build with the store for the Mac and iOS (not run).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_shared_store.py` (the rewrite, a spilled kernel, and on the GPU 64 threads storing neighbouring bytes and half-words over 64 dispatches); `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator>` adds the translated index byte store.

### Mesh stage memory of 6 KB and more in a device slice

- **Symptom:** a mesh shader whose stage array is 6-16 KB runs slower than its instruction count suggests, and the foliage mesh pass of Ocarina of Time takes a large share of a Kokiri Forest frame on an M3 Pro. Shrinking the array (timing only) made the same code faster.
- **Cause:** each 32-thread mesh group declares its whole stage array (`dmsh_meshstage.py` `memory()`: the input, the vertex and primitive attribute blocks, the primitive bytes) as threadgroup memory, 5-16 KB in the foliage meshes. Apple GPUs hold fewer groups at once the more threadgroup memory each takes, and the mesh bodies were occupancy-limited: about 0.09 ms per KB per 16,384 groups.
- **Fix:** `to_stage` takes the array from a device scratch buffer for a mesh shader (kind `ms`) of 32 threads and 6000 to 16384 bytes of stage memory. The stage body, the byte store helpers and the wrapper's barriers (now `mem_device | mem_threadgroup`) use the `device` address space; the mesh function takes `device uchar* dm_scr [[buffer(DMSH_MESH_SCRATCH_SLOT)]]`, slot 7 of the table the mesh stage shares with the vertex stage (a mesh draw binds only slot 0, the arguments, and this one; vertex draws rebind whatever slot they read, as `enc_r_vbuf` tracks every write). The reflection records `mesh_spill: 1` (`nvn_refl.m` into `rf.mesh_spill`).
  - **The pool.** One buffer in shared storage (`nvn_geometry.inc` `mesh_scratch`), made on first use: a head of 32-bit words, a flag word a slice, then a power of two of 16 KiB slices. The slices must outnumber the groups the GPU runs at once. With the array out of threadgroup memory an Apple GPU core holds at most 3072 threads, 96 groups of 32, and the pool is twice that so that a claim takes about two tickets at worst: the Mac reads its GPU's `gpu-core-count` from the I/O Registry (an M4 Pro's 20 cores: 4096 slices, 64 MiB), iOS cannot read it: an iPad's M-series GPU takes 2048 (32 MiB; at most 10 cores), and in NX 18 an iPhone's A-series GPU (6 cores from the A17 Pro to the A19 Pro, at most 576 groups at once) takes 1024 (16 MiB), still more slices than groups that can run, so a claim fails only after 64 slices in a row held by groups that 1023 later claims overtook; 16384 is the most. The GPU test measured at most 161 groups running at once on an M4 Pro, far below the bound.
  - **Claiming a slice.** Lane 0 takes a ticket with a relaxed atomic add, and claims slice `ticket & mask` when its flag is 0 or holds a tag no newer than the head's `DONE` word, by compare-and-swap to its own tag; up to `DMSH_MESH_SCRATCH_TRIES` (64) tickets. The group broadcasts the slice (32 threads are one SIMD-group), passes a device-scope fence, and zeroes the slice with `uint4` stores, as the threadgroup array started (the count byte relies on it). After its last read of the slice (a barrier after the index loop) and another device-scope fence, lane 0 clears the flag. A slice is handed out again only after its group finished, however long that group ran (GitHub pull request #2308: a first version took the slice at the ticket modulo the count, and a group that 8192 later groups overtook had its slice zeroed under it).
  - **Tags.** Before each draw of a spilled mesh shader the host writes the head's `TAG` word: the fence of the submission being encoded, so at least the fence of any submission whose groups read it (the commit follows the store, and the tag only grows). `post_complete` writes `DONE` as `g_done` advances, failed submissions included. A flag with a tag no newer than `DONE` belongs to a submission that is over, so its group runs no more: groups that a failed command buffer cut off leave their flags set, and the next claims reclaim them, with no wait on the GPU and no clearing pass. Tags compare by their signed 32-bit difference.
  - **No free slice.** A group that claims none in 64 tickets adds 1 to the head's `OVER` word, sets a primitive count of 0 and returns: it draws nothing and touches no slice it does not own. The host prints a red line once (`mesh scratch: … mesh groups found none of the … slices free and drew nothing`), which would mean the GPU ran more groups at once than the pool was sized for. An earlier version used its last ticket's slice without owning it after 8192 tickets.
- **Not taken:** below about 5.3 KB the device form gained nothing in the harness, so those shaders keep threadgroup memory. A group of more than 32 threads would broadcast its slice through a threadgroup word instead; no such mesh shader was measured, so they keep threadgroup memory.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): the real translated foliage mesh bodies in a standalone harness on an M3 Pro: 20-46 % faster for stage arrays of 6.5 KB and more (73dc0b90, 10.3 KB: 0.568 ms to 0.332 ms with a ticketed slice). In the game, Kokiri Forest on an M3 Pro (pull request #2308, the ticket-and-flag version against main, six alternating rounds): frame p50 24.70 to 23.65 ms standing and 24.95 to 23.87 ms on a replayed route, GPU p50 22.89 to 21.83 ms standing. The lease version on an M4 Pro (20 GPU cores, 2026-11-15, `scripts/bench.py` route `kokiri-deck`, three runs a side, no prebuild, the first run of each warming its own pipeline archive): 4096 slices, no group without one, no red line, the shots alike; in the two warm runs GPU p50 14.58 / 14.60 ms against 13.87 / 15.10 ms and p95 16.64 / 16.68 against 15.82 / 16.32, so the main side's frame step held two vsyncs (40 fps) and the slices' side mostly one (43.1 and 58.6 fps). Two runs are too few to call the GPU time different on that Mac.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_meshstage.py` (the wrapper's structure, and both depth conventions compiled for macOS and iOS). `DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_meshstage.py OnGpu` draws a spilled mesh stage on the GPU, 24,576 groups a draw, with the head's words set as the host sets them: (1) a long group among short ones, 8 submissions, where group 0 keeps reading its slice until 5,120 more tickets were taken from a pool of 4096: no lane may read a stamp another group wrote or zeroed, and every flag must be clear afterwards; (2) every flag holding a tag no newer than `DONE`, across the 32-bit wrap: all groups run; (3) every flag holding a newer tag: every group draws nothing and no byte of a slice changes; (4) a pool of 32 slices: the groups that run own their slices and the rest are counted. It skips when the GPU never let the pool's count of groups pass a running one. On an M3 Pro the ticket-modulo rule failed in the first draw (32 lanes). The host binding has no test of its own; it needs rebuilt shaders (the chain stamp changed with `dmsh_abi.h`).

### Geometry shaders with no Metal stage

- **Symptom:** a geometry shader fails to compile with `unknown main0_out`: SPIRV-Cross writes `unknown main0_out main0()` for the geometry stage, and the shader does not build.
- **Cause:** Metal has no geometry stage, so a geometry shader that does more than pick the layer has nothing to translate to. A geometry shader that only picks the layer is folded into the vertex shader instead ([Geometry shaders that only pick the layer](#geometry-shaders-that-only-pick-the-layer): `gs_slice`, `prog_fold_gs`; DRAGON QUEST XI S).
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Engage](../../dm/games/0100A6301214E000/README.md) (one geometry shader, in `unity_builtin_extra`; nothing in the opening scenes uses it); [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md) (fur fins, `RenderFinFur`, its one failing shader); [Captain Toad: Treasure Tracker](../../dm/games/01009BF0072D4000/README.md) (the fin fur geometry shader, as in Super Mario 3D World, is the one shader of its 7,258 that does not build); [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md) (53 geometry shaders); [FINAL FANTASY TACTICS - The Ivalice Chronicles](../../dm/games/010038B015560000/README.md) (one, `bahamuteffect.tec`); [Big Brain Academy: Brain vs. Brain](../../dm/games/0100620012D6E000/README.md) (Bezel's line renderer, below); [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md) (10 geometry shaders, one expands a box from two corners; no draw used one from the title through the opening cutscene to the lobby). The Unity games' built-in geometry shaders do not build either (the container entries above).
- **Note:** a program whose geometry shader has no library used to draw silently without that stage, with the vertex shader's positions taken as clip coordinates; `draw with geometry shader … which has no library` now names it (Luigi's Mansion 3).
- **Note:** Big Brain Academy's 12 geometry shaders (`_BezelSystemResources/line/shader/line.bfsha`) do not build (`unknown main0_out`) and their draws are skipped (`[RED] primitive type 11 not implemented`). They take a line strip with adjacency (primitive type 11) and emit up to 12 vertices per segment as triangle strips: thick lines with joins and caps (28 emit sites). From the main menu on, every frame draws one such line (width 10 in its uniform block) into the top-left 512x512 of the frame and copies that region into a texture, so the menu's chart loses its outlines. Not done.
- **Note:** geometry shaders that do more than pick the layer are now built as compute kernels from the vendored translator's geometry-to-compute transforms ([geometry shaders on Metal](../geometry-metal-research.md), [Passthrough geometry shaders](#passthrough-geometry-shaders)).

### Passthrough geometry shaders

- **Symptom:** the models a passthrough geometry shader draws are missing: its own draw (a depth prepass) shows nothing, and so do the draws that test against that depth for equality. Before the shader loaded at all, the same models showed as black silhouettes.
- **Cause:** bit 24 of the shader header's first word marks a Maxwell passthrough geometry shader (NV_geometry_shader_passthrough): the input primitive goes on to the rasterizer unchanged, and the shader only writes per-primitive outputs and memory. A viewport mask (`a[0x3a0]`) of 0 sends the primitive to no viewport, which drops it. Ryujinx emits the primitive itself only when the host reports no passthrough support. The translator's accessor kept the default answer, "supported", so the compute kernel emitted no vertex and nothing was rasterized.
- **Fix:**
  - `Program.cs` answers `QueryHostSupportsGeometryShaderPassthrough` with false for the compute geometry jobs;
  - the vendored translator then emits the input primitive at each return of the shader (`EmitterContext.PrepareForReturn`) with every attribute the shader does not write: it is translated without the next stage, against the fixed 144-word input map. It skips the primitive when the shader wrote a viewport mask of 0, and the mask starts at all viewports (`Translator.EmitOutputsInitialization`);
  - `geometry_finish` in `dmsh_build.py` takes the output vertex count and primitive class of such a shader from the input topology (the header declares one vertex of a triangle strip).
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): `IndexBuildIndependent_{b,x}{cw,ccw}.gso` are the depth prepass, one draw per mesh, and each also writes the mesh's culled index list and its count for the shading pass's indexed indirect draws.

### The topology table passed to a helper function

- **Symptom:** `[geometry failed]  missing geometry topology buffer` in the build, then `[RED] shader … (geometry) failed to load` and those draws skipped.
- **Cause:** when a block is reached from several places, SPIRV-Cross makes it a function and passes the table `trb_data` as a parameter. `topology_pointer` expected the one declaration in the argument buffer and only reads through it.
- **Fix:** it rewrites every declaration, the parameter too, and reads with or without the descriptor set in front.
- **Found on:** [Kirby Air Riders](../../dm/games/040077201BE50000/README.md): 26 of its 76 geometry shaders.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_geometry.py` (`TopologyPointer`).

### A captured draw's vertex IDs far apart

- **Symptom:** the host's memory watchdog closes the game (at 28.3 GB on Kirby Air Riders, whose Switch 2 has 9 GB), with the Metal heap full of frame arena blocks of 36 or 72 MB; it comes on quickly in a scene full of ribbons.
- **Cause:** a geometry capture sized its vertex space by the range of the draw's vertex IDs. Kirby Air Riders draws ribbons with 144 instances and vertex IDs spread over 65,537 or 131,073, so a draw of few real vertices asked 36 or 72 MB of scratch, per draw, every frame.
- **Fix:** `geo_prep` (`nvn_geometry.inc`) compacts a sparse draw when that saves at least `GEO_COMPACT_MIN` (1 MB): the vertices actually used are captured in contiguous runs (`nvn_geo_capture_runs`), one capture per run, and the geometry stage reads through a map from the original ID to the compact one (`nvn_geo_compact_map`). Up to `GEO_COMPACT_RUNS` runs; a kernel that keeps its original addressing has the original map.
- **Check:** `t_geometry` checks the runs, the map and a draw through both.
- **Found on:** [Kirby Air Riders](../../dm/games/040077201BE50000/README.md): resident from 28.3 GB (and closed) to about 10 GB through a race.

### Geometry passthrough and viewport masks in compute

- **Symptom:** geometry reader compilation fails with undeclared `gl_BuiltIn_5253` and a scalar `.elements` access. Compiling only the compute kernel also leaves native passthrough geometry without emitted vertices.
- **Cause:** the accessor advertises NV geometry passthrough, but Metal needs explicit compute emission. The reader tries to expose the unsupported NV viewport-mask array, and the raw SPH output bound can be one even though passthrough emits a complete input primitive. Geometry storage buffers also need their own driver-table base, and `BufferUsageFlags.Write` is bit 0.
- **Fix:** compute jobs report native geometry passthrough unsupported, activating the translator's explicit passthrough emission. `geometry_vertices` records the translated bound per input topology. `GeometryToCompute` applies viewport-zero mask visibility to emitted indices before strip compaction; the vertex reader skips the mask output. This covers the current replay's viewport-zero path, not replication across multiple viewports. Geometry storage-buffer slots decode from `c0 + 0x410` in reflection and replay, and writable reflection follows the translator's actual flag definition.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata US 1.0.3](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md) (four index-building passthrough shaders; all 1,225 compile; visible/rejected primitive GPU fixtures pass).

### Geometry capture indices deduplicated with a bounded bitset

- **Symptom:** sorting repeated mesh indices consumes much of the render thread's CPU time.
- **Cause:** every indexed geometry draw copied and comparison-sorted its full input index list to construct sorted unique vertex capture IDs, even for static meshes rendered every frame.
- **Fix:** mark indices relative to the validated minimum in a bitset, then enumerate set bits in ascending order. The existing vertex-capture bound limits the bitset to about 57 KiB; primitive assembly retains the original index order and restart boundaries. Non-indexed captures need no unique index list. No cross-frame cache or new hazard assumptions are involved.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): a Mac CPU sample attributes 316 of 687 replay samples to `qsort`. The geometry tests cover repeated indices, a sparse range spanning multiple bitset words, restart, base vertex and instancing (121 GPU checks, also passing with forced conservative waits).

### Geometry runs retain external buffer dependencies

- **Symptom:** later geometry uses stale shader-written data or appears in the earlier draw's position when draws are combined into a geometry run.
- **Cause:** a run captures all vertex inputs, dispatches all geometry shaders, then rasterizes all draws. That reorders a later capture before an earlier geometry write. Shader writes can also communicate with other geometry or fragment work in the run. Separate scratch outputs do not prove independence of the game's own buffers.
- **Fix:** record external vertex-buffer, uniform and storage-buffer access ranges for each job, including write access and read-only storage metadata. A read/write or write/write overlap stops the run before the next capture; read-only sharing and disjoint writes can still batch. Unknown sizes, range overflow, indirect storage pointers, image accesses and typed buffers conservatively stop batching. Existing encoder barriers then order the separate jobs. `t_geometry_hazards` exercises an actual geometry write followed by vertex capture through a storage binding and through a uniform alias, checking both the counter and literal covered pixels. All 14 checks pass with early submission enabled or disabled. Removing the dependency guard causes six failures: both counters are stale and both dependent triangles disappear from their expected positions.
- **Found on:** investigation of [NieR:Automata US shadow jitter](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md#library-shadow-sequence-and-geometry-ordering). This is a reproduced general replay bug; its contribution to that game's reported shadow motion is not yet established.

### Geometry dependency checks skip read-only pairs

- **Symptom:** the CPU spends substantial replay time checking dependencies among geometry jobs that share many read-only buffers.
- **Cause:** the original dependency predicate compares every pair of access ranges, including read/read pairs that cannot communicate. Repeating that quadratic scan across a run can dominate the check even when each job has only one write. Even without read/read pairs, checking each new job against every earlier job of the run stays quadratic in the run's length: in Donkey Kong Bananza (runs of about 56 jobs, 19 ranges and 2 writes a job, hundreds of geometry draws a frame) that loop alone was about 8 % of the submitting thread's samples.
- **Fix:** maintain a 64-bit write mask as ranges are added or promoted from read to write. A run keeps two sorted sets of merged half-open intervals (`geo_run_access`, `nvn_geometry.inc`): the union of its jobs' writes and of their reads. A new job conflicts with some earlier job exactly when one of its ranges meets the write union or one of its writes meets the read union. Overlap with a union is overlap with one of its members, so merging touching and overlapping intervals loses nothing. Each check is a branch-free binary search, and a range already covered is not inserted again. Unknown access, overflow, half-open bounds and the 64-range limit retain their conservative behavior; the run's ordering policy is unchanged.
- **Check:** `t_geometry_hazards` compares 4,096 generated runs of 1 to 63 jobs with the exhaustive scalar pairwise oracle (both outcomes occur), and covers bit 63, duplicate promotion, adjacent bounds, reads across two merged writes and overflow on either side. All 26 checks, including the existing real-GPU storage/uniform dependency cases, pass with early submission on and off. On the same scripted Donkey Kong Bananza route (`sample`, 25 s), the submitting thread's `geo_draw_` self time fell from 562 to 81 samples, plus 42 in the new set code, and the game's 60/30 interval switches fell from 30 to 12. In an isolated CPU benchmark of a 64-job run with 32 shared reads and one private write each, the run check takes 13 us against 54 us for the pairwise check.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata iPhone CPU capture](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md#iphone-resolution-and-frame-rate-controls) (the read/read pairs); [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (the quadratic run).

### An empty vertex stream kept every geometry draw out of a run

- **Symptom:** a game whose frames are mostly geometry-shader draws stays GPU-bound by attachment traffic: a `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` frame shows hundreds of two-draw render passes (each geometry draw, then the reopened pass of its raster) and gigabytes of attachment loads and stores, and no `geometry hoist` line ever appears in the log.
- **Cause:** the game leaves a vertex stream bound at address 1 with size 0. Three checks looked at every one of the 16 streams, used or not: the lookahead's alignment test in `geo_prep` (a run may not prepare an unaligned stream ahead of intervening draws) returned -1 for it; `geo_access_of` added it as a zero-length range, which `geo_access_add` marks unknown, so every pair of geometry draws counted as a dependency; and `csh_draw_reads` could not list the draw's reads, which stopped compute dispatches from moving out of the pass. `vbuf_prepare` and `bind_vbufs` also sent it to `vbuf_unaligned`, which logged a `[RED]` that it could not be copied although there were no bytes to copy.
- **Fix:** all of them skip a stream of size 0. It holds no guest memory that a draw could depend on: a draw that reads past it gets zeros, from a copy filled on the CPU with no ordering to keep (`vbuf_unaligned`), so neither the ordering nor the dependency proof changes for a stream that holds data. Geometry captures do not bound their streams (`geo_capture_one` clears `g_vbuf_need`), so a vertex shader that really reads an empty stream there still reads the memory at its address, or a stale binding if that address is not aligned, instead of zeros, with or without a run. That is open.
- **Check:** `t_geometry_hazards` forms a run across an empty stream at address 1, keeps a stream with data at an unaligned address out of one, and checks the lists of `geo_access_of` and `csh_draw_reads`; `t_vbuf` checks that an empty stream logs no red line and still reads zeros when a draw reads it. The frame rate below is the reporter's measurement, not reproduced here.
- **Found on:** FINAL FANTASY XII THE ZODIAC AGE (`0100EB100AB42000`, no profile yet): about 180 geometry draws a frame from two triangle-in, three-vertex-out shaders. On an M1 in handheld mode, gameplay went from 264 render passes and 2.9 GB load + 2.3 GB store a frame (GPU 52–68 ms, 12–17 fps) to 6,053 draws prepared in 153 runs by frame 1,500, GPU about 15 ms and a steady 30 fps, the game's own rate there.

### Parallel geometry index compaction

- **Symptom:** geometry-heavy rendering spends substantial GPU time compacting the geometry shader's emitted indices.
- **Cause:** the original `geo_pack_indices` kernel walks every invocation group in a single GPU thread.
- **Fix:** use upstream commit `16f4889`'s ordered count, offset-scan and write dispatches. The count kernel computes exclusive offsets within each 64-group block using SIMD scans and a threadgroup barrier; the middle kernel scans block totals, and the write kernel adds local and block offsets. Scratch holds one word per group plus one per block. The middle scan now uses 128 GPU lanes, each walking a contiguous chunk before scanning lane totals across SIMD groups. Draws with at most 1,024 invocation groups instead compact in one 128-thread workgroup, without global scratch or two extra dispatches. Both paths preserve exact invocation order, strip winding, restart boundaries and whole-primitive clipping at the output capacity. `DM_NVN_GEOMETRY_PACK=legacy` restores the earlier three-dispatch policy with its single-thread block scan.
- **Found on:** upstream OCTOPATH TRAVELER II; the blocked scan was measured on [NieR:Automata](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md). On an M4 Pro, a 65,536-group compaction benchmark with alternating warmed runs measured 3,918 us median for the upstream scan and 81 us for the blocked scan, with identical output. Production geometry checks pass 157 checks, including 257-group partial blocks, empty groups, restart boundaries, output capacity and pending index writes. Both normal and forced-wait policies pass. Full-game timing remains scene-dependent.
- **Further check:** the newer fused/parallel paths pass 914 geometry checks in normal, legacy and split-compute modes, including 0–1,024-group boundaries, points/lines/triangles, partial chunks and a 16,387-block prefix stress case. An alternating warmed 256-draw query workload improves 2.998 to 2.832 ms GPU median with identical query results; the large block-scan stress benchmark improves 1,221 to 45.8 us with byte-identical prefixes and indirect counts. The latter is an isolated stress workload, not a measured game frame. Game FPS with these additional changes remains to be measured.

### Geometry reader viewport masks

- **Symptom:** the geometry kernel compiles, but its `_bgv.metal` vertex reader fails with `use of undeclared identifier 'gl_BuiltIn_5253'` and `member reference base type 'int' is not a structure or union`.
- **Cause:** `ResourceReservations` packs `ViewportMask` into one output word; `GenerateVertexPassthroughForCompute` stores that word as a scalar although SPIR-V `IoMap` declares builtin 5253 (`ViewportMaskNV`) as `int[1]`. SPIRV-Cross emits an invalid array-copy expression for it, and Metal has no viewport-mask output. The mask still matters: zero discards a primitive, while bit zero selects viewport zero ([NV_viewport_array2](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/extensions/NV/NV_viewport_array2.txt)).
- **Fix:** `dmsh_geometry.reader` recognizes that generated scalar-array store, retains the mask's integer bits and moves vertices beyond the X clip plane when bit zero is clear. This runs before `vs_depth` makes both depth entries. Using the X plane preserves culling with depth clamp too; moving vertices beyond the far depth plane allowed masked points to render when depth clamp was enabled. Unknown mask expressions fail the build instead of silently losing culling. No pinned translator change is required.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): its four geometry shaders' readers.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_geometry.py` uses synthetic generated-reader MSL. Both Mac and iOS compiler checks reproduce the original error before the fix. Mac GPU readback covers six masks, points and triangles, depth clip and clamp, and both production depth entries (48 cases); zero-mask points under depth clamp fail with the earlier depth-plane cull. All four original readers also pass both platform syntax checks.
- **Limits:** replay binds viewport zero only. Multi-viewport fanout is not implemented, and per-vertex-disagreeing masks are not validated; the tested primitives use one mask for every vertex. These tests establish compilation and viewport-zero culling, not the appearance of those effects in gameplay.

### Vertex viewport masks apply only at rasterization

- **Symptom:** an ordinary vertex shader that stores `gl_BuiltIn_5253[0]` fails Metal compilation with `use of undeclared identifier 'gl_BuiltIn_5253'`. Culling in the geometry reader's way before the capture entries are made would instead change the positions a later stage receives.
- **Cause:** the translator writes a vertex shader's `ViewportMaskNV` output (builtin 5253), and SPIRV-Cross stores it to a name Metal does not have. Only the last vertex-processing stage's mask counts ([NV_viewport_array2](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/extensions/NV/NV_viewport_array2.txt)); capture, transform feedback and a later geometry stage see the outputs unmasked.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_entry.vs_viewport_mask` accepts one unconditional store of a value it proves draw-uniform (a literal, a `constant` scalar parameter, or a fixed word of a `constant` uniform binding) and keeps it in a local; the raster entry points (`main0`, `main0_z`) move the position past the X clip plane when bit zero is clear, as the [geometry reader](#geometry-reader-viewport-masks) does. The capture, discard, transform feedback and layer entry points keep the unculled body. Any other mask fails the build (`vertex viewport mask failed`) rather than losing the cull. The reflection's `viewport_mask` keeps such a vertex shader off the lean geometry path (`nvn_geometry.inc` `geo_lean_pick`), where its raster entry would run again after the geometry shader. Comments of earlier rewrites are ignored when proving the store unconditional. A chain change. Ported from NXNative `2e6f6fd0c` (PR #3074, MIT).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_vertex_viewport_mask.py` (the production `finish` entry section on synthetic sources, Mac and iPhone syntax); `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_vertex_viewport_mask` adds the GPU readback and the host's lean-path refusal.
- **Found on:** upstream's Mario Tennis Aces (no profile here). Multi-viewport broadcast is not implemented: only viewport zero is drawn.

### Passthrough geometry output bounds follow input topology

- **Symptom:** a passthrough geometry kernel writes beyond its vertex and raw-index scratch regions, and compaction reads the wrong groups; corruption can reach the following primitive-map buffers without a red diagnostic.
- **Cause:** the builder used the SPH's raw maximum output count for every topology. Ryujinx's `ShaderDefinitions` overrides that count for `GpPassthrough` (SPH word 0 bit 24) with the input primitive's vertex count excluding adjacency. A header can declare one vertex while the triangle variant writes three vertices and six raw index words per group. Allocating two index words per group then overlaps later scratch regions.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_geometry.output_layout` mirrors the translator's override for each input variant: points, lines, lines with adjacency, triangles, triangles with adjacency use vertex bounds `1, 2, 2, 3, 3` and index strides `2, 4, 4, 6, 6`. Passthrough also inherits the input primitive: native GLSL omits the SPH's output primitive declaration, so `ShaderDefinitions` and metadata use point, line strip or triangle strip accordingly. `geometry_finish` writes those bounds and topology into each variant's metadata, which replay uses for allocation and compaction. Ordinary geometry shaders retain their declared bound and topology. Rebuild shaders and the host for the new chain.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): its passthrough index-building geometry shaders; the triangle kernel's six-word stores disagreed with its two-word metadata while rendering flight. The game note tracks the visual result after correction.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_geometry.py` covers all five variants, ordinary bounds and malformed headers. With `DM_TEST_GEOMETRY` pointing at `t_geometry`, it passes production-generated layouts to a native emitter with independent topology bounds. GPU canaries protect the nominal vertex and raw-index allocations, and compaction must preserve the emitted count and order. The old rule produced 34 failures; the corrected metadata passes all 170 native checks with Metal validation.

### Passthrough geometry kernels lose raster vertices

- **Symptom:** a geometry kernel compiles and runs its storage-buffer side effects, but contains no `geometry_vb_output` binding and fills its index stream with restart markers; its following raster pass draws no geometry.
- **Cause:** the offline accessor inherited native geometry-passthrough support. Native NV passthrough forwards input vertices without an explicit emit, but Metal's compute stage must emit them. Disabling native support alone still loses generic attributes: independently compiled stages have no linked next-stage usage to populate `PassthroughAttributes`.
- **Fix:** fixed-input compute jobs report no native geometry-passthrough support. `EmitterContext` explicitly emits input positions and every captured generic attribute the GS did not replace, removing adjacency vertices as the input topology requires. This uses the complete Maxwell capture map; ordinary linked translation retains its existing usage mask. Copying all 128 generic components also exposes a Metal backend compiler failure at normal optimization (`Internal compiler error` when creating the pipeline, despite successful offline compilation). `dmsh_geometry.capture_input` keeps capture reads volatile, preserving their values and normal kernel optimization while preventing that failure. No game IDs or shader keys participate.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): its passthrough index-building shaders run before depth-writing raster passes during flight. The game note tracks rendering after rebuilding.
- **Check:** `PassthroughCompiler` translates a synthetic EXIT-only Maxwell passthrough shader for all five input topologies, deliberately declaring triangle output in every raw header. Both Mac and iOS compile checks run. With `DM_TEST_GEOMETRY`, production reflection and the argument encoder dispatch the actual kernels; readback checks positions, all 32 generic attributes, adjacency selection, two instances, raw indices, restart padding, primitive compaction and allocation canaries (9,066 native checks). The original pinned translator fails all five variants because raster output is absent. Removing volatile capture reads makes the 128-component synthetic pipeline fail to compile on the M4 Pro; reducing to 96 components or compiling at `-O0` independently removes that failure.

### Geometry capture waits for every indexed draw

- **Symptom:** geometry-heavy frames have a large submission wall/CPU gap despite short GPU execution; stack samples spend most of the main thread in `geo_draw` → `mdi_wait` → shared-event wait, with index sorting another substantial cost.
- **Cause:** every indexed geometry draw unconditionally committed and drained the GPU before assembling its primitives on the CPU. It also waited for vertex streams consumed only by native GPU capture. The capture list sorted every index reference to remove duplicates, even though the scratch bound already limits the vertex span.
- **Fix:** `nvn_geometry.inc` checks each actual CPU-read range against pending copies/updates/fills, current shader writes and previous live submission writes. Unknown or overflowing write sets retain the wait. Indirect records are checked before decoding; their selected index ranges are checked afterwards. CPU-converted vertex bindings retain the same hazard checks, while native capture follows GPU encoder ordering. A successful drain covers the remaining reads of that draw. Capture uniqueness uses a bounded vertex-span bitset and ascending set-bit enumeration; allocation failure or a wider span retains an in-place sort fallback. Non-indexed captures need no unique index list. The primitive map, restart handling, base vertex and instance semantics are unchanged.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): its flight geometry path. The game note records matched frame-time and visual validation.
- **Check:** `t_geometry` first reproduced five unnecessary drains for clean static and indirect inputs. Its 226 native checks cover clean versus pending/current/prior GPU-written ranges, unknown write sets, GPU-only and converted streams, indirect arguments selecting a different dirty index range, 8/16/32-bit indices, restart, base vertex and instances. Actual copy/compute writes and raster readback validate synchronization; converted data must see the new GPU-written value. Sparse duplicates, ascending output, short destinations, fallback and allocation canaries cover the capture list. These checks establish behavior; throughput is measured separately in gameplay.

### Geometry compaction serial prefix scan

- **Symptom:** geometry-heavy frames remain GPU-bound after CPU readback waits are removed; encoder timestamps attribute most compaction time to the offset scan rather than the geometry shader or index writes.
- **Cause:** count and write dispatches ran in parallel, but one GPU thread walked all group counts to compute ordered offsets. Geometry replay also created a separate compute encoder for each of the geometry, count, scan and write dispatches, with full queue barriers at every boundary.
- **Fix:** `geo_pack_scan` divides the groups into contiguous chunks across at most 256 threads. Each thread sums its chunk; a threadgroup prefix scan gives its starting offset; a second chunk pass writes the ordered offsets. The group count does not need to fit in the threadgroup. Totals use 64 bits; unrepresentable 32-bit offsets saturate, output writes use subtraction-based capacity checks, and indirect counts retain complete-primitive truncation. `geo_pack_encode` keeps the geometry and three compaction dispatches in one encoder, with explicit dispatch-to-dispatch device-memory barriers. Full barriers remain at its boundaries. The primitive order and large-draw algorithm are preserved; no shader-library chain change is required.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): its flight geometry passes. The game note records the baseline encoder timings and matched result.
- **Check:** `t_geometry` passes 1,066 checks with Metal validation. A GPU producer and the production compaction stages share one encoder; readback compares every offset and ordered index with an independent CPU result, including 129,032 groups. Cases cover points, lines and triangle strips, empty groups, zero/odd/thread-boundary group counts, 1/32/192/256-thread scans, short capacities, integer overflow and allocation canaries. The actual translated passthrough integration also passes all five input topologies through the same encoder path. Performance is checked separately with matched gameplay.

### Geometry draws above the capture scratch bound

- **Symptom:** `[RED] geometry draw needs 261 MiB of scratch, above the 256 MiB bound` (or `exceeds the capture or invocation bound`); the draw is skipped.
- **Cause:** the geometry path captures the vertex shader's outputs for every vertex between the draw's lowest and highest index, for every instance, and the geometry kernel's outputs take room per primitive (`nvn_geometry.inc` `geo_draw`). Unreal's one-pass point light shadow draws a whole level mesh through its geometry shader.
- **Fix:** such a draw is drawn in runs in the hardware's order (`geo_split`): one instance at a time, then runs of at most 4,096 primitives of a list topology whose index range fits 64 MB of capture. The index list is read once and the runs skip the GPU wait the outer draw took (a first version halved the draw recursively, each part reading the indices again and waiting: that frame took 805 ms). The index compaction after the geometry kernel was one thread walking every group; it is a count, an offset scan and a write dispatch now (`nvn_geometry.h`, `t_geometry`). Scratch arenas above 32 MB are kept while such draws go on and released after 600 idle submissions (`nvn_mtl.m` `arena_begin`), and `arena_new` takes the smallest free arena that fits.
- **Found on:** [OCTOPATH TRAVELER II](../../dm/games/0100A3501946E000/README.md): 129,032 triangles of one mesh (vertex `9214254d203a4e45`, geometry `cb17a9453d4789d0`, both from the default material) at the start of the first battle and in the scene after it; with the one-thread compaction each such frame took about 300 ms, now only the first is long (395 ms on the Mac, with two new pipelines and the scratch allocation).
- **Note:** strips, fans and primitive restart are not split. Guest primitive numbering retains the original draw's offset across supported splits: [Split geometry primitive IDs](#split-geometry-primitive-ids).

### Split geometry primitive IDs

- **Symptom:** a geometry shader that reads `PrimitiveID` repeats its visibility decisions or storage addresses after a large draw is split into compute dispatches.
- **Cause:** fixed-input `GeometryToCompute` lowered the guest ID to dispatch-local `GlobalId.x`. Each child dispatch starts at zero. That local index is correct for capture, topology remapping and scratch output addresses, but loses the guest draw's primitive offset.
- **Fix:** replay passes an explicit primitive base through recursive splits, keeping it unchanged when splitting instances and adding each primitive run's start when splitting the range. The counter resets between instances, as specified for [geometry primitive IDs](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/PrimitiveId.html). `vb_info.geometry_counts.y` carries it to the shader. Only the guest `PrimitiveID` load adds that base; helper addressing remains local, and ordinary draws start at zero. The rebuilt translator and shared chain stamp prevent older libraries from using the new ABI.
- **Found on:** review of [OCTOPATH TRAVELER II](../../dm/games/0100A3501946E000/README.md)'s large-draw splitter following the NieR integration. The primitive-ID failure is reproduced with a synthetic Maxwell shader; no new Octopath visual failure is attributed to it.
- **Check:** `test_geometry_split.py` checks 160 nested range/ID cases over all five supported list topologies, all index widths, non-indexed draws, two instance counts and nonzero ancestor bases. `ComputeSplitPrimitiveId` in `test_dmsh_geometry.py` compiles a real Maxwell `ALD PrimitiveID` shader for both Metal targets. Its native `t_geometry_primitive_id` replay checks every emitted ID and captured vertex/instance attribute in 40 GPU cases, with 1/8,199 primitives, five topologies, 1/2 instances and 1/3 shader invocations. The old translator repeats ID zero at primitive 4,096; the corrected translator preserves it. A GPU-independent ABI assertion catches stale translator binaries too. Feedback counts and an output canary also check that the offset never leaks into local scratch addresses.

### Recursive geometry splits retain their original draw range

- **Symptom:** a large geometry draw crashes in `geo_split` / `memcpy` while reading indices, sometimes preceded by `geometry draw exceeds the capture or invocation bound` for an unexpectedly small child draw.
- **Cause:** `geo_prep` passes a draw descriptor stored in the reusable `g_geo_run` job to the splitter. A child draw prepares its own job in the same slot, overwriting the parent's borrowed descriptor. Later chunks add their offsets to the previous child's index address or first vertex instead of the original draw's range. The job-based geometry integration exposed this lifetime error; the earlier splitter received a local descriptor.
- **Fix:** `geo_split` snapshots the input descriptors before issuing any child draw. Every chunk and recursive instance split derives its range from that immutable copy. This repairs ownership for indexed and non-indexed draws without changing split limits or skipping geometry.
- **Found on:** [OCTOPATH TRAVELER II](../../dm/games/0100A3501946E000/README.md), the 129,032-triangle shadow draw after Hikari's first attack, during the NieR geometry integration's cross-game review.
- **Check:** `test_geometry_split.py` compiles the production splitter with a callback that reuses the caller's descriptor exactly as `geo_prep` does. The expanded 160-case test checks each chunk's range, instance and primitive base for 8/16/32-bit indices and non-indexed draws, including 129,032 primitives and nested splits. Removing the snapshot reproduces the range drift.

### Geometry jobs preserve transform-feedback replay

- **Symptom:** geometry draws with transform feedback report `geometry draw cannot capture this pipeline` and write no feedback records after the geometry-job integration.
- **Cause:** the new preparation path rejected active feedback and omitted main's gather of the geometry shader's emitted vertices. Replaying a geometry reader through the ordinary vertex-feedback branch would also capture its synthetic draw a second time.
- **Fix:** full geometry jobs gather their packed output into feedback before rasterization or raster discard. Active feedback remains in draw order, and feedback state/buffer commands end lookahead. The subsequent synthetic raster draw does not capture vertex feedback again. Lean and mesh eligibility still exclude active feedback.
- **Found on:** final review of the [NieR geometry integration](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md#geometry-optimization-review), against main's existing geometry-feedback implementation; no game-specific condition is involved.
- **Check:** `t_geometry_feedback` replays actual capture, geometry, compaction, feedback pause/resume and rasterization with GPU readback. Its oracle checks every emitted vertex and saved count, excludes the draw made while paused, and runs with raster discard both enabled and disabled.

### Passthrough geometry shaders drawn by the vertex shader

- **Symptom:** geometry-heavy frames are GPU-bound on the emulation itself. A `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` frame shows, for every geometry draw, a capture pass (`0x0 0c`), a compute encoder and a one-draw reopening of the game's pass, with most of the frame's GPU time in the first two.
- **Cause:** a passthrough geometry shader (SPH word 0 bit 24, NV_geometry_shader_passthrough) cannot change the vertices it passes on; it only decides per primitive (the viewport mask) and may write storage. The emulation still captured all 144 words of every vertex, ran a kernel that copied 134 words per output vertex (volatile loads of the whole capture map, [Passthrough geometry kernels lose raster vertices](#passthrough-geometry-kernels-lose-raster-vertices)), and drew the copies through the reader.
