- **Fix:** for point, line and triangle input (no adjacency), `dmsh_build.py` adds a lean kernel `<key>_bl<topology>` (`dmsh_geometry.lean`): every output store but the viewport mask's word goes, the mask is written one word per output vertex, what only fed the removed stores is dropped (a worklist over the generated straight-line code), and the capture reads that remain (literal words of the record) are renumbered into a record of just those words (`capture_reads`). The capture writes each word once through a slot table and a record stride the host passes (`dm_geometry_capture`; the full kernel gets every word in order). At replay (`nvn_geometry.inc`) `geo_lean_cull` turns the vertices of masked primitives into restarts before the usual compaction, `geo_lean_index` maps the packed output vertices to the draw's own vertex indices (output vertex `g × n + k` is input vertex `k` of primitive `g`, the primitive map's word) and sets the base instance, and the program's own vertex and fragment stages draw them indexed and indirect. Instanced or multi-invocation draws, vertex shaders with storage writes (they would run twice), transform feedback and adjacency input keep the full path; so does `DM_NVN_GEO_LEAN=0`. The CPU side reads indices with typed loops and copies list topologies without restart directly into the primitive map; a `memcpy` per index was most of the translation time of such frames.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): its four `IndexBuildIndependent_*` shaders, about 80 draws a frame in the opening flight, most of which keep no primitive (they build culled index lists for later passes). The lean kernels read 4 of the 144 capture words and are about 970 lines instead of 10,500. On an M1 Mac at 720p: GPU work per frame 33–43 ms → about 10 ms, translation 75 M → 42 M instructions per frame, frame time 50 → 27 ms where the Mac was otherwise idle.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_geometry.py` covers capture slots, lean rewriting, both Metal targets and mesh composition. The native geometry suite covers capture, compaction and dependency ordering. All M1 measurements in this entry were reported for the original implementation in PR #416; they are not measurements of the reconciled host.
- **Fix:** the shared geometry run (`geo_run_walk`, `nvn_geometry.inc`) batches independent captures, geometry kernels and rasters, retaining #336's buffer dependency proof, exact-byte index cache, query positions and parallel compaction. A storage write that could affect another capture or raster ends the run. Lean triangle output uses fixed triangle slots and skips compaction; point and line output retains ordered compaction, then maps output vertices back to the draw's own indices. `DM_NVN_GEO_HOIST=0` disables batching for comparison. No game identity participates in these decisions.
- **Found on:** the same scene, M1 at 720p: render passes a frame 205 → 39, encoders 290 → 45, attachment traffic 860 → 190 MB, GPU busy 7.6 ms, translation 4.6 ms, frame time p50 / p95 16.66 / 17.26 ms: 60 fps held through four minutes of the flight, factory and ocean combat (frame time 16.6–16.8 ms in every 300-frame window), where it was 50 ms at first.
- **Fix:** the compute path still costs the CPU a capture, a kernel and a compaction per draw, and the GPU the vertex shader twice. A pair the game drew gets a mesh library `<vertex key>_<geometry key>_m3` at the end of `dmsh_build.py` (`dmsh_mesh.py`). Which pairs (`dmsh_chain.mesh_pairs`): a lean pair with no mesh library takes the compute path, and the host writes it once to the pipeline record as a `G` row (`geo_mesh_fn`), so the prewarm list a recording makes (`pso_manifest.tsv`) names the pairs to build, as many as the game really draws; a profile's hand-written `geo_mesh.tsv` adds pairs no recording has reached yet. The `G` rows are in the profile's input digest (`dmsh_chain.inputs`, only when there are any), so a new one asks for a full build, which takes every shader from the cache. The library holds the vertex shader's pulling entry ([vertex pulling](../../dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_pull.py)) and the lean triangle kernel, each in its own namespace, under one mesh entry per depth convention (`dm_mesh`, `dm_mesh_z`). A threadgroup takes 32 triangles of a list: 96 threads run the vertex shader once per index, write the vertex, and keep the words the kernel reads in threadgroup memory; the first 32 run the kernel body (capture reads from that memory, the topology map as the identity, the mask store into a variable, index stores dropped) and cull their triangle when viewport 0's bit is clear (`[[primitive_culled]]`). The kernel's storage writes and the subgroup operations that order them run as before, one triangle per thread, 32 consecutive triangles per SIMD group. The host (`geo_mesh_draw`, `DM_NVN_GEO_MESH=0` off) takes it for a non-instanced, non-indirect triangle list without restart whose vertex shader has a pulling entry and no storage writes: the draw stays in the open pass, with the pulling layout, the vertex and fragment arguments as for any draw, the kernel's arguments and the draw's index source in two free slots of the vertex table (which the mesh stage shares), and `drawMeshThreadgroups`. The mesh pipeline (the draw's colour state, as `pso_desc` gives it) is built in the background; until it is ready, the draw takes the compute path. The vertex shader runs per index instead of per vertex.
- **Found on:** the same game: its 24 shadow-map vertex shaders (`zmap_vs_*.vso`) with the four kernels, 96 pairs. In the Resistance Camp on an M1 at 720p, every geometry draw became a mesh draw: translation 184 M → 122 M instructions (16.3 → 12.0 ms) a frame, GPU 12.3 → 11.3 ms, attachment traffic 463 → 415 MB a frame, frame time 21.2 → 16.6–17.4 ms.
- **Which draws still take the compute path:** the log's `[nvn] geometry compute path up to frame N` line counts them per window by the first condition that kept each off the mesh path (`geo_mesh_fn`, `geo_mesh_draw`: not a triangle list, instanced, indirect, restart or index type, rasterizer discard or transform feedback, a vertex shader without a pulling entry, tessellation or mesh stages, no lean kernel, no mesh library, an index buffer outside a Metal buffer, a mesh pipeline being built, a depth split) and names the three pairs that drew most (`vertex+geometry (reason) count`). A pair with `no mesh library` is in the pipeline record as a `G` row, and the next recording into `pso_manifest.tsv` and build give it one; the other reasons need host work. It came from Donkey Kong Bananza, whose slow windows each drew 1,600-3,400 geometry draws a frame through the compute path (the `geometry hoist` line) and the 60 fps ones none, with nothing in the log to say why. `make check_t_geometry_mesh` checks the line: the draws made while the test's mesh pipelines are built, under that reason and the test's pair.

### A passthrough geometry shader that writes the layer

- **Symptom:** a pale blue wash over every 3D scene with atmosphere; the HUD is clean, and the albedo and normal targets are right. A 3D table (`texraw`) has data in plane 0 only, and the pass that fills it has no `L … layered pass` line and draws with `gs=-`.
- **Cause:** a passthrough geometry shader cannot change the vertices it passes on, but it may pick the layer. The [lean path](#passthrough-geometry-shaders-drawn-by-the-vertex-shader) draws with the program's own vertex shader, which has no layer output, so every plane went to plane 0 and the rest of the table stayed zero. The output reader the full path uses (`<key>_bgv`) does write it.
- **Fix:** `geo_lean_pick` keeps the full kernel and its reader when the reader writes the layer (`nvn_geometry.inc`). No chain change. Those draws cost the full path's capture.
- **Found on:** [Mario Tennis Fever](../../dm/games/0400D4201C6EA000/README.md): the atmosphere's table (`d9c3a99828b4f39f`, one 8x8 plane of 64 per draw, which the atmosphere pass `1cdcda78119d2e8d` reads) and the shadow map's `f4c2c8feea0727e9`. 6 of its 7 lean-capable geometry shaders write the layer.

### Mesh geometry primitive IDs restart per group

- **Symptom:** a passthrough geometry shader that uses `PrimitiveID` makes different visibility decisions or storage writes after the first 32 triangles when composed into a mesh shader.
- **Cause:** `GeometryToCompute` uses `GlobalId.x` for both the guest primitive ID and capture-buffer addressing. The mesh composer supplied a group-local lane as that ID to address local capture records, repeating guest IDs in every mesh group.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_mesh.compose` supplies the draw-wide primitive ID and rebases only capture loads to the mesh group's first record. Guest calculations and storage writes retain the original ID. A translated Maxwell fixture reads `PrimitiveID`, shifts it by five and combines it with a captured attribute to control viewport zero. Its GPU test covers 1–97 primitives, both depth conventions and non-indexed, 16-bit and 32-bit indexed draws with a negative base vertex. The original mapping fails at 33 triangles; the corrected mapping passes all 48 cases.
- **Found on:** review of [NieR:Automata](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md) and PR #416. Its four actual `IndexBuildIndependent_*` shaders load generic attributes only, with no `PrimitiveID` or physical attribute loads. This is a general mesh-composition defect exposed by the synthetic shader, not an observed NieR rendering failure.

### Mesh geometry groups sized to the vertex output

- **Symptom:** after a pair is added to `geo_mesh.tsv`, `[RED] geometry mesh pipeline: ... Total mesh size (37248) exceeds the maximum mesh size allowed (32768)`, and the frames that draw the pair get much slower than on the compute path (Donkey Kong Bananza: translation from about 10 to 22–31 ms a frame).
- **Cause:** a mesh threadgroup took 32 triangles, 96 vertices, whatever the vertex shader outputs. Metal limits a mesh threadgroup's output to 32 KB, and 96 vertices of 388 bytes (24 `float4` varyings, the position and the point size) are 37,248 bytes. The pipeline failed in the background, but the pair kept its mesh function, so `geo_run_walk` kept ending its runs at each of the pair's draws, and every draw went through the compute path alone.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_mesh.prims_for` takes the most triangles of 32, 16, 8, ... whose vertices and primitives fit 32 KB, from the size of `main0_out` (`out_bytes`, Metal's packed count). Any count other than 32 is in the entry's name (`dm_mesh_p16`, `dm_mesh_z_p16`), so libraries of 32 keep their names and the host reads the count from the name (`geo_mesh_prims`). A triangle's kernel invocations then share a SIMD group with fewer active lanes, which the guest's subgroup operations already allow. A mesh pipeline that fails sends its pair back to the compute path for good (`geo_mesh_pso`). A mesh draw's storage writes are noted as its arguments are encoded, so it keeps a drain's list of later writes ([CPU reads after a drain wait only for later writes](rendering.md#cpu-reads-after-a-drain-wait-only-for-later-writes)). With every geometry draw a mesh draw, two per-draw lookups showed: the pair's mesh function (a key string and a dictionary lookup) and whether the pass's colour attachments can be left out (`geo_color_omit`, resolving each attachment's Metal texture). Both now keep the last answer, the second keyed by the targets with their serials, views, clears and colour mask: translation 7.20 → 6.64 ms a frame on the same route.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_geometry.py` checks the sizes (388 bytes takes 16 triangles, the test shader 32, arrays and half types, an unknown member type and a vertex too large for one triangle refused) and composes and compiles a 16-triangle mesh shader. On Donkey Kong Bananza's scripted route, its one geometry pair as mesh draws against the compute path (`DM_NVN_GEO_MESH=0`), each run alone: translation 7.20 against 9.94 ms a frame, GPU p50 9.0 against 11.8 ms, no red lines. Frames through gameplay show the terrain, rocks, vines and props.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#found-on-this-game): its `geo_mesh.tsv` pair, about 600 geometry draws a frame.

### Mesh geometry draws gathered into one dispatch

- **Symptom:** a depth prepass of many small geometry-shader draws, drawn as mesh draws, leaves the GPU nearly idle: the per-pass profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`) shows an idle gap before the pass that grows with its draw count (about 2.3 µs a draw), and the GPU counters show vertex-only time at about 4 % occupancy.
- **Cause:** a Metal mesh draw has a fixed cost on the GPU, whatever it draws. On an M1, 480 one-triangle mesh draws of Donkey Kong Bananza's pair took 1.24–1.32 ms (about 2.7 µs each), and 480 draws of the prepass's sizes (15–350 triangles) 1.5–1.7 ms, where one draw of the same 67,000 triangles took 0.5–0.6 ms.
- **Fix:**
  - `dmsh_mesh.compose` gives each mesh entry a run twin (`dm_mesh_run`, `dm_mesh_run_z`, `..._p16`). It takes a table at the draw slot (buffer 12) of `dm_mesh_rec`: each draw's arguments, the running total of its threadgroups and the addresses bound at the vertex table's slots for it. A threadgroup finds its draw by halving, takes the vertex set, the pulling layout and the kernel's arguments through those addresses, and runs the same body with the draw's own threadgroup and primitive numbers. A pair whose vertex resources are not all constant references gets no twin.
  - The host (`nvn_geometry.inc`, `geo_mesh_take`) lets a mesh draw that fits wait instead of dispatching: its record copies `g_vt_addr`, the shadow of the vertex table the mesh stage shares, after its arguments are bound. The run is one dispatch of the twin, drawn before any operation other than bindings, a program bind and draws (`geo_mesh_keeps`, from `mtl_exec`), before a draw of another pair, pass or kind (`geo_mesh_draw`, `draw_`), and before its pass ends (`rp_end`, `defer_fallback`). Bindings only change the replay state and dirty flags, so nothing else reaches the encoder between the draws of a run.
  - The GPU may rasterize the threadgroups of one dispatch in any order, so a run takes only draws whose result does not depend on their order: no colour writes to an attached target, no stencil writes, depth written only with a test that keeps the nearest or the farthest (NEVER, LESS, LEQUAL, GREATER, GEQUAL), and a fragment shader that writes no memory (no writable storage buffer or image, no image atomics, no global-memory write log). The fragment table is not in the record, so the run is drawn with the table as it is then: a change of a fragment table address while a run waits draws the run first (`enc_r_args` → `geo_mesh_ft_change`; the log's `a fragment binding change`), and every draw of a run read the same fragment bindings. The first version took only fragment shaders with no resources at all. Donkey Kong Bananza's alpha-tested prepass draws (fragment `7f7781d3621f0ce6`: two uniform buffers, the support buffer and a discard) then drew 1,700-2,800 mesh draws a frame one by one, at 22,000 instructions a draw against 11,000 and 14-23 fps in those areas. The mesh draw also marked every stage's arguments dirty after it (`geo_mesh_draw`), so the next draw re-encoded its fragment block at a new arena address with the same bindings, which would have split every run again; it keeps the fragment stage's block now, as the raster program's fragment shader encodes it from the same bindings. Occlusion query counting may be on: a report ends the run and a pass keeps one query slot, so each query's interval holds whole runs, and whether any of its samples passed is the same in any order (the nearest fragment of each pixel passes); only the exact count can differ. The first version refused runs while counting was on, and counting stays on for the rest of the game after the first samples-passed reset: in Donkey Kong Bananza no draw ever joined a run (the log's `0 of them in 0 runs`). A refused draw's reason is logged once (`geometry mesh run: a draw of vertex … draws alone`). The kernel's storage writes are unordered between separate mesh draws of a pass already (NVN orders them only with a barrier, which ends the run).
  - The twin's pipeline is built in the background like the pair's own; until it is ready, and when it fails, the draws are single. `DM_NVN_GEO_MESH_RUN=0` turns runs off. The `geometry mesh draws` line of the log counts the draws drawn in runs, the runs, and what ended them.
- **Check:**
  - `test_dmsh_geometry.py` checks the twins' text (the table, the halving, the resources from the record, the same body in all four entries) and that a non-constant vertex resource leaves the twin out. With `DMSH_TEST_XLATE` and `DMSH_TEST_GPU=1`, `mesh_primitive_id.m` also draws 97 translated triangles as four draws (31, 1, 33, 32) in one run dispatch for both depth modes and all three index types, each draw numbering its own primitives.
  - `make check_t_geometry_mesh` replays six geometry draws through `mtl_exec`, each with its own uniform (its depth) and its own storage counter, a scissor change between the fourth and the fifth, in runs, with `DM_NVN_GEO_MESH_RUN=0`, and in runs on the encoding thread: the same depth in every pixel column, every counter equal to its draw's triangles, two runs, under Metal validation. Then again with a fragment shader that reads a uniform buffer and discards when it says so, bound for the third draw alone: that draw's columns stay clear, the rest keep their depth, four runs.
  - A benchmark on an M1 with Donkey Kong Bananza's pair (`f9c807930abd1234` / `5d537f7630ec3671`): 480 draws of the prepass's sizes 1.50 → 0.67–0.72 ms, 480 one-triangle draws 1.24–1.32 → 0.28 ms, 2,100 draws of 32 triangles 4.37 → 0.66 ms.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#found-on-this-game): the 960x540 depth prepass of its heavy areas in handheld mode, 420–550 geometry draws a frame, 1.0–2.0 ms of idle GPU before the pass.

### Mesh stage memory reserves the primitive index bytes for every primitive block

- **Symptom:** white blotches over Kokiri Forest's foliage, walkway and ground in Ocarina of Time, which are the house light tiles. The light-tile pass of task `a5288d0a6c4971fd` with meshes `e8c667282ba7bd9b` and `d4171cf9adf0504b` writes constant white through fragment `ea1bf96b72e72476`, and its triangles were corrupt.
- **Cause:** the guest writes the U8 index bytes of primitive `t = min(count - 1, lane + 32k)` for each of its primitive blocks, `ceil(max_p / 32)` of them. A meshlet count of 0 wraps `count - 1`, so all 128 lanes of a `max_p` 126 mesh write. With `max_p` 126 the index area was `4 + 378` bytes, so lanes 126 and 127 wrote bytes 2,702 to 2,707: word 676, past the 676-word threadgroup array, into neighbouring workgroups' memory. Meshes whose `max_p` is a multiple of 32 were never affected.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_meshstage.memory` reserves `4 + 3 * ceil(max_p / 32) * 32` bytes (rounded to a word) for the index bytes: whole primitive blocks, not the vertex blocks a mesh with more vertices than primitives has as well. Meshes of 128 vertices and 64 primitives did not write past `4 + 3 * 64` before the fix, so the guest's loop goes by primitive blocks. All 500 of Ocarina's mesh shaders were run standalone on zeroed inputs, each with a guard band past its stage array (Metal shader validation does not check static threadgroup arrays): 387 meshes wrote past the array before the fix, every `max_p` 126 mesh, and none after (pull request #1575, measured with every vertex and primitive block reserved; the primitive-block size was not run through the guard band). In the game on an M2 Pro the blotches are gone and the house doorways and windows glow as intended. This does not fix the intermittent GPU hangs seen in buffers with these meshes; those are a separate open issue.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md). The other mesh-shader games in the games table (Yoshi and the Mysterious Book, Mario Kart World, Splatoon Raiders) rebuild their shaders with the chain change; none was checked in the game.

### Mesh attributes: every vertex block before the primitive blocks

- **Symptom:** a mesh shader's per-primitive outputs read as other values once it has more than 32 vertices: Ocarina of Time's Poe Sisters and Phantom Ganon paintings (mesh shaders `ee4ef51a4fddc28e` and `83c0dbc51c5481e3`, 128 vertices, 64 primitives, a per-primitive colour) came out solid green or magenta (pull request #1590). A mesh shader that draws points with a point size takes the size as its position's x.
- **Cause:** the wrapper read the attribute area as blocks of 32 vertices and 32 primitives side by side, each block holding the vertex components and then the primitive components. The guest lays out all its vertex blocks first, then all its primitive blocks, each block only its own SPH's components: `ee4ef51a4fddc28e` (6 vertex components, 4 primitive components) writes vertex `v` at `(v / 32) * 768 + (v % 32) * 4` and primitive `p` at `3072 + (p / 32) * 512 + (p % 32) * 4` past the attribute area's start. The SPH's point size (byte 0x35 bit 3) takes the vertex block's first component, ahead of the position: Ocarina's point mesh `3a6340a04bf09797` writes the size at component 0 and the position at 1 to 4. A primitive's index bytes are control +0x5f of them (1 point, 2 line, 3 triangle); Ocarina's 5 mesh shaders with +0x5f 1 are the 5 with the point size, the other 495 have 3.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_meshstage.memory` and `to_stage` take the vertex blocks (`ceil(max_v / 32)`, the vertex components each) and then the primitive blocks (`ceil(max_p / 32)`, the primitive components each); the point size goes to `[[point_size]]`, the topology and the index bytes a primitive follow +0x5f, and any other value stops the shader's build. The primitive index bytes stay reserved for every primitive block ([Mesh stage memory reserves the primitive index bytes for every primitive block](#mesh-stage-memory-reserves-the-primitive-index-bytes-for-every-primitive-block)), `+0x5f` bytes a primitive.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): the layout and the point size checked against the guest's address arithmetic in the translated shaders; the paintings not seen in the game since. Pull request #1590 found the layout and the point size.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_meshstage.py`.

### Mesh shader per-primitive system values: the primitive count overwritten, the primitive id lost

- **Symptom:** a mesh-shaded draw shows nothing, though its workgroups run: every workgroup ends with a primitive count of 0. Once that is mended, a visibility buffer written from `[[primitive_id]]` holds only the index within the meshlet, and each pixel is shaded from the wrong cluster.
- **Cause:** a mesh shader's per-primitive SPH can enable system-value outputs besides generic attributes: OMAP byte 0x34 and byte 0x35 bits 0..3, attribute 0x40 + 4 × bit (0x35 bit 0 is the primitive id; bit 1 the layer, bit 2 the viewport). `dmsh_meshstage.info` dropped them, so `memory` left them no room, and the primitive count and index bytes began where the guest keeps the per-primitive blocks: its store of primitive 0's id landed on the count byte. The wrapper also gave Metal no per-primitive id, so the fragment stage's `[[primitive_id]]` was Metal's own index, not the value NVN's `gl_PrimitiveID` reads (here `(cluster << 7) | primitive`).
- **Fix:** `info` lists the per-primitive system values (`primitive_system`, `primitive_builtins`); a primitive block holds them first, then the generic attributes, and the primitive bytes follow every block. The primitive id goes to Metal as the mesh primitive's `[[primitive_id]]` (`PRIMITIVE_BUILTINS`), which the fragment stage then reads; the same fragment function in a vertex pipeline still reads Metal's index. The layer and the viewport get room but no Metal output: no mesh shader is known to write them. Ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up ([PR #1802](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1802)); its other part, a storage buffer table at `c0` + 0x210 for mesh shaders in the hull slot, is not taken, since `c[26]` there is the mesh stage's buffer bank, its push block 2 ([NVN2 graphics stages' buffer banks](#nvn2-graphics-stages-buffer-banks-c25-to-c29-are-push-blocks)).
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): 494 of its 539 mesh shaders enable the primitive id, among them the title's lunar terrain (`5aab38bb5ba67ef7`, `28ef097c07ceb429`) and the visibility pass (`765e6d9d2588ce70`, `bc8b4e0fcbb79159`).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_meshstage.py`.

### Mesh shaders that also address their vertices as records

- **Symptom:** thin streaks or stray triangles from a mesh shader over parts of the picture its geometry does not cover (PRAGMATA: white vertical streaks over the cockpit window in the opening).
- **Cause:** besides the SoA blocks (`ISBEWR.O.ATTR.SKEW.32`, mode 0xf8), a mesh shader may write its vertices as records (`ISBEWR.O.ATTR.32`, mode 0xb8): vertex `v`'s `n` components at `aos + 4n * v + 4c`, `aos` being the end of its SoA area, with the same values as their SoA words. The translator took mode 0xb8 as a word at the outputs' base, so the records ran over the primitive count and the index bytes.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_meshstage.memory` passes `stage_memory[4..6]` (the records' start, `n`, their size); in a mesh shader `AStageOut` and `AIsberd` (`InstEmitAmpereMem.cs` `AosToSoa`) send a mode 0xb8 address inside the records to the SoA word of the same vertex and component, so both writes coincide. `v = w / n` is a reciprocal multiply where that is exact for every word of the records (`AosVertex` checks it), a division otherwise. Ported from [PR #1802](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1802).
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): `6adf14e9a4100082`, `4fcffc73d65dc1ed`, `c0e44513c35d209a`.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`test_vertex_records_go_to_their_soa_word`).

### Mesh shaders that read their own outputs back

- **Symptom:** `NotSupportedException: Ampere 923 …: ISBERD mode f8 in a task or mesh shader` in the shader build; those mesh shaders are left out and their draws skipped (`[RED] DrawMeshTasks without its task and mesh shaders`).
- **Cause:** ISBERD's mode bits as nvdisasm names them: 0x80 .O (the output area), 0x40 .SKEW, 0x30 .ATTR, 0x08 .32. Modes 0xf8 and 0xb8 read back a word the shader's own ISBEWR of the same mode wrote; the translator took only 0xb8, as a task's payload.
- **Fix:** `AIsberd` reads both from the stage memory's outputs, 0xb8 through the vertex records (previous entry). From uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up ([PR #1407](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1407)).
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): `5aab38bb5ba67ef7` and `28ef097c07ceb429`, the title's lunar terrain, read back each triangle's positions to swap two indices of a mirrored instance's triangles.

### Subword task and mesh payload accesses

- **Symptom:** task and mesh shaders fail translation with `ISBERD mode 34 in a task or mesh shader` or `stage output store mode b4 outside a task or mesh shader`; the host cannot load their libraries when the race uses them.
- **Cause:** the stage-memory emitters accepted only 32-bit attributes and payload words. Bits 74–75 of ISBERD/ISBEWR select unsigned 8-bit, unsigned 16-bit or 32-bit access, independently of the attribute, skew and output bits ([Mesa NAK's encoder](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chaotic-cx/mesa-mirror/main/src/nouveau/compiler/nak/sm70_encode.rs), `OpIsberd` / `OpIsbewr`). Mode 0x34 reads a 16-bit task-payload entry; 0xb4 writes one. Treating it as a whole word would overwrite the adjacent entry.
- **Fix:** `AIsberd` and `AStageOut` decode width separately from the stage-memory area and use the existing byte/halfword load and store lowering. Subword vertex-record accesses preserve the byte offset within the remapped SoA word. The existing 32-bit paths are unchanged; there is no shader-key or build gate.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` checks unsigned input extraction, the high half of a word, subword vertex records and correctly sized store helpers. `test_dmsh_shared_store.py` follows a translated halfword store through the Metal rewrite and tests neighboring byte/halfword writes on the GPU.
- **Found on:** [Fast Fusion](../../dm/games/040041E0212AE000/README.md).

### Indexed Ampere patch outputs

- **Symptom:** `[translation failed] NotSupportedException: Ampere 322 at 0x6d0: AST with an offset register and an address` for a hull shader.
- **Cause:** `AST.P` can select a patch word with a register plus its immediate byte offset. The emitter rejected every such non-physical attribute store, and usage tracking treated its outputs as per-control-point attributes instead of patch attributes. Welcome Tour's hull sets the register to `InvocationId * 4`, writes an outer tessellation factor there, then writes its inner factor at 0x10. The instruction fields are independently described by [Mesa NAK's SM70 encoder](https://github.com/chaotic-cx/mesa-mirror/blob/main/src/nouveau/compiler/nak/sm70_encode.rs).
- **Fix:** `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs` emits each component at register + immediate + 4 times its component index. Patch words 0–3 select the outer factors, 4–5 the inner factors, and the rest select the patch's user location and component. Locations are dispatched as constants for the SPIR-V interface; components remain indexed. `DecoderAmpere.cs` records patch output usage without enabling indexing of per-control-point outputs. Scalar and vector stores, immediate offsets, the end of the patch map and 32-bit address wrap are checked numerically on Metal. Ordinary non-physical indexed `AST` without `.P` remains unsupported.
- **Found on:** Nintendo Switch 2 Welcome Tour (upstream's profile `040057C02159A000`, not in NX 18; fixed upstream in `8d62601f3`), Bezel PBR hull shader `0b9e5d475e9afd21`, in `_SystemBuiltinRenderer.ounce.bea` (`bezel_pbr.bfsha`). Upstream's repaired hull compiles through its tessellation lowering; NX 18 has none ([Tessellation stages are not drawn](#tessellation-stages-are-not-drawn)), so here the fix only lets such a hull translate.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_patch.py`: a synthetic hull's indexed patch stores (one word at 0, four from 0x10, four from 0x1f8 past the map's end) translate and convert to MSL. Upstream also checks every patch word on the GPU through its tessellation lowering. A translation-chain change, with no game or build binding.

### Indexed texture handles in NVN2 compute c0

- **Symptom:** a shader library builds but cannot load: `reflection conversion failed: B binding cp_t2d_cb196608_10568000 has no resolved slot number`, followed by `the dispatched program has no compute shader`. The first race's sky is black.
- **Cause:** NVN2 compute places texture handles in a per-shader c0 layout. Fixed reads already pass through `AMapC0` into the canonical c2 driver tables, but an indexed LDC keeps its native bank and byte offset. `BindlessToArray` turned that read into an array of a game's uniform-buffer handles instead; native c0 has no such uniform slot.
- **Fix:** when the texture-array pass finds an indexed native compute c0 sampled-texture handle, it uses the control section's existing `QueryAmpereComputeC0Layout` metadata to map the recovered index into the canonical c2 combined or separate-texture table. A fixed separate sampler keeps its own c2 word. Reflection and replay then use their existing driver-table array path. Storage images, other constant-buffer banks and shaders without an NVN2 compute layout retain their previous lowering.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_nvn2_buffers.py` covers indexed combined handles and separate textures ORed with a fixed sampler; both declare c2 resources and retain no c0 read. The before/after shader-source comparison and rendered result are in the game's note.
- **Found on:** [Fast Fusion](../../dm/games/040041E0212AE000/README.md).

### Threadgroup stores in warp-synchronous code

- **Symptom:** a small scan or sort in a compute shader gives wrong results every frame, with no barrier in its loop (Orbitals: Niagara's sorted particle indices).
- **Cause:** Ampere compilers rely on lockstep warps and leave the barrier out of small scans: a thread stores its word to shared memory, then loads its neighbour's. For one thread the load and its own earlier store do not alias, so Metal may move the load above the store and read the neighbour's old value.
- **Fix:** the translator's `SharedMemoryFence` pass (`Translation/Transforms/SharedMemoryFence.cs`, after the lowering passes) calls the helper `dmsh_tg_fence()` before each shared memory load that some path reaches from a shared memory store with no barrier or fence in between. It is a forward pass over the function's blocks to a fixed point, so a store at the bottom of a loop reaches the load at its top, and a store on one branch reaches a load after the join. One fence orders every store before it, so a run of stores takes one fence. Stores at the end of the shader take none: a compute kernel reads nothing after them, and a task or mesh shader's wrapper puts a barrier after its body. Stage memory, which lies in shared memory, is treated as shared memory. `tg_fence` in `dmsh_rewrite.py` makes the helper's body a simdgroup-scope memory fence (`atomic_thread_fence(mem_flags::mem_threadgroup, …, thread_scope_simdgroup)`). That is not an execution barrier, so it is safe in divergent code. A kernel whose arrays spill to device memory gets the device scope too (`tg_spill`). From uncraftablexd's Orbitals bring-up ([PR #1775](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1775)). Its first form, a fence after every threadgroup store in the Metal source, gave Ocarina of Time 25,108 fences in 699 shaders and crashed Apple's compiler on an M2 (next entry).
- **Found on:** Orbitals (no profile here), by PR #1775; taken for [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md)'s GPU-driven passes.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_tg_fence.py` (placement through the translator; the rewrite alone without it).

### A fence after every shared memory store crashes Apple's compiler

- **Symptom:** `[RED] task / mesh pipeline prewarm failed: task … mesh … fragment …: … XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED` once per run, at the prewarm before the first frame; `MTLCompilerService` crashes with `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` in `llvm::MachineOperand::setReg` under `AGCModule<AGX::Impl::MeshProgramKey>::compileLLVMObject`. Runs with the fences also hung on the GPU more often (7 of 9 long runs against 0 of 3), which is suggestive, not proven.
- **Cause:** the first form of the previous entry's fix put a simdgroup fence after every store to a threadgroup array: 17,641 in Ocarina of Time's 500 mesh shaders (most after the stage output writes, which nothing reads before the wrapper's barrier), 85 in its 20 task shaders and 7,382 in 179 compute shaders. Apple's GPU compiler on an M2 (macOS 27.0, compiler 32023.922) crashed on one foliage mesh pipeline in every run; the same Metal source with only those fences removed compiled. An M4 Pro on macOS 27.2, and `metal-tt -arch applegpu_g14g`, compiled main's fenced pipeline, so the crash is in the M2's system compiler.
- **Fix:** fences only where a load follows a store with no barrier between, decided in the translator (previous entry). Ocarina of Time's 699 shaders went from 25,108 fences to 1,566: none in its 500 mesh shaders (the foliage mesh shader `f85bb2e8d78b864c` among them), 1 in its task shaders, 1,565 in its compute shaders, 1,030 of them in two particle shaders (`agl_technique_pfx`) that store and load the same array in a chain of branches. Before that the pass counted each call of the HMMA helper as a store, and Ocarina's movie networks took a fence before every HMMA of a chain (15,536).
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): task `a7f22b4a795ff4d1`, mesh `f85bb2e8d78b864c`, fragment `72fba2ec8558f6d4` (Foliage.Product.110, var0), reported on an M2 MacBook Air in [GitHub issue #2481](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2481).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_tg_fence.py`; on an M2, the prewarm log has no such line.

### Tessellation stages are not drawn

- **Symptom:** `primitive type 14 not implemented`; patch draws skipped.
- **Cause:** SPIRV-Cross already writes the hull shaders as kernels and the domain shaders as post-tessellation vertex functions, but Metal 4's render encoder and pipelines have no tessellation (no `drawPatches`, no factor buffer). The hull kernels also write quad factors (`MTLQuadTessellationFactorsHalf`) for triangle patches.
- **Fix:** not done. The draws would need a tessellator in a compute kernel and domain shaders rewritten as plain vertex functions.
- **Found on:** [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md): 15 hull and 18 domain shaders. Two tessellated programs (vertex `28a39709d0adfcfa` / hull `51d2d156bb12c260` / domain `0b95742e863bec50`, and `d4d89931b5a9a514` / `8aa6feeb7d33e9f6` / `f254b4dce2d6c012`) draw twice a frame for about 30 frames, in the title scene behind the file select as it slides out before a new game. [Bayonetta 3](../../dm/games/01004A4010FEA000/README.md): two domain shaders do not build (`DS_Vanish05_*.dso`: `SPIRV-Cross threw an exception: Subgroup builtins are not available in this type of function`); the one-lane rewrite (`spv_single_lane`, [Subgroup operations rewritten for a one-lane subgroup](#subgroup-operations-rewritten-for-a-one-lane-subgroup)) covers vertex shaders only, and tessellated draws are skipped anyway.
- **Note:** the vertex shaders that feed a hull or geometry shader can be found from the shader headers (SPH): the generic attribute map at +0x18 of the consumer's header (inputs) equals the one at +0x36 of the vertex shader's (outputs). In Luigi's Mansion 3, 40 vertex shaders match a hull shader and 49 a geometry shader.

### Ampere shader code (Switch 2)

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's shaders do not translate (the translator reads Maxwell: 64-bit instructions behind scheduling words, a 0x50-byte SPH), or, rewritten as Maxwell code, UI text draws as solid boxes or not at all.
- **Cause:** NVN2 ships Ampere (sm_86) code: 128-bit instructions, a 0x80-byte SPH (version 4; its first 0x50 bytes keep version 3's layout), uniform registers UR0–UR62 and uniform predicates, convergence barriers (BSSY / BSYNC) instead of the SSY / SYNC stack, CALL / RET with the return offset in a register, and scoreboards: a variable-latency instruction (one that sets a write barrier, control bits 110..113) writes its registers when its result arrives, and an instruction that does not wait on that barrier (wait mask, bits 116..122) reads the old values. A rewrite into Maxwell code got several meanings wrong: IPA (0x326, 0x526) is IPA.PASS and the shader multiplies by 1 / a[0x7c] itself; IPA's indexed form takes its index from a uniform register; TEX's bit 79 (`.F16`) packs the results as half pairs; an `LDG.128 R0, [R0.64]` must read its address once.
- **Fix:** the translator decodes Ampere natively (`TranslationFlags.Ampere`, job `arch: sm86`; `Decoders/DecoderAmpere.cs`, `Instructions/InstEmitAmpere*.cs`) into the shared IR. Uniform registers and predicates are register types of their own; a CALL into code with no reachable RET is a jump; BRX targets come from the c1 jump table its LDC reads (BRXU's from the uniform LDC of its uniform register), up to the next table another BRX of the program reads: the compiler puts a shader's switch tables back to back in c1, and read on to the end, Donkey Kong Bananza's `SilverStarMaterial` vertex shaders took every later switch's targets, 400 and more nested comparisons, past the Metal compiler's 256 nested brackets (11 shaders). An instruction with a write barrier writes into shadow registers that are copied to its destinations before the first instruction that waits on the barrier, at DEPBAR and at the end of the block (`Translator.EmitOpsAmpere`). Field positions follow Mesa NAK's SM70 encoder, CuAssembler's sm_86 tables and the facts of other projects (Eglantine). Maxwell code takes the old path, unchanged (byte-identical SPIR-V on 7,168 shaders).
  - Semantics that differ from Maxwell: `MUFU.SIN` / `COS` take the angle in turns (the compiler multiplies by 1 / 2π first; Maxwell's came through RRO), of which they use the fraction ([MUFU.SIN and MUFU.COS of a large number of turns](#mufusin-and-mufucos-of-a-large-number-of-turns)); bit 76 of FMUL / FFMA is .FMZ (a product with a zero factor is zero).
  - Warp-cooperative instructions (`InstEmitAmpereTensor.cs`): HMMA (m16n8k8 / m16n8k16, F16 sources, F16 or F32 accumulator; in a compute shader, m16n8k16 with an F16 accumulator goes through Apple's matrix hardware instead: [Ampere HMMA through simdgroup_matrix](#ampere-hmma-through-simdgroup_matrix)), LDSM (1, 2 or 4 8×8 matrices, plain or transposed) and MOVM run per thread: each lane gathers the fragments it needs from the lanes that hold them with subgroup shuffles (Metal's SIMD-groups are 32 wide, as the warp), in the layouts NVIDIA documents for PTX `mma.m16n8k16`, `ldmatrix` and `movmatrix`. HSETP2, HSET2 and HMNMX2 are the packed-half compares; MATCH (.ANY / .ALL, 32- or 64-bit) compares a value across the warp with a ballot of the active lanes and 32 shuffles, and REDUX reduces one into a uniform register the same way.
  - UP2UR takes any mask: bit k of the result is UPk where bit k of the mask is set, as P2R does (Mario Kart World's pixel shaders save UP3 or UP6 with masks 8 and 0x40 and test the register against URZ later). SURED (0x59f) is SUATOM's layout with no result; only SURED.ADD.U32 has been seen (Mario Kart World's `ab77eaee44cfef88`, adding 1 at a 2D coordinate), and another form is refused.
  - Geometry stage: OUT (0x124, emit / cut; none is OUTFINAL) and ISBERD (the vertex index passes through, as on Maxwell). A geometry shader that ends through an exit stub sets it up with LEPC and 0x357, and the stub runs OUTFINAL and 0x957 before its EXIT; per thread there is nothing to finish, so LEPC writes its own address and 0x357 / 0x957 do nothing.
  - NVN2 compute lays out `c[0]` per shader, from the control section: from 0x10 its push constants (+0xa9, in words), then its uniform buffer entries (+0xb1 of them) and storage buffer entries (+0xb2), 0x10 bytes each, then its texture handles (+0xb3), [separate texture and sampler handles](#nvn2-computes-separate-handles-in-c0) and image handles, 8 bytes each; the u16 at +0xba is the total (checked on all 205 of Donkey Kong Bananza's compute shaders). `dmsh_build.py` passes the counts (job `nvn2_cs`) and the frontend maps each range onto the tables the host already fills (`AMapC0`): push constants to `c[24]` (`DMSH_PUSH_BANK`), uniform entries to the compute uniform table, storage entries to the compute storage table, handles to `c[2]`. Graphics stages keep NVN's fixed tables. The push constants themselves come from `nvnCommandBufferPushGraphicsConstants` / `PushComputeConstants` (`OP_PUSH_CONST`, a `DMSH_PUSH_SIZE` block a stage group).
  - A storage address added from two constant buffer values (a `c[0]` entry plus an offset from `c[1]`, by UIMAD.WIDE) put the `c[1]` term second, and `GlobalToStorage` took the second operand as the base; it now prefers the driver segment's operand when only one is from it (Maxwell output unchanged, 28,669 shaders).
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#shaders): 22,751 of 22,822 shaders translate; its menu renders, and its gameplay, including agl's upscaler network (`agl_ndsr.pck`, 24 compute shaders with HMMA).
- **Resolved:** 0x593 is SUATOM, an image atomic through a handle (`ASuatom`, `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs`): SUST's handle (40..59) and dimension (61..64), ATOMG's operation (87..91), size (73..76) and predicate result (81..83, PT here). Its two shaders, agl's `agl_technique_pfx` compute shaders, take an append counter with it (SUATOM.ADD.U32 on a 1D image, the old value shuffled to the warp, then their SUST), and now translate: all 22,852 of the package's shaders do. Neither has been met in play so far, so the picture is not checked; `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` translates the encoding. A handle in another bank or a predicate result is refused.
- **Open:** loads and stores through addresses read from memory, which had no storage buffer to become, go through the memory pools now ([Global memory at an address loaded from memory](#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)).

### Storage words read one by one

- **Symptom:** a lit pass far slower on an M1 than the M4 Pro's figures suggest, the time spread over every material that loops over the scene's lights; dropping one material's draws (`DM_NVN_UB`) saved only its share.
- **Cause:** Ampere reads a storage buffer 64 or 128 bits at a time (LDG.E.64 / .128). The translator splits each read into 32-bit LoadGlobal operations, and spirv-cross writes each word with an index of its own, `uint(int(uint(base + 4) >> uint(2)))`. Metal's front end cannot tell such words are neighbours (each index is the shift of its own sum, then widened to 64 bits), so every word stays a load of its own with its own address arithmetic: about 240 in Splatoon Raiders' Hoian_UBER, most of them in its light loop (80 bytes a light).
- **Fix:** `wide_loads` (`dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_wide.py`, a step of `dmsh_build.py` after `pool_arrays`): the words of one read-only (`const device`) buffer at byte offsets k, k+4, … from one value, with no brace between them, become one read of a `packed_uint2/3/4` at the first word, each word taking its lane. In the AIR that read is still one load per word, but each at a constant offset from one address, so the words share the address arithmetic they each had; computing the index once in the translator (`(base >> 2) + i`, unsigned or signed) leaves every word its own widened index and gains nothing. A packed vector needs 4-byte alignment only, and the shader does not write the buffer through that binding. A run ends at any line that may write memory or change a value (a store through a subscript or a pointer, an atomic, a barrier, an image write, an assignment to an existing variable), so a write through another binding to the same memory cannot fall between its words; the values are the same. In TotK's 28,669 and Wonder's 16,266 built shaders that rule left every run as it was. A run starts on a 16-byte step of the common value and holds at most four words, as the original reads did.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md#rendering): 4,558 of its 27,155 shaders change. On an M1 in the opening dialogue scene, the lit pass's median GPU time went from 70.2 to 15.6 ms and the frame's from 91.5 to 30.1 ms (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF=every:60`, 15 and 35 profiled frames in the scene); the 40 shaders that did not build before are the only ones that fail.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_wide.py`.

## Bindings and handles

- **Note:** the layout of NVN's driver segments `c0` and `c2` (the handle tables, the storage and uniform buffer tables, the words the host fills) and the binding kinds of the reflection JSON have one definition, `dm/gfx/nvn/dmsh_abi.h`. The host includes it (`nvn_mtl.m`, `nvn_refl.m`, `nvn_obj.h`); `dmsh_build.py` reads it through `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_abi.py`, which stops on any line it cannot read. Both files are part of the chain version. `dm/tools/tests/test_dmsh_abi.py` checks that the tables fit together, that the host converts every kind the build may write, that the translator's own copy of the `c2` layout (`Program.cs`) agrees, and that the values do not come back as literals in the host or the build. The entries below cite the offsets.

### Images and buffer textures with their handle in a uniform buffer

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding cp_i_cb3_0_ has no resolved slot number` at load, then `the dispatched program has no compute shader`; in the build, `SUATOM handle in bank 3` or `SURED handle in bank 3`, and bindings of an unrecognized kind for a typed buffer. Compute passes are skipped.
- **Cause:** the compiler reads a texture handle from wherever the game put it, and bindless textures in uniform buffers were already [read at bind time](#storage-buffer-addresses-in-the-games-own-uniform-buffer). Images and buffer textures were not, as no game had done it: Unreal Engine 5.4 for the Switch 2 keeps every one in `c[3]`, uniform slot 0 (about 87,000 SURED, 15,000 SULD and 121,000 SUST in one game's shaders, none of them in another bank).
- **Fix:** binding kinds `img_ubo` and `tbuf_ubo` (`dmsh_abi.h`), `NVN_RB_IMGU` and `NVN_RB_TBUFU` in the host, which read the handle as `NVN_RB_TEXU` does and otherwise bind as an image slot or a typed buffer: usage upgrades for written images (`image_prepare`), the uniform shadow check, and the pass logic that treats any bindless shader cautiously. In the translator SUATOM and SURED pass their bank on. With no descriptor to ask at build time, an atomic takes its format from the instruction's size (`UniformHandleAtomicFormat`).
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md).

### Texture and sampler words in either order

- **Symptom:** a scene lit black with correct geometry. In `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`, the lighting shader's bindings show `tex … tid=0 dummy` where the G-buffer should be, and a sampler bound where a texture is expected.
- **Cause:** a handle from two words (`fp_t_cb262147_10020016`: words 0x16 and 8, handle type 1) is the OR of a texture and a sampler word, and the GPU does not care which comes first. The build took the first as the texture and the second as the sampler. Unreal Engine 5.4's lighting shaders read a shared sampler's word first, so each G-buffer texture came out as ID 0.
- **Fix:** each half of such a binding carries the other word (`soff`, `sreg`), and the host ORs the two as the GPU does (`ubo_bind_handle`). A shader whose order was as assumed gets the same handle.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): its lighting, with this as the last step from a black scene to a lit one.

### Compute texture handles loaded through uniform addresses

- **Symptom:** `Failed to find handle source for bindless access of type "textureBuffer".` in compute shaders; the build reports deleted reads whose results become zero.
- **Cause:** compute code can load its handle words through the uniform addresses in the canonical c0 table (`DMSH_C0_CS_UBO`), instead of direct constant-buffer instructions. `GlobalToStorage` lowers these loads to storage-buffer operations before `BindlessElimination`, which recognized only direct constant-buffer handle words. A two-word handle then lost its known uniform origin and its buffer fetch was deleted.
- **Fix:** `ResourceManager.TryGetComputeUniformSlot` recovers the uniform slot from the storage binding's descriptor origin. `BindlessElimination.UniformHandleWord` resolves a fixed word in a representable game-uniform slot to the existing constant-buffer binding metadata, retaining both words of an ORed handle. It changes no ordinary load. Other stages, unrelated storage descriptors, reserved slots and dynamic word offsets keep their existing paths. This is bound to the NVN compute driver layout, with no game/build gate or pool-array workaround.
- **Found on:** Sid Meier's Civilization VI (upstream's profile; none here): all 382 deleted accesses in four compute shaders restored. Their later gameplay use has not been exercised.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_compute_uniform_handles.py`; synthetic Maxwell checks both handle orders and descriptor/offset boundaries, and executes a translated fetch on Metal. `t_tbuf_handle` checks production binding of both uniform words.

### A store only a dispatch's first invocation makes

- **Symptom:** objects from a compute-built list flicker: each is in one of two states, on and off irregularly and independently of the others, while the draws are the same frame to frame and the camera is still. Whole groups may vanish or appear in one frame and stay that way until the list's next rebuild. Shots of consecutive frames show the change per frame jumping between two levels.
- **Cause:** a compute shader's preamble `if (-local.x == workgroup.x << log2(local size)) buffer[k] = 0` resets an append counter on the dispatch's first invocation, while the others add to it hundreds of instructions later. Nothing orders them in the API; it works where the first workgroup starts ahead of the rest. Apple's GPUs start workgroups in no order, so a workgroup that appended before the reset lost its entries to the next one's.
- **Fix:** `first_store` in `dmsh_build.py` recognises exactly that condition, for a flat local size or one with rows (the same test on y and x, with workgroup z zero), and takes the stores out of the shader; the reflection's `first_store` has the host make them in order before the dispatch (`nvn_mtl.m`). The stores may be one guarded block each, and a value may be a constant or a local set to one under the same guard. A guarded store it does not understand leaves the whole shader as it is: a first invocation that does more than plain stores (Mario Kart World's `649e36162cad9e79`, whose setup loops over a uniform's count) keeps the race.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): `029d9c3ec1fcb39f` (`Hoian_Proc`), the plants' instance lists, the only one of the game's 9 compute shaders with atomics that has it. The change per frame over the plants: 1.1, 2.4 or 4.5 at random, to a flat 1.0-1.3; pixels that jumped and returned the next frame: up to 88 % of them, to at most 21 %. The buffers the shader reads alternate frame by frame, which turned out unrelated. [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md): `2ce9071c2f22d74f` and `5fea9b7ddb30e8a8` (the scenery lists, four guarded blocks) and `0823b08c2027a38d` (local size 8x8), which the first form of the recogniser missed. Trees and bushes vanished in blocks with a still camera; over a title run a rebuild of the list lost 50 % of its entries on average, some well inside their draw range (406, 202, 797, 104 entries in turn), and 4 % after the fix, with no entry lost inside its range among those logged. `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` of one frame shows no writer, as the list is rebuilt only every few frames: look over many.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_first_store.py`.

### Goto elimination forcing loops around a whole program

- **Symptom:** `metal` runs for hours on a shader of about 100 MB of MSL, mostly indentation, stuck in LICM (`hoistRegion` → `ScalarEvolution::forgetValue`), then the GPU compiler's connection breaks; even `-O0` and finished libraries crash it. Hundreds of forced one-pass loops nest around the program.
- **Cause:** `MoveOutward` in `GotoElimination.cs` encloses what follows an if in an else when it moves a goto out. When another goto jumps into that range, it needs a loop of its own, and each one encloses the others.
- **Fix:** the else is skipped when a goto would have to jump into it (`HasGotoInto`, the goto's own label included). Only shaders that got a forced loop change.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): 26 such loops around the whole program in its uber shaders: the mid-size shader went from 176 loops to 0, the largest from 364 to 5, and 8 workers stalled for 5 h 40 before that. Three test shaders then compile in 89 s.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_control_flow.py`.

### Ampere carries, PLOP3's second result, POPC / FLO immediates, LEA.SX32 and LEA's negated B

- **Symptom:** none met in a game yet: found by differential testing (single instructions with nvdisasm-checked encodings, run on an Apple GPU and compared with an interpreter on Mesa NAK's hardware-checked folds). A 64-bit three-operand add off by 2^32, `POPC R, 0x800000ff` giving 23, a predicate pair from `PLOP3` that reads its own first result, a negative 32-bit index added to a 64-bit address as if positive.
- **Cause:** IADD3 set its two carry predicates to the bits of the carry count, where the first is sum >= 2^32 and the second sum >= 2 * 2^32 (the high word's IADD3.X adds both); PLOP3 wrote its first destination before computing the second; POPC and FLO applied bit 63 as a bitwise not in every form, where in the immediate form (4) it is the immediate's top bit; LEA ignored `.SX32` (bit 73: the high word is the sign extension of a); LEA negated B arithmetically with `.X` (the bitwise not there) and without it left the `+ 1` of `~B + 1` out of the carry.
- **Fix:** `AIadd3`, `APlop3`, `APopc`, `AFlo` and `ALea` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` follow those meanings.
- **Found on:** no game; encodings from the CuAssembler sm_86 corpus, nvdisasm-guided fuzzing and variants.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`CarriesAndModifiers`).

### FSETP / ISETP pairs on their own accumulator, and IMAD's B

- **Symptom:** a predicate pair that disagrees with itself (`FSETP.LT.AND P0, P1, R2, R3, P0`: P1 combined with the new P0, not the old); a multiply-add off by `2 * a * b` (IMAD read as `a * -b + c`).
- **Cause:** FSETP and ISETP wrote their first predicate before computing the second, which reads the accumulator again, as PLOP3 did ([previous entry](#ampere-carries-plop3s-second-result-popc--flo-immediates-leasx32-and-leas-negated-b)). The decoder read bit 63 (bit 75 in the register-B forms) of IMAD, IMAD.WIDE and IMAD.HI as B's negation; IMAD has none there (nvdisasm prints no `-` on B for either bit), only C's negation.
- **Fix:** `ASetPredPair` computes both results before writing either (FSETP, ISETP); `AImadSrcs` gives B without modifiers to the three IMAD forms (`InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`). Ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up ([PR #1800](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1800)).
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md), from PR #1800; no picture or hang here has been traced to either alone.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`SingleInstructionSemantics`: the four fail on the translator before).

### Ampere jump tables read past their end

- **Symptom:** `BRX with no target`, or code about 20 % larger than it should be.
- **Cause:** a BRX's table in c1 stores no length. The decoder read entries until one failed, so a table ran on into the next one; a target of 0 counted as a jump to the start of the program; and it looked only on one path for the `LDC` that loads the table's base, so a base loaded before an if / else join was not found.
- **Fix:** `FindBrxTargetsAmpere` reads a table one new target per pass, so the next table is known before this one overruns it, and stops at the next known base; an entry of 0 is filler for unused cases. `FindBrxTableAmpere` walks every predecessor and takes the base only when all paths reach the same `LDC`.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): 18 `Hoian_UBER` vertex shaders, 5 of them used at boot.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_control_flow.py`.

### An Ampere jump table loaded in a block split later

- **Symptom:** `BRX with no target found in the c1 jump table`, although the shader's `LDC` of the table's base is on the only path to the BRX.
- **Cause:** `Block.Split` gives the right half the left half's successors but leaves their predecessor lists naming the left half. `FindBrxTableAmpere` walks those lists back to the `LDC`, so when the block holding the `LDC` was split after its successors were linked (a later branch lands inside it), the walk from the BRX started in the left half, before the load, and met another write of the register or the program's start.
- **Fix:** `Block.Split` points the moved successors' predecessor entries at the right half, for both decoders: the Maxwell decoder's walk from a BRX (`FindBrxTargetRange`) reads the same lists. No other code reads them except for their count.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md): 16 pixel shaders (an `LDC c[0x1][R20+0x790]`, then a conditional BRA to the BRX).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_control_flow.py` (`test_table_loaded_in_a_block_split_later`).

### MUFU.F16

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 308` refused; a neural upscaler's programs missing, so the frame never reaches the window (a black 3D scene).
- **Cause:** MUFU has an F16 form (bit 73): the source is the half that bits 60..61 select (bit 62 its abs, as in every B slot), and the result goes back as the low half of the register, the high half zero. MUFU is the multi-function unit (op 2 EXP2, 3 LOG2, …), not a multiply.
- **Fix:** `AMufu` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): 10 compute shaders of `agl_ndsr.pck`.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### MUFU.SIN and MUFU.COS of a large number of turns

- **Symptom:** part of the picture without ambient occlusion, bounded by an arc whose centre is off the screen (the top-left corner), the rest correct; on Donkey Kong Bananza a wedge in the lower right of the SSAO's half-size target when dynamic resolution is at its top (1152x648 for a 2304x1296 scene, docked), a fifth of the picture with the effects at full size (1676x943).
- **Cause:** Ampere's `MUFU.SIN` / `COS` take turns and use only their fraction, which is exact for any float. The translator multiplied the turns by 2π in float and took Metal's `sin` / `cos` of that: the product rounds the fraction away (at 2^20 turns a step of 0.4 rad), and Metal's fast `sin` / `cos` return 0 from about 2^20 turns (6.6e6 rad) on, both of them. Bananza's SSAO (`a6f04605183d9915`) turns its kernel per pixel by (10 (3 (x mod y) + x y) + 5.652) / 2π turns; past x y ≈ 659,000 the rotation (cos, sin) was (0, 0), every sample landed on the pixel itself and nothing occluded it. The SSAO runs at half the scene's size, which dynamic resolution moves: at 838x471 (a 1676x943 scene) the product stays below 395,000, no hole, but angles up to 0.23 rad from the hardware's; at 1152x648 (the docked scene's top, 2304x1296) 5,425 pixels of the lower-right corner (0.7%) have no occlusion; at 1676x943 (the effects at full size) 346,009 (22%).
- **Fix:** `AMufu` lowers both as `sin` / `cos((t − rint(t)) · 2π)` (`InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` `ATurnsToRadians`). `t − rint(t)` is exact and lies in [−0.5, 0.5], where fast `sin` / `cos` are accurate, and a small angle stays itself. The angles are the hardware's (within 2e-7), at every size; past 2^21 turns the fraction itself has only 4 values, as on the Switch. Maxwell code is unchanged (its `MUFU` follows `RRO` and takes radians; a huge angle there is open). Any Ampere shader that passes `sin` / `cos` an angle that large, such as a per-pixel rotation built from pixel coordinates, had the same fault; only Bananza was checked. Every Switch 2 game's shaders that use `sin` / `cos` change (8,258 of Bananza's 23,638 shaders in `regress.py check`): a chain change.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md), its SSAO: a corner wedge at the top of dynamic resolution (1152x648), and a fifth of the picture with the effects at full size (1676x943, a local code patch of its effects-resolution levels, 0.5 to 1.0).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> t_mufu_turns && dm/gfx/tests/t_mufu_turns` (the lowering on the GPU against `cos` / `sin(2π · fract(t))` in double over the SSAO's angles at 838x471, 1152x648, 1676x943 and 2560x1440: the earlier lowering collapses 0, 5,425, 345,255 and 1,893,254 rotations); `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`MufuTurns`: the lowered MSL).

### Scaled shared memory addresses

- **Symptom:** a compute shader's results in shared memory land in the wrong elements, or only part of its memory is cleared. An exposure histogram came back with its bins at about four times their index, the scene several stops too dark.
- **Cause:** LDS and STS with a register carry a stride at 78..79, so the register counts elements of 4, 8 or 16 bytes (stride 1, 2, 3), not bytes. The decoder ignored it. The field is a scale, not the access size: stride 3 occurs on 4, 8 and 16-byte accesses alike.
- **Fix:** `AStride` in `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs`, for LDL, LDS, STL, STS and ATOMS. LDSM keeps its mode at those bits (1 is a transposed read, MT88), so it takes its address unscaled (`stride: false`): scaled by that mode, [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md)'s upscaler (`7629d857a0a93833`, 12 of its 27 LDSM) read four times too far along, and the picture came out as an 8x8 lattice of garbage for as long as the upscaler ran, with nothing in the log.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md): 2 LDS and 2 STS with a register in its 16,888 shaders, all in the Lighting compute shaders; `d5bc99ffd183fa20` bounds its register by the element count (0x20) before `[R2 * 8]`. [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md): 921 accesses with stride 1, 68 with 2 and 3,312 with 3, among its 131,426 shaders; `2118ab534fac88c6`'s histogram increments `[R3 * 4]`. [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md): its froxel shaders (`03923de3c507135e` and 35 variants) keep a float4 a thread at `[R * 16 + 0x500]`; unscaled, pull request #1151 reports the race picture in rainbow bands by depth slice. The stride is not the access size there: `f94552a2a2bff505` stores 8 bytes at `[R9 * 16]` and 4 at `[R9 * 16 + 8]`.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`Shared`: each stride, on 4, 8 and 16-byte stores in the games' encodings).

### Compute shared memory past 32 KB

- **Symptom:** `[RED] failed to create compute pipeline: Threadgroup memory size (49152) exceeds the maximum threadgroup memory allowed (32768)`, thousands a run; the dispatch is skipped. Once moved to device memory: with more than 512 workgroups in a dispatch, or an indirect one, workgroups found other workgroups' data in their shared memory (no game seen doing so).
- **Cause:** Ampere gives a workgroup up to 100 KB of shared memory, and an Apple GPU 32 KB (`maxThreadgroupMemoryLength`). SPIRV-Cross declares the shader's shared memory as `threadgroup` arrays of its full size.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_rewrite.py` `tg_spill`: a kernel whose threadgroup arrays exceed `DMSH_TG_MAX` takes them from a device buffer at compute buffer index `DMSH_TG_SLOT` (`dmsh_abi.h`), one slice of their total per workgroup, at the workgroup's index in the whole grid (in 64 bits). Its threadgroup barriers order device memory too (`mem_device | mem_threadgroup`), and the reflection's `tg_spill` gives the bytes per workgroup. `nvn_mtl.m` `dispatch` binds a slice of the submission's arena for every workgroup of the dispatch. NX 18 also caps the buffer at a 16th of the GPU's recommended working set as well as Metal's largest buffer, and skips a dispatch over that (`[RED] … exceed Metal's largest buffer or a 16th of the GPU's working set; skipped`): on an iPhone a grid of thousands of 48 KB workgroups would otherwise ask for hundreds of MB of shared memory at once (from the code, not seen). An indirect dispatch's grid is in GPU memory: the replay waits for the GPU, as an indirect-count multi-draw does (`mdi_wait`), and reads it first, logging the first such dispatch.
- **Measurement:** before, the slice was the workgroup's index modulo 512, on the assumption that workgroups 512 apart are not on the GPU together, and an indirect dispatch always got 512. Apple's GPUs start workgroups in no order. On an M6 (12 GPU cores), 64-thread workgroups ran up to 537 at once, and groups 512 apart overlapped from 1,024 workgroups on: 2 to 5 of 1,024 and 187 to 427 of 16,384 found their slice taken by another. The 48 KB kernel of the check, rewritten that way, got 126,988 of 262,144 sums wrong over 1,024 workgroups. Pull request #1242 found it, without a game's symptom; Mario Kart World never dispatched more than 3 workgroups of these shaders (below).
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md#shaders): 168 compute shaders of `DrivePassShaderDefault` declare 48 KB (`spvUnsafeArray<uint, 12288>`), 84 of them with barriers and none with shared atomics; all 168 compile rewritten. From the title to the first race all their dispatches are direct, of 1 to 3 workgroups, 6 a frame in the race.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_tg_spill.py` (the rewritten kernel compiles where the Metal compiler is installed, and with Xcode runs over 1,024 workgroups on this Mac's GPU, each summing data its workgroup wrote to its shared memory); `make -C dm/gfx/tests check_t_tg_spill` runs `dispatch` over 1,024 such workgroups, direct and indirect, the indirect counts written by the GPU after the CPU's copy named one workgroup.

### Uniform references past the first kilobyte

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader <key>_b reflection conversion failed: fetch call 0: frag_pos uniform reference out of range`; the shader does not load and its draws are skipped.
- **Cause:** the reflection keeps a uniform it reads on the host (a pixel-position sample's scale, an interpolated output's affine terms) as slot, vector and component in 16 bits, which held vectors up to 63, the first kilobyte of a uniform buffer; a uniform buffer is 64 KB.
- **Fix:** such references are 32 bits (`nvn_refl.m`): slot << 16 | vector << 2 | component, vectors 0 to 4095.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md): pixel-position samples whose scale is at c[6] vector 989; 611 load failures in a run of its title.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_refl check_t_feedback`.

### HADD2 and HMUL2 with an immediate

- **Symptom:** the GPU hangs (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang`) from the first 3D scene on, or frames of about 88 ms, in a shader with a half-float loop.
- **Cause:** the two-source half-pair operations took their second source from the operand order HFMA2 needs: in forms 2, 3 and 7 that is the unused register field at bit 64, not the immediate, constant or uniform register. A blur stepping its loop with `HADD2 Rd, Rd, imm` stepped by whatever R0 held. An immediate also took modifiers from its own bits 62 and 63, flipping the sign of a negative constant.
- **Fix:** `AHalfSecond` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` for HADD2, HMUL2, HSETP2, HSET2 and HMNMX2; `AHalfSrcs` leaves an immediate's bits alone.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md): 25,726 of its shaders use those forms; `85446a6753300fc8` is the blur.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`Pokopia`: constants, a negative immediate's sign) and `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`HalfImmediate`: on a live register).

### HFMA2 .H0_NH1

- **Symptom:** wrong shading where a shader needs a cross product or a 2D rotation of half pairs; on Eden-NS2, which first read the mode as a plain negation of the high lane, sun and cloud shadows were lost.
- **Cause:** bit 86 of HFMA2 rotates B: the name gives the lanes high first, so the high lane reads B's low half and the low lane B's high half negated. The decoder ignored the bit.
- **Fix:** `AHfma2` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`. The lane order comes from Eden-NS2's fix (checked there against Switch 2 footage), not from a shader that proves it here.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md): 10,177 instructions in 7,590 programs. The fix made no visible difference in its tutorial area.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### Global memory: descriptors, ATOMG.CAS, INC / DEC

- **Symptom:** a global load, store or atomic at the wrong address in code that uses `desc[URb][Ra.64]` (the descriptor's value added to the address); a shader with `ATOMG.CAS` refused (`Ampere instruction 3a9 ... is not implemented`); an `ATOMG.INC` / `ATOMG.DEC` / `RED.INC` / `RED.DEC` with no effect.
- **Cause:** `AGlobalAddress` added the uniform register in every uniform form. nvdisasm (sm_86) names two forms: `[Ra.64+URb]`, where URb is part of the address, and `desc[URb][Ra.64]` (bit 76 of LDG / STG, bit 71 of ATOMG / RED), where it is a memory descriptor (the compiler's cache policy) and the address is Ra.64 alone. Unreal Engine 5.4's bounded read (`LDG.!P0 R5, [R5 + UR4]`, `GuardedWrites`) is the first form and is unchanged. Opcode 0x3a9 was not registered; INC and DEC have no case in `EmitAtomicOp`.
- **Fix:** `AGlobalAddress` takes the form's descriptor bit; `AAtomgCas` (0x3a9, 32 bits: Rb expected, Rc at 64 the new value, the old value returned); `AGlobalIncDec`, a compare-and-swap loop as ATOMS's, for ATOMG and RED.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`GlobalMemory`: the three fail on `main`). A GPU cross-check of 300 LDG / STG / ATOMG / RED from an nvdisasm-validated corpus (sm_86 and sm_80 compiled code), as one-thread compute shaders through a gmem_map the harness builds as the host does (`dmsh_abi.h` `DMSH_GMEM_MAP_*`), production options: 135 identical to 300. Which forms NVN's compiler emits for games is not known here: the descriptor form is CUDA's usual one, and no game note in this repository shows it.
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter; no game run.

### Maxwell PRMT byte permutation

- **Symptom:** the shader build counts `Shader instruction PrmtI is not implemented.` (or `PrmtR`, `PrmtC`, `PrmtRc`) among the translator's messages, and the shader still loads: the instruction's destination keeps its old value.
- **Cause:** the Maxwell decoder knew the register, immediate-selector and both constant-buffer forms of PRMT, but each emitter only logged that line. Ampere's PRMT has its own emitter and was not affected.
- **Fix:** `InstEmitPermute.cs`: PRMT picks four bytes from its two data operands. In the generic mode each selector nibble names one of the eight bytes and its bit 3 replicates that byte's sign; F4E, B4E, RC8, ECL, ECR and RC16 use the selector's low two bits and copy bytes without sign replication, as NVIDIA's PTX `prmt` table gives them. The `Rc` form takes its selector from the register field and its upper data word from the constant buffer. A reserved mode stops the shader's translation. A chain change. Ported from NXNative `33b0cf51e` (PR #3072, MIT).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_maxwell_prmt.py` (constant operands folded through the translator, every form and mode compiled for the iPhone). Upstream's GPU check over runtime operands is not ported: NX 18 has no runner for it.
- **Found on:** [EA SPORTS FC 27](../../dm/games/0100C49025D3E000/README.md): four shaders with `PrmtI`. Upstream also found it in Farming Simulator 26, which has no profile here.

### Maxwell ATOM.CAS

- **Symptom:** the shader build counts `Shader instruction AtomCas is not implemented.` among the translator's messages, and the shader still loads: its global compare-and-swap does nothing and its result register keeps its old contents (a hash-table insertion or a lock-free update that never lands).
- **Cause:** Ryujinx's Maxwell `AtomCas` only logged. Its fields, from envytools' `gm107.c` (the 0xeef0 row: `atommem0_imm`, `tabeef0sz`) and nvdisasm's sm_61 records in CuAssembler: Rd at 0, Ra at 8, Rb at 20, a signed byte displacement at 28..47, `.E` at 48, `.64` at 49. `ATOM.E.CAS R3, [R2], R10, R11` compares the word with Rb, swaps in Rb + 1 and returns the old word (CUDA atomicCAS); the second source is implied. Every recorded form has an even Rb and bits 50..51 (Ryujinx's `BcRz`) clear.
- **Fix:** a chain change (`InstEmitMemory.cs` `AtomCas`): a global compare-and-swap on the 40-bit address, Rd the old word, through the same storage buffer or pool table lowering as `ATOM`. The 64-bit form (Metal has no 64-bit compare-and-swap), set bits 50..51 and an odd or RZ Rb fail translation (`ATOM.CAS at 0x…: only the 32-bit form …`), as unsupported image atomic forms do; no compiled code has them.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 -m unittest -v dm/tools/tests/test_global_memory.py`: the old word, the swap, a mismatch, both displacement signs, predication, a 32-bit address, Rd equal to Ra, Rb or Rb + 1, Rd = RZ, and the pool table route, all 1,024 words of the buffer checked on the GPU (`dm/gfx/tests/t_global_memory.m`); the rejected forms fail. Each `ATOM.CAS` case fails on the translator before the fix.
- **Found on:** [EA SPORTS FC 27](../../dm/games/0100C49025D3E000/README.md): compute shader `90ce32c070f208a5` (`ATOM.E.CAS R6, [R6], R8, R9`), the only `ATOM.CAS` in the Maxwell games' shaders on this Mac's drives; not met in the menus or a match, so no change on screen is known. Retranslating the game's 3,222 compute shaders changed that one alone. Also reported in LOLLIPOP CHAINSAW RePOP (pull request [#684](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/684), whose profile is not in this repository), in a feedback-compaction compute shader on its prologue route, with no measured change on screen.

### A negative displacement on a 40-bit global address

- **Symptom:** a load through a pointer the shader read from memory gives 0, and a store or atomic through one does nothing, when the instruction has `.E` and a negative displacement (`LDG.E R4, [R2+-0x4]`); a `RED` with a negative displacement does nothing on either route.
- **Cause:** two errors in Ryujinx's Maxwell global addresses. `Get40BitsAddress` added the displacement to the low word and the carry out of it to the high word, but no sign: a negative displacement is 2^32 − k unsigned, whose add carries unless the low word borrows, so the high word came out one too high, 4 GB up. Accesses the translator turns into a descriptor's storage buffer take their offset from the low words and were right; the memory pool table's lookup takes the whole address ([Global memory at an address loaded from memory](#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)), finds no pool there and drops the access. And `Red` passed its 20-bit displacement unsigned (`atommem0_imm` is signed, as `ATOM`'s), 1 MB on instead of back.
- **Fix:** a chain change (`InstEmitMemory.cs`): a negative displacement adds −1 to the high word with the carry; `Red` sign-extends its displacement.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_global_memory.py` (above): its `pointer_*` cases with negative displacements, among them one whose low word borrows (0x2_00000004 − 8), fail without the high-word change, and its `red_*` cases without the sign.
- **Found on:** reviewing the `ATOM.CAS` work of pull request [#684](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/684).
