Among the Maxwell games' shaders on this Mac's drives, only Sid Meier's Civilization VII (upstream's profile; none here) has `.E` accesses with negative displacements (one `ATOM` shader, five `LDG` / `STG` in another), and none goes through the pool table, so no game picture is known to change.

### ATOMS.INC / ATOMS.DEC, and ATOMS.CAS's result

- **Symptom:** a shared-memory counter that never moves (`ATOMS.INC`, `ATOMS.DEC`: CUDA's atomicInc / atomicDec, the old value read as 0); a lock-free update on shared memory that loops or takes a wrong branch after `ATOMS.CAS` (its result 0 or 1 instead of the old value).
- **Cause:** `AAtoms` hands operations 3 and 4 to `EmitAtomicOp`, which has no case for them: it logs "Invalid atomic operation" and gives the constant 0, with no access. `AAtomsCas` gave every compare-and-swap CAST.SPIN's result; nvdisasm names three forms by bit 87 (CAS clear, CAST set, CAST.SPIN with bit 88 too), and a plain CAS gives the old value (PTX `atom.cas`).
- **Fix:** INC (`old >= b ? 0 : old + 1`) and DEC (`old == 0 || old > b ? b : old - 1`) are a compare-and-swap loop in `AAtoms`; `AAtomsCas` gives the old value when bit 87 is clear, 1 / 0 for CAST and CAST.SPIN as before (Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave's retry loop).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`SharedAtomics`: the three fail on `main`). A GPU cross-check of 400 LDS / STS / ATOMS from an nvdisasm-validated sm_86 corpus, run as one-thread compute shaders with the production options (debug and reduced on, MSL 2.3), against a reference: 84 mismatches (all INC, DEC and CAS) to none; the same on encodings from real compiled code.
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter; no game run. Also seen with `debug: false` (the AST optimizer, off by default): a shared store of 8 or 16 bits makes the translator throw, and an ATOMS whose result goes to RZ is dropped.

### FRND of a half (FRND.F16)

- **Symptom:** `[translation failed] NotSupportedException: Ampere 307 at …: FRND of a float that is not 32-bit`.
- **Cause:** the emitter took 32-bit floats only. `FRND.F16` rounds a half to an integer value: both sizes are 1 (bits 84..86 and 75..77), the rounding at 78..80 as for a float.
- **Fix:** a chain change (`AFrnd` in the vendored `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`). The half bits 60..62 select (as F2F's) is unpacked, rounded in 32 bits and packed into the low 16 bits. The pack is exact: every half from 2^10 up is an integer already, and an integer below 2^11 has at most 11 significant bits. That the half is selected as F2F's is taken from F2F, not from a disassembly of a high-half case (all of PRAGMATA's read the low half). A source and result of different sizes still fail.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): 65 shaders (47 compute, 11 vertex, 7 pixel), nearest and floor.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py -k Frnd`.

### Ampere forms met on PRAGMATA: TLD through a uniform register, I2F of an immediate, SUATOM.CAS

- **Symptom:** `[translation failed] NotSupportedException: Ampere instruction f66 … is not implemented`, `Ampere instruction 595 … is not implemented`, or `Ampere 906 …: I2F byte select of a 32-bit source`.
- **Cause:** three forms the emitters did not take. 0xf66 is TLD with its handle in a uniform register (40..46), the fetch beside 0xf60 (TEX), 0xf63 (TLD4), 0xf69 (TMML) and 0xf6c (TXD): its other fields are 0xb66's. That reading is the family's pattern (0xf60 + 3n beside 0xb60 + 3n), not a disassembly. 0x595 is SUATOM's compare-and-swap through a bound handle: Mesa's NAK encodes the bindless ones as 0x394 (atomic) and 0x396 (compare-and-swap, its data the pair compare value, new value), and 0x593 is 0x394 with the handle bound. I2F in its immediate form (0x906) has the value in 32..63, so 60..62 select no byte there; an immediate of -2 read as select 7.
- **Fix:** a chain change (vendored `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs`: `ATex` takes 0xf66, `ASuatomCas`; `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`: `AI2f` reads no select from an immediate).
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): 17 shaders with 0xf66 (11 vertex, 6 pixel), compute shader `50fece0d891feba4` with 0x595, 3 compute shaders with the I2F. All build; none has been watched drawing.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py -k I2fImmediate`; the other two forms are checked by PRAGMATA's build only.

### Indexed fragment inputs lose per-location interpolation

- **Symptom:** some objects flicker between bright and dark as the camera moves. Their pixel shaders read a fragment input through an indexed `IPA` and use another input, meant to be flat, as an integer bit pattern (a lighting record's index, for example).
- **Cause:** an indexed `IPA` (Maxwell `IPA.IDX`, Ampere 0x526) sets `ShaderDefinitions.IaIndexing`, and `Declarations.DeclareInputOrOutput` then declares every user input of the pixel shader as one `vec4[16]` SPIR-V `Input` array at location 0, with location 0's interpolation only (`ImapTypes[0].GetFirstUsedType()`). The other locations lost their `Flat` or `NoPerspective` and were interpolated like location 0: a flat bit pattern blended between the three vertices is another number.
- **Fix:** a chain change (vendored `CodeGen/Spirv`, upstream NXNative `a7df99f84`). When `IaIndexing` is set on a pixel shader and a used location's interpolation differs from location 0's (an unused location 0 counts as perspective), `Declarations.TryDeclareSplitIndexedFragmentInputs` declares one `vec4` `Input` per location 0..15 with its own `Flat` / `NoPerspective`, and the indexed reads go to a `Private` `vec4[16]` (`Instructions.GetPointer`) that `SpirvGenerator.Generate` fills from the 16 inputs at the start of `main`. Shaders whose used locations all share location 0's interpolation keep the single `Input` array, so their output is unchanged. A location whose four components mix interpolation modes takes its first used component's mode and leaves a translator note; the shader's other locations are still fixed.
- **Found on:** [Metroid Prime 4: Beyond](../../dm/games/010019A01E2F2000/README.md#found-on-this-game), upstream (NXNative PR #3408): 44,121 indexed Maxwell pixel shaders; with the flat qualifier back, the flicker on MacKenzie and on spherical decorations went away in play there. Not seen on the owner's games here, and no game's shaders were rebuilt for this port.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_indexed_interp.py`: synthetic Maxwell and Ampere indexed pixel shaders through `dmsh_xlate` and `spirv-cross`, then drawn on this Mac's GPU, checking perspective, `flat` (the provoking vertex's exact bits), `noperspective` and an indexed read. Seven of its eight cases fail with the translator before this change.

### IPA at an offset, and the barycentric coordinates

- **Symptom:** `[translation failed] NotSupportedException: Ampere 326 at …: IPA at an offset`; before the offset is reached, the build's log has `Attribute offset 0x3B4 is not valid` and the read gives 0.
- **Cause:** two things the attribute emitters did not have. IPA's sample location 2 (bits 76..78) interpolates at an offset from the pixel centre, the offset word in the second source register (32..40): x in bits 8..11 and y in bits 24..27, signed, in 1/16 pixel (Mesa NAK's `load_barycentric_offset` builds a signed 12-bit value in 1/4096 per half and clamps it to -0.5..0.4375, so only those four bits vary; GLSLC's code masks them with `0xf00` and `0x0f000000`). And `a[0x3a8..0x3b0]` and `a[0x3b4..0x3b8]` are the pixel's barycentric coordinates, screen-linear (three words) and perspective (two: the third is 1 - x - y), Mesa's `NAK_ATTR_BARY_COORD_NO_PERSP` and `NAK_ATTR_BARY_COORD`; the perspective pair is read like any perspective attribute, divided by w, and the shader multiplies by 1 / `a[0x7c]` read at the same place.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `AttributeMap.cs` maps both groups to new inputs (`IoVariable.BaryCoord`, `BaryCoordNoPersp`: SPIR-V `BaryCoordKHR` / `BaryCoordNoPerspKHR` with `SPV_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric`, MSL `[[barycentric_coord]]`), and `EmitIpa` multiplies the perspective pair by `gl_FragCoord.w`. For the offset (`AIpa` passes the register, `EmitIpa`), what IPA.PASS reads (an attribute over w, 1 / w, a screen-linear attribute, the position) is linear over the screen within the primitive, so its value at the offset is the centre's plus the offset times its screen derivatives, which are exact for a linear function: `v + dFdx(v) * ox + dFdy(v) * oy`. That needs no `interpolateAtOffset`, which SPIRV-Cross can give a user attribute only (an `interpolant<>` member), not the built-ins this shader reads. A constant (flat) read and the integer attributes take no offset. Maxwell's IPA has the same location field, not met and not changed.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): pixel shader `4a9d4507eea6c837` reads 1 / w and the perspective barycentric pair at the position of the sample it runs for (the table byte at `c0 + 0x620`, shifted into the offset word); it builds; no draw with it has been watched. No other of its 36,779 shaders has either.
- **Open:** on a multisampled target SPIRV-Cross moves `gl_FragCoord.xy` to the sample, while IPA of `a[0x70]` / `a[0x74]` at the default location is the pixel centre: a shader that adds the sample offset itself (PRAGMATA's 154 sample position readers) would add it twice. Not met: the game's per-sample shaders have been seen on single-sample targets only. Derivatives are taken where the IPA stands; one inside non-uniform control flow has not been met.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py -k IpaAtOffset`.

### The shared compare-and-swap loop retries with the value it got

- **Symptom:** a shared-memory atomic built from a compare-and-swap loop (INC, DEC, the 64-bit and float operations) spins longer than it should or stores a value computed from stale data when other threads write the word.
- **Cause:** the loop `GenerateSharedAtomicCasLoop` (`HelperFunctionManager.cs`) writes reloaded the word at the head of every iteration instead of taking the value the failed exchange returned, which is the word's current value. The reload is a plain load between atomics, with no ordering against them.
- **Fix:** the old value is loaded once before the loop; each iteration computes from it, and a failed exchange's result becomes the next iteration's old value. Ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up ([PR #1796](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1796)).
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md), from PR #1796; not traced to a symptom here.

### Half-pair swizzle on a constant, and .F32

- **Symptom:** wrong values in the high lane of half-pair math that scales by one half of a uniform (`HMUL2 R37, R37.H0_H0, c[0x0] [0x168].H0_H0`), or a garbage operand where a half-pair operation reads a 32-bit float (`R20.F32`).
- **Cause:** `AHalfLanes` took a constant operand as is, ignoring its swizzle (bits 60..61 in the 32 slot, where a constant lives), so `.H0_H0` / `.H1_H1` on a constant gave the high lane the constant's other half. Swizzle value 1 was read as `.H1_H1`; nvdisasm (sm_86) names it `.F32` in all three slots (A 74..75, the 32 slot 60..61, the 64 slot 81..82), the operand's own 32-bit float. Only an immediate has no swizzle: those bits are its own.
- **Fix:** `AHalfLanes` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` applies the swizzle to constants and takes `.F32` as the float itself in both lanes (the lanes are computed in 32 bits already). MUFU.F16, whose bits 60..61 pick one half (`.H0` / `.H1`), keeps its own reading. `.F32`'s exact rounding on NVIDIA (whether the float is rounded to a half first) is not known; the float itself is the closer reading than its top 16 bits.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`HalfSwizzle`: the three fail on `main`), and `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`'s `MufuF16`. A GPU cross-check of 900 HADD2 / HMUL2 / HFMA2 from an nvdisasm-validated sm_86 corpus against an exact binary16 reference (one rounding, FMA included) went from 86 mismatches on constant swizzles to none; what is left is `.FMZ` (0 times infinity). The [half .RELU NaN correction](#hfma2mma-the-half-pair-fma-on-the-tensor-pipe) is shared by ordinary HFMA2 and HMUL2.
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter; no game run.

### HSETP2 / HSET2 .FTZ

- **Symptom:** a half comparison with `.FTZ` on a subnormal operand passes where NVIDIA's fails (or the reverse): `HSET2.BF.GT.FTZ R6, R19, RZ` gives 1.0 for a positive subnormal half, 0 on NVIDIA.
- **Cause:** bit 80 of HSETP2 and HSET2 was dropped. `AFtz` (FSETP / FSET) does not fit: the half lanes are unpacked to 32-bit floats, where a half's subnormals are normal, so `FlushDenormal`'s bound never flushes them.
- **Fix:** `AHalfFtz` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` flushes each lane under the half's own bound (2^-14) before the comparison. The arithmetic shares the signed flush described in [Half-pair .FTZ flushes f16 subnormals](#half-pair-ftz-flushes-f16-subnormals).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`HalfCompareFtz`: two fail before the comparison fix). The GPU cross-check with subnormal and zero inputs: 4 HSETP2 / HSET2 mismatches in 400, none with the fix. HMNMX2.FTZ now shares the source flush and has [synthetic boundary coverage](#half-pair-ftz-flushes-f16-subnormals).
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter (NAK's `OpHSetP2` gives the `.H_AND` reading the translator already has); no game run.


### I2F and F2F directed rounding

- **Symptom:** integer division results one off for some operands (the compiler's 32-bit division takes its reciprocal from `I2F.U32.RP`), or a half one ulp off (an overflow giving infinity instead of the largest finite value) after `F2F.F16.F32.RM` / `.RP` / `.RZ`.
- **Cause:** the rounding field (78..79: `.RM` 1, `.RP` 2, `.RZ` 3) was decoded but not applied: I2F and the pack of F2F round to nearest. For a 32-bit integer above 2^24, I2F rounded to nearest is one ulp off the directed result about half the time. A first fix of F2F also met the optimizer's `PropagatePack`, which takes an unpack of a pack for the packed float itself (dropping the half's rounding).
- **Fix:** `AI2f` (32-bit source, 32-bit float) and `AF2f` (F32 to F16) in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` compare the rounded result with the exact value (the integer converted back, exact below 2^31 / 2^32; the half read back through a mask) and step its bits to the neighbour `ADirected` picks. Narrow integer sources are exact; I2F to a half keeps rounding to nearest.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`DirectedRounding`: two fail on `main`). A GPU cross-check against an exact reference (one rounding, IEEE directed modes; NAK's hardware test for F2F.F16.F32.RZ's overflow): I2F 45 mismatches in 300, none with the fix; F2F 25-31 in 300, 6 left, all with results at or below 0x050d (half subnormals and the smallest normals, where the GPU's own conversion differs). Also left: FRND / F2I of a 32-bit subnormal (FLOOR of a tiny negative is -1 on NVIDIA; the GPU flushes it to zero).
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter; no game run.

### I2F from a 64-bit integer rounds once

- **Symptom:** `I2F` from a signed or unsigned 64-bit register pair to f32 can give the wrong float. A negative integer close to zero can lose its entire value during cancellation.
- **Cause:** `AI2f64` rounded the high and low words separately as floats before adding them, and discarded the rounding mode. The division sequence that introduced the emitter used a zero high word, where the old nearest-rounding calculation is exact.
- **Fix:** form the magnitude as two integer words, normalize to recover the 24 retained bits and the round and sticky bits, and round once in the encoded mode. Set the low bit of the leading-bit search operand to keep the shift counts valid for zero. Return positive zero for a zero input. This applies to the Ampere register-pair form with an f32 destination.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922. Signed nearest-even changes from 16 differences to zero across 1,024 recorded Jetson Orin lanes. All 112 synthetic boundary lanes and a separate 36,244-lane exact-reference comparison match on the Mac for signed and unsigned inputs in RN, RM, RP and RZ. All eight synthetic shaders validate as SPIR-V and compile offline for iOS. The directed modes and unsigned conversion are covered by synthetic references. No games or iOS device ran.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2045](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2045). The opcode appears in the [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave lighting path](#texture-handles-of-sdk-22s-ampere-code).

### F2FP packs BF16 and applies RELU and RZ

- **Symptom:** `F2FP.BF16.PACK_AB` returns a pair of F16 values, and `F2FP.RELU.PACK_AB` keeps negative values. A toward-zero pack can round away from zero or turn a finite value into infinity.
- **Cause:** `AF2fp` used the same nearest-even F16 pack for all `PACK_AB` instructions. It ignored the format, clamp and rounding bits.
- **Fix:** clamp negative inputs, including -0 and -infinity, to +0 for `.RELU`. Preserve NaNs through the clamp and emit canonical `0x7fff` in `.RELU` lanes. BF16 uses the upper 16 input bits, with a nearest-even bias when requested, and canonicalizes NaNs. F16 `.RZ` widens the rounded half's magnitude and steps it back one code when a finite input rounded away from zero. Plain F16 `.RN` and Pokopia's plain `.MERGE_C` keep their existing conversion.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922. Recorded differences fall from 1,880 to zero for BF16 and from 913 to zero for F16 `.RELU`, each across 2,048 halves. Plain F16 `.RN` has the same 44 NaN-bit differences (Orin `0x7fff`, Metal `0x7e00`). Six synthetic forms cover BF16 ties, combined BF16 `.RELU` and F16 truncation. All 64 exact boundary halves pass with default and relaxed Mac math, and a separate sweep of 2,072,574 halves also matches its exact reference. Eight shaders compile offline for iOS. Plain F16 `.RN` and both plain Pokopia `.MERGE_C` examples emit identical MSL on main and the fix.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2046](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2046). The new combinations and `.RZ` are checked against synthetic references. No game or iOS device ran.

### IDP integer dot products

- **Symptom:** an Ampere shader using `dp4a` or `dp2a` fails translation with `Ampere instruction 226 ... is not implemented` for the register form.
- **Cause:** the Ampere decoder had no IDP entry for opcode `0x026` in the low nine bits.
- **Fix:** register `InstName.Idp` and use the shared ALU operand reader. `AIdp` extracts the four bytes of each source for DP4A or extracts two halfwords of A and B's low or high pair of bytes for DP2A. Signedness is independent for each source. Integer multiplies and adds accumulate the products into C with 32-bit wraparound. Before optimization the lowering emits 16 IR operations for DP4A and eight for DP2A.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922. All five recorded register programs were refused on main and now match all 5,120 Orin lanes. Four synthetic shaders cover the B and C constant-buffer slots for DP4A and DP2A, with all 16 lanes matching using a nonzero Metal argument-buffer word at `c[3][5]`. Another 144 synthetic lanes cover independent signedness, DP2A low and high pairs, wraparound, immediate and uniform-register inputs. All 27 IDP shaders validate as SPIR-V and compile offline for iOS.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2052](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2052). Only register forms have recorded Orin outputs in this fixture. No affected game is documented. No games or iOS device ran.

### VABSDIFF, the absolute difference with an add

- **Symptom:** an Ampere shader using `sad.u32` fails translation with `Ampere instruction 214 ... is not implemented` for the register form.
- **Cause:** the GPU compiler emits `VABSDIFF.U32`, and the Ampere decoder had no entry for opcode `0x014` in the low nine bits.
- **Fix:** `AVabsdiff` uses the shared ALU operand reader and emits `max(a, b) - min(a, b) + c` with 32-bit wraparound. Bit 73 selects signed ordering. Bits 81–83 are the `Pu` predicate field. The recorded form uses `PT`, so other `Pu` values and unrecognized modifiers are rejected. The lowering emits four integer IR operations before optimization.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922. The previously refused unsigned register program now matches all 1,024 recorded lanes, including 416 wrapping additions. All 64 synthetic lanes match, including signed extrema, immediate and uniform-register sources, and 16 lanes using nonzero constants at `c[3][5]` in slots B and C. Separate probes confirm that a non-PT `Pu` and an unrecognized modifier fail translation. Nine supported shaders validate as SPIR-V and compile and link offline for iOS.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2053](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2053). Signed and non-register cases use synthetic references. No affected game is documented. No games or iOS device ran.

### A guarded load whose destination is its address

- **Symptom:** none in the log: everything drawn with an engine's scene data lands on the first instance's transform, or at nothing. In the MSL, a bounds-checked read has a constant index (`vp_s2._m0[0]`) just after a comparison of the register it should use.
- **Cause:** a uniform-form LDG (`0x981`) with a predicate is zero where the predicate is false, so the translator zeroed the destination first and then read the address. With `LDG.P R5, [R5 + UR4]` the address was already gone.
- **Fix:** `ALdg` reads the address into locals before anything is written.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): Unreal Engine 5.4's GPU scene reads, every one of them; the first step away from a black scene.

### A deferred write under a false guard

- **Symptom:** the scene goes white with a yellow fringe: a few objects hold the largest value their target can, and bloom spreads it over the picture. Clamping a shader's terms one by one finds a value that is a register's stale contents. Or characters lit from an environment cube turn bright white in certain places only, and the cube's mean is far above its value elsewhere.
- **Cause:** an instruction with a write barrier [writes through a shadow register](#ampere-shader-code-switch-2), which starts as the register's value and is copied back at the first instruction that waits on the barrier. A guarded one whose guard is false writes nothing on hardware, but the copy still ran, and overwrote whatever had been written meanwhile. Here: `EXP2 R17` under P4, `R17 = R29 * R29` under !P4, then the wait; under !P4, R17 got back a squared distance from long before, and the light's falloff with it.
- **Fix:** `EmitOpsAmpere` keeps the guard as it was at issue in a shadow register of its own and commits only where it held. A predicated last instruction of a block no longer branches straight to the next block when writes are pending, which skipped their commits. A later guarded write no longer discards an earlier pending one, which may still be the one that lands.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): the first light's falloff, 65,000 in R11G11B10 on leaves and eggs. [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) (GitHub issue 1100): DK and Pauline white between Hilltop's rocks. The terrain capture shader `7612419c02d496b9` picks its material with `@P0 IPA R8` then `@!P0 MOV R8` as a block's last instruction, and always got material 0, a placeholder with an emissive scale of 30, so the environment cube's red mean reached 53 against about 1.2 elsewhere. A hand patch of that one selection, on the old chain, brought it to 0.45 at the spot with DK normal. On chain 3dd02e581c0c the shader's MSL has the right selection; the spot itself was not revisited on that chain.

### Ampere forms with a handle in a register, and SURED's other operations

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction f63` (or `f6c`, `364`, `367`) `is not implemented`, or `SURED form 0310e000`, in the build.
- **Cause:** 0xf60 and 0x361 were known for TEX. The same pairs exist for the others: 0xf63 and 0xf6c are TLD4 and TXD through a uniform register at 40..46, 0x364 and 0x367 are TLD4 and TLD through a register, the first of the second source block, each otherwise laid out as its bound form. 0x364's meaning was inferred from these siblings, and its use agrees: Yoshi's single-layer water converts each of its four results as a device Z (`c4[66]`), which only a gather of a depth texture gives. SURED takes SUATOM's operation field (87..91): MAX, OR and MIN on 32 bits turn up.
- **Fix:** `ATex` maps them to the bound forms, `ASured` takes the operation. TMML has the same pair, 0xf69 and 0x36a (Kirby Air Riders' cube map LOD, 4,776 pixel shaders), with a binding of its own only when the handle is bound by its slot. SURED.MAX.U64 (33,550 uses, Nanite's visibility buffer) is now [a 64-bit texture atomic](#64-bit-image-max-and-min-suredmaxu64) on Apple GPU family 9; this game has not been rebuilt with it.
- **TXQ:** 0xf6f is TXQ (0xb6f) through a uniform register at 40..46, Ra the LOD (`ATxq`; [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): 12 shaders); the size query reads the pools like any handle no slot holds. Check: `GuardedWrites.test_size_query_through_a_uniform_register_translates`.
- **Found on:** [Kirby Air Riders](../../dm/games/040077201BE50000/README.md) (TMML); [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md). Its shaders' jump tables also ran past their code on two compute shaders (`ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, in `GetCode`): an address past the code is now no code, which ends the table as the decoder expected.

### Ampere BMSK and the read of a covered pixel

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 21b at … is not implemented` (`…0405721b`) or `Ampere instruction 925 at 0x0 is not implemented` (`040e0200000e4000 …7925`) in the build.
- **Cause:** 0x21b, beside SGXT's 0x21a, is BMSK: a mask of b bits from bit a. Star Fox's `58046f286de779e1` takes it from two bytes of a word, ANDs it with the value shifted by the same a and hands the result to an atomic, a bit field written into a word. 0x925 has no name in the tables at hand (the part of CuAssembler's sm_80 table read so far; Mesa's NAK encoder could not be fetched); what it reads comes from its two shaders, quad efficiency views. They rebuild its value at the quad's three other pixels through SHFL and FSWZADD and add the four: one indexes a 4-entry palette with the sum minus 1, so a shaded pixel's sum is at least 1, and the other draws 0.03125 + 0.15625 sum in red. So it is 1 for a covered pixel and 0 for a helper invocation.
- **Fix:** `ABmsk` follows PTX's `bmsk` definition, its `.W` at bit 75 as SGXT's (only the clamping form has been seen). `APixelCovered` loads a new `IoVariable.HelperInvocation`, Metal's `simd_is_helper_thread()`, as an integer; any other form of 0x925 stays refused (chain change). `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` covers both.
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md): 1 compute and 2 pixel shaders, none asked for in play so far, so neither instruction has run. Neither opcode is in the shaders of Donkey Kong Bananza, Splatoon Raiders, Mario Tennis Fever or Pokopia.
- **Form 3, the sample index:** `[RED] … 0x925 form 3 outside a covered pixel's read` for 154 of [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md)'s pixel shaders, at their first instruction. The register indexes the driver's sample position table (`LDC c[0][R + 0x620]`, a byte of x and y nibbles), which is what GLSLC's Maxwell code does with PIXLD.MY_INDEX (numbered 5 there), so form 3 is the index of the sample the fragment runs for: `IoVariable.SampleId`, Metal's `[[sample_id]]`. Check: `PixelCovered.test_form_3_reads_the_sample_index`.

### VOTEU writes the uniform predicate

- **Symptom:** no wrong picture seen so far. In the MSL, a shader with a 0x886 vote has `if (false)` and `false ? a : b` where a uniform predicate guards a value or a branch, and a lane guard on the same number reads `simd_all` or `simd_any` of its own predicate.
- **Cause:** the destination at bits 81..84 of 0x886 is the uniform predicate UPd: nvdisasm prints `@!P1 VOTEU.ANY UP2, P2` for `0x000000000104010000000000003f9886` (CuAssembler's sm_86 records), and NAK encodes `OpVote` with a uniform destination as 0x886 and a lane one as 0x806, with its source and guard always lane predicates. The translator wrote the lane predicate of that number for both. An unwritten predicate reads false, so a uniform guard on the vote never held, and the lane predicate of that number became the subgroup's vote. Across Yoshi and the Mysterious Book, Pokémon Pokopia and The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, 14,960 of the 17,889 votes that name a destination are next read as a uniform predicate, through a uniform guard, UPLOP3, USEL or PLOP3's uniform source (bit 67), and the 2,929 next read as a lane predicate all have an earlier lane writer. 0x806's next readers are all lane reads.
- **Fix:** `AVote` writes UPd for 0x886 (`dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py`, class `Vote`). No other instruction in CuAssembler's sm_86 tables has a uniform predicate destination without already being registered as uniform.
- **The same fault in a registered one:** UIMAD.WIDE (0x0a5, registered as uniform) wrote the carry out of its 64-bit addition (Pd at 81..84) to the lane predicate, while it read its carry in from the uniform one. `AImadWide` writes UPd for it now (class `ImadWideCarry`). No game met uses that carry: Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment and Ocarina of Time have 149 UIMAD.WIDE, all with PT there.
- **Reach:** every shader with such a vote changes: 7,560 translated of 9,231 in Yoshi (the rest stop at SURED, both ways), all 471 in Pokopia, 321 translated of 329 in Ocarina of Time; none in Mario Kart World, Fire Emblem: Fortune's Weave or Donkey Kong Bananza's 5,219 surveyed. Before, 1,815, 454 and 284 of them had a guard folded to a constant. In the shaders read, the vote is on a value that is one per draw, such as `c3[87].z != 11`, so the old code goes wrong only where that vote is true. Open: no played scene has shown such a vote. In Pokopia's first area the one changed pixel shader drawn, the full-screen `075fd7285c64b798`, has all 7 votes false and no mixed subgroup (a build that turns the frame red where one fires stayed untinted over a walk, and its control, forced on, was red all over), so the picture is unchanged there. In Yoshi's 1,124 keys asked in play, the one changed shader, `cbd80cb96ad38a61`, votes on a uniform buffer value and the uniform registers it guards are unused.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md), [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md).

### TXD through a uniform register kept no handle

- **Symptom:** `[RED] <key>: the translator deleted N bindless accesses` in the build ([Losses a shader build reports](#losses-a-shader-build-reports); `Failed to find handle source for bindless access` in its reflection's notes), for a shader with TXD through a uniform register (0xf6c). The MSL has no gradient sample, and what it read comes back 0.
- **Cause:** a bindless read takes its handle as source 0. `ATex` adds it in every other form, but TXD has a branch of its own, which left it out. BindlessElimination then took the first coordinate for the handle, found no source for it and deleted the read.
- **Fix:** the TXD branch adds the handle first, as the others do.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): in 14,079 of its 133,154 shaders, none of them among the 1,519 the playtests asked for, so no route has shown it in a picture. Found by [PR 1261](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1261).; [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md) (281 pixel shaders, the characters' fur shells among them)
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### Handles built under a guard the read's guard cannot be tied to

- **Symptom:** `Failed to find handle source for bindless access` in the build's translator notes, for a read whose handle is two words of the game's own uniform buffer, and what it read comes back 0. Or nothing in the log: the read binds `fp_t_cb3_<word>` or `fp_t_cb2_<word>`, a texture word with no sampler word, so its sampler is `samp_tex`'s guess, where the shader's other reads of that word bind both.
- **Cause:** the compiler builds a handle in a register under a guard (`R = word`; `R |= sampler word`) and reads through it under a guard of its own. The register's phi then merges the handle, its first word and what the register held before. `Utils.FindLastOperation` picks the phi's input only when both guards are the same operand in the same branch form, which misses `!UP0` with UP0 the vote of `P0` against a read under `!P0`, `P4` against a read under `P5 = P4 && !P1`, and `P6` against a read past `BRA.!P6`. `TryTableHandle` then took only c2 words, so a handle in another bank was deleted. In a register with no earlier value the first word's phi is (undefined, word): `FindLastOperation`'s rule that the one defined source of such a phi is its value was also applied to that phi inside the outer phi, so the first word came back as the whole handle.
- **Fix:** in the vendored translator. `TryTableHandle` also takes a texture word ORed with a sampler word of the same bank or a constant sampler from any constant buffer, including when only the OR's source is a phi and the handle is not. A word of a game's uniform buffer alone is no candidate, as it may be other data, so the first word and a float left in the register drop out without a look at the guards. A c2 word alone that an ORed candidate also takes is that handle on its way, not a second candidate, which also saves a read chosen at run time. `FindLastOperation` applies the undefined rule only to the phi it was asked about. Proving the guards equal instead would take a boolean prover over predicate logic and the vote's meaning. A chain change.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): of 128,113 translated shaders, 130 reads in 78 were deleted (the pool's water surface on Chapter 1's page) and 189 reads in 152 bound the texture word alone. SPIR-V of every shader against the translator before: those 230 change there; 364 of 22,072 in [DAVE THE DIVER](../../dm/games/010097F018538000/README.md) and 2,633 of 69,924 in [DRAGON QUEST I & II HD-2D Remake](../../dm/games/0100D7C01F254000/README.md), of which 319 and 1,780 bound the texture word alone and the others lose a second read; 16 of 13,980 in [OCTOPATH TRAVELER II](../../dm/games/0100A3501946E000/README.md), a second read. None of Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave's 16,888, nor of Blasphemous, NieR:Automata, Return of the Obra Dinn, Catherine: Full Body and FINAL FANTASY X/X-2. Only Yoshi's water was compared in a picture. FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE's 26 reads with [no c2 word](#bindless-reads-whose-handle-phi-also-carries-other-values) may be this form, not checked: its shaders are not split on disk.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (the three guard forms, an unguarded OR, and a c2 handle read once).

### Layer entry points with the point size passed by reference

- **Symptom:** `no member named 'gl_PointSize' in 'main0l_out'` and `does not compile` in the build, which still exits 0, then `[RED] <key>: its library builds only without its layer entry points` ([Losses a shader build reports](#losses-a-shader-build-reports)). A slice-writing geometry shader on it logs `has no layer entry points`.
- **Cause:** Metal builds no pipeline for a vertex function that writes both the layer and a point size, so the layer's entry points lose the output. `dmsh_build.py` removed it with a regex over `out.gl_PointSize = …;`, but Unreal Engine 5.4's bodies are functions of their own, called as `_88(out.gl_Position, out.gl_PointSize)`, which that left behind.
- **Fix:** `drop_point_size` removes the writes as before, and gives a vertex function that still passes the output by reference a local float instead (the same for `vs_layer` and `vs_layer_entry`).
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): 38 of the vertex shaders of the 1,519 its playtests asked for lacked layer entries. The first habitat's picture does not change, and it logged no `has no layer entry points` before or after. Found by [PR 1261](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1261).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_geometry.py PointSize`.

### Losses a shader build reports

- **Symptom:** in the build's log, `[RED] <key>: the translator deleted N bindless accesses (<type> N): their results read 0` or `[RED] <key>: its library builds only without its layer, integer output, dual source entry points`, and `[RED] lost without failing: …` with the counts per kind (the NSP script repeats that one line). In `regress.py`, `NEW-FAIL: N shaders with more … than in the baseline`.
- **Cause:** both leave a shader that builds, so the build's status said ok and it exited 0. BindlessElimination deletes an access whose handle it cannot trace and writes 0 to its results (`Failed to find handle source`, kept in the reflection's notes). `finish` in `dmsh_build.py` rebuilds a library that does not compile with its extra entry points without any of them. Nothing compared these between builds, which is how [14,079 of Yoshi's shaders](#txd-through-a-uniform-register-kept-no-handle) and [38 of its vertex shaders](#layer-entry-points-with-the-point-size-passed-by-reference) lost something unnoticed.
- **Fix:** the reflection holds both (`notes`, `entries_lost`), so a build that restores a shader from its cache reports it too. `dm/gfx/tools/shader_regressions.py` holds what counts as a loss (`losses`) and prints the lines (`report_losses`). `regress.py` compares each shader with the baseline (`loss_diff`): more accesses of a type or a new entry point lost in any shader is `NEW-FAIL`, however the totals move, and a shader new in the candidate starts from none. What the baseline has too is listed as known, and the game's note says what is known about it.
- **The build still exits 0:** the NSP script runs it for players, and one shader the game may never reach must not stop a pack. `regress.py` is the check for a change, so there is no stricter exit code. On a baseline from before `entries_lost` existed, every lost entry point counts as new.
- **Not counted yet:** the translator's other messages that lose something: `Shader instruction … is not implemented` and `Failed to find targets for BRX instruction`, which the build summary still counts among its translator lines.
- **Found on:** the 79 games with a library on the Mac (2026-11-05, those shaders rebuilt on the current chain): 1,984 deleted accesses in 964 shaders of 13 games, the most in [Ghost Trick](../../dm/games/010029B018432000/README.md) (1,030 in 616) and [Super Monkey Ball Banana Rumble](../../dm/games/010031F019294000/README.md) (490 in 245), and [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md) has 26 by its note. No shader built without its extra entry points when rebuilt (65 of Sparks of Hope's, which look as if they did, could not be: its package is not on the Mac): the 25 compiles that failed in the logs were all [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md)'s, before the point size fix. Each game's note lists its own. A listed loss is not proved harmless: none has been traced to a picture.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/tools/tests/test_shader_regressions.py Losses`, `python3 dm/tools/tests/test_regress.py Regress`.

### A guard band cull in every vertex shader (0x80d)

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 80d ... (000fc80003fe0000c43211110000780d) is not implemented` for every vertex shader of the game, so nothing draws.
- **Cause:** the compiler ends the position's computation with a test of its own. FSETP sets P0..P5 where x, y or z lies beyond +/- w times the system registers 30 and 31, which nothing else reads: a guard band. Two 0x80d follow, immediate 0xc4321111 with no predicate result, then immediate 0x80000000 with a result, and that predicate guards an EXIT before the shader writes its position and outputs. All 6,319 vertex shaders hold this once; no other game's do. What the instruction reads is not known: it is inferred from what surrounds it, and no other instruction reads its result.
- **Fix:** `AGuardCull` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`: the result is false, so no vertex leaves early. Every vertex writes its outputs and clipping does the rest, as for a shader without this; only the optimization is lost. A form with no predicate result at all (PT, as the first of the two) writes nothing and is skipped in any stage ([PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md)'s mesh and pixel shaders hold it). Any other form of 0x80d stops the shader.
- **Found on:** [Kirby Air Riders](../../dm/games/040077201BE50000/README.md).
- **Open:** the instruction's real meaning. A vertex output at `a[0x5c]`, written by 4,771 vertex shaders (`Attribute offset 0x5C is not valid`), is dropped; it has no known meaning either.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### Ampere instructions new in Pokémon Pokopia

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction <op> at 0x… is not implemented`, `F2FP.MERGE_C` or `F2I from a float that is not 32-bit` in the shader build; those shaders are left out and their draws and dispatches skipped.
- **Fix:**
  - F2FP.MERGE_C (bit 78): b converted into the low half, c's low half into the high one.
  - F2I from a half (source size 1): the half that bits 60..61 select.
  - ATOMS.POPC.INC (0xf8c): an add of 1 with no source register, also counted as using shared memory. The game's one encoding is accepted; any other stops the shader.
  - UP2UR (0x883): `URd = UP0 ? 1 : 0`. The game's one encoding (URZ, mask 1) is accepted; any other stops the shader.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### Ordered not-equal becomes unordered in MSL

- **Symptom:** an ordered not-equal comparison is true with a NaN operand, so a NaN guard lets it through. An inverted result is wrong too; NVIDIA's compiler can implement unordered equal by inverting ordered not-equal. No game symptom has been identified.
- **Cause:** the ISA translator emits the correct SPIR-V `OpFOrdNotEqual`, but SPIRV-Cross's MSL emitter maps it to `!=`, which is unordered in MSL. The pinned upstream emitter has a TODO at that case. Writing `(a < b) || (a > b)` is insufficient under Metal fast math: the compiler can fold it back to `!=`.
- **Fix:** `spirv_cross_bin/ordered-ne.patch`, applied by its pinned `build.sh`, makes the MSL emitter write `!isunordered(a, b) && a != b` and preserve both operands' expression dependencies. The ISA translator remains unchanged. Logical and integer not-equal and unordered floating-point not-equal keep their existing lowering. This fixes the shared converter used by NVN, Vulkan and OpenGL.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_spirv_ordered_ne.py`: synthetic scalar and two-, three- and four-component f32/f16 modules, assembled and validated as SPIR-V, compiled with the chain's Mac flags and checked by GPU readback; the iOS compiler checks the same modules offline. Ordered, unordered, inverted, integer and Boolean results cover signed zeros, finite values, infinities and signed quiet/signaling NaNs. The old converter fails every width and type; changing only its emitter passes. PR [#2042](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2042)'s five targeted Orin-fixture operations also pass outside the fixture's separate denormal allowance (M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922).
- **Found on:** synthetic SPIR-V and a Jetson Orin's recorded instruction results (PR #2042); no game symptom identified. The [Cuphead Mac route](../../dm/games/0100A5C00D162000/README.md#mac-routes) supplies regression coverage.

### Comparisons with .FTZ

- **Symptom:** single bright dots at thin far geometry (railings, track edges), changing frame to frame as the camera moves, already in the lit picture before anti-aliasing. Most of them come from another cause (below).
- **Cause:** a float comparison has an .FTZ flag (Ampere: FSET and FSETP bit 80, after the comparison at 76..80; Maxwell: FSETP bit 47, FSET bit 55, FCMP bit 47): on NVIDIA a denormal operand compares as zero. The translator decoded it and dropped it, and Apple's GPUs keep fp32 denormals. A guard like `sum != 0` before `1 / sum` then lets a denormal sum through: its reciprocal overflows, the products give NaN, and a `min(x, 1.0)` (`precise::`, see [min, max and clamp with a NaN operand](#min-max-and-clamp-with-a-nan-operand)) turns it into 1.0, a fully lit pixel.
- **Fix:** a comparison flushes each operand where the flag is set: `|x| < FLT_MIN ? 0 : x` (`FlushDenormal`, `InstEmitAluHelper.cs`, from `AFset` / `AFsetp` and from Maxwell's `EmitFset`, `EmitFsetp` and `EmitFcmp`, which flushes only the source it compares). An immediate or RZ needs no code.
- **Limit:** only the comparisons. The compilers set .FTZ on nearly every float instruction of every shader, so it is in effect a mode of the whole shader, and Metal has none (`MTLCompileOptions` has math modes, no denormal control). Flushing the arithmetic would be about 3.3 million more sites in Mario Kart World, so its FADD, FMUL and FFMA keep denormals. Four denormal weights can still add up to a normal sum, which NVIDIA sees as zero; it stays finite, so it makes no NaN.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md): its deferred lighting `635061c90d75a8d1` normalises four depth-weighted half-resolution samples by `1 / sum` only where `FSETP.NEU.FTZ` says the sum is not zero. No Switch 1 game has shown the symptom; Maxwell's comparisons follow for the same semantics (the flag is on all of them in the 8 Switch 1 games counted).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`CompareFtz`, on the game's encoding) and `test_dmsh_maxwell_ftz.py`.

### Compute threads that return before a barrier

- **Symptom:** none on the Mac. Suspected as a cause of GPU hangs (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang`) in compute, task and mesh shaders that return before a `threadgroup_barrier`.
- **Cause (checked, not a bug):** on NVIDIA's GPUs a thread that has exited counts as arrived at a barrier, and Switch compute shaders rely on it (`if (lid * 4 >= count) return;`, then a barrier). Metal's specification wants every thread of the threadgroup at each barrier, and the translator keeps the return in the MSL. On an Apple M6 a returned thread counts as arrived too. Seven shapes all complete with every remaining thread's value right: whole simdgroups returning, all but the last simdgroup returning, half the lanes of every simdgroup, returns between two barriers, a barrier loop after the returns, threads leaving a barrier loop at different iterations, half the lanes before a `simdgroup_barrier`. Each ran with 4,096 threadgroups of 1,024 threads, 280 dispatches in all with no hang.
- **Fix:** none: the translator keeps the return. A pass that kept a returned thread arriving at every later barrier (`BarrierExits.cs`, [PR #1774](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1774)) was not taken. It fixes nothing measured: PRAGMATA's hang went away only together with a cap on every shader loop. That hang was its cloth solver's loop, which never ends when the iteration count it reads is 0, and the cap hid it.
- **Limit:** measured on an M6 only. M1 to M5 have not run it. A failure of `t_barrier_exit` on one of them would bring the pass back for those GPUs.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA (no profile; 156 of its compute shaders return before a barrier) and Orbitals ([PR #1783](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1783)), as PR #1774 reports; neither game's files were run here. Not [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md)'s GPU hang: none of its 520 task and mesh shaders has a barrier in its body. Only 12 of its compute kernels return before a barrier, none of them in the pass that hangs.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> t_barrier_exit && dm/gfx/tests/t_barrier_exit`.

### MEMBAR is a fence, not a barrier

- **Symptom:** a GPU hang (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang`, `[RED] the host has waited 10 s for GPU work`) in a compute shader whose threads poll a flag in a loop with no BAR.SYNC: a persistent work queue.
- **Cause:** MEMBAR (and the ERRBAR after it) orders memory and does not wait for other threads. The translator emits it as SPIR-V's `OpMemoryBarrier`, which SPIRV-Cross writes as `threadgroup_barrier(mem_flags::mem_device | mem_flags::mem_threadgroup | mem_flags::mem_texture)`: an execution barrier every thread of the threadgroup must reach. A thread that leaves the polling loop early never reaches it again, and the threads still in the loop wait for it for ever.
- **Fix:** SPIRV-Cross writes the chain's MSL at version 3.2 (`dmsh_chain.CROSS_MSL`), where `OpMemoryBarrier` becomes the fence it is, `atomic_thread_fence(…, memory_order_seq_cst, thread_scope_threadgroup)` for MEMBAR.CTA and `thread_scope_device` for MEMBAR.GL / .SYS and Ampere's MEMBAR, with no execution barrier. BAR.SYNC's `threadgroup_barrier(mem_flags::mem_threadgroup)` stays a barrier. First fixed by a text rewrite of that line (`membar_fence`, ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up, [PR #1407](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1407), which NX 18 took with f8f42ae8), which made every MEMBAR a device fence and lost the BAR.SYNC after one (next entry).
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): its culling queue `12a52b683eec95c1` polls a done flag with `MEMBAR.SC.GPU; ERRBAR` at the end of the loop body.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_membar_fence.py` (with `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1` the shaders also compile for the Mac and the iPhone; with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE` a Maxwell MEMBAR and BAR.SYNC go through the translator).

### Compute loops that outlive the process

- **Symptom:** the game exits but compute work keeps the Mac GPU busy, `kill -9` does not reclaim it, and WindowServer can stop until a reboot. PRAGMATA's reporters needed seven reboots, including after a game abort on main `9ccbd6b1` ([issue #2664](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2664)). Kirby Air Riders' particle submission hung twice in two races; the loop guard's author reports none in sixteen later sessions, whose builds also carried other changes ([PR #2254](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2254)).
- **Cause:** a loop whose exit condition never arrives in the translated execution. PRAGMATA's persistent queue kernels poll shared counters; why those counters disagree is **open**, as is Kirby's failing loop/input. Existing memory and fence fixes may affect the trigger, so a reproduction on current main is still needed. The platform failure is that committed GPU work is not reliably reclaimed at process exit. A CPU-raised abort was retracted: only 2 of 24 lone-kernel trials observed the store; the 876 ms bounded trial is evidence against that CPU approach, not a timing of the GPU approach below.
- **Fix:** declared host/chain containment, **not the root fix**. `dmsh_rewrite.compute_loops`, based on PRs #2254 and [#2709](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2709), adds a budget of 2^18 loop-condition checks per function invocation to compute shaders and their reachable helpers. Nested and consecutive non-barrier loops spend the same function budget. Fragment, vertex, geometry, task and mesh shaders are unchanged. The first capped thread raises a **GPU-written** abort word shared by the submission; other non-barrier loops check it every 1,024 checks. Helpers receive the state through their callers. A loop containing an execution barrier, directly or through helpers, gets a separate counter reset on each entry and never reads the abort word. Unequal work in earlier or nested non-barrier loops therefore cannot consume its barrier budget, and an asynchronous abort cannot split its threads. Every submission gets a fresh abort word.
- **Diagnostics:** each kernel in a submission has its own hit record, separate from the abort word. On completion the host reads it before recycling the arena and logs `[RED] loop cap hit: submission N, kernel <hash>, loop site N`, once per loaded kernel. Only reaching the kernel's own budget records a hit; observing another kernel's abort does not. The site indexes `loop_sites` in `<hash>_b.json`, with its function, source line before containment and barrier status; the emitted MSL also marks each site. If multiple sites hit, the record names the lowest numbered one. This identifies capped work, not proof of the original runaway's cause.
- **Limits:** this changes the result of capped or canceled work, including persistent state used in later frames. A legitimate long function can exhaust the budget: a 512 × 512 nested count produces 261,632 increments instead of 262,144 and reports its cap hit; a 256 × 256 count completes unchanged. No absence of hits on a smoke route proves all workloads fit. Helpers have independent budgets; barrier loops can still run to the full budget, and each queued submission must raise its own abort. Large grids, non-compute work and time spent before the first cap are not bounded in milliseconds. This is safer reproduction, not a guarantee against another reboot. #2664 stays open for the bad loop/input and its loosest correct binding.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 -m unittest dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_compute_loops.py`. Bounded GPU inputs exercise actual host dispatches and completion readback, a capped helper through a loop-free wrapper, propagation to later ordered dispatches, attribution without blaming canceled kernels, helper barriers following unequal per-thread work, once-per-kernel logging, next-submission isolation and valid/exhausted cumulative budgets in both encoding modes. Original inputs remain bounded if containment is broken. Checked on an Apple M6 with Metal compiler 32023.922; the authors' M4 Pro synthetic GPU measurements at a smaller 2^14 test cap were 59.8 → 8.6 ms for eight dispatches and 809.3 → 118.8 ms for one large dispatch, compared with the cap alone. Isolated containment overhead is unmeasured.
- **Validation:** [Fast Fusion's race](../../dm/games/040041E0212AE000/README.md#validation) and Nintendo Switch Sports Resort's title route (upstream's profile `04008E001F098000`, not in NX 18) exercise guarded kernels. Static inspection accounts for every loop in both rebuilt compute libraries, including helpers. Such checks qualify those workloads, not every legitimate loop or a maximum completion time. The game comparisons used chain `968f7d1ea898` against `19d159c0b938`, before the landing rebase onto current main. The rebased chain `7d5d6482def7` passes all nine containment tests, including the GPU host fixtures in both encoding modes, and 63 documentation/chain/reflection checks. An additional game comparison after rebase was not run: an interactive PRAGMATA root-cause investigation held the shared GPU measurement lock.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md) and [Kirby Air Riders](../../dm/games/040077201BE50000/README.md). Current-main trigger and gameplay retests on those titles remain open.
- **NX 18:** ported with the M4 chain batch (upstream `bb574c70f`). Every compute shader with a loop is guarded on the iPhone too, where the GPU's own watchdog already ends a hung command buffer; each guarded check is a counter increment and compare, every 1,024th an atomic load. Its cost on the iPhone (GPU time and power) is not measured yet; check it in the post-rebuild battery runs of the compute-heavy games (Mario Kart World, Ocarina of Time).

### A BAR.SYNC right after a MEMBAR lost

- **Symptom:** a GPU hang on the iPhone (`Caused GPU Hang Error`, `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang`, then `FATAL GPU access revoked`) in a scene that ran before the `membar_fence` rewrite (f8f42ae8, upstream ccd834e0); the failed command buffer lists a compute shader with shared memory. Shared memory read before the other threads of the group wrote it can also give wrong values without a hang.
- **Cause:** below MSL 3.2 SPIRV-Cross writes `OpMemoryBarrier` as a `threadgroup_barrier` on every memory, an execution barrier, and so leaves out an `OpControlBarrier` that directly follows one as redundant (`spirv_msl.cpp`, `OpControlBarrier`). The translator emits MEMBAR then BAR.SYNC exactly so: `MEMBAR.CTA; BAR.SYNC` is how NVIDIA's compiler writes GLSL's `memoryBarrierShared(); barrier();`. The `membar_fence` rewrite then made that one line a fence (two such lines in a row one fence), so the group had no barrier left: its simdgroups raced on shared memory. Both MEMBAR scopes printed the same line, so MEMBAR.CTA also became a device-scope fence, about 6 times the cost of a threadgroup-scope one in a kernel with one per iteration on upstream's M6 (1.9 ms against 0.25 to 0.3 ms).
- **Fix:** MSL 3.2 for every shader of the chain (previous entry; upstream 6dad34067ce): SPIRV-Cross writes each barrier and each fence with its own scope, and `membar_fence` is gone. The per-shader MSL 3.1 for image atomics (`dmsh_atomic.cross_args`) went with it. NX 18's `tg_fence` (the simdgroup-scope fence after each threadgroup store) is unchanged; `tg_spill` turns a spilled kernel's 3.2 fence and barrier to order its device slice as it did the old ones. 3.2 changes images too: SPIRV-Cross writes an image the translator decorated Coherent as a device-coherent texture (`memory_coherence_device`), and gives one the shader only reads `access::read` where 2.3 gave it no access qualifier (sample). Only Maxwell code sets that decoration, on an image whose last SULD or SUST in the shader has the .CG cache operation (`InstEmitSurface`; Ampere's surface loads and stores take .CA and .WB). Such a source compiles for the Mac and the iPhone, but no game here has one yet (below), so how that texture path runs on the iPhone, in frame time and power, has not been measured. `dmsh_atomic.wide_atomics` reads that declaration too: a 64-bit atomic image that a .CG load also reads still fails the shader, rather than keeping its two 32-bit atomics on the rg32ui image.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md) upstream, on an iPhone 15 Pro (A17 Pro), compute shader `b0a3586f7a5ead00` (64 threads, 512 bytes of shared memory) in the failed command buffer ([issue #2786](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2786)); not run here. Measured on NX 18's own shaders before the change (the chain at 2.3 with `membar_fence` against 3.2, every source each build compiles): of [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md)'s 11,560 shaders and [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md)'s 42,848, 14 compute kernels of 64 to 512 threads with 2 to 16 KB of shared memory (6 of Mario Kart World's `DrivePassShader`, 8 of agl's technique kernels in Ocarina of Time) had lost both of their BAR.SYNCs and get them back; 11 more change only by the comment the rewrite added; every other source of those two games is the same byte for byte, and the reflection too. No MEMBAR changes scope there: the translator writes every Ampere MEMBAR at device scope, whatever its own scope (`AMembar`), so only Maxwell's MEMBAR.CTA becomes a threadgroup-scope fence. On Maxwell, the same comparison over 88,748 shaders (139,516 sources): every shader of [Super Mario RPG](../../dm/games/0100BC0018138000/README.md) (82,032), [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md) (5,317) and [NieR:Automata](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md) (1,225), and the compute and geometry shaders of [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) (44), [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) (101) and [Mario Kart 8 Deluxe](../../dm/games/0100152000022000/README.md) (29). Not one source or reflection changes: none has a MEMBAR, and none a Coherent image (their few written images are stored without .CG). So neither a MEMBAR.CTA at threadgroup scope nor a device-coherent texture has been seen in a game's shader yet.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_membar_fence.py` (`test_maxwell_membar_then_bar_sync`: a fence and a barrier for each pair); `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_tg_fence.py` (`test_a_spilled_kernel_keeps_membar_fence_and_barrier`); `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_coherent_image.py` (Coherent images at 3.2 and at 2.3, through the translator, compiled with both lines; `test_a_64_bit_atomic_image_read_with_cg_still_fails`).

### Barrier moves around warp shuffles

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction f55 at 0x… (000fe20000000000000000001a0f7f55) is not implemented` in the shader build; those shaders are left out.
- **Cause:** `0xf55` and `0xf56` (BMOV) save convergence barrier B15 into R26 before a block of warp shuffles (SHFL) and restore it after. The translator had no entry for either.
- **Fix:** both are no-ops, as the barriers they save are (BSSY, BSYNC, BREAK, WARPSYNC): per thread they do nothing. In the shaders found, no other instruction reads R26 after them, and the register beside the barrier field (R15) is read live by an FMUL, so neither writes a general register (`InstEmitAmpere.cs`).
- **Found on:** [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment](../../dm/games/04003F40232DE000/README.md#shaders): pixel shaders `02e49c189b769074` and `49241cf469c76830`, 16 pairs each (chain 95ba69b5403e).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`BarrierMoves`: the game's encodings leave the translated shader unchanged; it fails on the translator before).

### LDC with the bank in its address register (`.IS`)

- **Symptom:** `Ampere b82 at 0x…: LDC with a bindless bank or an address mode (…00008a00…7b82)` in the shader build; those shaders are left out.
- **Cause:** LDC's address mode 2 (`.IS`, bits 78..79; 3 is `.ISL`) takes the bank as the instruction's plus Ra's upper 16 bits, and the offset from Ra's lower 16 bits plus the instruction's, as Maxwell's LDC does. The compiler uses it for an indexed read of c1 with the bank folded into the address (`(index * stride) + 0x10000`). The Ampere path refused the mode, and the shared load picks a run-time bank only among the banks the accessor reports, none here, so even the Maxwell path reads 0 for it.
- **Fix:** `ALdc` lowers `.IS` / `.ISL` to that bank and offset, and the load selects among the banks the shader can reach: its own constant segment c1, the instruction's bank and every bank its other constant reads name, but not c0 (`ARuntimeBanks`, from a scan of the decoded program; `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`). The host fills the driver segment only at offsets known when the shader is built and does not load a shader that indexes it at run time (`dmsh_reflect.py` `unfillable`): with c0 among the candidates, the pixel shader below, which reads c0 elsewhere, translated but was not loaded. Maxwell shaders are unchanged. A run-time bank 0 in a compute shader is not remapped as a constant one is (`AMapC0`).
- **Found on:** [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment](../../dm/games/04003F40232DE000/README.md#shaders): vertex shader `cc679c7b4d3e703c` reads its quad corners (−1,1), (1,1), (0,0), (−1,−1), (1,−1) from c1 by vertex index % 5, and pixel shader `059a0a910ff87a26` reads c1 from 0x60 (chain 316fb82e2f22). The vertex shader is also [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md#shaders)'s.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`LdcIndexedBank`; it fails on the translator before).

### LDC's 16-bit immediate: signed beside a register, the address alone

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_c15: 536839244 bytes read at fixed offsets` at load, and the draw is skipped; the shader's MSL reads `fp_c15._m0[33552452]`, a slot the game binds with a few words.
- **Cause:** Maxwell's LDC (`c[bank][Ra + imm]`, the immediate at bits 20..35) took the immediate as signed in every form (`InstEmitMemory.Ldc`, `Imm16ToSInt`). Added to a register that is right, and the sum is 32 bits: nvdisasm prints `0xef94003fffc7050b` as `LDC R11, c[0x3][R5+-0x4]`; Xenoblade Chronicles 2's pixel shaders read c6 at R − 8 to R − 128 (858 LDCs in 76 shaders); and to reach past 32 KiB the compiler adds 0x10000 to the register first, which a 16-bit wrap would make pointless (Yoshi and the Mysterious Book's pixel shader `461579dbf57658b4`: `IADD3 R40, R26, 0x10000` and `LDC.64 R24, c[6][R40 + 0xaa88]`, 438 shaders there and 290 in Orbitals, all on Ampere). Alone (Ra = RZ) the immediate is the address as it is: the compiler reads c[14][0x8448] with `LDC.U16 R49, c[14][0x8448]` beside a plain read of c14[0x844C]. Sign-extended, that read's byte address was 0xFFFF8448 and its word 0x3FFFE112, whose high bits ran into the operand's 5-bit slot field (`Operand.PackCbufInfo`): a read of c14 became c15 at word 33,552,452, which stretched c15's fixed extent (`NoteUniformRead`) to 512 MB.
- **Fix:** a lone immediate (Ra = RZ) is unsigned; added to a register it stays signed (`InstEmitMemory.Ldc`). Ampere's fixed forms, ULDC (0xab9) and LDC with RZ (0xb82, mode 0), take the immediate unsigned the same way (`InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`); no Switch 2 shader in the local builds has one at 0x8000 or above. A constant word past the 64 KiB bank reads 0, as a read past the size the game bound does, instead of being packed into the operand (`ConstantFolding`, the Load case), and `PackCbufInfo` throws for an offset or slot that does not fit rather than change the slot. Reading every immediate unsigned (the first proposal) moves XC2's reads 64 KiB away; wrapping every `Ra + imm` at 64 KiB agrees on all the shaders above but is not what the compiler assumes, and would put an AND in every indexed constant load whose `(Ra + imm) >> 2` shape `BindlessToArray` and the uniform handle matchers read.
- **Found on:** Mortal Kombat 11 (`0100F2200C984000`, v1703936, draft profile in [seedds/nxnative#2799](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2799); diagnosed in [seedds/nxnative#2800](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2800)): 27 rejected shader loads, 11 distinct keys, each with one `LDC.U16 c[14][0x8448]`. The signed form beside a register: [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (pixel shader `09030ba6b1d1283e`, `LDC.64 R4, c[6][R59 - 0x40]`), which the unsigned reading proposed first would have moved 64 KiB away. Corpus: the shader packages of 13 Switch 1 and 9 Switch 2 builds (chain 53cf95b181aa).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ldc_offset.py` (the games' own encodings; 4 of 5 fail on the translator before, the signed register cases pass on both).

### IMAD.HI adds a register pair, and IMAD.X its carry

- **Symptom:** 64-bit arithmetic in a shader gives wild results: the 64-bit division behind an exposure average returned 893011 for 218, so the exposure stayed at the end of its range. With only the pair fixed it still gave 2 for 340 / 1.
- **Cause:** IMAD.HI is the high word of `a * b + {c + 1:c}`, the same 64-bit addition as IMAD.WIDE, with the carry out on the predicate at 81..84. The decoder took the high word of `a * b` and added `c` alone, losing the upper word of the pair and the low word's carry. The compiler's 64-bit multiply chains the two: `R9:R8 = R6 * R13 + {R9:R8}`, then `R8 = high(R7 * R11 + {R9:R8})`. Separately, all three IMAD forms have .X (bit 74, the carry predicate at 87..90), which the decoder dropped; as with IADD3.X, a negated addend is then its bitwise not.
- **Fix:** `AImadHi` and `AImadCarryIn` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md): the Lighting compute shaders' division, run line by line from its translated MSL on 208 known inputs, with dividends up to 2^44: 26 wrong with only the pair fixed, none with both. Other Switch 2 games share the decoder and have not been rebuilt with this.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### NVN2 compute reads its workgroup counts at c0 + 4

- **Symptom:** a scene lit by many small lights comes out too dark, with its clustered light lists empty for most clusters.
- **Cause:** a light culling shader computes its cluster as `(z * c0[8] + y) * c0[4] + x`. NVN2 compute keeps the dispatch's workgroup counts at the start of its driver segment, below the per-shader layout at 0x10, and the host wrote them only at c0 + 0x200, Maxwell's place. At zero, every cluster of a column wrote the same entry.
- **Fix:** with a Switch 2 `sdk` (`dm_sdk_ounce`), `encode_args_` also writes them at `DMSH_C0_DISPATCH_NVN2`, and an indirect dispatch copies its GPU-written counts there too. x and y are read by the shader; z at c0 + 12 follows their order and has not been seen read.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md): `35d12d6f98fa05e2`, read from its translated MSL. The picture's change has not been measured on its own.

### Texture handles of SDK 22's Ampere code

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 361` (or `f60`, `370`, `38d` and others) refused, or hundreds of `Failed to find handle source for bindless access of type "textureCube"` when the handle was taken from the wrong register: units drawn white, the rest of the scene right.
- **Cause:** the bindless form of a texture instruction is the bound opcode + 1 with 0x300: 0xb60 becomes 0x361 and 0xb6f becomes 0x370. 0x361 takes its handle first in the second source block (bit 59), 0xf60 in a uniform register at 40..46, and 0x370 (TXQ) in Ra with the level in Ra + 1. A census of all the game's instructions gave the position.
- **Fix:** `ATex` and `ATxq` in `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs`. The same bring-up adds, from their encodings in the game: 0x301 BREV and 0x2be its uniform form, 0x311 F2I.U64, 0x312 I2F of a 64-bit pair, 0x38c ATOMS, 0x38d ATOMS.CAS (Rd is 1 when it swapped, not the old value: the game's only use is a retry loop until Rd == 1), 0x992 MEMBAR, 0x9ab ERRBAR, and the carry out of IMAD.WIDE.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md): 902 shaders had the wrong register at first; all 16,888 translate now. [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md) lists 0x2be, 0x301 and 0x38c among its refused instructions; it has not been rebuilt with this.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### NVN2 compute's separate handles in c[0]

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding cp_t_cb196610_10280074Smplr has no resolved slot number` at load, then `the dispatched program has no compute shader`; the shader's image has no slot either (`cp_i_tcb_AA_`).
- **Cause:** after the uniform and storage entries, NVN2 compute keeps four runs of 8-byte handles in `c[0]`: textures (control +0xb3), separate textures (+0xb4), separate samplers (+0xb5) and images (+0xb6). `AMapC0` knew textures and images, so everything past the textures landed in the image table and beyond. Star Fox's `1002069a331ceded` shows the order: 13 uniform entries, 4 textures, 32 separate textures, 13 separate samplers and 5 images make its 0x290 bytes (+0xba) exactly, and its handles fall on separate textures 22, 24 and 31, samplers 11 and 12 and image 4.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` passes the two counts (job `nvn2_cs`) and `AMapC0` maps them to NVN's fixed tables, separate texture r to `c[2]` 0x168 + 8 r and separate sampler r to 0x568 + 8 r (chain change). A shader with neither maps as before. `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` covers the image past both.
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md): one compute shader, dispatched in the prologue. Any NVN2 compute shader with separate handles reads them this way.

### NVN2 compute's second push block holds buffer entries

- **Symptom:** the GPU never finishes the first 3D frame: `[RED] the host has waited 10 s for GPU work`, with persistent compute shaders (a loop on a work counter, `threadgroup_barrier` inside) among the failed command buffer's shaders.
- **Cause:** `nvnCommandBufferPushComputeConstants(cmd, block, offset, size, data)` writes block 0 or block 1. NVN2 compute's `c[0]` lays out, after its 0x10-byte header, push block 0 (control +0xa9 words, rounded to 16 bytes), then push block 1 (+0xaa words), then the uniform entries (+0xb1), the storage entries and the handle tables of [the entry above](#nvn2-computes-separate-handles-in-c0) (the SDK's accessor at `sdk+0x5e6f20` reads byte +0xa9 + block). PRAGMATA pushes 16-byte `{address, size}` entries, 1 to 3 of them, into block 1 before its dispatches, and its shaders read buffers through those addresses (`ubo_ptr`-style global loads). The host kept one block, so block 1 overwrote block 0, and the translator placed block 1 after the uniform entries: the persistent shaders spun on a counter in the wrong buffer.
- **Fix:** the host keeps `DMSH_PUSH_BLOCKS` (2) blocks, block b at `b * DMSH_PUSH_SIZE` of `c[24]` (`push_const` in `hle_nvn.c`; a block past them is a red line). The translator maps `c[0]` reads in push block 1's range to `c[24] + 0x100` (`AMapC0`, `nvn2_cs` field 7 from +0xaa), and `GlobalToStorage` takes `c[24]` as an address bank like `c[0]`. A storage buffer whose address the game pushed binds through the bytes the replay holds (`sbuf_push`, `NVN_RB_SBUFQ`). Chain change.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): with it, the hang at frame ~1,300 is gone.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_nvn2_buffers.py`.

### NVN2 graphics stages' buffer banks (c[25] to c[29]) are push blocks

- **Symptom:** `Ampere … bank 25` (to 29) refused, or `stage N cbN has no uniform buffer bound` for graphics shaders that read buffers through an address. Then, with those banks taken for the uniform slots: `[RED] graphics push constants into block 5 (0 + 0x10): the host keeps 2, dropped`, `sbufp reg3 dummy` (to reg5) for a mesh shader in the pass dump, and everything a visibility buffer draws comes out black while the rest of the frame is lit.
- **Cause:** NVN2 graphics stage i (vertex 0, hull or mesh 1, domain 2, geometry 3, pixel 4) reads buffer entries from a bank of its own, `c[25 + i]`: 16-byte `{address, size}` entries, or 8-byte handles. They are not the uniform slots the game binds. They are what the game pushes with `nvnCommandBufferPushGraphicsConstants` into push block 1 + i, as compute reads its own entries from push block 1 (block 0 holds the constants). The translator mapped the banks onto the uniform slots, and the host kept two push blocks and dropped the rest. PRAGMATA pushes its mesh stage's six entries into block 2 and its pixel stage's five into block 5 before its visibility and material passes. Its mesh shader `5aab38bb5ba67ef7` binds uniform slots 0 to 2 only and read its rows 3 to 5 (the visible-cluster tables) from unbound slots, so the primitive IDs it writes into the visibility buffer were garbage. The material depth pass `93590d7bd6125d1c` took its cluster table from uniform slot 3, a 16-byte buffer, and every raw load it made missed. No material strip passed its depth test, and the G-buffer stayed black.
- **Fix:** a chain change, general to NVN2.
  - **Translator:** `InstEmitAmpere.cs` `AStageBlock` maps the stage's bank to its block of `c[24]`, at `(1 + i) * 0x100` (`AMapC0`, `AIndexedBank`). The addresses read there become `sbuf_push` bindings, which the host reads from its push blocks (`NVN_RB_SBUFQ`, handles decoded).
  - **ABI:** `DMSH_PUSH_BLOCKS` is 6.
  - **Host:** `push_const` keeps graphics blocks 0 to 5, and compute's 0 and 1.
  - **Removed:** the per-stage fill of `c[0] + 0x210` for graphics stages; only compute reads that table.
  - **Check:** `Nvn2Buffers.test_a_graphics_stage_bank_is_its_push_block` fails on the old translator.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): `c[25]` in 9,944 vertex shaders, `c[26]` in 538 mesh and 3 hull shaders, `c[27]` in 37 domain shaders, `c[28]` in 1 geometry shader, `c[29]` in 12,195 pixel shaders. In one intro frame it pushes block 1 157 times, block 2 51 times and block 5 92 times. A host-only test (each stage's rows filled from its block) brought back the ship's engines and the tower's lights at the moon base.

### Uniform words read through a buffer address (cx)

- **Symptom:** `Ampere … is not implemented` for ALU forms, ULDC (0xab9) or LDC (0x582) with bit 91 set.
- **Cause:** bit 91 makes a constant operand bindless: `cx[UR][offset]` reads a word of the buffer whose 64-bit GPU address is in the uniform register pair UR, UR + 1 (ALU forms: UR at bits 32–37, offset at 38–54; ULDC: UR at 24–29; 0x582 LDC adds Rb at 64–71). The address is a plain GPU address, not a handle: decoding it as one read through wrong pools and page-faulted the GPU.
- **Fix:** `ABindlessConstant` reads it as a global load at UR + offset, through the memory pool table the shader walks for raw global memory (`gmem_map`) (`AUldc`, `ALdcBindless`, `AGet`). Chain change.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): RE Engine's materials read their constants this way; its 0x36d (TXD with the handle leading the first block) and the plain form of [0x80d](#a-guard-band-cull-in-every-vertex-shader-0x80d) came up in the same shaders.

### A handle pair ORed sampler word first

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t_cb2_170 has no resolved slot number` at load (the draw is skipped), the build summary counting the binding under *slot was not decoded*; or, once the read's other inputs are known, `Failed to find handle source for bindless access of type "texture2D"` and the read answers 0. `DMSH_BINDLESS_TRACE=1` on a `dmsh_xlate` job prints what a refused handle is made of.
- **Cause:** the compiler ORs the separate texture word and the separate sampler word of c[2] in either order. The bindless elimination took the OR's first operand as the texture: with the sampler word first (`c[2][0x5c0] | c[2][0x248]`, word 0x170 of the separate sampler table at 0x568), the sampler's word was named as the texture, a slot the host cannot decode.
