Through a phi (the OR predicated, the bare sampler word on the other path) the bare word was a candidate too, so the read was refused.
- **Fix:** `BindlessElimination.cs` orders a pair of c[2] words by the table they lie in: a word of the separate sampler table (`dmsh_abi.h` `DMSH_C2_SAMP`, 128 slots) is the sampler, the other the texture, in the plain OR and among a phi's candidates; a bare sampler word is no texture candidate (chain change).
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md) (62 pixel shaders, 5 of them loaded at the prologue cinematic).

### Ampere HMMA through simdgroup_matrix

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's frame rate swings between 60 and 30 fps (Donkey Kong Bananza's own controller, `main+0x204a18`, drops to 30 after two frames of 20 ms from their start to the GPU's end, and comes back after six), and the GPU profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`) shows one compute dispatch of 2-6.5 ms a frame: agl's upscaler network (`agl_ndsr.pck`, shader `7629d857a0a93833`).
- **Cause:** its 144 HMMA.16816 a thread (F16 sources and accumulator) were emulated lane by lane: 32 subgroup shuffles and 64 FMAs a lane for each, 4,922 shuffles in the shader. Without its shuffles (each returning its own value; a wrong picture, measured only) the dispatch took 0.24-0.40 ms. On this GPU shuffles cost more than threadgroup memory: one HMMA a SIMD-group took 5.42 ns emulated, 1.08 ns through threadgroup memory and `simdgroup_matrix`, 0.65 ns with the tiles loaded straight from where LDSM reads them, and 2.73 ns moving the register fragments into `simdgroup_matrix` elements with shuffles (Apple's 8×8 layout gives each lane a pair of columns of one row too, but in another order). Metal's matrix types have no SPIR-V form spirv-cross converts (its cooperative matrix support is GLSL only).
- **Fix:** in a compute shader, HMMA.16816 with an F16 accumulator becomes `MatrixMultiplyAdd` (`AHmma`), then a call of the helper `dmsh_hmma16816_f16` (`TensorPass`, `HelperFunctionManager.GenerateHmma16816F16`), named in the SPIR-V. Each SIMD-group has 256 words of a shared memory of its own (`hmma_scratch`, 1 KB a SIMD-group): the lanes store their fragment words as they are, A row-major, B transposed and C row-major, and after a SIMD-group barrier (the new IR instruction `SubgroupBarrier`, Metal `simdgroup_barrier`) the helper's body computes D from them lane by lane. `dmsh_build.py` (`hmma_msl`) replaces that body in the Metal source with `simdgroup_load` (B with its transpose flag), four `simdgroup_multiply_accumulate` and a `simdgroup_store` into the same block; the name, the arguments and the block are the contract, and the translator's body stays correct where the rewrite does not apply. Other HMMA forms (m16n8k8, an F32 accumulator) keep the per-lane emulation. A chain change.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_hmma.py`: the rewrite of the helper as spirv-cross writes it, and on the Mac's GPU the rewritten body against a CPU reference of PTX `mma.m16n8k16` on random fragments (4 SIMD-groups a threadgroup; largest error 0.009 against a reference that rounds once: the matrix hardware rounds the F16 accumulator as it goes, as Ampere's does). The translator's body agreed with the same reference to 0.002.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md): its 24 NDSR compute shaders. On one save and route, the upscaler took 1.1-1.6 ms a frame instead of 0.1-5.1 ms (2.5-4.5 typically), a frame's GPU span 8.9-19.4 ms instead of 13.8-23.3, and the game's 60 ↔ 30 switches fell from 87 to 68 in the same run length; the picture is unchanged.
- **Note:** three of four of these HMMAs start an output tile, with C = 0. A variant for those calls that neither stores nor loads C (`simdgroup_multiply` first) was measured and not kept: 0.767 against 0.820 ns a warp-HMMA (6 %) in an isolated loop of the build's two bodies on an M4 Pro, about 0.04 ms of the upscaler's 1.6-1.9 ms a frame, and no measurable change in the game.
- **Open:** LDSM and MOVM (27 and 72 a shader) are still emulated with shuffles (314 left in the upscaler, whose floor without them is about 0.3 ms); loading A and B straight from LDSM's source and keeping D as matrices between HMMAs (0.65 ns an HMMA) needs the translator to follow LDSM → MOVM → HMMA → HMMA chains. Done for accumulator chains and for A read by an LDSM: [HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group](#hmma-chains-keep-their-matrices-in-the-simd-group); B and MOVM remain.

### HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's movie or upscaler network runs slow on the Mac and iPhone, and the GPU profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF` with `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_OWN=1`) shows its compute dispatches, small grids of one to four SIMD-groups a threadgroup that loop over tiles, at 1-3 ms each; the dispatches' shaders call `dmsh_hmma16816_f16` hundreds of times ([Ampere HMMA through simdgroup_matrix](#ampere-hmma-through-simdgroup_matrix)).
- **Cause:** each call took every operand through the SIMD-group's scratch block: the lanes stored A (4 words), B (2) and C (2), the matrix hardware loaded them, and D went back to the registers through the block, three SIMD-group barriers a call. In these networks almost every HMMA continues an accumulation (C is the D of the call before it, which nothing else reads: chains of 9 to 36), four calls in a row share A, and A comes straight from an LDSM whose rows are a strided tile in shared memory. That LDSM's emulation cost four shuffles a lane on top of the round trip. Measured on an M4 (8 GPU cores) in Ocarina of Time's movie networks (14 dispatches a frame): without the helper's body the frame's compute fell from 26.9 to 15.4 ms; without the shuffles as well, to 11.0 (wrong pictures, measured only, over four frames).
- **Fix:** `TensorPass.Prepare` gives each HMMA flags, a constant argument after C, from the optimised IR of its block: an accumulator slot (8 a function) for a chain, whose D stays there as the next call's C (`FlagCSlot`, `FlagDSlot`); A that is the previous call's (`FlagAReuse`); C = 0 (`FlagCZero`); and A that is four matrices of one LDSM of the block with no shared memory write or barrier between (`FlagADirect`, `FlagATrans` for .MT88), with the LDSM's row address, each matrix's offset from lane 0's row and the row stride. That needs the address to be U + f(lane) with U the same in every lane and f known: a divergence analysis of the function (the lane and the thread index components a subgroup spans are divergent, a loop header's phis are not when every exit branches on a uniform condition and, for a loop with several back edges, every other branch in it too, phis where paths meet are) and an evaluation of f for the 32 lanes, whose rows must be 16-byte aligned and strided. An LDSM in a compute shader is now a `MatrixLoad` a matrix, lowered to its per-lane code after that. The translator's own body reads only the registers, so every flag is a promise about values it computes anyway. `dmsh_rewrite.py` `hmma_msl` declares the slots and A's matrices in each function that calls the helper and passes them by reference, so the inlined helper keeps them in registers, and passes the caller's shared memory array (the one threadgroup array besides the scratch; with none or several, A goes through the block): A loads with `simdgroup_load` at lane 0's row (`simd_broadcast`) plus its offset, which leaves the LDSM's per-lane code unused. A chain's middle call stores only B. A chain change.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_hmma.py`: the rewrite's arguments and callers, and on the GPU a run of four calls with those flags (A from shared memory plain and transposed, A kept, C = 0, accumulators in two slots), each given the registers the translator's body would read (a stale A where A comes from shared memory), against a CPU reference (64 SIMD-groups, largest error 0.014 for two HMMAs with an f16 accumulator between them). With `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`, the flags the translator gives two chained HMMAs on an LDSM's matrices. `DMSH_TENSOR_TRACE=1` on a `dmsh_xlate` job prints why an HMMA's A is not loaded where its LDSM read it.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#frame-rate): its title and opening movies run eleven NDVE network shaders with 2,217 HMMAs, 1,890 of them continuing a chain. Measured on the title screen, the median over 321 profiled frames of the frame's compute: 26.65 ms before, 18.85 after on an M4 with 8 GPU cores (quartiles within 0.1 ms); on an M6 with 12, 14.78 before and 11.41 after (frames from 3,000 on, 1,057 and 851 profiled, quartiles within 0.6 ms), the GPU span 15.63 and 11.87 ms; the title and the opening movie unchanged to the eye. 1,789 of the HMMAs take no A through the scratch (540 load it where its LDSM read it, 1,249 keep the previous call's); of the other 428, 427 (most of `d15370a382630d4e`'s) have a shared memory store between their LDSM and the HMMA. agl's NDSR upscaler, in this game and in [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) (24 compute shaders, 5,328 calls: 2,664 keep A, 1,325 continue a chain, 3,996 start from C = 0), loads none of its A where its LDSM read it: its LDSM rows are addresses read from a table in shared memory, which the analysis cannot prove strided, so A goes through the scratch there. Bananza's opening and first play render as before.
- **Note:** what is left is B's trip through the block: with B loaded from shared memory too (a fake address, measured only), the frame took 12.6 ms. In these networks B is the weights, read by LDSM.MT88 before the tile loop and kept in registers (72 pairs in two of the shaders), while the shared memory is reused for activations, so it cannot be loaded where it was read. Turning B into matrices once where it is defined and keeping them in registers took 18.9 to 17.4 ms but slowed one shader (`0a56002cdc7e1fce`, four SIMD-groups a threadgroup, 144 matrices) by 13 %; not kept. Removing the barrier at the end of a chain's middle call changed nothing (18.84 against 18.88 ms).
- **B kept where it repeats** (`hmma_bslots`): two calls have the same B when their B arguments come, through copies (`x`, `as_type<T>(x)`, `int(x)`), from the same variables, each written once in the function and declared at its top level. Out arguments, a non-const reference or a taken address count as writes. The first such call at the function's top level keeps B's two matrices in a slot (`dm_bm`), and the calls of that B after it take them, with no store, load or barrier for B. It is a literal argument at each call, so the branches fold away when the helper is inlined. Ocarina of Time: 10,293 of 16,620 HMMAs in 56 of its 71 HMMA shaders take B; `fa3033d05c0a41d6` takes it in 118 of 176. Its largest shaders hold up to 168 slots, and they still gain: on an M6, a latency-bound chain (24 threadgroups of 4 SIMD-groups) with 10 to 160 weight pairs ran 16–28 % faster, with the same bits. In the game, standing on the Kokiri Forest deck, the 4-dispatch compute encoder that holds the upscaler's network went from 984 / 979 µs to 951 / 946 µs (two runs each in one session, pictures the same within noise). A contributor measured that network's largest dispatch on an M4 Pro at 779 → 655 µs (discussion #1789).
- **Ruled out: building the matrices from the lanes' registers.** Apple's matrix hardware fills a matrix quickly only through `simdgroup_load`. The 8x8 lane layout is fixed: on an M6 lane l holds row (l/16)·4 + (l/2)%4 and columns c, c+1 with c = 4·((l/8)%2) + 2·(l%2), the layout published for the M1. With it, each lane can set its elements through `thread_elements()` from SIMD shuffles, which gives the same bits. On a 64-call chain this took 1.716 ms with B built that way, against 1.448 through the block, and 8.9 ms with every operand built that way. So B's trip cannot get cheaper per call, and the slots leave it out where B repeats. With no trip at all the same chain took 1.147 ms. The exact Ampere rounding (f32 blocks, two conversions a lane) is the larger cost of a chain: 1.448 ms against 0.734 with f16 accumulation, which gives different bits.

### LDSM reads rows other lanes stored

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's movie or upscaler network output has dotted streaks, every other pixel of a few rows wrong, in other places each run, while its input is clean. Through agl's NDSR upscaler a few such pixels a frame grow into white blotches over the whole picture: Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest on an M4 Pro on chain 844bf1a5ea8f, with the game's own upscaler (the default); with `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx` in its place, no blotches.
- **Cause:** a warp stores tiles to shared memory (STS) and reads them back with LDSM, often with no BAR between them. The Switch runs a warp's shared memory accesses in order. Metal orders another lane's threadgroup store and this lane's load only through a barrier, and its compiler moves them past each other. Since [HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group](#hmma-chains-keep-their-matrices-in-the-simd-group), a compute shader's LDSM is a `MatrixLoad` that `TensorPass` lowers late to its per-lane code, and NDSR's LDSMs all go that way: its picture went bad on that chain.
- **Fix:** `InstEmitAmpereTensor.ALdsm` puts a subgroup barrier (`simdgroup_barrier(mem_flags::mem_threadgroup)`) before an LDSM's reads and one after them, so the stores have landed and no lane's next store reaches the rows before every lane has read them; in a compute shader they bracket the `MatrixLoad`s and so the code that replaces them. LDSM is warp-collective, so the whole SIMD-group reaches both. `TensorPass` does not count a subgroup barrier as a shared memory write, so an HMMA still loads its A where its LDSM read it. A chain change.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` `Shared.test_ldsm_reads_between_subgroup_barriers` (fails on the translator before). Ocarina of Time on an M4 Pro, Kokiri Forest autosave, the game's upscaler: white blotches over the forest on chain 844bf1a5ea8f, none on fbd67412d49d at the bridge and on the village floor. GPU time through the title movie unchanged (median 12.7 and 13.6 ms a 300-frame window, both chains).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): the movie library's NDVE networks first (five of them have no BAR between STS and LDSM; MetaIX, GitHub pull request #1376), then agl's NDSR in play. Not the cause of the dotted horizontal rows that remained in the scene with either upscaler (GitHub issue #1537): [An HMMA outside a chain overwrote the chain's accumulator](#an-hmma-outside-a-chain-overwrote-the-chains-accumulator).

### An HMMA outside a chain overwrote the chain's accumulator

- **Symptom:** dotted horizontal rows across the picture at a regular spacing, in fog and light, with the game's upscaler and with `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx` alike (MetalFX then replaced only `agl_ndsr.pck`'s shaders): Ocarina of Time in Kokiri Forest, Hyrule Field and the castle town since chain 23a1ea1db092. The rows are the tiles of agl's `agl_ndsr_vr.pck` / `agl_ndsr_vr_light.pck` networks (about 28.8 pixels of the 1536-wide input).
- **Cause:** [HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group](#hmma-chains-keep-their-matrices-in-the-simd-group) gives a chain an accumulator slot, `acc[k..k+1]` with k from the flags. The helper did all its work in `acc[k]`, and a call outside every chain has no slot bits, so it worked in slot 0: an unchained call between the two calls of a chain held in slot 0 overwrote that chain's accumulator. Each of those 30 networks has such calls (2,515 in all, of 7,558); their outputs came out wrong where the overwritten chains land in each tile.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_rewrite.py` `HMMA_BODY` computes C and D in matrices of its own; a slot is read only for a chain's C (`FlagCSlot`) and written only for its D (`FlagDSlot`). The flags are constants at each inlined call, so the copies cost nothing. A chain change.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_hmma.py` `HmmaOnGpu.test_calls_outside_a_chain_leave_its_slot_alone` (a chain in slot 0 around two calls outside it, on the GPU against a CPU reference): 8,090 outputs over tolerance (largest error 9.6) with the helper before, none after. Ocarina of Time on an M4 Pro, the same Kokiri Forest view: rows on chain 23a1ea1db092 and later; none on 0f510fd5's chain, with the helper's rewrite left out, or with this fix.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (GitHub issue #1537). Counted in the translated MSL (calls outside a chain while slot 0 is held): Ocarina of Time 3,700 in 52 of its 62 HMMA shaders (its NDSR upscaler and movie networks too); [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md)'s NDSR 7 in 5 of its 24, so a few of its outputs were wrong as well (not looked at in the game).

### HMMA rounds its f16 accumulator once per 8 products

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's movie or upscaler network gives values a few f16 steps off the console's, in about half the outputs of each HMMA. Nothing looks broken; it is the network's arithmetic, compounded layer by layer.
- **Cause:** `HMMA_BODY` accumulated on `simdgroup_half8x8`, whose multiply-accumulate rounds the f16 accumulator after every product. Ampere's HMMA with an f16 accumulator adds 8 products to C and rounds once (Khattak and Mikaitis's A100 model, N_FMA 8; [MATLAB-tensor-core](https://github.com/north-numerical-computing/MATLAB-tensor-core)): an HMMA.16816 is k 0-7 rounded to f16, then k 8-15 on top, rounded again. Against the toolbox's 5,000 recorded A100 fp16 cases (C as f16), per-product rounding matches 2,420; summing the 8 products in f32 and rounding once matches all 5,000. On a Jetson Orin (sm_87, `mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k16` through PTX, 3 x 4,096 warps of random halves in [-1, 1]), per-product rounding matched 34.9 % of the outputs and the once-per-8 sum 99.97 % (fxd0h, PR #1897).
- **Fix:** `HMMA_BODY` accumulates each block of 8 into a `simdgroup_float8x8` (half operands and a half C, which `simdgroup_multiply_accumulate` takes) and rounds it to f16 per lane through `thread_elements()`. A multiply-accumulate with zero A and B into a half result rounds the same, but took NDSR's network twice as long. The host's NDVE network (`ndve_engine.metal` `hmma()`) rounds the same way, into a new matrix: written into its accumulator arrays' own `thread_elements()`, its layers ran 15 to 50 times slower on an M6. A chain change.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_hmma.py`'s GPU runs compare every output bit for bit with the two-block reference: main's body differs in 21,461 of them on an M5 Max and on an M6, the new one in none; `test_ndve_engine.py` keeps the host's network bit for bit with the helper. Isolated on an M5 Max, NDSR's `fa3033d05c0a41d6` takes 0.316 ms against 0.276 and `d99fbeeb53d38847` 0.446 against 0.393; their outputs change in 1.5 % and 0.8 % of halves. On an M6, a chain of 258 calls takes 2.56 ms against 1.69 for 2,048 threadgroups of 4 SIMD-groups, and the host network's seven layer kernels at the movie's sizes 2.94 ms against 2.85 (best of 100 each, summed). Not measured in a game.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): its NDVE movie network and agl's NDSR upscaler (PR #1897).

### SHFL takes its lane from the low 5 bits

- **Symptom:** a warp shuffle whose lane comes from a register with bits above bit 4 set returns the thread's own value instead of the requested lane's. Orin probes found 207 wrong IDX results, 196 BFLY results and 179 segmented IDX results, each out of 1,024 rows (PR #2044).
- **Cause:** SHFL uses only b[4:0] as its lane or offset in every mode ([PTX ISA](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/parallel-thread-execution/index.html#data-movement-and-conversion-instructions-shfl-sync)). The translator's `HelperFunctionManager.cs` `GenerateShuffle*` helpers consumed the whole register, so the high bits affected the source lane and validity predicate. Both Maxwell's `InstEmitWarp.Shfl` and Ampere's `AShfl` lower to these helpers; the c operand's clamp and segment mask were already decoded correctly.
- **Fix:** mask the lane argument to `& 31` in all four shared helpers (IDX, UP, DOWN, BFLY). General shader ISA semantics, with no game or build gate; at most one additional integer AND per shuffle. Rebuild the native translator and regenerate its lock and chain header.
- **Check:** on an M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1 (26B131), Metal 32023.922, the PR's recorded Orin SHFL programs through current main's chain reproduce 207/196/179 differences with the baseline native translator and zero with the rebuilt one. UP, DOWN and immediate BFLY still match. `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_xlate_bin/dmsh_xlate DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_shfl.py` checks synthetic Maxwell and Ampere shaders on Metal: all four modes, raw high-bit operands, immediate lanes, result and predicate, and clamp/segment boundaries. UP uses clamp zero; its separate nonzero-clamp issue is unchanged. No game or iOS device run.
- **Found on:** no game; the Jetson Orin's sm_87 results (CUDA driver 12.6, PR #2044). The Orin fixture now runs in `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py`; IDX, BFLY and segmented IDX are no longer expected failures.

### Ampere instructions checked against an Ampere GPU

- **Symptom:** none that shows on its own: a translated instruction gives other bits than the console's GPU for some inputs (a NaN operand, a lane index past 31, a directed rounding), and the picture is off only where a shader depends on them. The translator tests check the MSL a few encodings give, not the values the shader computes.
- **Cause:** nothing compared the translated instructions' values with an Ampere GPU's, so a meaning read wrong from the encoding, or lost on the way through spirv-cross and Metal, went unseen until a game showed it.
- **Fix:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` runs about 190 operations, each the GPU compiler's own machine code for one PTX operation, through the whole chain: the translator with the build's options, spirv-cross with the chain's arguments, every MSL rewrite `dmsh_build.py`'s `finish()` makes (read from its source, so a new rewrite is included), and the chain's Metal flags. On this Mac's GPU each takes the 1,024 input rows of its fixture, and every output word is compared bit for bit with what a Jetson Orin (sm_87, CUDA 12.6 driver) computed from the same rows. The inputs mix special values (signed zeros, denormals, infinities, NaNs with several payloads, the limits of each integer type, ties for the roundings), random bits and ordinary values; warp operations run 32 threads to a group on both sides, so a lane's neighbours are the same. `dm/gfx/tools/ampere_orin/capture.py` records the fixture on any Linux machine with an Ampere GPU and NVIDIA's driver: the driver compiles each op's PTX itself (no CUDA toolkit, no compiler on either side), runs it, and the same machine code is rewritten for the translator, its global loads and stores becoming shared memory ones and the CUDA parameter reads and the addressing they feed becoming NOPs (`prog.py`), which the device's `nvdisasm` checks. `ampere_orin/ampere_orin.sass` lists each program as the translator gets it.
  - Allowed on every op: what Metal's f32 arithmetic itself gives, NaN bits (Ampere writes the canonical NaN, 0x7fffffff) and flushed f32 denormals (`compare.PLATFORM`).
  - `KNOWN` lists the ops that still differ, each with its reason ([the tracking issue](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2040)); `APPROX` the ones that run Metal's own approximation (MUFU, and the divisions built on it), which are reported and never fail. An op outside both lists that differs fails the test, and so does a `KNOWN` op that matches, so a fix takes its entry out.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_xlate_bin/dmsh_xlate python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` (about 5 s on an M5 Max); it prints every op's differing lanes by cause. `AMPERE_ORIN_ONLY=<ops or families>` runs some, `AMPERE_ORIN_REPORT=<file>` writes the results as JSON. To record again: `python3 dm/gfx/tools/ampere_orin/capture.py --host <user@machine> [--arch sm_86]`, with NVIDIA's `nvdisasm` (the `cuda_nvdisasm` redistributable) in `~/ampere_orin` on that machine.
- **Found on:** no game; a Jetson Orin's results. The HMMA comparison it extends is PR #1897's.

### Directed rounding in FADD, FMUL and FFMA

- **Symptom:** FADD, FMUL and FFMA with `.RM`, `.RP` or `.RZ` can return the round-to-nearest result, one ULP away from the result requested by the shader.
- **Cause:** the Ampere and Maxwell emitters discarded the directed modes. Metal's floating-point operations round to nearest, so these modes need a correction in the translator.
- **Fix:** the shared helpers use the sign of the rounding error to step the nearest result by one ULP when required. FADD orders the addends by magnitude before recovering the error, which prevents an intermediate overflow when the sum is finite. FMUL recovers the product error from the significands and scales the operand that keeps the intermediate in range. FFMA computes the error on scaled terms so a finite fused result survives an overflowing separate product. Directed `.FTZ` flushes inputs and results with their signs. The same FMUL rule applies to the `1/(2π)` literal used before MUFU.
- **Check:** the nine directed programs in `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` cover 9,216 recorded Orin lanes. On M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1 and Metal 32023.922, rounding differences fall from 2,857 to zero. The remaining differences are 486 NaN encodings (Orin `0x7fffffff`, Metal `0x7fc00000`), 179 with denormal inputs and 14 with denormal outputs. Another 48 synthetic lanes compare exact bits for finite overflow boundaries, signed FTZ, scaling, the trig literal and fused cancellation.
- **Coverage:** those 48 synthetic Ampere lanes and 45 synthetic Maxwell lanes pass with default and relaxed Mac math modes. Their 33 shaders validate as SPIR-V and compile offline for iOS. At the normal/subnormal boundary `FMUL.RP.FTZ` on `0x00800000 * 0x3f7fffff` still gives +0 on Metal, where exact arithmetic with round-up before flushing gives `0x00800000`. That case has no NVIDIA hardware readback. The approximate `MUFU.SIN` and `MUFU.COS` programs differ in 746 and 581 of 1,024 lanes, down from 780 and 606. No iOS device ran the shaders.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2051](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2051). Mac regression runs cover [Fast Fusion's menus](../../dm/games/040041E0212AE000/README.md#validation) and [Cuphead's map](../../dm/games/0100A5C00D162000/README.md#mac-routes).

### Half-pair .FTZ flushes f16 subnormals

- **Symptom:** HADD2.FTZ, HMUL2.FTZ and HFMA2.FTZ keep half subnormals instead of flushing them. A subnormal times infinity consequently gives infinity instead of NaN.
- **Cause:** the translator dropped bit 80, as for f32 arithmetic, but Metal keeps half subnormals. The Orin reference shows that both inputs and the rounded half result flush to signed zero. Flushing only inputs or only results leaves 57 or 60 HMUL2.FTZ lanes wrong; dropping the zero's sign leaves at least 125.
- **Fix:** `AHalfFlush` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` flushes unpacked inputs below 2^-14 while preserving their sign. `AHalfResult` clears a packed result lane's mantissa when its exponent is zero, after rounding to half. A `.F32` result bypasses half-result flushing. HSETP2/HSET2 share the source helper; HMNMX2.FTZ uses it too. No game or build gate is needed. Non-FTZ forms emit the same code; FTZ forms add source comparisons/selects and integer result masking.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_xlate_bin/dmsh_xlate AMPERE_ORIN_ONLY=f16x2 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py`. On M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922 (metalfe-32023.922.4), the rebuilt native translator through current-main rewrites changes differing halves as follows: HADD2.FTZ 246 → 215; HMUL2.FTZ 449 → 234; ordinary HFMA2.FTZ 418 → 332. Every remaining difference is Orin `0x7fff` versus Metal `0x7e00`, both NaNs; differences beyond NaN bits fall from 31, 221 and 93 respectively to zero. These are not bit-exact matches. The JSON report records NaN patterns separately, and a classifier test keeps NaN-versus-infinity failures distinct from NaN-bit differences.
- **Coverage:** the hardware fixture uses HFMA2.MMA, translated by the [shared HFMA2 emitter](#hfma2mma-the-half-pair-fma-on-the-tensor-pipe); derived `hfma2_alu` / `hfma2_alu_ftz` copies change only opcode bit 2 to exercise ordinary HFMA2 with the recorded inputs/reference. Synthetic min/max FTZ cases cover both inputs, signed zeros and the smallest normal half (64 halves each); a synthetic `.F32` result case preserves ±2^-15 (32 floats). These synthetic checks are not new NVIDIA recordings. `.F32` source swizzles and HSETP2/HSET2.FTZ lack hardware-reference coverage. No game frame-time benefit or cost is established.
- **Found on:** no game symptom; [the Orin comparison](#ampere-instructions-checked-against-an-ampere-gpu). PR #2048 reports about 7,700 FTZ half instructions in Ocarina of Time, a static census rather than a timing measurement.

### HFMA2.MMA, the half-pair FMA on the tensor pipe

- **Symptom:** `NotSupportedException: Ampere instruction 235` or `435` prevents a shader from translating. Half `.RELU` arithmetic also turns NaN into zero instead of retaining NaN.
- **Cause:** the translator registered ordinary HFMA2 (low-nine-bit opcode 0x031) but omitted its tensor-pipe form 0x035. NVIDIA's compiler emits the latter for `fma.rn.f16x2` and for constant loads such as `-RZ * RZ + imm`. The result emitter used a plain maximum with zero for `.RELU`; unlike `.SAT`, [.RELU preserves a NaN result](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.0.1/parallel-thread-execution/index.html#half-precision-floating-point-instructions-fma).
- **Fix:** register 0x035 with `AHfma2` and `InstName.Hfma2`, preserving the same operand forms, predicates, constant-bank discovery and result modifiers. `AHalfResult` selects a NaN through before taking the maximum with zero on HFMA2, HFMA2.MMA and HMUL2 `.RELU` forms. The existing input/result `.FTZ` handling remains shared. This is general Ampere ISA behavior, with no game or build gate; ordinary non-RELU HFMA2/HMUL2 output is unchanged.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_xlate_bin/dmsh_xlate python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py`: all 200 recorded/derived/synthetic operations pass on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1 (26B131), Metal 32023.922 (metalfe-32023.922.4). Main rejects 17 programs at HFMA2.MMA; 13 now run, while four reach their separate FCHK, F2I.S64, DADD or DMUL refusal. Eight constant-loading conversion/integer programs are bit-exact. HFMA2, HFMA2.FTZ and HFMA2.RELU differ in 317, 332 and 314 of 2,048 halves respectively, all Orin `0x7fff` versus Metal `0x7e00` NaNs; saturation is bit-exact. Reciprocal remains a Metal approximation, not a bit-exact result.
- **Coverage:** derived ordinary-HFMA2.RELU changes only opcode bit 2 and retains the Orin inputs/reference: main has 314 NaN-versus-zero errors, the fix none beyond NaN bits. Synthetic HMUL2.RELU checks NaN inputs, zero times infinity, infinities, negative clamping and positive subnormals: main has 24 NaN-versus-zero errors in 64 halves, the fix 24 NaN-bit differences and no numerical errors. `HalfFmaAlias` in `test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` checks identical emitted MSL and resource metadata across all seven operand forms, each plain or with SAT/F32/RELU/FTZ (35 comparisons). These translation checks do not provide new NVIDIA recordings for constant-bank/uniform forms. The #2049 fixtures have no triple that triggers the independent [half-FMA double-rounding error](#hfma2-single-rounding-to-half).
- **Found on:** synthetic Jetson Orin sm_87 programs (CUDA driver 12.6); the existing RELU path is present in [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md). No game symptom is attributed to it.

### HFMA2 single rounding to half

- **Symptom:** a packed HFMA2 result is one half ULP off near a rounding midpoint. For example, `-3136 × -8.125 + 0.000124693` produces 25488 on Orin and 25472 with the old translation.
- **Cause:** rounding the fused result to f32 can turn a value just beyond a half midpoint into a tie. Packing that f32 value then rounds the tie to the wrong half.
- **Fix:** the Ampere emitter selects a typed half FMA for HFMA2 and HFMA2.MMA when the sources and result are halves. SPIR-V widens the already rounded result exactly for the shared result handling, so even a later unpack sees the half-rounded value. Operand FTZ runs before FMA and result FTZ after half rounding. The shared .RELU still retains NaNs and clamps negative values, and .SAT still saturates. .F32 sources, .F32 destinations and FFMA use the f32 path.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` runs the 4,570 input triples recorded for [PR #1902](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1902) through the actual translator. On an M4 Pro with macOS 27.2.1 and Metal 32023.922, 70 numeric differences in 9,140 lanes fall to zero. The 1,160 NaN encodings still differ (Orin `0x7fff`, Metal `0x7e00`). Separate cases check .RELU and .FTZ together, packed results read back as floats, .F32 operands and results, and float FMA followed by an explicit pack.
- **Found on:** Jetson Orin sm_87 probes. The issue was found in arithmetic comparisons.

### The NDVE movie network by the host

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's movies and the title behind them run below 60 fps on a Mac with a smaller GPU, and the GPU profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`) shows almost all of the frame in a dozen compute dispatches of `nn::ndve`'s movie network: Ocarina of Time's title at about 24 fps and 38 ms of GPU a frame on an M1 Pro with 16 GPU cores, 24.8 ms on an M1 Max ([issue 1375](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1375)), 18.9 ms of compute on an M4 with 8 ([HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group](#hmma-chains-keep-their-matrices-in-the-simd-group)); 11-14 ms of the 16.7 on an M6, an M4 Pro or an M5 Max.
- **Cause:** `nn::ndve` (a Nintendo library linked into the game's `main`: `nn::ndve::RouleauArchiveIO`) runs the first network of its model, `Movie/Ndve/Ndve-Nvn2-vid_seq5.dat` ([`nn::ndve`'s movie enhancement file](#nnndves-movie-enhancement-file)): a Rouleau archive (footer `RLA1`: four entries, two networks and two small shaders) whose networks hold a table of their 9 compute shaders, `0x3c320` bytes of weights and the shaders, compiled for a 1920x1080 picture. The graph is in the library's code, not in the archive: a U-Net over the picture packed 2x2 at 960x540 (16, 32 and 64 channels), eleven HMMA.16816 convolution dispatches, the last convolution added to the picture (`5a7d69b0`), then a 4/3 resampler with a small network of its own (`4f41ccf2`, from the archive's NDSR pack). Its code is written for NVIDIA's register file: each convolution loads its weights before the tile loop (LDG.128, STS.128, LDSM.MT88.4) and keeps them in registers through it, up to 72 B pairs (144 registers a thread), each used once a row. Translated, that is register pressure and a trip through the SIMD-group's scratch for every HMMA's B. No general lowering found does better on an M4 Pro (best ms of the five heaviest dispatches standalone, chain `dedc9391c30b`: `e6be82d8` 1.34, `0a56002c` 1.69, `5a7d69b0` 0.97, `d15370a3` 0.80, `a8b4fb48` 0.54): B as `simdgroup_matrix` registers once before the loop gave 1.18 / 2.29 / - / - / 0.49 (worse where four SIMD-groups a threadgroup each hold their own 72 pairs); B loaded at every HMMA from its storage buffer, where `TensorPass` proved it came from (built and bit-exact), 1.75 for `e6be82d8` and 0.94 for `5a7d69b0`; B in threadgroup slots of their own, 2.16 for `e6be82d8` (the memory costs occupancy). A variant that read every B from one fixed place looked 35 % faster, but only because those 256 bytes were always in the cache.
- **Fix:** `nvn_mtl.m`'s dispatch hands the network to `nvn_ndve.inc`, which runs it as kernels of the host (`dm/gfx/msl/ndve_engine.metal`, built with the host's other shaders): 8x8 `simdgroup_matrix` tiles straight from the tensors, weights loaded once a tap, 3x3 inputs staged per threadgroup, the network's tensors in buffers of the host's with a zero border, one encoder for the 13 kernels. The Mac's default; on iOS and iPadOS the network is skipped and the picture scaled by MetalFX instead ([the NDVE movie network skipped for MetalFX on the iPhone](#the-ndve-movie-network-skipped-for-metalfx-on-the-iphone)). `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=1` runs this network on either, `0` the game's dispatches, `mfx` the MetalFX scale, `ab` alternates the MetalFX scale with this network under `DM_AB`. It is bound to the library's model, not to a game: the keys are the hashes of the model's shaders and the weights' offsets follow from its layers (each layer's weights, then its biases, each padded to 256 bytes, to the archive's `0x3c320`), so any game that ships this model runs it. Recognition: the 14 keys in order, each dispatch's weights and biases at the offsets the layers give from the first's, the picture the last convolution adds being the one the first read, the sizes and formats, the resampler's constants. Anything else (another model, the archive's second network, other sizes) leaves the game's dispatches running, logged once; a mismatch after some dispatches were skipped turns the host's network off for the rest of the run. The colour conversion `49dff010` stays the game's. The kernels compute what the translated shaders compute, in their order: each HMMA as `HMMA_BODY` does it (two `simdgroup_multiply_accumulate` on `simdgroup_half8x8`, input channels 0-7 then 8-15, `simdgroup_multiply` for a chain's first call), a chain over the taps and then the 16-channel blocks, the epilogue's D + bias in f32, ReLU and one rounding; the picture's sum in the game's order under safe math.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_ndve_engine.py`: every kernel on random inputs and weights at the network's sizes, bit for bit against references that call `dmsh_hmma16816_f16` as `hmma_msl` rewrites it, in PTX's fragment layout with TensorPass's chain flags (all 10 identical on an M1 Pro, an M5 Max and an M4 Pro; with an f32 accumulator per HMMA instead, the 9 with HMMAs differ in 6-25 % of their outputs), and the weight offsets the layer table gives against those Ocarina of Time binds. Standalone on one title-movie frame on the M1 Pro (16 GPU cores): main's 13 translated dispatches 36.3 ms of GPU, the host's 13 kernels 10.8 ms (PR #1894). In the game, on an M4 Pro with 20 GPU cores (Ocarina of Time's `title-movie` route, `scripts/bench.py`, three runs each, the window kept in focus): the frame's GPU time 12.78 ms with `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=0`, 6.77 with the host's network (p50), both at 60 fps. `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=check` runs the game's dispatches and the host's network and resampler beside them into images of its own and compares them texel by texel (logged every 300 frames): over 1,500 title frames, the resampler identical in every frame, the network in all but 10 texels, each 1/1023 off, the same 10 with `DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD=1`. The translated shaders are built with Metal's fast math, which may contract and reorder those sums: `5a7d69b0` built with `-fno-fast-math` instead gives other bits in a few texels of random inputs; the host's kernels keep the game's order.
- **Note:** run standalone on fixed inputs, 4 times each, the 10 translated dispatches gave the same output every time on an M4 Pro, on chain `dedc9391c30b` and on `39417042cfe8` ([issue 1893](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1893) reported outputs that change from run to run on an M1 Pro, measured by another harness; not reproduced).
- **Note:** the host's network rounds each HMMA's f16 accumulator once per 8 products, as the translated helper does since [HMMA rounds its f16 accumulator once per 8 products](#hmma-rounds-its-f16-accumulator-once-per-8-products) (resolved; before that both rounded after every product, as `simdgroup_half8x8` does, and differed from the Switch in the low bits of most HMMAs). The host's network follows the helper, and `test_ndve_engine.py` fails when the two differ.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#frame-rate): its title and opening movies ([issue 1375](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1375), PR #1894).

### An interrupted compute compile broke the NDVE sequence

- **Symptom:** green and grey striped blocks over the opening movie after `XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED`. The host logs `[RED] NDVE engine: a dispatch out of the network's order` and turns the engine off for the rest of the run.
- **Cause:** when Apple's `MTLCompilerService` died, the replay's dispatch kept the interrupted compute compile as a permanent failure (`cps_failed`). The dispatch returned before the NDVE engine saw the layer, so the next layer looked out of order, and the game's own network then ran with that dispatch missing for the rest of the session.
- **Fix:** `dispatch` (`nvn_mtl.m`) gives a recognized NDVE dispatch (`ndve_dispatch_index`) to the engine after its bindings are prepared and before its translated pipeline is asked for (`dispatch_pso`). A compile the compiler service's death interrupted (`err_compiler_died`) is not latched: that dispatch is skipped and a later one asks again; a refusal is still kept. Ported from NXNative 9df3c09fc (MIT).
- **Check:** `t_dispatch_pipeline` (with `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=1`) injects the error at the Metal 4 compiler through the real dispatch path, checks that the next dispatch builds the pipeline and writes its result, that a refusal is asked once, and that the engine takes two network layers in order without the replay asking Metal for the first one's pipeline.
- **Found on:** upstream, [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on an M2 MacBook Air on macOS 27.0 ([issue #2920](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2920)).

### The NDVE movie network skipped for MetalFX on the iPhone

- **Symptom:** on an iPhone 18 Pro Max, Ocarina of Time's opening movie (about 30 s) keeps the GPU about 95 % busy with 2 draws a frame (GPU p50 16.2-17.6 ms a frame at the full clock), and the phone goes from thermal state 0 to 1 during the movie and to 2 about a minute later, when the 30 fps heat hold comes on ([A thermally serious iPhone holds 30 fps](frame-rate.md#a-thermally-serious-iphone-holds-30-fps)).
- **Cause:** even as the host's kernels ([the NDVE movie network by the host](#the-ndve-movie-network-by-the-host)), `nn::ndve`'s network is about 15.7 billion multiply-adds a movie frame, worked out from the kernels' shapes: 10.6 billion in the U-Net (a learned correction added to the 1920x1080 picture at its own size, most likely clean-up of the AV1 picture) and 5.1 billion in the resampler's small network (the 4/3 scale to the 2560x1440 canvas, a network per 8x8 block, then dither). On the phone that is about 11-12 ms of the 16.5 ms a movie frame takes (an estimate: no kernel was timed there), at 60 pictures a second, and the 2560x1440 canvas is then scaled down to the screen's ~1320 lines.
- **Fix:** with `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=mfx`, the default on iOS and iPadOS (no setting; `nvn_ndve.inc` `ndve_mfx_scale`), the movie frame is recognised as for the host's network (the keys in order, the weights where the model's layers put them, the sizes and formats, the resampler's constants), but nothing is computed for the U-Net's 11 dispatches and the last convolution `5a7d69b0`. At the resampler `4f41ccf2`, MetalFX's Metal 4 spatial scaler (`MTL4FXSpatialScaler`) writes the game's 2560x1440 RGBA8 canvas, the resampler's own image, from the colour conversion's 1920x1080 RGBA8 picture, the one the network's first layer reads. The colour conversion `49dff010` stays the game's.
  - The scalers are made once, in the background with the engine's pipelines, for the model's fixed sizes and RGBA8 on both sides, in perceptual mode: the picture is video colour, gamma-encoded in 0-1 in a plain RGBA8 texture, and the canvas keeps that encoding, as the resampler writes it. A second scaler writes a 2048x1152 canvas, with the resampler's constants of that size: docked on the iPhone and iPad the host answers graphics scale tier 1 and the game's output is 2048x1152 ([the graphics scale does not follow the operation mode](services.md#the-graphics-scale-does-not-follow-the-operation-mode)). Whether the movie's canvas follows the tier is not seen; the owner's phone logs so far are handheld. The host's own resampler takes 2560x1440 only, as before.
  - Under `mfx` alone the network's tensors and the resampler's luma (about 92 MB of private buffers) are not made: only when the network runs after all (a fallback below), on the submitting thread, zeroed by the first network encoder before its kernels. `1`, `ab` and `check` make them with the pipelines, as before.
  - Each side is the game's texture when MetalFX can take it (its usage; a private canvas), else a texture of MetalFX's own in the same format with a plain copy in before or out after. A resampler whose constants flip its rows (`c4.w` on, `c4.z` 1439) gets the picture flipped into MetalFX's input (`flip_rows`): a spatial scale of the flipped picture is the flipped scale.
  - Order: the work before lands (the colour conversion), then the input copy if any, `mfx_order` (a real copy behind a queue barrier, since MetalFX's own encoders take none), the scaler, the output copy behind a queue barrier of its own, and the relaxed end cleared, so the game's next pass or dispatch, which opens with a queue barrier, reads the finished canvas.
  - The resampler's dither is dropped. It rounds the network's 10-bit picture (RGB10A2) plus its delta to 8 bits, and its noise hides the steps of that rounding. Here MetalFX writes 8 bits from the 8-bit picture itself, so there are no such steps to hide, and the dither would cost a pass of its own over the 2560x1440 canvas for noise only.
  - What is lost: the network's learned clean-up and the resampler's learned detail. The look is the owner's call on the phone.
  - Fallbacks, never a black or frozen movie: no Metal 4 MetalFX, or no scaler made, and the host's network runs the movie, with one line. A frame whose resampler MetalFX cannot draw (its bindings or constants not the model's, a texture missing, a side that cannot be made) gets the network it skipped after all, into the image the resampler reads, then the host's resampler or else the game's, with one `[RED]` line, and the host's network from then on. The same when another compute dispatch comes between `5a7d69b0` and the resampler and binds the network's image (another resampler, say), or the next frame's colour conversion or a network dispatch comes first: the skipped network is written into the image before that dispatch runs (`ndve_mfx_settle`). A dispatch that does not bind the image may come between. Not seen: a draw or a copy that reads the image there; if the resampler never comes, the next colour conversion turns MetalFX off, after that one frame. A chain that is not the model's leaves the game's dispatches, as before.
  - Under `ab`, the `[RED]` line of a fallback says the A/B is void from that phase on: the `[ab]` lines still name `NDVE_ENGINE` in its on phases, which then run the host's network too.
  - The first MetalFX frame logs one line with the sizes, which sides were copied, and that `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=1` brings the network back. `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=1` in `Documents/env.txt` (a player build keeps it) is the network side of a phone A/B; `ab` alternates the two in one run with `DM_AB`; `mfx` forces MetalFX on the Mac.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0400486024DEC000 check_t_ndve_mfx`: the 14 dispatches of a movie frame through the replay's hook on synthetic textures (a gradient picture, zero weights in a memory pool). With `mfx`: the 12 network dispatches and the resampler taken, the network's image left unwritten, the canvas kept at the game's texture and size, every texel written, each channel's mean within 4 of the picture's 255 steps, the gradient the right way up, upside down for a flipping resampler, the same through MetalFX's own textures and copies; a missing canvas gives the game's resampler the network's image, MetalFX failing at the resampler gives the host's network for that frame and the ones after, weights out of place leave the game's dispatches; a 2048x1152 canvas, the right way up and flipped; the tensors not made until the network runs; a dispatch that does not bind the network's image between `5a7d69b0` and the resampler leaves the frame to MetalFX, one that binds it, or the next colour conversion with no resampler before it, finds the image written and MetalFX off. With the encoding thread and `DM_NVN_BARRIER=game` too; the Mac's network unset, and with `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=1` under Metal validation (which stops MetalFX's Metal 4 scalers); `ab` with `DM_AB=1`: the host's network in the even phases and MetalFX in the odd ones, a phase change in the middle of a sequence leaving it as it started. `test_ndve_engine.py` keeps the host's network bit for bit, as before.
- **Note:** the approach is MacNativeGames' (its Ocarina of Time port: `games/oot`, [github.com/ly05010419/MacNativeGames](https://github.com/ly05010419/MacNativeGames), MIT, `dm/third_party/macnativegames/LICENSE`). They skip the network after learning the chain on the first movie frame and write the canvas with MetalFX, always on, with `OOT_MOVIE_BYPASS=0` to bring the network back; on an M4 with 10 GPU cores, 29 ms down to 1.3-1.7 ms of GPU a movie frame, dropped presents from 289 of 300 to 0, the picture checked by mean brightness. This entry follows their idea on NX 18's own recognition; none of their code is copied.
- **Note:** not yet seen on the phone. The saving there is inferred: about 16.5 ms down to about 2-4 ms of GPU a movie frame (the conversion, the scale and any copies), so the phone should stay near its clock floor and thermal state 0 through the movie. Check it with an on-battery A/B of the opening movie, `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=1` against no line: GPU busy and the thermal state through the movie, and the owner's look.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#frame-rate) on an iPhone 18 Pro Max (the Sitting 2 log).

### Driver-segment reads outside fragment shaders

- **Symptom:** a vertex or compute shader reads zeros from NVN's driver segment `c0`.
- **Cause:** `dmsh_reflect.py`'s `drv_reads` scan matches only fragment-shader names (`fp_c0` / `fp_c2`), so vertex and compute shaders that read `c0` were never marked, and the host passed them an all-zero `c0`; these reads are not listed in the JSON.
- **Fix:** the host treats a declared driver-segment binding as read (`nvn_refl.m`). The words the host fills are in the entries below.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md), [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md), [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md).

### Compute `c0`: dispatch size and uniform buffer table

- **Symptom:** flickering hard-edged white tiles (tiled lighting with every tile row getting row 0's or other rows' lights).
- **Cause:** a compute shader that computes its tile index as workgroup y × `c0[0x200]` + workgroup x reads the dispatch's workgroup count from the driver segment, which the host left zero.
- **Fix:** for compute, `c0` carries the dispatch size at 0x200 (x, y, z) and the uniform buffer table at 0x210 + 16 r (address, size) in front of the storage buffer table at 0x310 (`encode_args_`).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md) (Mac and iPhone): the step of Marin and Tarin's doorway. The room shaders use tiled forward lighting: the fragment position picks a 64×64-pixel tile (`trunc(y / 64) × ceil(width / 64) + trunc(x / 64)`), whose light count and light indices come from two storage buffers that a compute shader (`light_culling_compute.bfsha`, one thread per workgroup, one workgroup per tile) fills every frame. Every row's workgroups wrote row 0's entries, the last writer won (a different one each frame), and the bottom tile row, where the step is, got the lights of other rows; a dump of the tile lists showed only the first of 17 rows filled. After the fix every tile row has its lights and the doorway shows the game's soft light from outside.
- **Check:** Breath of the Wild (101 compute and 4 vertex shaders read `c0`) and Super Mario 3D World (11 and 1) render the same as before on the Mac.

### Geometry-stage storage buffer table

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader ... reflection conversion failed: B binding gp_s0 has no resolved slot number`; a geometry shader reading driver segment `c0` reports that its storage-buffer table base was never measured.
- **Cause:** geometry shaders access their storage buffers through `c0 + 0x410 + 0x10 r`, but the builder's `SSBO` map and replay's `c0_ssbo_base` covered only vertex, pixel and compute. Running the lowered geometry shader as a Metal kernel does not change its logical NVN stage or driver table.
- **Fix:** geometry uses base `0x410` in both reflection and driver-buffer population. `encode_args` keeps NVN stage 2 for these kernels, so storage slots and driver-table rows come from the geometry bindings. Hull and domain bases remain unmeasured and unresolved.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): the original shader's constant-buffer operands and the pinned translator's metadata agree on addresses in rows 0 and 2.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_reflect.py` resolves synthetic geometry descriptors to slots 0 and 2 and rejects unknown or unaligned table addresses. `t_geometry` passes synthetic metadata through native reflection and the production argument encoder; GPU readback checks both rows' guest addresses and sizes with different compute-stage bindings, plus a write through the geometry storage pointer.

### Storage-buffer write flag in translation metadata

- **Symptom:** a storage buffer the shader stores to or uses atomically has `rw: false` in its generated reflection; replay does not record its GPU writes for dependent buffer reads.
- **Cause:** Ryujinx's `BufferUsageFlags.Write` is bit 0 (`1`), and `Program.cs` serializes that enum unchanged. The builder tested bit 1 (`2`), incorrectly marking every such descriptor read-only. Native reflection honors the explicit `rw` field, so its older fallback to the MSL pointer qualifier could not correct it.
- **Fix:** `reflect` tests bit 0 for both driver-table storage buffers and addresses carried in guest uniform buffers. Replay then uses its existing write-access and pending-write tracking paths. Rebuild shaders to replace the old metadata.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md): its geometry counter atomic and output stream both emitted translator flag `1` but reached reflection as read-only.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_reflect.py` exercises writable and read-only translator descriptors, including an address in a guest uniform. `t_geometry` verifies that native reflection preserves explicit true and false flags and that the writable geometry output enters the GPU-write tracker before its dispatch.

### Graphics `c0 + 0x610`: the largest point size

- **Symptom:** point sprites of size 0 (Odyssey's Cap Kingdom fog never parted around Mario).
- **Cause:** a vertex shader that writes `gl_PointSize = min(size, c0[0x610])` read 0, because the host had nothing at that offset of the driver segment.
- **Fix:** `c0 + 0x610` holds the largest point size, and the host writes 2047 there for the graphics stages. Writing the `SetPointSize` value instead (always 1 in Odyssey) left one-pixel splats whose disc weight came out 0 (the fields held `-0.0`).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) (reported on an iPhone; fix checked on the Mac): on the Switch the ground fog opens around Mario as he walks through it. The fog's shading reads velocity fields that a small fluid simulation updates every frame: two 256×256 and two 128×128 RG16F targets, with a two-pass blur and a blended composite. Its only input is a point-sprite splat per mover, drawn with `alPointSpriteShader`: one vertex per mover, its size in the vertex data (Mario's main splat asks for about 18 px, others 3–5 px) and a soft disc in the fragment shader (`clamp(1 - 4r², 0, 1)` × velocity). The simulation fields stayed exactly zero (raw dumps with `DM_NVN_TEXDUMP` + `DM_NVN_SNAPRAW`; the 8-bit dump shows velocities as black either way). After the fix the fields hold velocities up to ±44, and the fog clears around Mario.

### Graphics `c0 + 0x644`: the draw's base instance

- **Symptom:** occlusion queries that always return 0 samples (Z-A: characters' legs folded back from the hips once they walked); every instanced draw's transform taken for instance 0.
- **Cause:** a shader that picks its transform with (instance index − base instance) + `c0[0x644]`: Maxwell's instance ID leaves the base instance out, and the NVN driver keeps it in its constant segment, where GLSLC reads it for `gl_InstanceID + gl_BaseInstance`. The host left `0x644` at 0.
- **Fix:** `encode_args` writes the draw's base instance there for graphics stages and does not reuse a stage's argument block across draws with different base instances. The word before it, `0x640`, is added to the vertex ID by 58 of Z-A's shaders and stays 0 until what the driver puts there is known.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md): from the first step on, the player's legs stuck straight out behind her at hip height, and the rival's forward in a later cutscene; standing still beforehand looked right. The bone palette the game computes on the CPU was already bent. The cause was six occlusion queries a frame, one 36-vertex box each (`a793f436dcabd350`, base instance 61, 62, …), drawn against mip level 2 of the depth pyramid: every box was box 0, every query came back with 0 samples, and the game took its characters for hidden and posed their legs that way. Forcing every result to visible fixed the legs, and depth testing with ALWAYS did not. 793 of the game's vertex shaders read the word.
- **Note:** how it was found: the raycasts were logged with scratch observation gates (all correct), then `DM_HLECOUNT` showed nothing walking-specific, then the counter reports.

### The base instance of indirect draws comes from Metal

- **Symptom:** flat grey or background-coloured triangles with straight edges across part of the scene, changing shape from frame to frame while the camera stands still; dropping the colour pass's fragment shaders in groups (`DM_NVN_SKIP_FS`) did not remove them, as the instances are misplaced in the depth pre-pass too, whose depth the colour pass tests for equality.
- **Cause:** for an indirect draw (`DrawElementsIndirect` / `DrawArraysIndirect`) the base instance is in the argument record, which the game's GPU culling writes earlier in the same submission; `encode_args` could only write the command's base instance into `c0 + 0x644`, 0 for an indirect draw. A vertex shader whose instance is (instance index − base instance) + `c0[0x644]` then read its transforms from the first records for every indirect draw.
- **Fix:** the translation reads the word from Metal's `[[base_instance]]`, which holds every draw's base instance, direct, indirect or masked (`dmsh_entry.py` `vs_base_instance`: in vertex functions `c0[100].y` becomes the base instance's bits, and `main0` gets the parameter when it lacks it). `encode_args` still writes the word for the other stages.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Party Jamboree](../../dm/games/0100965017338000/README.md): every board. Its culling (`2b5485a2a731a231`, 62 points with rasterization off) writes 80-byte transforms per visible instance into a 30 KB buffer and counts them, `276504dc5c987ecf` writes nine indirect records from the count, and the instanced draws (`5a805bbb08421b86` in the depth pre-pass, `4b05556737e9535f` and `eaa3a44ba28f0744` in the colour pass) draws them, both reading record = instance + `c0[0x644]`. 5,249 of its vertex shaders read the word.

### Pixel `c0 + 0x620`: the sample index and GLSLC's sample position table

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: reads driver-bank locations at run time that the replay layer cannot fill: c0 dynamic index c0+0x630` (or `c0 dynamic index` alone), and that shader's draws are missing (`[RED] shader … (pixel) failed to load`); the build notes `Shader instruction Pixld is not implemented` for pixel shaders; `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnProgramSetSampleShading`.
- **Cause:** GLSLC reads the sample index with `PIXLD.MYINDEX` (RZ, offset 0; nouveau reads `gl_SampleID` the same way), which Ryujinx's translator left unwritten, so the register read as 0. For `gl_SamplePosition` (or interpolation at a sample) it then indexes a byte table the NVN driver keeps at `c0 + 0x620`: index = ((((y & w) << z) + (x & z)) << log2 samples) + (sample & mask), with {mask, log2 samples, z, w} at `c0 + 0x630` and x, y the pixel (`gl_FragCoord` over the render scale); each byte holds the sample's x (low nibble) and y in 1/16 pixel. The replay filled neither row, and the build refused any computed index into `c0`.
- **Fix:** the translator writes the sample index for `PIXLD.MYINDEX` (`IoVariable.SampleId`, SPIR-V `SampleId` with `SampleRateShading`; MSL `[[sample_id]]`, so the fragment function runs per sample on a multisampled target, and SPIRV-Cross moves `gl_FragCoord` to the sample); the other modes still log. `encode_args` fills both rows for the pixel stage from the pass's sample count (`c0_sample_rows`: Metal's default positions, the same for every pixel, so the grid masks are 0; one sample is 0x88, the pixel centre), and `dmsh_build.py` counts them as filled for pixel shaders (`SAMPLE_ROWS`). **NVN2's table is signed from the pixel centre** (upstream `224ecb186`): PRAGMATA's 154 readers compute `(byte ^ 0x88)` nibbles / 16 - 0.5 and add it to the pixel centre, and its pixel shader `4a9d4507eea6c837` passes the nibbles unchanged to IPA's offset, so `c0_sample_rows` writes each coordinate minus 8 when the sdk is the Switch 2's (`dm_sdk_ounce`: one sample is 0x00, not 0x88); with the corner form every sample position read half a pixel up and left. NVN1's form is unchanged and unchecked (open), with a computed `c0` index accepted when the shader reads the 0x630 row, or when its range is proven (`index_range`: an integer expression of literals and the sample index, below 16, through single-assignment temporaries) to lie in the filled rows. `nvnProgramSetSampleShading` returns true: Metal has no switch to force per-sample shading, which a shader gets by reading the sample index.
- **Found on:** [Metroid Prime Remastered](../../dm/games/010012101468C000/README.md): 438 of the 562 pixel shaders of `MaterialArchive.arc` read the sample index; in 30 the translated shader keeps it, and there it reaches the table, whose byte the G-buffer shaders pack into an integer target (`04023899305e93e1`). Its targets are single-sample (1600×900), so sample 0 was right and only the table mattered: 0 there put every sample in the pixel's corner. No other game here reads either row (58 games' libraries checked). The nibble order for several samples is the hardware's, not seen in a game. [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): 156 pixel shaders read the byte at `0x620 + sample` without the grid and without the 0x630 row (`a689530d00090ebf`: `_m0[(sample + 1568) >> 4]`, row 98), and were refused (`c0 dynamic index`) until the build bounded the index; all 156 load, the other 6,753 of its shaders that read `c0` or `c2` keep their result. Check: `DriverReads.test_sample_byte_by_the_sample_index_is_filled_in_a_pixel_shader`.

### Texture and sampler pools with overlapping IDs

- **Symptom:** a menu's labels drawn as plain bars, other menus empty except for the cursor (one pool's textures overwritten by another's); a black screen when IDs past a fixed table read as empty slots.
- **Cause:** a game that binds two texture pools and two sampler pools whose IDs overlap; the replay had one ID table.
- **Fix:** each pool has a base in the table, in creation order; `RegisterTexture` / `RegisterSampler` add it, and binds recorded on a command buffer add the base of the pool that command buffer last set (`bind_handles` in `hle_nvn.c`, the base kept in `cmd_t`, [next entry](#bases-of-bound-handles-kept-in-the-command-buffer); log line `pool #2 (…): numbered from N`). Handles read from uniform buffers take the base of the pool bound at replay (DARK SOULS: REMASTERED, `ubo_tex_id` / `ubo_samp_id`, [Handles from uniform buffers take the bound pools' bases](#handles-from-uniform-buffers-take-the-bound-pools-bases)). Deferred binds are not remapped (red line; [Deferred texture binds read at execution](#deferred-texture-binds-read-at-execution)). The table's capacity is the sum of the pools.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md): the scene binds a 12,544-entry texture pool and a 3,856-entry sampler pool, the 2D UI's command buffer binds 4,096-entry ones, and both fill IDs from 256. The scene's default 8×8 white texture overwrote the UI's textures (the title menu's labels drew as plain bars). The scene registers each draw's textures into fresh IDs of a ring (8,729 and up a few seconds in), which used to read as empty slots and left the screen black.

### Bases of bound handles kept in the command buffer

- **Symptom:** red lines from the list of command buffers that bound a non-first pool (6,734 in one DARK SOULS: REMASTERED run).
- **Cause:** that list had 64 entries and was never cleared, and the game binds a non-first pool in more than 64.
- **Fix:** the bases of bound handles live in the command buffer (`cmd_t` `tex_base` / `samp_base`).
- **Found on:** [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md).

### Handles from uniform buffers take the bound pools' bases

- **Symptom:** distant walls and floors smeared sideways (DARK SOULS: REMASTERED): tiling textures took a clamp-to-edge sampler and repeated their edge column.
- **Cause:** the map materials read their combined texture / sampler handles from a uniform buffer (`fp_t_cb13_*`, resolved at replay by `ubo_handle`), and the game keeps several sampler pools, each with its own samplers under the same IDs, one per command buffer that records the scene. The replay resolved uniform-buffer handles in the first pool.
- **Fix:** the replay keeps the texture and sampler pools bound (`OP_SET_TEX_POOL` / `OP_SET_SAMP_POOL`, which it used to skip; a command buffer that binds none keeps the queue's, as on the GPU) and adds their bases to handles read from uniform buffers (`ubo_tex_id` / `ubo_samp_id` in `nvn_mtl.m`).
- **Found on:** [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md): the map materials `FRPG_Phn_*Lit*`; 12 sampler pools of 2,304 entries (ID 261 is clamp-to-edge with LOD 0..0 in one pool, repeat with mips in others); the walls' tiling base textures (u ≈ −3.45).
- **Check:** found by highlighting the six most common fragment shaders of the scene pass (`DM_NVN_HILITE`), then the next six, and reading the wall draw's binding lines (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP_ALL`).

### Deferred texture binds read at execution

- **Symptom:** the 3D scene black, only particles and the HUD drawn; pass dump `B` lines all `tid=0 dummy`.
- **Cause:** `nvnCommandBufferBindTexturesDeferred` passes a pointer to the handle, and "deferred" means the GPU reads the handle from that address *when the command executes*. A game that records its command blocks once, submits them every frame and writes the handles afterwards got the handles the host copied at recording, mostly zeros, so draws read the dummy texture 0.
- **Fix:** the bind record keeps the source address (`p_bind_handles.defer / src`) and every replay-time reader refreshes the handles first (`bh_fresh` in `nvn_mtl.m`).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): the engine binds almost every texture this way (`main+0xec2e64`, one handle, the pointer being the handle field inside the texture-sampler object); the deferred-lighting composite (one full-screen triangle per material class, blending into the HDR target) read texture 0 for all of its inputs.
- **Note:** how it was found: a frame data-flow table built from `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP_ALL` (per pass: attachments, shader keys, sampled serials) showed the half-resolution lighting outputs never being read, and the composite pass's `B` lines were all `tid=0 dummy`. Without `_ALL` the dump lists only the first draw of each pass, which hid the composite. Deferred binds are not remapped by pool base ([Texture and sampler pools with overlapping IDs](#texture-and-sampler-pools-with-overlapping-ids)).

### Handles in uniform buffers written earlier in the same submission

- **Symptom:** bindless handles read from a uniform buffer name the wrong texture when the game writes them earlier in the same submission.
- **Cause:** the replay reads handles on the CPU, and a write queued earlier in the submission has not landed yet.
- **Fix:** inline uniform updates keep where their data sits, so the handle is read from the newest one covering it (`pend_read`). The old limit of 256 writes per submission, after which every address counted as written, is gone: pending writes are now indexed by 4 KB page (8,192 per submission).
- **Found on:** [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md) (it queues more than 256 writes per submission).

### Storage buffer addresses in the game's own uniform buffer

- **Symptom:** a storage buffer reported with `cb_slot` 3, which had no NVN slot.
- **Cause:** the shader reads a storage buffer's address from the game's own uniform buffer (c3 + 0x38), which Ryujinx reports with `cb_slot` 3.
- **Fix:** the build marks it `sbuf_ubo` (uniform slot `cb_slot - 3`, byte offset), and the replay reads the 64-bit address there at bind time, as it reads bindless handles.
- **Found on:** [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md) (about 30 compute shaders).

### Global memory at an address loaded from memory

- **Symptom:** whatever a shader writes through a pointer it read from memory never happens. The build summary counted `Failed to find storage buffer for global memory operation "Store"`, and the shader's MSL had a store function with an empty body. In Donkey Kong Bananza the tunnel's walls and floor were black and only the loose rocks drew.
- **Cause:** Ryujinx turns a global memory access into a storage buffer access by tracing its address back to a constant buffer entry (`GlobalToStorage`). An address the shader loaded from memory traces to none, and Ryujinx dropped the access: a store did nothing, a load read 0. Donkey Kong Bananza's gsys vertex shader `c9a56f796ea4a4cd` draws 96 points every frame. Each point loads one indirect draw record's 64-bit address from its storage buffer and writes {0, 1, 0, 0, 0} there (count 0, one instance) before the terrain's culling geometry shader adds its triangles to the count. Without those stores the records kept instance count 0, and their counts grew frame after frame. NVN2 draws instance count 0 as nothing. Its indirect draw (`sdk+0x6049b0`) calls macro 27 (driver constants at 0x640) and macro 40 with the record's five words. Macro 40, decoded with Mesa's MME decoder after running the driver's macro upload (`sdk+0x60fd40`) in an emulator, skips its draw loop when the instance count is 0 and never writes the record.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain. Accesses traced to one or more storage buffers translate as before; only those traced to none change:
  - **Translator** (`GlobalToStorage.TryGenerateRawStorageOp`): reserves the host's pool table as a storage buffer under a sentinel constant buffer slot (`DMSH_KIND_GMEM_MAP`, `dmsh_abi.h` `DMSH_GMEM_MAP_*`). It finds the pool holding the address by the entry it chose last ([Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry](#raw-global-memory-lookups-that-start-from-their-last-entry)), else a tagged hint for its 2 MiB chunk, else a branchless binary search over the table's starts, adds that pool's delta and accesses the result through a physical storage buffer pointer. In MSL that is `reinterpret_cast<device uint*>(ulong)`.
  - **Why a delta:** a no-copy Metal buffer's `gpuAddress` is not its CPU address, so the guest address has to move by the pool's delta (GPU address minus guest address).
  - **Host table** (`nvn_mtl.m` `gmem_map`): built from the memory pools whenever they change, 8,191 ranges at most (a pool past that is not reached, with a red line). It lists only the pools something gave a GPU address in, and those are made resident while such a shader is in use ([Memory pools nothing gave an address to stay out of the raw pool table](memory.md#memory-pools-nothing-gave-an-address-to-stay-out-of-the-raw-pool-table)). Each 2 MiB chunk wholly inside an entry gets a hint in entry (chunk & 8191): the chunk as a tag, and the index of the entry holding it. A colliding chunk or a smaller pool searches.
  - **Write log:** where a store through a loaded pointer lands is known only once the GPU has run it. Counting it as a write anywhere refused every uniform shadow of the submission: Donkey Kong Bananza's tunnel ran at about 70 ms a frame, with 4,533 passes ended to land deferred updates. So each store or atomic also sets the bit of its table entry in a write log the host hands each draw (`DMSH_KIND_GMEM_LOG`, `gml_take`), and the completion handler reads the bits against the same table. The word is read first: a bit is set once, so nearly every store costs a load, not an atomic ([A shader that writes a whole frame through raw global memory](frame-rate.md#a-shader-that-writes-a-whole-frame-through-raw-global-memory)). A bitmap cannot run out of slots, as the hash set of pages it replaced did.
  - **Learned pools:** the host takes each logged entry's range. The stores count as a write anywhere until the learned pools have stayed the same over 8 logs, then as writes over those pools. A later log that shows another range adds it, without going back to a write anywhere: a write anywhere makes every later geometry draw of the submission wait for the GPU (`geo_cpu_hazard`), and going back for 8 logs after each miss gave Donkey Kong Bananza streaks of 300-2,000 ms frames (38 such frames in 5,400, 6 after the change, the geometry wait in one 25 s sample 3,490 → 264 ms). The host then checks whether the miss could have mattered: every uniform shadow taken from that submission on (its own and those of the later submissions encoded while it ran, each recorded with its fence) is compared with the ranges the stores wrote outside the prediction, whole pools now, so this check can report a shadow elsewhere in the pool. Only an overlap, or a record that has wrapped before the check, prints a `[RED]` line; a miss no shadow saw is logged with the pools' ages.
  - **Why pools, not pages:** the game allocates new records as the level streams in, in the same pools. Learned page by page, the tunnel still met a new page 213 logs in.
  - **Result in Donkey Kong Bananza:** 4 pools learned over 9 logs at first, 60 fps (p50 16.64 ms), and no pass ended to land deferred updates. Further into the level the stores keep reaching pools the set does not hold yet: 12 in a 17,700-frame session, 10 in a scripted 13,500-frame run. Those pools are not new: they were added 3.5 to 294 s before the miss (the game streams records into pools it made earlier), so a new pool would not have announced them. None of the 7 misses checked had a page under a shadow (3 to 5 shadows each).
  - **Still dropped:** an address in no pool. Atomics on 8- and 16-bit values are not implemented.
- **A full table loses geometry silently:** a pool past the table's end is not reached, so a load from it reads 0. Ocarina of Time holds about 4,170 ranges, and at the former 4,095 entries its foliage and moss were missing: black shapes where moss should cover them, and the sky through the ground (restored at 8,191 entries, and back again with the host capped at 4,095). The only sign was one red line (`raw global memory: N more memory pools than the … entries`), so treat that line as a picture fault.
- **Open:** the prediction is checked, not proven: a miss that does reach a shadow is reported (`[RED]`) after the frame, not prevented. The records' pointers come from memory, so the pools they will reach are not known ahead of the draw.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md): its terrain, and agl's compute shader `49bc2268925e25b6` (one load); rebuilding the chain changed only these two of Donkey Kong Bananza's shaders. Vertex shader `588e242a52f5f7aa` (five stores) has one too, the same shader in [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md) and [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md), so likely one the SDK ships; neither drew it in its regression route.

### Global memory at a storage buffer's address on some paths only

- **Symptom:** GPU-driven geometry never draws: its indirect draw records keep count 0 and its visibility buffer stays clear, although the culling compute runs every frame. A persistent culling kernel takes its work items (its work counter passes the item count) and writes nothing. Its build notes do not say `traced to no storage buffer`: the loads are traced, to a storage buffer the address is in on only some paths. In PRAGMATA the title's Earth and Moon and the intro's ship and cockpit were black.
- **Cause:** for an address that is a phi, `GlobalToStorage.TryGenerateMultiTargetStorageOp` collects the storage buffers its sources trace to and makes a helper that range-checks each one. A source it cannot trace (an address the shader loaded from memory) was skipped without a word, and the helper returned 0 when the address was in none of the buffers. That case is exactly the untraced source. PRAGMATA's cluster culling `251426e9d44fd558` reads each instance's mesh header at an offset of its push+0x170 buffer on one path and at an address from its table at push+0x110 on the other, in other pools. Every header read gave 0, every instance 0 clusters, so the chain after it (`3c5dde027a045df7`, `1b16be7a9ee2c490`, which fills the mesh draws' records) had nothing to count. Found with counters added to the kernel's MSL (a copy laid over the library from a folder beside its image): 59,000 items reached the header test, 0 reached the header read, 0 reached either output store.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain (`GlobalToStorage.cs`). A source that is neither traced nor another phi marks the access. Its helper keeps the range checks, and on a miss calls the pool table helper (`TryGenerateRawStorageOp`, [Global memory at an address loaded from memory](#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)) instead of returning 0. A tag after the buffers keeps it apart from a helper whose sources are all traced. An access the table cannot serve (an atomic on an 8- or 16-bit value) keeps the 0. The build notes say `traced to a storage buffer on some paths only`.
