- **Found on:** PRAGMATA (`0400C76024710000`): the title's 3D and the intro's mesh-shader geometry.

### Bindless constant buffer handles in the hardware's layout

- **Symptom:** an NVN2 shader that reads a constant buffer through a bindless handle (`cx[UR][offset]`) gets a dummy buffer or zeros, with `[RED] shader <key>: the buffer address the game pushed at c[24] + 0x… is 0x1800830776a28, in no memory pool: bound to a dummy buffer` (an "address" of 49 bits, with a small number in its top bits).
- **Cause:** the handle is not an address. Its layout is the hardware's (as NVK's `nvk_bindless_cbuf`): bits 0..44 hold the address >> 4 and bits 45..63 the size >> 4. The translator (`InstEmitAmpere.cs` `ABindlessConstant`) used the handle as the address, and that worked only for the handles the host writes itself (compute's uniform entries in c[0]), because the host wrote plain addresses there. A game that builds its own handles reached no pool. PRAGMATA pushes them: its SDF compute shader `b12078fe3bce1273` takes three at push block 1, `0x5a0083055d124` = 32 bytes at `0x83055d1240`, the other two 192 bytes each. Its pixel shaders also load such handles with `LDG.64` from the game's own buffers. An earlier attempt to decode the handle in the shader alone met the host's plain addresses: wild reads, a GPU timeout and page faults at the first 3D frame.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain and the host.
  - **Translator:** `ABindlessConstant` decodes the handle: the address is `(handle & (2^45 - 1)) << 4`.
  - **Storage pass** (`GlobalToStorage.cs` `TryUnwrapHandle`): traces that address (handle << 4) back to the handle's constant buffer words as it traces an address, and marks the target (`SearchResult.Handle`, bit 15 of a packed target). A multi-target helper compares the decoded address and size.
  - **Host** (`nvn_mtl.m`): writes NVN2's uniform entries in the hardware's layout (`ucb_handle`; NVN1 shaders read the plain address there, Link's Awakening's `c0[0x280]`). The readers of a pushed or a uniform-buffer word (`NVN_RB_SBUFQ`, `NVN_RB_SBUFU`) decode a handle (`ucb_handle_addr`): a handle of any size has a bit at 45 or above, which no guest address has, so a word with a plain address is read as before.
  - **Traces unchanged:** a 312-shader sample of PRAGMATA's library (every stage, with uniform, pushed and raw bindings) built with the same bindings and raw-access notes as before.
- **Open:** a read past the handle's size returns what lies there; the hardware returns 0.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA (`0400C76024710000`).

### Raw global memory through a pointer read when the draw is bound

- **Symptom:** a raw load reads 0 when its pointer names a pool left out of the raw table. Upstream: Ocarina of Time's eight culling task shaders then loop 134,217,727 times on two zero range ends, an isolated GPU hang (PR #1585's diagnosis).
- **Cause:** [the address filter](memory.md#memory-pools-nothing-gave-an-address-to-stay-out-of-the-raw-pool-table) lists the pools whose GPU addresses NVN handed out. A pointer the game derives can reach another pool. Refreshing the table at commit handles pools made after encoding, not these existing, unaddressed targets.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator, reflection and host (upstream NXNative `f15d65850`). The translator (`GlobalToStorage.FindPointerSite`) notes the fixed storage-buffer words a raw load's address comes from (`translator: gptr site k: c0[w] +byte`); `dmsh_reflect.gptr_sites` records up to `DMSH_GPTR_MAX` (64) `[NVN storage slot, byte offset]` sources on the existing `gmem_map` binding (`pointers`). Before argument-block reuse as well as a fresh encode, `gmem_discover` reads each complete pointer word (a covering queued inline update first), after checking the binding's size, the source pool and its sparse mapping, and a valid target admits its logical pool or mapped run through `mtl_pool_addressed`; the commit-time table refresh then applies. No extra GPU binding or lookup path.
  - NX 18: the sources live in a block of their own (`nvn_refl.gptr`, malloc'd, NULL without any), not upstream's 64-entry array in every shader's reflection (196 bytes a shader; see the reflection tail block). `pool_held` refuses an overflowing range.
  - **Not in yet:** the translator half (`GlobalToStorage.cs`, `ResourceManager.cs`, a rebuilt `dmsh_xlate`) is the translator group's part of the shader batch. Until it lands no reflection has `pointers`, and `gmem_discover` never runs.
- **Limits:** a pointer the GPU makes later, a dynamically indexed source or an unsupported source descriptor still relies on the addressed table. Admitted pools become resident, so memory can grow by the pools a game's pointers reach; the memory readout's `raw pointer discovery` line counts the words read and the newly addressed ranges (logical MB, not wired memory).
- **Found on:** Ocarina of Time upstream ([The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md)); not reproduced in NX 18.
- **Check:** `t_gmem_words` (an omitted target, the commit refresh, changed pointer data without rebinding, bounds, queued inline updates), `t_refl` (the metadata: limits, malformed sources, none), `test_dmsh_reflect.py` (`gptr_sites`). Upstream's `test_dmsh_raw_pointers.py` needs the translator half.

### Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry

- **Symptom:** a pass whose shaders read memory through loaded addresses takes several milliseconds on the GPU for little work. `[gpuprof]` names the pass, and its shaders' build notes say `Global memory operation "Load" traced to no storage buffer`. Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh shader `73dc0b90c4082e43` (`Foliage.Product.110`, 135 such reads) took 10.9 ms of a 683x384 frame; 2,241 of the game's shaders (vertex, compute, fragment and mesh) have such a lookup, some hundreds of them.
- **Cause:** each 32-bit word found its pool on its own ([Global memory at an address loaded from memory](#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)): the hint of its 2 MiB chunk, and on a miss the 13-step search of the pool table. A shader reads a structure word after word from one pool, so all but the first lookup of a run chose the entry the first one chose.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator (`GlobalToStorage.TryGenerateRawStorageOp`). Each helper keeps the entry it chose last in a private word of the invocation (`ResourceManager.AddRawLastEntryMemory`, zero at the start through a SPIR-V null initializer, `MemoryDefinition.ZeroInit`; spirv-cross declares it `uint _N = 0u;` in `main0` and passes it to the helper). An address inside that entry's range takes it without the hint or the search; a hint or a search records its choice. Loads, stores and atomics all do it, and a store's write log bit is the chosen entry's. It holds because the table's ranges are disjoint (`nvn_mtl.m` `gmem_map`) and the host binds one table for a whole draw or dispatch; entry 0 is empty, so an invocation's first lookup searches. `dmsh_meshstage.py` keeps thread variables declared ahead of the stage memory in `dm_stage_body`: until then Ocarina of Time's 98 mesh and 11 task shaders with such a lookup failed the mesh stage (`no kernel main0 starting with its stage memory`).
- **Shorter load check (upstream NXNative `736a2ecbb`, PR #1833):** the host stores a 32-bit byte span in word 7 of a pool entry (`gmem_map`) when the range fits within one 4 GiB address window and its length fits in 32 bits. A load can reuse the last entry by comparing the high address word and checking the unsigned offset against that span. A zero span sends loads through the full lookup, including for ranges that straddle a 4 GiB boundary. Stores and atomics use exact ranges and the submission's write log. Guest-visible NVN addresses stay CPU-compatible.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_last.py`: the translated helper's shape, and on the GPU its reads over runs of addresses that stay in, leave and come back to pools (adjacent pools, a gap, a hinted chunk above 4 GiB); the scalar and vector checks also cover a range straddling a 4 GiB boundary and a miss with the same low word in another window; without the cached entry's end check 768 of its 1,024 reads go wrong. `test_dmsh_meshstage.py` for the thread variables. Ocarina of Time on an M6 Mac (macOS 27.2), main's host, the 2,241 shaders of the new chain laid over main's libraries (the other 2,592 shaders built with them came out with the same MSL), the same scripted walk from `save_ref` with warmed pipeline archives, two runs a side: at the spawn point right after loading (the same view, about 2,500 draws a frame) the GPU span p50 went 16.53 / 16.41 → 14.11 / 14.57 ms, and over the walk 15.7 → 14.0 ms. Screenshots at the spawn point match, and so do the red lines.
- **Open:** over the walk the frame time p95 went 19 → 26 ms. The runs ended in different places (the route is timed and the game moves at its frame rate), the new side's render target loads were higher (the game's dynamic resolution taking the headroom), and the Mac had 8 GB of swap in use, so this is not settled. Keeping the range itself in registers (PR #1464's form, no table reads on a hit), tried on an M4 Pro (2026-11-08) as an overlay of Ocarina's eleven hot task and mesh shaders, changed nothing (GPU p50 16.45 → 16.61 ms, two runs each): the table reads hit the cache. The time went to the words of each 128-bit load being read one by one ([128-bit raw global loads read one word at a time](#128-bit-raw-global-loads-read-one-word-at-a-time)).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md); the diagnosis, the cache and the first measurements in [PR 1464](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1464) and [PR 1405](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1405#issuecomment-6678379920).

### Raw global lookups built once for every call

- **Symptom:** a pipeline whose shader reads memory through loaded addresses takes seconds to compile on a cold Metal cache, and the GPU waits at its first draw ([Native mesh pipelines compiled at their first draw](#native-mesh-pipelines-compiled-at-their-first-draw)); its library is large for its MSL. Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh shader `3439aaa071d868ae` makes 141 raw accesses; its pipeline took 1.9 s on an M2 Air, and the route's 54 task / mesh pipelines 25 s at the first start.
- **Cause:** the pool table lookup of a raw access ([Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry](#raw-global-memory-lookups-that-start-from-their-last-entry)) is a helper function, and spirv-cross marks every function `static inline __attribute__((always_inline))` (it ignores SPIR-V's `DontInline`). Metal's compiler built a copy of the last-entry check, the chunk hint, the binary search and the physical-pointer access at each access.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain. The translator puts the chunk hint and the binary search in one function per shader, `int dmsh_raw_find(address low, address high)`, which returns the entry (`GlobalToStorage.cs` `GetRawFindFunction`; `SpirvGenerator` keeps `dmsh_` names), and the access helpers call it past the last entry's check. The build (`dmsh_rewrite.py` `raw_noinline`, called from `dmsh_build.py` `finish` for every stage) writes that function `static __attribute__((noinline))`: one copy the misses branch to. The last entry's check, the entry's end, the delta and the access stay inline at each access, so a hit runs as before. Writing the whole access helper `noinline` ([PR 2769](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2769)'s first form) compiled faster still but put the last entry and the vector words behind a call at every access: on an M4 Pro, Ocarina's still Kokiri Forest scene took 17.4 / 17.7 ms of GPU time a frame against 15.0 / 15.2 on main, in every pass with raw accesses.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md), upstream on its main 74181da8 (2026-11-16; ported in NX 18's chain M4, not yet measured on NX 18 or the iPhone). Offline Metal 4 pipeline compiles (`metal-tt`, 5 runs each in turn, on an M4 Pro), main → this fix: `3439aaa071d868ae`'s mesh pipeline with pixel shader `bd09ab5fc4fd7680` 1.67 → 0.66 s for the M2 (`applegpu_g14g`; whole helper `noinline`: 0.48 s), 1.81 → 0.70 s for the M4 Pro (`applegpu_g16s`), its archive 874 → 425 KB; the vertex pipeline of `ffc2a130a97593c3` (216 accesses) with pixel shader `9ef3ef25d67ef099`, 1.38 → 0.73 s for the M2, 828 → 449 KB; the compute kernel `d15370a382630d4e` (384 accesses) 132 → 109 s (median of 3; most of its time is elsewhere), 3.2 → 1.6 MB. The 938 playtested shaders with raw accesses have main libraries of 233 → 109 MB; the playtested build compiled in 14 instead of 19 minutes. GPU time on the M4 Pro, the still Kokiri Forest route (`kokiri-still`, 1920x1080, warm pipelines, steady frames): 15.2 / 15.2 / 15.3 ms a frame on main, 14.8 / 15.0 with the fix. Earlier, with the whole helper `noinline` on an older chain (c39ed5ebd458) and a 16 GB M2 Air, the pipeline compiled cold in 1.92 → 0.46 s and the Kokiri Forest walk's frame time stayed within its runs' noise at 683x384.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_noinline.py`: `dmsh_raw_find` marked, the access helpers and a function of the same shape without the name left inline, the pass idempotent, and the result compiles with `xcrun metal`. `test_dmsh_raw_last.py` (with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`) runs the translator's scalar and 128-bit lookups on the GPU with the search inline and `noinline`.

### 128-bit raw global loads read one word at a time

- **Symptom:** a pass of mesh, vertex or compute shaders whose build notes say `Global memory operation "Load" traced to no storage buffer` takes milliseconds of its stage's time for little drawing, at any resolution. Metal System Trace's Shader Timeline shows no samples for task and mesh shaders, so such a pass looks idle there; the encoder's vertex-stage time and an overlay A/B tell. Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh pass took 4.6 ms of vertex-stage time a frame at 683x384, 0.2 ms of fragment.
- **Cause:** the emitters split an `LDG.64` / `LDG.128` into 32-bit loads (`InstEmitAmpereMem.ALdg`, Maxwell's `InstEmitMemory.EmitLdg`), each with its own 64-bit address. Traced to no storage buffer, each word then went through the pool table on its own ([Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry](#raw-global-memory-lookups-that-start-from-their-last-entry)) and was read through a pointer of its own, built from two 32-bit halves. Metal's compiler cannot see that four such pointers are consecutive, so one 128-bit read became four word loads, each waiting on its own address. Ocarina's foliage mesh shader `eed1add7c89f5d34` read 464 of its 506 raw words as pieces of 116 such loads. Measured on an M4 Pro: the lookups' table reads cost nothing (kept in registers, no change), and the reads were the time (replaced by a value made from the address, the frame's GPU time fell 16.8 → 13.0 ms).
- **Fix:** general, in the translator. The emitters mark the words of one access (`Operation.GlobalVectorCount`, `GlobalVectorWord`). For such an access whose address goes through the pool table, `GlobalToStorage` (`ReplaceGlobalMemoryWithStorage`, `GenerateRawVectorLoad`) calls a vector helper once (`…RawV2` / `…RawV4`, `TryGenerateRawStorageOp`): the first word's lookup, then one 64- or 128-bit load through a physical pointer (`StorageKind.GlobalMemoryVector`, the SPIR-V backend's `GenerateRawVectorLoad`, aligned as a word like the scalar loads). Word 0 is returned and the others go to the shader's `gmem_vec` local memory, which the loads of those words read right after the call (they become copies, so the pass does not take them for global accesses again). The first word's entry holds the whole access: an `LDG.64` / `LDG.128` is aligned to its size on the GPU, and every pool table entry starts and ends on a pool's 4 KiB boundary (`nvn_mtl.m gmem_map`). Ocarina's `eed1add7c89f5d34` makes 122 vector calls and 22 word calls instead of 506 word calls, and its MSL shrinks from 7,949 to 5,656 lines.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): the Kokiri Forest route (`bench.tsv` `kokiri-deck`, its partial shader set rebuilt, with [occlusion conditions read once landed](rendering.md#occlusion-conditions-read-once-landed)) on a 20-core M4 Pro went from GPU p50 16.3 / p95 18.6 ms at 34.7 fps (a 30 fps frame step) to 12.6 / 13.6 ms at 51.8 fps, frame p50 16.69 ms (60 fps most of the time), two runs each, picture unchanged.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_last.py` `RawVector` (with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`): an `LDG.E.128` through an untraced address translates to one call of a helper with one `uint4` load and no word call, and on this Mac's GPU each of its four words is the pool's.

### A 64- or 128-bit raw global load in no pool reads undefined upper words

- **Symptom:** a 64- or 128-bit load through an address in no pool (a null or stale pointer, a gap between pools) reads 0 in its first word and whatever the invocation's `gmem_vec` held in the others: the words of the previous vector load, or undefined data on the first. Before the change in [128-bit raw global loads read one word at a time](#128-bit-raw-global-loads-read-one-word-at-a-time), every word of such a load read 0. A shader that tests a loaded record's first word for 0 is unaffected; one that uses the other words (a bounding box, a count, a second pointer) gets garbage.
- **Cause:** the vector helper (`…RawV2` / `…RawV4`, `GlobalToStorage.TryGenerateRawStorageOp`) returned 0 on a miss but wrote nothing to `gmem_vec`, which spirv-cross declares in the entry point without a value and the caller reads right after the call.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator: before its miss return, the vector helper stores 0 to each of the other words, as the word loads it replaced read on a miss.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_last.py` `RawVector.test_a_miss_zeroes_the_other_words` and `test_a_64_bit_miss_zeroes_its_other_word` (the 128- and 64-bit helpers' miss paths store 0 to `gmem_vec[0..2]` / `gmem_vec[0]`) and `test_reads_on_gpu`, whose kernel now sets the three words to `0xdead` before each call: a read in no pool must leave all four 0. All three fail with the previous translator (on the GPU, 256 of its 1,024 reads: every miss).
- **Found on:** found by reading the translated vector helper; no game's wrong picture is traced to it yet.

### A word of one 128-bit load read from another's vector

- **Symptom:** wrong data, no error: of two `LDG.64` / `LDG.128` in one block, a word of the second reads a word of the first load's memory, and the second load's own memory is never read.
- **Cause:** `GenerateRawVectorLoad`'s follower scan took any later load with the same word count and a word above 0 as a word of its call. When the first load's later words are unused (so the scan does not end at its last word) and the second load's word 0 is unused too (so it has no call of its own), a used later word of the second load became a read of the first call's `gmem_vec`.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator: the emitters give each access's words a group of their own (`Operation.GlobalVectorGroup`, counted by `EmitterContext.GlobalVectorGroups`), and the scan ends at a word of another group. That word then takes a lookup of its own.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_last.py` `RawVectorPair` (two adjacent unpredicated `LDG.128`, the first using word 0, the second word 2): one vector call, no `gmem_vec` read, one scalar lookup for the second load. It fails with the previous translator, which read `gmem_vec[1]`.
- **Found on:** reported by lunks on PR #1813 from reading the code; none of Ocarina of Time's 2,055 shaders that call a vector helper has the pattern.

### Bindless handles through a loop phi

- **Symptom:** `no resolved slot number`, the shader does not load; the translation has a 32-entry texture-pool array (`fp_t2d_cb0_40000000`, handle type Direct).
- **Cause:** the handle is `c2[0x1A0] | 0x10500000` (separate texture slot 7, constant sampler 0x105), kept in a loop variable. Ryujinx's bindless elimination compared only the phi's direct inputs, gave up, and turned the access into an index into a 32-entry texture-pool array, which the replay cannot bind.
- **Fix:** the vendored translator now follows the phis that feed a phi, and treats two `constant buffer | constant` handles as the same value ([third_party/ryujinx/README.md](../../dm/third_party/ryujinx/README.md)). Those accesses become ordinary type-3 handles.
- **Found on:** [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md) (218 pixel shaders).

### Bindless reads whose handle phi also carries other values

- **Symptom:** none in the log: texture reads come back 0. Particle meshes collapse to a point (their scale reads 0), so effects are missing or show as dark patches. The translator's own message (`Failed to find handle source for bindless access of type "textureBuffer"`) used to be dropped, because `dmsh_xlate`'s accessor had no `Log`; its messages now go into the shader's notes, marked `translator: `, and the build summary counts them apart from unanswered queries.
- **Cause:** the handle reaches the read through a phi. Some inputs are c2 handle words, the others are not handles: undefined, or a value the compiler kept in the same register on a path where the predicated read does not run (float arithmetic, a texture result, the absolute value of a uniform word). Some phis merge two or more different c2 handles too (a float and a half-precision buffer). `TryConvertBindless` cannot trace one handle, and `GenerateBindlessAccess` refuses a phi with such an input, so bindless elimination deleted the read and wrote 0 to its results.
- **Fix:** only for a phi `GenerateBindlessAccess` refuses (`TryTableHandle` in the vendored `BindlessElimination.cs`, marked `nxnative:`), the read takes the c2 handles among the phi's inputs (a word, or a texture word ORed with a sampler word or a constant sampler). With one handle, it takes the phi's place. With several, one read is made per handle and the result is the read whose handle equals the phi's value at run time: the host fills c2 with the bound handles, or with slot indices for `tex_dyn_slots`. A candidate that other reads of the shader use as a texture of another shape is left out, so those reads are converted in a second sweep once every other read has registered its texture (`ResourceManager.GetTextureTypes`). An image store or atomic with several candidates is left alone, since one per candidate would write each of them. A phi made only of handle words keeps `GenerateBindlessAccess` (the dynamic arrays of [Texture slots picked at run time](#texture-slots-picked-at-run-time)), so no other shader changes: in FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE, the SPIR-V of the 69,449 other shaders is byte for byte what it was. A chain change.
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md): 58,122 reads removed (57,861 of them texture-buffer reads, mostly in Unreal's Niagara particle vertex factories), of which 58,096 in 3,197 shaders are now resolved: 21,380 with one handle, the rest choosing among 2 to 5 at run time. 26 reads in 14 shaders, whose handle comes from no c2 word, are still removed. Reported in GitHub issue #168.

### Texture slots picked at run time

- **Symptom:** the shaders' bindless arrays have no slot and used not to load; the translator falls back to a 32-entry array such as `array<texture_buffer<float>, 32> vp_tb_cb0_40000000`.
- **Cause:** the shader reads one of several c2 texture-table words, chosen by branches. With separate textures (Octopath Traveler 0) it ORs the word with a sampler-table word; with combined ones (Paper Mario) the handle is read from the combined texture table (c2 `0x20 + 8 × slot`, constant offsets only). The translator cannot trace that to one slot and falls back to Ryujinx's bindless arrays, `array<texture_buffer<float>, 32> vp_tb_cb0_40000000` and `array<sampler, 32> vp_s_cb0_40000000`, indexed by the handle's texture ID (low 20 bits) and sampler ID (high 12), capped at 31; the build leaves them without a slot.
- **Fix:** for buffer textures (and images). A sampled texture of another kind indexes the whole texture pool instead, whatever the handle's source ([Texture handles from game memory index the pools](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools)), which took over Paper Mario's cube and 2D arrays. With the profile key `tex_dyn_slots` the reflection reads them as `NVN_RB_TEXD` / `NVN_RB_SAMPD` arrays (`nvn_refl.m`), the replay writes slot indices into that shader's c2 tables (texture slot r → r, sampler slot r → r << 20; in the combined table `r | r << 20` in slot r) and binds slot k at element k, so the shader's own arithmetic lands on the binding: the handle names element r, which holds the texture bound in slot r. The array's other elements hold whatever the other slots do, mostly 2D textures where the array is of cubes; an array element bound to a 2D texture gets the zero cube without a type mismatch report (`tex_fit_elem`), since the shader does not pick it there. The host logs each such shader's first encode with the slots that held textures. Host only: the chain version does not change.
- **Check:** check first that the handles come only from c2's tables: in Octopath Traveler 0 every integer read of c2 in those shaders is a table slot (texture slots 0-10, samplers 0-4).
- **Found on:** [Octopath Traveler 0](../../dm/games/01005270232F2000/README.md): 61 Unreal vertex shaders, particles reading their data from buffer textures (separate textures and samplers). [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md): 2,077 particle vertex shaders. [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md): 2,386 pixel shaders (5,476 cube samples, 487 2D), slots 0, 7, 8, 13 and 28 (combined table), until the pools took them over.

### A texture chosen at run time between two uniform-buffer handles

- **Symptom:** Ryujinx's fallback arrays (`fp_t2d_cb0_40000000`) in pixel shaders whose handle comes from the game's own uniform buffers; the shaders do not load and their draws are skipped.
- **Cause:** the handle is one of two words of the game's own uniform buffers, chosen by a flag, packed as texture ID | sampler ID << 20. `tex_dyn_slots` covers only handles from the driver's c2 tables ([Texture slots picked at run time](#texture-slots-picked-at-run-time)).
- **Fix:** the pools: whichever word the flag picks, its texture ID indexes the texture pool ([Texture handles from game memory index the pools](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools)). Not checked on Big Brain Academy, whose files are not on the Mac the change was made on.
- **Found on:** [Big Brain Academy: Brain vs. Brain](../../dm/games/0100620012D6E000/README.md): ten pixel shaders (six in `03GamePerception.bnbshpk`, two PBR, two in `main`); in `e10f24835bbaa5f6` the handle is c3 +0x130 or c12 +0x1d0, chosen by a flag in c12. None of the activities checked uses them; a long run of activities met one once.

### Texture handles from game memory index the pools

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: a texture handle the translator could not trace to one slot (profile key tex_dyn_slots off)`, then `failed to load`, and the draws are skipped: in Mario Party Superstars every character and NPC in a lit scene is a flat grey silhouette (the depth pre-pass draws, the colour pass does not), and the opening scene's pipe is grey.
- **Cause:** a lit pixel shader loops over the lights, reading each light's shadow map, cube map and sampler handles from the game's storage buffer (`fp_s1`) at a computed offset. Ryujinx cannot trace such a handle to a slot and falls back to arrays indexed by the handle's IDs (`array<texturecube<float>, n> fp_tcube_cb0_40000000`, `array<sampler, n> fp_s_cb0_40000000`, texture ID in bits 0–19, sampler ID from bit 20), clamped to `n − 1`. The translator's accessor gave `n` = 32 and the replay had no pool to bind there: only `tex_dyn_slots` arrays, whose handles must come from c2.
- **Fix:** a chain change. The accessor sizes these arrays to the whole ID ranges (`Program.cs` `QueryTextureArrayLengthFromPool` 2²⁰, `QuerySamplerArrayLengthFromPool` 2¹²) for sampled textures other than buffer textures; buffer textures and images keep 32-entry slot arrays (vendored `BindlessElimination.cs`, `NvnSlotArrayLength`). `dmsh_reflect.py` `pool_arrays` turns each such array into a pointer to `struct spvPool_<name> { uint n; array<element, 1> e; }`, read as `(&e[0])[min(index, n)]`, and renumbers the argument IDs (a pointer takes one). The struct declares one element because Metal's argument encoder walks every element of a declared array (`AGX::IndirectArgumentLayoutGen2::parseArray`): with 2²⁰ textures each pool shader's encoder took 57 ms and 66 MB, and the prewarm of 733 pipelines left the Mac host at 12.5 GB resident (9.7 GB of large mallocs); the elements are 8-byte resource IDs, so the pointer reaches the others (checked on the GPU with a test kernel); the reflection names them `tex_pool` (with the element type) and `samp_pool`. The host keeps one buffer per element type and pool base (`nvn_mtl.m` `pool_gpu`): the bound n, then from `DMSH_POOL_HEAD` the resource ID of each texture (sampler) ID of the pool bound at the draw, the dummy where an ID holds nothing or a texture of another type and at n. It is rebuilt when a registration or a texture's storage changes (`g_reg_gen`, `g_ser_gen`), the old buffer released after its submissions, and an argument block holding a pool address is not reused past a rebuild. Handles read from game memory are the bound pool's IDs, so the pool takes that pool's base (as `ubo_tex_id`), and a pool shader's c2 holds the bound handles without the base `bind_handles` added, as the game wrote them. Every texture is already in the residency set. A walk over every ID also reaches IDs no draw binds, which a game leaves registered after it is done with them: the first walk once crashed in `nvn_sparse_protect` making such a texture (a garbage `pool->vp`; the ID was not caught). So a texture no draw has made is made only while its storage exists (`tex_storage_live`: the memory pool object still holds its magic, and an ordinary pool's storage is in a pool the host maps), and an ID whose memory pool the game finalized is skipped until it is registered again (`treg_t.gone`, set in `treg_drop_pool_views`). Every other route is covered too: [A texture read after its memory pool object was freed](nvn-objects.md#a-texture-read-after-its-memory-pool-object-was-freed).
- **Found on:** [Mario Party Superstars](../../dm/games/01006FE013472000/README.md): 6,370 of its 82,790 shaders, every character and NPC material (`_chara/pc/*.bnbshpk`, `_chara/npc/*.bnbshpk`) and some board variants (`hsmn_bg.bnbshpk`); a 2,304-entry texture pool (1,286–1,314 2D textures, 65 cubes) and a 512-entry sampler pool, about 0.1 ms per rebuild once the textures exist.

### Handles a shader computes index the pools

- **Symptom:** `[RED] <key>: the translator deleted N bindless accesses (<type> N): their results read 0` for thousands of shaders, and `lost without failing: …` in the build; textures read as black (PRAGMATA: 65,241 reads in 8,630 shaders, its intro a saturated cyan).
- **Cause:** the handle is no word of any buffer but a value the shader computes: RE Engine's materials read a heap index from game memory (`cx[UR][off]`) or from a uniform buffer at a computed offset, clamp it to the heap (0x100–0x7fff), and OR it with a sampler ID (`| 0x10800000`, or with a separate sampler word of c2). `GenerateBindlessAccess` read such a handle through [the pools](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools) only when it was loaded straight from a constant buffer, an input or a storage buffer (Ryujinx's `IsBindlessAccessAllowed`, kept there to spare its pool walks), so every other read was deleted.
- **Fix:** a chain change. A sampled texture other than a buffer texture indexes the pools whatever made its handle (vendored `BindlessElimination.cs`): its texture ID indexes the texture pool and its sampler ID the sampler pool, as on the GPU. Images and buffer textures keep the old rule, since their slot arrays are bound only for handles from c2's tables.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): no access is deleted after the change. Any game with deleted sampled reads in its build log ([Losses a shader build reports](#losses-a-shader-build-reports)) changes with it.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py GuardedWrites.test_a_computed_handle_reads_the_pools`.

### A handle loaded from memory on one path only

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding … has no resolved slot number` at load, then `the dispatched program has no compute shader` each time the game dispatches it; the build log has `Bindless handle taken from the one constant buffer handle among its phi inputs` for the shader.
- **Cause:** the handle is a texture ID ORed with a constant sampler, and the ID's register holds a word loaded from memory on the path that reads the texture and something else on the other (a pushed buffer address's high word, `c[24] + 0x114`). `TryTableHandle` walks the OR's phi inputs for words of a uniform buffer, did not count the loaded word (a global load `GlobalToStorage` served is a copy of its helper's result, not a `Load`), and took the stray word alone as the handle: a binding the host has no slot for.
- **Fix:** a chain change (vendored `BindlessElimination.cs`). For a read that [indexes the pools](#handles-a-shader-computes-index-the-pools), a handle with a loaded word among its phi inputs (`IsLoaded`: a load, a call, or a copy that keeps a storage kind), alone or as a source of the OR, is left to `GenerateBindlessAccess`: the read takes the texture its ID names, whatever path made it. Images and buffer textures keep their candidates.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): compute shader `02e1292114121308`, which reads a material's texture by an ID from a material record.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_nvn2_buffers.py -k test_a_texture_id_loaded_on_one_path_indexes_the_pools`.

### One handle read as a texture and as an array

- **Symptom:** `[metal failed]` in the build, the compiler's message `vector component access exceeds type 'float2'` on a line that samples a `texture2d_array` with a two-component coordinate and `uint(rint(coord.z))` as its layer; the shader and its alpha test entry points do not build.
- **Cause:** a handle has one binding, declared with one type, but Ryujinx gave the declaration the type of the last instruction that read it and the descriptor the first accurate one, while each instruction built its coordinates for its own type. A 2D read (`TEX` 2D at level 0) of a binding another instruction read as `ARRAY_2D` (`TXD`) gave no layer, which is invalid SPIR-V that SPIRV-Cross wrote out as it was.
- **Fix:** a chain change (`ResourceManager.UnifyArrayTypes`, run once every function is translated). A 1D or 2D binding that some instructions read as an array is an array for all of them, in the declaration and in the descriptor the host binds by; an instruction that gives no layer reads layer 0, which is what the GPU reads through a non-array instruction. Level-of-detail queries take no layer either way. Cube and 3D bindings and arrays of handles are left as they were.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): 24 pixel shaders (`21ba8fea5a5eb03c` is one), each reading `cb65542` handle `0x3041000E` both ways.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_nvn2_buffers.py -k test_a_handle_read_as_2d_and_as_a_2d_array_is_an_array`.

### An array of handles in a uniform buffer

- **Symptom:** the host stops at the shader's first load: `unexpected argument buffer layout: encodedLength=640 maxAid=48`.
- **Cause:** a loop reads one handle per light from the game's uniform buffer at a computed offset; Ryujinx's texture array from a buffer indexes it by the handle's byte offset ÷ 8 (an NVN handle is 64-bit, the shader computes `(word >> 2) << 1 | (word & 3) >> 1`). The build reads the name as one bindless texture (`tex_ubo`, offset 0), so the argument buffer was 32 entries longer than the reflection said.
- **Fix:** the host's reflection reader takes the array length from the MSL type, and the replay fills element k from the handle at byte 8k of that uniform slot (`NVN_RB_TEXU` / `SAMPU` with `n`); elements whose bytes are not a registered texture or sampler ID read the dummy, silently. No chain change: the build already wrote the array type. Sampled textures have since moved to [a table the replay builds](#a-table-of-handles-longer-than-the-array); this form remains for images and buffer textures.
- **Found on:** [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md): the reflection shaders (`reflection.bfsha`: `array<texture2d<float>, 32> fp_t2d_cb14_0` and its samplers), c14 (uniform slot 11). The first run showed elements 0–3, 6 and 8–12 holding textures, among them the shader's fixed handles at +8, +16 and +24 and the lights' from +64.

### A table of handles longer than the array

- **Symptom:** characters and other moving objects have almost no ambient light: lit only from where the sun reaches, black underneath, while the scenery around them is right. Nothing is red. On Kirby Air Riders a dark red Kirby on a bright course, where the game's own tutorial movie shows him bright pink.
- **Cause:** an [array of handles in a uniform buffer](#an-array-of-handles-in-a-uniform-buffer) was declared with 32 elements and its index clamped to the last, but the game's table is longer. Kirby Air Riders' compute shader `f59db619b5c0be33` traces each object's eight light probe values from atlases named in c[4], and its index was 390 for the player's objects: clamped to element 31, which held nothing, so the eight values were black and the vertex shaders that read them lit the character with nothing. Objects whose probes happened to sit under element 32 were lit. The table has 197 handles up to element 392, in a buffer of 4,096, and nothing in the shader tells how long it is.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `Program.cs` `QueryTextureArrayLengthFromBuffer` gives a game's uniform buffer 8,192 elements (64 KB of 8-byte handles), `dmsh_reflect.py` `pool_arrays` turns such an array and its samplers into pointers with a run-time bound, and the replay builds the table from the buffer (`nvn_mtl.m` `htab_gpu`), as long as the last handle, looking again in each pass and keeping the buffer when the elements are the same. Images and buffer textures stay arrays of 32, bound element by element. Reading these handles through [the pools](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools) instead gave the same picture and cost too much: the walk over 65,824 IDs made all 34,000 of the game's registered textures, 16 GB resident against 9.7, and 9 to 25 fps against 60.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_pool_arrays.py` checks the rewrite and the reflection.
- **Found on:** [Kirby Air Riders](../../dm/games/040077201BE50000/README.md): most of its lit materials and light probes.

### Reads of a handle table marked through a null address

- **Symptom:** a pass that samples through [a table of a uniform buffer's handles](#a-table-of-handles-longer-than-the-array) takes hundreds of milliseconds on the GPU; nothing is red and the picture is right. In Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave on an M1 Pro, the lighting pass at Alcineus Bridge took 196.7 ms and the scene ran at 4 fps.
- **Cause:** `dmsh_reflect.py` `pool_arrays` gave the table's texture reads the same mark as [the pool's](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools) (`dm_pool_mark`, an atomic load, and an OR when the bit is clear, at the bitmap address in the buffer's header). `htab_gpu` never set that address, so every read went to address 0. The mark had no use there anyway: a table's index is its element, not a texture ID, and the replay has made every texture the table names before the draw.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `pool_arrays` marks only reads of the texture pool (`_cb0_40000000`); a table is read plainly, and a fragment shader with no other mark keeps its depth test where Metal puts it. The header keeps its layout, with the address 0 in a table. PR #1738 (HammerChain), which found the null address, pointed it at a premarked bitmap in the host instead; with that the bridge ran at 56.1 fps and the lighting pass took 2.35 ms (M1 Pro, 1080p output from 960x540). The chain change takes the mark away as well, which leaves every table read a plain load.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_pool_arrays.py` (`test_table_of_uniform_handles`).
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md#rendering) at Alcineus Bridge.

### A handle table kept past a write to its handles

- **Symptom:** a draw or dispatch that reads [a table of a uniform buffer's handles](#a-table-of-handles-longer-than-the-array) samples the texture or sampler of an earlier draw: the one before an inline uniform update (`nvnCommandBufferUpdateUniformBuffer`) in the same pass, or, in a submission with compute work only, the one the previous submission read. Nothing is red.
- **Cause:** `nvn_mtl.m` `htab_gpu` kept a table without reading its handles again while the pass serial, the registrations and the size were the same. Neither covers a write queued in the submission over the handles (an inline update an open pass defers, a copy or a fill), and a compute-only submission keeps the last render pass's serial, so the guest memory read in the submission before was taken for this one's.
- **Fix:** a table is kept only in the same pass and submission and when no write queued since its last look lands on its bytes (`htab_written`: `pend_since` scans the writes queued since then, or any when the host lost count). Otherwise its handles are read again as the GPU will see them (`ubo_read` with the queued inline updates); changed handles go into a new buffer, as commands already encoded hold the old one, and the same handles keep it. A write elsewhere keeps the table without reading it, as games update other uniform blocks before nearly every draw; the table log line counts the reads. Handles written by a buffer copy, a fill or a shader are still read as the CPU sees them (see [Handles in uniform buffers written earlier in the same submission](#handles-in-uniform-buffers-written-earlier-in-the-same-submission)).
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/t_htab_update.m` encodes real draws and dispatches through `encode_args` with red and green textures and clamp and repeat samplers: full and partial inline updates between two draws in an open pass (the first draw's pixels unchanged), a write beside the table (kept without a read), two dispatches around an update, and a new compute-only submission. Main before the fix failed 8 of its checks.
- **Found on:** source review in PR #2228 (akratch); no game's symptom is attributed to it yet. It reaches the games that read handle tables: [Kirby Air Riders](../../dm/games/040077201BE50000/README.md), [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md#rendering) and [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md).

### An array of uniform-buffer handles with its sampler word in c2

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t2ds_cb196614_10570000 has no resolved slot number`, then `failed to load`; those draws are skipped, and the scene is dark or black where it is lit by them.
- **Cause:** handle type 1 on [an array of handles in a uniform buffer](#an-array-of-handles-in-a-uniform-buffer): the texture words in the game's c[6], one separate sampler word in the driver table (slots 0x30006: the high half is bank + 1, so bank 2; word 0x15c, c2 byte 0x570). `decode_ubo_tex` took only a uniform buffer for that word.
- **Fix:** `decode_ubo_tex` answers ('c2', byte), the reflection gives the sampler `sreg` `DMSH_SREG_C2`, and the replay takes every element's sampler from that c2 word (`sampu_id`), whose IDs are moved already, as those of `NVN_RB_SAMPA`.
- **Found on:** [Kirby Air Riders](../../dm/games/040077201BE50000/README.md): about 5,700 pixel and compute shaders, its shadows and lights.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_reflect.py` (`UniformArraysWithDriverSampler`).

### A table of handles past the array's length

- **Symptom:** characters shaded black; a shader indexes a table of cube maps, and every element it reads clamps to the array's last one, which holds no handle.
- **Cause:** the shader reads `c[14][8 * i + 0x340]`. `BindlessToArray` starts an array of handles at the buffer's start, so with a base past the array's length (32) each index ran off its end.
- **Fix:** `TryArrayBase` finds an index plus a constant base; when the base is past the length, the array starts at the base and the index is the handle's place in the table. `nvn_mtl.m` fills element k from the array's offset + 8k (`NVN_RB_TEXU`, `NVN_RB_SAMPU`). A base inside the array keeps the array at 0, as before.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md): `e496014da6497fad` and the character shaders.

### An array of handles from the driver table c2

- **Symptom:** `shaders with a binding whose slot was not decoded 588`; shadow cascades read through `array<texture2d_array<float>, 32> fp_t2das_cb2_0`.
- **Cause:** Ryujinx's texture array from a buffer, as Paper Mario's uniform-buffer arrays ([An array of handles in a uniform buffer](#an-array-of-handles-in-a-uniform-buffer)), but over the driver table c2, indexed by (0x80 + a cascade offset) ÷ 8.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_reflect.py` `c2_array` names it `tex_c2a` / `samp_c2a` with the c2 byte offset of element 0; the replay fills element k from the slot whose c2 word is at that offset + 8k (combined, else separate), and the dummy elsewhere (`NVN_RB_TEXA` / `NVN_RB_SAMPA`, `c2_handle`).
- **Found on:** [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md) (586 pixel shaders, the shadow cascades).

### Arrays of c2 handles cover the whole table, with a separate sampler word

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t2d_cb196610_10570000 has no resolved slot number`, then, with that resolved, `… reads driver-bank locations at run time that the replay layer cannot fill: c2 dynamic index`; the shader does not load and its draws are skipped.
- **Cause:** two gaps in [An array of handles from the driver table c2](#an-array-of-handles-from-the-driver-table-c2). The translator sized every texture array from a buffer at 32 elements (`Program.cs` `QueryTextureArrayLengthFromBuffer`) and clamps the index to the last one, but over c2 the index is any handle word's byte offset ÷ 8: EA SPORTS FC 27's pixel shader `dc446d7f52c14ea6` loops over separate texture slots i = 1, 2, …, reads each handle word at c2 byte 0x168 + 8i and samples element 45 + i, so every element it picked was clamped to 31 (a combined slot). And the handle is type 1: `BindlessToArray` ORs one fixed separate sampler word (c2 byte 0x570, separate sampler slot 1) into every element's handle, which `c2_array` did not decode (it took only `_cb2_` with type 0), so the texture array had no slot and its sampler array took that one slot.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `Program.cs` gives an array over c2 one element per handle word up to the end of the separate sampler table (`C2HandleWords`, 301; other buffers keep 32), `c2_array` decodes type 1 with the sampler word in c2 too (`soff` in the reflection), and the replay ORs that word into each element's handle for the samplers (`NVN_RB_SAMPA`; `c2_handle` now also answers the separate sampler table). `nvn_rbind.n` is 16 bits and `NVN_REFL_MAXARG` 768 (such a pair takes 602 argument entries). The same loop reads each handle word itself at a computed c2 index (`fp_c2._m0[int(_237)]`, to test the light's handle), which `unfillable` refused for any bank; the replay fills c2's handle tables whole (`DRV2` in `encode_args`), so a computed index into c2 is accepted, and one into c0, filled only in its tables, still refused. Luigi's Mansion 3's cascade arrays become 301 long as well; the host fills the elements past the handles with the dummy.
- **Found on:** [EA SPORTS FC 27](../../dm/games/0100C49025D3E000/README.md) (its lights in a match).
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_reflect.py` (`DriverTableArrays`, `DriverReads`) and `test_dmsh_abi.py` (the translator's table length agrees with the header).

### A texture slot that shares its c2 word with an image slot

- **Symptom:** characters and props glowing white, about 10⁴ too bright, in DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined's opening cutscene only, while walls and gameplay look right.
- **Cause:** a size query (`GetDimensions` on a `RWTexture3D`) is a TXQ on the image's handle, c2 `0x120` (image slot 0): the build numbers it combined texture slot 32, the slot that shares that word, and with no type in the facts table the translator read it as 2D. The host bound the empty texture slot 32, and the 1×1 dummy's size passed only thread (0, 0, 0).
- **Fix:** two general fixes: a texture binding of slot 32–39 reads the image bound in slot 0–7 when there is one, as c2 holds it (`nvn_mtl.m` `tex_slot_h`; Hades II's texture slot 34 without an image still reads the texture), and a query on an image handle the facts do not type is translated again with the type of that image from the first translation (`dmsh_xlate` `ImageTypesOf`).
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined](../../dm/games/0100A9D01C446000/README.md): the per-pixel volumetric lightmap read brick (0, 0, 0) of the brick atlas for almost every cell. Unreal merges each streamed level's lightmap into one atlas on the GPU: brick copies (`0a7ff4171bd1a82a`), then a patch of the shared 16³ `RGBA8_UINT` indirection texture (`7b958a7b21e72801`) whose bounds come from `GetDimensions` on the very `RWTexture3D` it writes. Only cell (0, 0, 0) was written, every other cell kept size 0, and the shader fell back to the atlas origin: the first daytime level's bricks, baked about 2¹⁵ brighter (the same bricks in gameplay hold 0.5–1.4). [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md) has four such size queries (`cp_t_tcb_48` / `_4A`).
- **Check:** how it was found: the raw scene colour (`texraw`) put the characters at 1,000–2,000 and the walls at 0.05; a debug build of one character shader that wrote its terms in alternating 8-pixel columns isolated the volumetric lightmap ambient term; dumps of the atlas (`texlist` / `texraw`), of the uploaded level bricks (the 3D upload dump) and of the indirection texture, with the cell computed from the primitive's origin and View `VolumetricLightmapWorldToUVScale / Add` (View 220 / 221), gave the rest.

### A size query on a 3D texture gets a 2D slot

- **Symptom:** `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader … declares slot 128 as Metal type 2, texture ID … is type 7 (NVN target 2, …); bound as is`, for a shader whose MSL has a `texture3d` and a `texture2d` on the same register and only calls `get_width` / `get_height` on the `texture2d`.
- **Cause:** a size query (TXQ) carries no texture type, and a table built without the game's PC shaders does not answer one, so `QuerySamplerType` defaults to 2D. A handle ORs a texture word and a sampler word, and the query's has another sampler word than the sample's, so Ryujinx's merge by handle (`MergeTextureMeta`) keeps them apart. The shader then has a second binding of the slot, typed 2D, beside the sample's own `texture3d`, and the host has no 2D view of a 3D texture (`tex_fit`). In Princess Peach: Showtime!, 1,490 slots in 1,463 of 56,594 shaders had that pair. The same pair with a cube (27 slots, `get_num_mip_levels`) or a 2D array raises no red line, since those have a 2D view.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator: when a query's guess meets another type on its texture slot, `dmsh_xlate` translates again with that type (`TextureTypesOf`), as `ImageTypesOf` does for images. Types go by slot, as the answer table does; the table comes first, a query with nothing else on its slot stays 2D, and a slot two instructions give different shapes keeps the guess. The values these shaders read do not change: every query of the 1,490 3D slots reads only the width, or the mip count on the 27 cube slots, and an Apple M6 returned the right width through the old binding for all six sizes tried. What changes is that Metal now defines the binding.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_txq_type.py` (synthetic shaders; the fix's tests fail on the translator before it). Princess Peach: Showtime! has no such pair left, and its route into the ninja play went from 15 red lines to none, with the same picture at the same spot. Of the 69 local games' shader folders scanned, only two others hold the pattern: [Ghost Trick](../../dm/games/010029B018432000/README.md) (2 shaders) and [EA SPORTS FC 27](../../dm/games/0100C49025D3E000/README.md) (1), each with a 2D array.
- **Found on:** [Princess Peach: Showtime!](../../dm/games/01007A3009184000/README.md#found-on-this-game): up to 19 red lines per run, with no visible fault over the first play.

### More than 64 bindings in one shader

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: 66 bindings, over the limit 64`, then `failed to load`, and the draws with it are dropped (a black map).
- **Cause:** the reflection is converted into fixed arrays of `NVN_REFL_MAXB` bindings (`nvn_refl.h`), which was 64. NVN gives a stage 32 textures and 32 samplers beside its uniform and storage buffers and images, so a shader can have more.
- **Fix:** `NVN_REFL_MAXB` is 128; the argument buffer entries keep their own limit (`NVN_REFL_MAXARG`, 768, see [Arrays of c2 handles cover the whole table](#arrays-of-c2-handles-cover-the-whole-table-with-a-separate-sampler-word)). It was raised from 96 when every sampled sampler got a twin binding ([Border colours Metal has no sampler state for](rendering.md#border-colours-metal-has-no-sampler-state-for)): that shader then has 97 bindings.
- **Found on:** [OCTOPATH TRAVELER II](../../dm/games/0100A3501946E000/README.md): the 3D world map of the traveler selection (`UiMM_WorldMap3D`, pixel shader `f7c8373b7323687d`: 30 textures, 30 samplers, 3 uniform buffers and the c1 / support / driver segments).

### Argument buffer members with attributes after their id

- **Symptom:** the host stops at a shader's first load: `FATAL shader … has an unexpected argument buffer layout: encodedLength=88 maxAid=8` (crash in `c1_store` / `pull_load`), with a storage buffer or image missing from its reflection json.
- **Cause:** a pixel shader that writes a storage buffer and images inside a fragment interlock (the translator's FSI, `PixelInterlockOrderedEXT`) gets those members as `[[id(n), raster_order_group(0)]]` from SPIRV-Cross. `dmsh_reflect.py` `reflect` matched only `[[id(n)]]`, so it left them out, and the host's argument buffer came out longer than the reflection.
- **Fix:** `reflect` reads attributes after the id, and a member it still cannot read fails that shader's build (`reflection failed`) instead of loading with a short reflection. A chain change.
- **Found on:** [Mario Party Superstars](../../dm/games/01006FE013472000/README.md): `light_primitive_point_cluster.bnsh` (`56071a5cf62dccc3`, the engine's clustered point lights), first drawn on the way into a party.

### Image atomics

- **Symptom:** the replay put a texture resource ID where the shader wanted an address; with the old 64 KB dummy buffer, pointers in the game's objects and in the host's program objects got their upper 4 bytes replaced and the game or the replay crashed, or `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`, after which macOS ignores the process's command buffers (GitHub issue #101). Later, `[RED] image atomics on a block-linear texture … not followed, the atomics go to a scratch buffer`, and compute shaders with atomics on a 3D image did not build (`array subscript is not an integer`).
- **Cause:** below MSL 3.1, SPIRV-Cross emulates an image atomic with a raw device pointer beside the texture (`texture_buffer<uint> cp_i_cb2_4C_r32ui` and `device atomic_uint* cp_i_cb2_4C_r32ui_atomic`), indexed by the bound image's width (`spvImage2DAtomicCoord`), so the host had to bind memory in that packed layout. A buffer image's pool memory has it. A block-linear image's Metal texture is a deswizzled copy whose pool memory has another layout, so its atomics went to a scratch buffer that nothing read, and a pointer shorter than the image (or an invalid coordinate) wrote over other GPU memory. For a 3D image SPIRV-Cross indexes the pointer with the `int3` coordinate itself.
- **Fix:** image atomics are Metal's native texture atomics (MSL 3.1: `texture3d<uint, access::read_write>::atomic_fetch_max`). SPIRV-Cross writes every shader at MSL 3.2 (`dmsh_chain.CROSS_MSL`; until upstream 6dad34067ce only shaders with image atomics, an `OpImageTexelPointer`, at MSL 3.1 and the others at 2.3), and a shader whose MSL still has the pointer form fails the build (`dmsh_atomic.check_msl`). The atomics act on the texture every other access of the image uses (a buffer image's view of its pool, a linear image's pool memory, a block-linear image's Metal texture), for buffer, 2D, 2D-array and 3D images. Metal runs texture atomics only on an R32Uint / R32Sint texture made with `MTLTextureUsageShaderAtomic`: NVN images of those formats take that usage when they are made (`nvn_mtl.m` `img_usage`; a view keeps working through its base), the build marks the slots a shader uses atomically (`"atomic"` in the reflection, `nvn_rbind.atom`), and the host binds an atomic-capable write sink, logged red, in place of any other texture there (`dummy_img_atomic`). The translator's coordinate guard stays ([Bounds of image atomics](#bounds-of-image-atomics)).
- **Found on:** [Octopath Traveler 0](../../dm/games/01005270232F2000/README.md): 2,084 shaders with atomics on buffer images (Unreal's GPU particles; three ran in the first 100 seconds) and 16 on a 3D image. [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md): an atomic max into a 480x270 r32ui block-linear 2D array (fragment shader `2bd5037003980716`, the scene after the story opening's first cutscene), which ran about 1.4 MB past the 64 KB dummy buffer into zero-copy pools over the game's heap and into unmapped addresses (GitHub issue #101), then went to a scratch buffer. [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md) (8 shaders) and [Ghost Trick](../../dm/games/010029B018432000/README.md) (5, one of them a compare-and-swap): atomics on 3D images.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_image_atomic_bounds.py` with the rebuilt translator, `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 DMSH_TEST_GPU=1`: synthetic Maxwell atomics on buffer, 2D, 2D-array and 3D images, compare-and-swaps among them, run on native atomic textures of a Mac GPU under the Metal validation layer (`t_atomic_return.m`). `t_refl.m` checks the atomic flag of a reflected image.

### Buffer image reflection keeps its texture type

- **Symptom:** `image atomics: unsupported or overflowing IMG extent` at the first dispatch, despite a valid four-byte counter buffer.
- **Cause:** `nvn_refl.m` classified `texture_buffer<uint>` as the default 2D texture. The atomic admission check of the pointer emulation then rejected the actual Metal buffer texture against that 2D descriptor.
- **Fix:** the shared reflection type decoder recognizes `texture_buffer` as `MTLTextureTypeTextureBuffer`, so a buffer image keeps its buffer-image shape. `t_refl.m` checks the reflection converter with a buffer image.
- **Found on:** [SHIN MEGAMI TENSEI V: Vengeance](../../dm/games/010069C01AB82000/README.md), compute shader `2d2093c1f1d3a7e0`, image slot 2 (one R32Uint texel).

### Image atomics on a 32-bit colour texture

- **Symptom:** `[RED] image atomics on a texture Metal cannot run them on`, and whatever the shader computes through them is lost: its writes go to a sink.
- **Cause:** Metal runs texture atomics only on R32Uint and R32Sint textures and views. NVN lets a shader run them through an R32 view of any 32-bit colour texture: agl's anti-aliasing (`d17eefaff4f0bc23`) ORs its blend weights into RGBA8 that way, then reads it as RGBA8.
- **Fix:** `nvn_mtl.m` `tex_upgrade`, `tex_atomic_alias`: at the first such dispatch the texture moves to R32 storage holding the same bytes, and its own format becomes a view of that storage for sampling, rendering and copies. Packaged, multisampled, 3D and buffer-backed textures keep their storage. The design is [PR 1312](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1312)'s.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0400486024DEC000 check_t_image_alias`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### Bounds of image atomics

- **Symptom:** Metal shader validation reports invalid image-atomic stores even with an image-sized scratch buffer; without validation, GPU page faults can freeze rendering.
- **Cause:** the translator lost the surface instruction's `IGN` bounds mode before lowering an image atomic, so a coordinate outside the image reached memory. Neither a pointer nor a native texture atomic outside its image is defined on Metal.
- **Fix:** the translator (`TranslationFlags.MetalImageAtomics`, Metal only; other backends keep their behaviour) keeps the bounds mode and whether the result is used, queries the bound image's size and tests every original coordinate, the layer included, before the atomic. Negative coordinates fail an unsigned comparison; coordinates are not clamped or flattened. Invalid coordinates leave memory unchanged and return zero ([ARB_shader_image_load_store, issue 30](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/extensions/ARB/ARB_shader_image_load_store.txt)): a returning `IGN` atomic joins the old texel from the valid branch and zero from the other with an SPIR-V phi. A compare-and-swap (`SUATOM.CAS`: compare with Rb, swap in Rb+1) is Metal's texture compare-exchange behind the same guard; SPIRV-Cross loops `atomic_compare_exchange_weak` until it succeeds or the texel differs. Predicate-returning, `TRAP` and unknown modes fail translation, as do 64-bit, floating-point and half2 sources (checked before the descriptor format can erase their size), descriptors that are not R32 integers, bindless handles that do not resolve statically, and indexed or multisample images. Source 1D and 1D-array surfaces keep the translator's one-row normalization to 2D.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md#rendering) ([PR #178](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/178)); [SHIN MEGAMI TENSEI V: Vengeance](../../dm/games/010069C01AB82000/README.md), whose two buffer-image counter shaders dispatched at boot consume their returned values.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_image_atomic_bounds.py`. Portable tests check the MSL version choice and the rejection of the pointer form. With `DMSH_TEST_XLATE` set to a rebuilt translator (and `spirv-val` on `PATH`; spirv-cross is the in-repo build) the synthetic shaders are translated, the guard's dataflow is checked in the SPIR-V (the size query and comparison dominate the atomic, the phi carries zero on the invalid edge), and the MSL is produced; `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1` compiles and links it (`DMSH_TEST_METAL_SDKS=macosx,iphoneos` for both SDKs) and `DMSH_TEST_GPU=1` runs the returning fixtures (above). Changing this path changes the chain version: rebuild the translator and update the lock before building shaders.

### 64-bit image max and min (SURED.MAX.U64)

- **Symptom:** `NotSupportedException: Ampere 59f at …: SURED form 0110e400` in the shader build, then the scene's 3D picture black behind a UI that draws: `[RED] shader … (compute) failed to load` and `[RED] the dispatched program has no compute shader`. A texture dump shows the visibility target (RG32Uint) all zero.
- **Cause:** a visibility buffer written by compute rasterization keeps, per pixel, the nearest primitive as one 64-bit value, depth in the high word and the primitive id in the low word, through SURED.D.2D.MAX.U64 on an RG32UI image. The decoder took only the 32-bit sizes, and Metal's texture atomics were bound only on R32 textures. SPIRV-Cross has no 64-bit atomics at all.
- **Fix:** `ASured` sends the U64 form to `EmitSuatom`, which allows only a 2D, 2D-array or 3D max or min with no result (Metal has no 64-bit texture add, fetch or bitwise atomic). `GenerateImageAtomic` writes it as two 32-bit atomics of the same operation on the same texel, low word then high word, a marker that never runs. `dmsh_atomic.wide_atomics` turns each pair into one `texture2d<ulong, access::read_write>::atomic_max` / `atomic_min` (MSL 3.1) and fails the shader on any other access of that image. The host makes RG32Uint images with the shader-atomic usage on Apple GPU family 9, M3 / A17 Pro and later (`pf_atomic`), and the reflection marks such a binding `atom` 2. Older GPUs have no 64-bit texture atomic, and those pipelines do not build there. Ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up ([PR #1801](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1801)).
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): 336 compute shaders, among them `26166d37ed56d6bb` and `3bc957e2739551e7`, dispatched at the title.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py Sured64 WideAtomics`; `test_image_atomic_bounds.py` (the source-size gate).

### Reads past a uniform binding's size

- **Symptom:** the GPU hangs (Metal command-buffer timeouts, `GPU access revoked`, exit 70; on the Mac WindowServer can lose its watchdog check-in and the session restarts) or a draw reads garbage, where a shader reads a uniform slot further than the game bound it. No `[RED]` line.
- **Cause:** Maxwell bounds every constant buffer read by the size the game bound (`nvnCommandBufferBindUniformBuffer`): past it a read gives 0, and games rely on that. The translator declared each slot as `float4 _m0[4096]` behind a plain pointer, and Metal has no bounds of its own, so such a read reached the guest memory after the binding. Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door's `forward.bfsha` variant `f1e0f08cc43e21e3` reads its light count at byte 0x900 of uniform slot 11 and loops over that many lights, filling a 19-entry local array and reading c14 at `0x440 + 0x30 i`; the game binds 2,384 bytes to the slot for some materials and 16 bytes for others (501 of 1,024 bindings sampled in Peach's invisibility-potion scene). On the 16-byte bindings the count was whatever lay past them.
- **Fix:** in the chain and the host, for every game (`TranslationFlags.MetalUniformBounds`, Metal only), in two parts, since a slot's bound size is one value for a draw:
  - **Reads at fixed offsets, in the host, once a binding.** The translator leaves such a read as it is and reports how far they go in each game uniform slot (c[3] to c[16]): the bytes from the slot's start to the end of the highest word read so (`ResourceManager.NoteUniformRead`, `BufferDescriptor.FixedSize`, `fixed` in the translator's report and in the reflection's `cbuf` binding, `nvn_rbind.fixw`). When a slot's readable size (below) is shorter than that, the argument-buffer encode copies the binding into an arena block with zeros up to there and points the slot at it (`ub_padded` in `nvn_mtl.m`; the bytes are the ones the slot would have pointed at, the deferred updates' or a partly updated block's copy included), and logs the first such shaders with a running count (`uniform slot N of shader K (stage S) is bound with B bytes (R readable: rounded up to 256 where its pool reaches) and read up to byte E at fixed offsets`). Every other binding costs one comparison on the CPU. The code generator refuses a fixed read past the reported extent, so the two cannot disagree silently.
  - **Reads at computed offsets, in the shader.** The word's index is compared with the slot's size in words; out of bounds the read goes to row 0 and gives 0, so it reaches no memory past the binding (`TryGenerateBoundedUniformLoad` in `CodeGen/Spirv/Instructions.cs`). The sizes come from a table in a bank of its own, `DMSH_CBSIZE_BANK` (25), a u32 for each of the 14 slots in four `uint4` (binding kind `cbsize`, `NVN_RB_CBSIZE`), which the host fills from the current bindings at each argument-buffer encode, 0 for an unbound slot (whose reads already go to [the zero bank](rendering.md#an-unbound-uniform-slot-reads-the-zero-bank)), at most 64 KiB. Only a shader with such a read declares the table.

  **The size that bounds a slot is the bound size rounded up to 256 bytes** (`ub_bound` in `nvn_mtl.m`), which is what the console's driver gives the GPU: `nvnCommandBufferBindUniformBuffer` writes `(size + 0xff) & ~0xff` in NVN1's driver (method 0x8e0; Sonic Superstars' `sdk`, 0x467170) and `((size + 0xff) >> 8) << 20` in NVN2's (method 0xe02; Ocarina of Time's `sdk`, 0x67bc80). A read between the bound size and the next multiple of 256 gives the memory there. One rule serves every host reader of a binding: its readable size is the rounded size where a pool holds that much from the binding's address, else the bound size (`ub_live` / `ub_map`). A slot is padded only when that readable size stops short of its fixed-offset reads, the padded copy carries those readable bytes (the deferred updates', a partly updated block's shadow or the memory) and zeros after, the shadow of deferred updates and its check (`cbuf_slot`, `ubd_conflict_sh`) cover them, and the size table holds that size; the dirty and hazard checks (`ubd_dirty`, `csh_draw_reads`, `csh_cs`) take the rounded size. The padded copy's slot address is taken where it is written, before a shadow can move the arena to a new block. `t_arena_trim` case 6 binds 16 bytes read up to byte 512: the copy holds the deferred update, the memory to byte 256 and zeros to 512; case 7 checks a 192-byte binding read to byte 256 points at the memory, one at a pool's end is padded from its bound size, and the size table holds 256 and the bound size. The host used the exact size until [GitHub issue #2218](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2218); Ocarina of Time binds 16, 64, 144, 192, 464, 976, 16,960 and 41,600 bytes. Open: what an Ampere GPU returns past the rounded size is not measured (Maxwell gives 0), and whether this is what streaked Ocarina's animated models is not shown: the streaks did not appear on an M-series Mac here with the exact size either (the slingshot aimed at Link's house, the ReDeads of Castle Town).

  The driver segments (c0, c2), the shader's own c1, NVN2's push constants (c[24]) and compute's uniform pointers (`ubo_ptr`, global memory on Maxwell too) are not bounded. The host takes a uniform word for its own proofs only from inside the binding (`ubo_float` refuses a word past the size).
- **History:** the first form (chain `3510ea767778`) compared every read in the shader, fixed offsets too (`(576u < (sizes[2].w >> 2u)) ? c14[144].x : 0.0`). spirv-cross wrote each as a conditional expression, a branch for every read, until [they were written as selects](#bounded-uniform-reads-written-as-select) (chain `eaa843a79149`). The branch form cost little where shaders read few uniform words (NieR:Automata's opening flight, GPU p50 +1.6%) and a fifth of the GPU's time in Donkey Kong Bananza, whose material shaders read many: 14.6 → 17.5-18.1 ms a frame on the Freezer Layer on an M4, which took the game from 55 to 30 fps ([GitHub issue #2066](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2066), the reporter's measurement on their own build). Of the 8.3 million uniform reads in its 23,638 shaders, 95.5% are at fixed offsets, and 73% of the shaders have no other kind.
- **Found on:** [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md#rendering) ([GitHub issue #1860](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1860)): the reporter found the variant and the short bindings, and padding those bindings with zeros let Peach's scene through on an M3 Ultra Mac and an M4 iPad Pro. [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#found-on-this-game) ([GitHub issue #2066](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2066)): the cost of comparing every read.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_dmsh_abi.py` `test_uniform_bounds_agree` (the translator's bank and slots are the header's); `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` `test_fixed_uniform_reads_are_bare_and_counted` (with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`: two fixed reads of c[3] give no size table and `fixed` 28). On NieR:Automata's 1,225 shaders built with this change: all 148,022 reads at fixed offsets are bare, all 28,604 at computed offsets are compared (and `select_guards` writes each of them as a select, none refused), 455 shaders declare the size table, and each slot's `fixed` ends in the highest row its source reads at a constant index. Not run in a game yet (open): the host's padding has met no short binding, the potion scene's retest by the reporter of #1860 is open, and so is Bananza's frame time on the Freezer Layer. The first form's numbers, `bench.py` on NieR:Automata's opening flight (M6, two runs each, 60 s window): GPU time p50 6.43 → 6.53 ms (+1.6%), p95 9.12 → 9.70 ms (+6.4%), frame time unchanged at 60 fps. Changing this path changes the chain version: rebuild the translator, update the lock and rebuild the shaders.
- **Computed row bounds:** the translator compares an unsigned row index to the size table's byte count divided by 16 when it can prove the component is in 0–3. The host's size entries are multiples of 16. Comparing the row directly avoids a left shift that could discard the high bits, and the MSL rewriter keeps the load and select. Fixed-offset reads still use the reflected extent. In the author's Mario Kart World docked race (M3 Max, uncapped), the original row optimization reduced scene pass time from 2.43 to 2.34 ms and 2.42 to 2.23 ms in the two run orders. The translator implementation is common to every profile.

### Bounded uniform reads written as select

- **Symptom:** on an M1 Pro (macOS 26), blue, green and white blocks for one or two frames on trees that dither-fade (OCTOPATH TRAVELER's S'warkii, walking behind a tree); a texture dump shows NaN pixels in the scene colour and holes in the normal G-buffer target, which depth of field spreads into blocks. Not seen on an iPhone or on newer Macs. On every GPU, a branch for each uniform word a shader reads.
- **Cause:** the translator's bounded read ([Reads past a uniform binding's size](#reads-past-a-uniform-bindings-size)) is a load and a choice, OpLoad then OpSelect, but spirv-cross writes OpSelect on a scalar as a conditional expression and forwards the load into it: `cond ? read : 0.0`, which reads only when the condition holds. Metal's compiler makes a branch, a load inside it and a phi of each (its LLVM IR at -O1 and -O2, `xcrun metal -S -emit-llvm`), where `select(0.0, read, cond)` is one load and a select with no branch. The M1's compiler gets that form wrong in OCTOPATH TRAVELER's tree material (pixel shader `040208cd98c2ac2f`): its volumetric lightmap word (G-buffer target 5) comes out wrong although every guard is true (all its bindings are full size, and a size table of 64 KiB for every slot changes nothing), and the lighting turns it into NaN. The same shader at -O0, without the guards, or with them as `select` is right; `-fno-fast-math` does not help; only removing the guards of the whole lightmap block (c3[174..177]) fixes it, not either half. Found by bisecting to `26062f9e` (5 against 0 of 96 shots), then the shaders, then the guards inside the one shader. **Open:** which step of Apple's compiler fails is not known, nor whether it is the Metal front end of macOS 26, which built those libraries, or the M1's GPU compiler: a platform fault that the select form avoids and does not fix.
- **Fix:** every game, a chain change. `dmsh_rewrite.select_guards`, called by `dmsh_build.py` on each spirv-cross output (the shader, the geometry stage and its reader), writes every bounded read as `select(0, read, cond)`, the translator's own meaning. Since [GitHub issue #2066](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2066) only reads at computed offsets are bounded in the shader (the fixed-offset reads this was found on are plain loads again, the ones Metal made before reads were bounded; the host bounds them). At a computed offset the index already chooses row 0 when out of bounds (`bool _5 = word < size; select(0.0, c14[_5 ? _3 : 0][_4], _5)`), so the load stays inside the binding without the branch. A bounded read it cannot write as a select fails the shader (`uniform bounds rewrite failed`), so a change in what spirv-cross writes cannot bring the branches back unseen.
- **Found on:** [OCTOPATH TRAVELER](../../dm/games/010057D006492000/README.md) ([GitHub PR #2127](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2127): the bisection, the shader and the select form, on an M1 Pro).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_fetch.py UniformBounds` (the forms spirv-cross writes at a computed offset, the error for an unknown form or a fixed offset compared in the shader). On the M1 Pro, the dog route in S'warkii (walking up and down behind the tree left of the dog by the weapon shop): 5 to 14 of 96 shots with blocks before, 0 of 192 with the fixed-offset reads as selects; not run there with those reads plain (open: the author's retest).

## Shaders compiled at run time

### A GLSL language preamble is not a complete shader

- **Symptom:** the NSP script stops with `captured only 0 run-time shaders` although the game reaches its frame loop with precompiled shaders.
- **Cause:** the detection counted every `#version` occurrence. Snowdrop carries a GLSL header containing extensions and type macros, without an entry point; importing `glslcCompile` alongside it does not establish a boot shader compilation.
- **Fix:** `profile_guess.py` counts embedded source strings containing both `#version` and `void main(...)` before their terminating NUL. The survey, build and split share that detector. Three Houses still has its nine complete boot sources; NRO imports retain their separate capture rule.
- **Found on:** [Mario + Rabbids Kingdom Battle](../../dm/games/010067300059A000/README.md), whose only `#version` string is the 646-byte language preamble also carried by Sparks of Hope.

### Built-in shaders compiled with the game's own GLSLC

- **Symptom:** shaders missing from the map that no game file holds.
- **Cause:** a game whose shaders come from its own GLSLC at boot has no file holding their output. KTGL compiles 9 built-in GLSL shaders at boot with the game's own GLSLC (`glslcCompile` from `main+0x9c89f8`; the sources are strings in `main`).
- **Fix:** they are captured once on the Mac (`DM_GLSLC_CAPTURE`): `DM_GLSLC_CAPTURE=<dir>` saves every compile's output (`hle_glslc.c`; the compile object's first word points at {status, output}), and `shader_split.py` reads `<version dir>/glslc_capture` (the NSP script makes it for any game whose `main` imports `glslcCompile` and holds GLSL sources or, with none there, whose RomFS holds `.vert` / `.frag` files). The outputs are identical on every run. `shader_split.py` asks for captured GLSLC outputs only when a module of the profile's defines `glslcCompile`, as `profile_guess.py` does.
- **Found on:**
