  - [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md) (SDK 6.5.0): the 2D primitive, text and loading-feather shaders.
  - [Catherine: Full Body](../../dm/games/0100BF00112C0000/README.md) (SDK 8.2.1): its two built-in programs are captured on the Mac the same way; they compile only once the host reports the GLSLC's GPU code version ([next entry](#glslc-gpu-code-version-against-the-drivers-range)).
  - [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md): `subsdk0` is NVN's runtime GLSL compiler and `main` imports `glslcCompile*`, but up to the house, the beach and the menus every shader the game loaded was in the offline tables.
  - [Captain Toad: Treasure Tracker](../../dm/games/01009BF0072D4000/README.md) (the rule that a module must define `glslcCompile`): `main` imports `glslcCompile` and holds agl's GLSL sources, but no module defines it, so nothing is compiled at run time.

### A run-time compile that waits for a button press

- **Symptom:** the NSP script stops with `captured only 0 run-time shaders` while the capture run reaches the game's frame loop and runs to the end of its 30 s.
- **Cause:** the game compiles its run-time programs only after its title screen has had a button press, and the capture run had no input: 0 outputs in 200 s without a press, both of them 3 s after the first A.
- **Fix:** the capture run presses A every 4 s from 5 s on (`GLSLC_KEYS` in `build_ipa_from_nsp.py`, through `DM_KEYS`). The run keeps its own save and temporary storage, so whatever the presses choose is thrown away with them; a game that compiles at boot (Three Houses) is done before the first press.
- **Found on:** Crash Team Racing Nitro-Fueled (`0100F9F00C696000`, update 1.0.15, no profile yet): both outputs within the 30 s run.

### Shaders compiled when the game's shader cache misses

- **Symptom:** objects or whole floors are missing in one part of the game, with `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map` lines (17 keys at once) and then `[RED] program has no vertex shader (or it is not mapped)`, right after a save loads, while the boot and the first stage show no red line. The same keys appear at the same point on the iPhone and on the Mac.
- **Cause:** the game loads each material's shaders by file name from a pre-compiled cache that lacks many combinations, and compiles a missing one with its own GLSLC at that moment. Catherine: Full Body names them `data/shaders_binary/<n>___<flags>.vs` / `.ps` (1,190 names with both stages, 626 with only `.ps`, 44 with only `.vs`); loading a save at Night 2 needs 12 combinations that are not there (3 lack only the `.vs`) and compiles 21 shaders, which no game file holds. The NSP script's GLSLC capture boots the game for at most 30 s ([Built-in shaders compiled with the game's own GLSLC](#built-in-shaders-compiled-with-the-games-own-glslc)), which catches only the 2 shaders of the boot.
- **Fix:** the shaders compiled in play are collected where they are played and folded into the next build:
  - Without `DM_GLSLC_CAPTURE`, the host keys each stage of a GLSLC output as the split and the renderer do (`nvn_shkey.h`: the same reading as `glslc_shaders`, the same key as `skey` and `keys_hash`, with the profile's `shader_key_skip`) and asks the shader map the renderer loads (`mtl_shmap_has`). An output with a stage whose key the map lacks is saved to `glslc_play/` beside the save, named by its FNV-1a 64: the app's `Documents/glslc_play`, on the Mac beside `save/` (`DM_GLSLC_PLAY=<dir>` another folder, `off` none). The first one gets a `[RED] glslc: the game compiled a shader that this build's shader map does not have (key …)` line. Its draws stay missing until the next build, since shaders are translated offline only (`hle_glslc.c`).
  - The keys decide, not the file names. A game that compiles a combination of stages its files hold already (Portal and Portal 2 combine shaders of `game.zip`) saves nothing and gets no red line; the split would give such an output no row, so saving it would bring the same red line and the same file at every run. An output is saved and reported once a run for its combination of stages: the same shader compiled again, or another output of the same stages, adds nothing; a program that pairs a missing stage with other stages is saved too, so the split takes its variation pair (`shader_pairs.tsv`, which `dmsh_build.py` pads the vertex shader by). An output whose stages neither the host nor the split reads is not saved (one red line).
  - The NSP script takes the `glslc_play` folders of the NSP folder and the build folder, the Mac run command's and the Mac app's, and `--glslc-play DIR`, into `<version dir>/glslc_play`, made again at every build. A file must be a whole output named by its FNV-1a 64, as the host names it, that the split reads a shader out of (`gather_glslc_play`). Its messages go by the split's keys (`shader_split.py --glslc-keys FILE…`): a profile `/glslc_play/` row is held when some file has its key, only keys the profile's tables lack are new, and a file holding only shaders the game files have is said to add nothing.
  - `shader_split.py` reads that folder after every other source, so a play output gets a `/glslc_play/<name>#GLSLC@0x0` row only for a key no game file or boot capture has; a file it reads nothing out of is left out with a red line. The boot capture still requires only the `/glslc_capture/` rows (`glslc_wanted`).
  - The profile's tables are compared without the `/glslc_play/` rows, as without installed DLC (`dlc_only_shader_change`). A build that lacks some of the profile's play captures, or has others, gets a shader-only derived profile with its own tables, and says how many the profile names that no folder holds.
  - Two play captures can bring the same new stages in other combinations: the map is then the same and only the pairs differ. Such a build derives too when every pair row that differs (`shader_pairs.tsv`, and `pso_pairs.tsv` where the profile has one) holds a key of a `/glslc_play/` row; a pair of game-file keys alone that differs still stops the build. Ported from upstream NXNative ae5d8f51e (PR #2869, MIT), whose Mac-only translation of missing stages at run time is not taken (it needs a shader chain change and NX 18 translates offline only).
- **Check:** `test_glslc_play.py`, with synthetic GLSLC outputs of one stage and of a two-stage program: the split's rows with and without a play folder, its keys, the gathering and its key-based checks, the comparison (a pairs-only change derives only when every differing pair holds a play key), and `hle_glslc.c` compiled with a stand-in `glslcCompile` and map lookup: an output whose stage keys the map has (under other file names) saves nothing; one missing stage saves that output once, with one red line; the same shader again in the run saves and says nothing; after the split folds the saved files into the tables, the next run saves nothing; a profile's `shader_key_skip`; no map, `off`, an unreadable output, and the capture run.
- **Found on:** [Catherine: Full Body](../../dm/games/0100BF00112C0000/README.md) (SDK 8.2.1): the floor and the carpet of the Night 2 landing were missing on the iPhone and on the Mac. With the 21 outputs in the tables, the Mac drew them and logged no red line. The host's own `glslc_play` from that load held the same 21 files, byte for byte, as `DM_GLSLC_CAPTURE` had written. With the host deciding by keys, the same load with the new tables saved nothing and logged no red line at all, and with 0.5's shader map it saved those 21 files again, byte for byte, with one `[RED] glslc` line.

### Offline coverage after an empty GLSLC capture

- **Symptom:** The Mac capture boot submits shader programs but writes no GLSLC capture files.
- **Cause:** The source/import heuristic finds a compiler that the game can call. A cache hit lets the game submit a precompiled output without calling it.
- **Fix:** If the profile requires no capture files, use an empty shader map for the capture boot to observe submitted shader keys. An empty capture can continue only after the offline split supplies every observed key. A stopped host, failed capture, missing key or no observations fails the check. This verifies coverage of the observed boot.
- **Found on:** Alien: Isolation (SDK 8.2.0).

### GLSLC GPU code version against the driver's range

- **Symptom:** a null read at `main+0x3316dc` in `nnMain`, right after the window setup (the program object's interface pointers at +0xd0 / +0xd8 both null).
- **Cause:** the game compiles two built-in programs at boot with its own GLSLC (`subsdk0`, the same build as Link's Awakening's), and first checks that GLSLC's GPU code version against `nvnDeviceGetInteger` items 0x2E–0x31 (the driver's supported range, `main+0x330dcc`). The host reported Super Mario Odyssey's SDK 7 driver range, 1.5–1.14; this GLSLC writes 1.15, so nothing was compiled and the first uniform lookup read the empty program.
- **Fix:** every SDK's driver runs the code of the GLSLC in the same SDK, so the host now reports at least the version the game's own `glslcGetVersion` returns (`hle_glslc.c` `dm_glslc_gpu_code`, `hle_nvn.c`). The host also advertises the translator's supported range for precompiled shaders ([Newest GPU code version reported by the driver](nvn-objects.md#newest-gpu-code-version-reported-by-the-driver)).
- **Found on:** [Catherine: Full Body](../../dm/games/0100BF00112C0000/README.md) (SDK 8.2.1, Atlus's GFD engine, CRI middleware; one boot).

### GLSL ES 1.00 sources without a `#version` line

- **Note:** the OpenGL layer and `gl_shaders.py` were removed in 0.5 (in git at `93c34371`); this entry and the next are their history.
- **Symptom:** (GL layer, removed in 0.5; history) `gl_shaders.py` failed on the captured sources: `ES shaders for SPIR-V require version 310 or higher`.
- **Cause:** GameMaker's runner writes each shader variant as **GLSL ES 1.00 with the version statement left out** (`attribute`, `varying`, `texture2D`, `gl_FragColor`, `precision mediump float;`), and uses `sample` as a variable name, a reserved word since ES 3.00.
- **Fix:** `gl_shaders.py`'s `es100()` pass, run when the source has no `#version` line or says `#version 100` (that line is dropped): drop `precision <p> <t>;` lines (the pattern has to accept `\r`, the captured files mix CRLF and LF), strip `lowp`/`mediump`/`highp` (GameMaker has `#define LOWPREC lowp`, so a macro would otherwise expand to nothing useful), rename `attribute`→`in`, `varying`→`out` (vertex) / `in` (fragment), declare `out vec4 gm_FragColor;` and rename `gl_FragColor`, rename `sample`→`gm_sample`, then prepend `#version 310 es` with `precision highp float; precision highp int;`. Texture functions stay with the existing `fixups()` so the rename happens after `glslangValidator -E` and a macro cannot hide one.
- **Found on:** [Well Dweller](../../dm/games/0100217023F6C000/README.md) (93 run-time shaders; see its [`gl_capture.tsv`](../../dm/games/0100217023F6C000/gl_capture.tsv)).

### Struct uniforms become member rows

- **Symptom:** `uniform LightEnv { … };` failed with `uniform u_LightEnv: type LightEnv (structs are not implemented)`, so every program that used one was missing from the library.
- **Cause:** GameMaker declares several uniform blocks as struct uniforms, and the layer's `glGetUniformLocation` compares the name the game asks for (minus a `[n]` suffix) against the table's rows, whose name field is 48 bytes — the game asks for `u_LightEnv.m_dirLights_dir` and similar dotted member names.
- **Fix:** `gl_shaders.py` gained `uni_members()`, which walks a struct type's members and emits one row per member with the dotted name (`u:u_LightEnv.m_dirLights_dir[0]`), adding each member's offset to its parent's (the reflection gives a nested member's offset relative to its parent); arrays of structs return an error.
- **Found on:** [Well Dweller](../../dm/games/0100217023F6C000/README.md), whose lighting programs were the missing ones.

## Pipelines and caches

### A compile that does not return stalls every flush and draw

- **Symptom:** the pipeline prebuild stops at the same line on every start (`pipeline prebuild: 7000 of 20740 pairs, 1987 built, 5013 already archived, 0 left to the draws, 0 failed`), behind *Preparing pipelines* or, with `DM_PSO_PREPARE=0`, as a black screen while the sound plays on; the NSP script's `[7/7] Pipeline prebuild` does not end (`dmhost` at 0% CPU, `MTLCompilerService` at 100%), and `MTLCompilerService` crash reports (`EXC_CRASH`, `llvm::report_bad_alloc_error`) come every 6 to 10 minutes. The bases built since the last file are never written, so the next start builds them again and stops at the same pair.
- **Cause:** one base Apple's GPU compiler cannot build, and a host lock that let its compile hold everything else. The compiler grows `MTLCompilerService` past several GB until it aborts in `llvm::report_bad_alloc_error`, and Metal hands the request to the relaunched service; only after four aborts (33.7 minutes on an M6 with 16 GB, macOS 27.0) does `newRenderPipelineStateWithDescriptor` return, with `Compilation failed due to an interrupted connection: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED. This error occurred after multiple retries.` Metal's serializer cannot be written while it captures: the write (`-[_MTLBinaryArchive airntSerializeToURL:options:error:]`) runs on the archive's `_serializeQueue` and then empties the archive (its entries, AIR objects, library cache and file mapping) on its `_queue`, while each function a compile captures adds itself from the compile's completion (`__122-[MTLCompiler compileFunctionRequestInternal:…]_block_invoke`: a new `MTLBinaryEntry` from the request's AIR script and the function's bitcode, mapped by `newArchiverIdWithBinaryKey` before it takes `_queue`, then added under it, before the compile reports back). A compile under way across a write came back with both nil and crashed in `dispatch_data_create_map` (seen in lldb at the crash, the write's thread waiting for `_serializeQueue`). So every compile the archive captured held a read lock (`g_arch_rw`) and a flush its write lock. The flush at the next 1,000 bases waited behind the compile, and every other compile, draw and the present path waited behind the flush. The prepare step also waited for the last compile to return.
- **Fix:** flush periods (`ArchEpoch` in `nvn_mtl.m`). Each period has its own compiler and serializer; a compile the archive captures joins the current period (`arch_capture`) and leaves it when it returns. A flush (`arch_flush`) gives the compiles after it a new period and returns at once; the old period's serializer is written (`arch_write`, on the archive queue) once every compile in it has returned. No compile waits for a flush, and a compile that does not return holds back only its own period's file. The prebuild's flushes every 1,000 bases no longer wait; its last one (`arch_flush_wait`) waits for every period before `prebuilt.tsv` is written. Specializations go through a compiler without a serializer (`g_comp4`). The prepare step waits until every pair is handed to a worker; compiles still under way then (at most one per worker) finish in the background, and the log says `pipeline prepare: every shader pair handed out after … s; N compiles still under way finish in the background`. Pairs that share such a compile's vertex function wait behind it, put back ([Two captures of one shader function crash in Metal's binary cache](rendering.md#two-captures-of-one-shader-function-crash-in-metals-binary-cache)); once no pair has finished for 10 s the step leaves them to the background too (`pipeline prepare: no shader pair finished for 10.0 s …`). The prebuild tool still waits for every compile, since it writes them all. The base itself was made buildable by [Front end at -O1](#front-end-at--o1-apples-control-flow-lowering) (11.4 s at run time on the same M6). A base that fails no longer keeps `prebuilt.tsv` from being written (every start compiled it again in the background, half an hour at `-O2`): the pair goes into `pso_archive/prebuild_failed.tsv` with the system build and the stamp it failed under (`pre_fail_load`), and a later start of that system build with that stamp leaves it to the draws without compiling it; another system build (a new GPU compiler) or stamp tries it again. A failure Metal reports has already been retried by Metal itself. The log says `pipeline prebuild: N pairs whose base failed are left to the draws (…prebuild_failed.tsv; M of them failed on an earlier start of this system build, not built again)`. **Open:** a compile still under way when the game exits leaves no result, so a base that takes longer than a session to fail is compiled again in the background on the next start.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md#files-and-shaders), update 1.4.1, GitHub issue #294: the base of pair 5641, `3c3435a62e05975b` + `007d7e411ef14ebd` (`alRenderMaterialUber.bfsha` variation 0, 444,013 lines of MSL), found by a reporter's bisect of the pair list with `DM_PSO_PREBUILD=tool`, with that chain's `-O2` front end. On an M6 with 16 GB (macOS 27.0), the prebuild tool on the whole list with the `-O2` library and 8 workers: the old host stopped at 7,000 of 20,740 at 65 s and wrote nothing more in 5 minutes; with flush periods 20,000 pairs were done at 262 s, and 17 files held 18,739 of the other 20,739 bases, only the 2,000 in that compile's period waiting for it (three compiler aborts in 40 minutes). With the `-O1` library (all 20,740 built): 253 s cold and 227 s with the old host, 213 and 225 s with flush periods; the files written gave all 20,740 back, none compiled.
- **Check:** `t_pso_stall`: a compiler that holds one prebuild compile; while it is held, the prebuild hands out every pair and builds the others, a draw's compile returns and a later period's flush writes its file, and a base of a written period still specializes; once released, its period is written and the prebuild stamps. A pair whose compile fails: stamped all the same and listed; not compiled again on a later start of the same system build, also without the stamp; tried again with a list from another system build, and the list removed once it builds. On Odyssey's pair 5641 alone with the `-O2` library (M6, macOS 27.0): Metal's error after 36.6 minutes, the pair listed and the stamp written; the next start had nothing to do (1 s), and one without `prebuilt.tsv` left the pair to the draws without compiling it. The Metal behaviour, by a probe outside the tree on an M6 (macOS 27.0), eight threads compiling 1,500 pipelines: one serializer written every 150 ms while they compile crashed 3 runs of 3 in `newArchiverIdWithBinaryKey`; a write that first waits for the compiles under way but lets new ones start, 6 of 6; one that lets no compile start until it is done, 1 run of 111, during the write itself, with [issue #342](#a-compaction-flush-that-crashes-then-crashes-every-start)'s signature (`dispatch_retain(NULL)` in `materializeAirNTFromOnlineForSlice`); a compiler and serializer per period, none of 118, every pipeline found in the files, also with 121 periods in one run (no memory growth), and making a period took about 30 µs.

### Shader-pair tables in a Mac app

- **Symptom:** a self-contained Mac app (`--platform mac-app`) logs `pipeline prebuild: no .../Resources/dm/shader_pairs.tsv, nothing to build` although every shader library is there; it draws no hybrid stand-ins, and each pipeline seen for the first time compiles on the submit thread (frames of 100–550 ms).
- **Cause:** `dm/mac/pack_app.py` copied the shader map, the prewarm manifest, `c1_full.tsv` and `cb_extent.tsv` beside `DM_NVN_SHMAP`, but not `shader_pairs.tsv` and `pso_pairs.tsv`, which `build_ipa.sh` copies into the IPA and the host's pipeline prebuild reads there (`pso_prebuild`). Without them the app makes no pulling bases. For a profile without `pso_pairs.tsv` a Mac run (`mac_run`) passes the shader split's copy as `DM_PSO_PAIRS`; the app had neither. A full shader-library build does not build the pipeline bases by itself.
- **Fix:** the packer copies both tables when the profile has them, and the split's `pso_pairs.tsv` (`--pso-pairs`, which the NSP script passes) when the profile has none. They stay optional. An app packed before the fix needs repacking. An NVN game's app also carries its pipeline archive made offline for this Mac's GPU, so the first start builds no base: [A Mac app](../development.md#a-mac-app).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Echoes of Wisdom](../../dm/games/01008CF01BAAC000/README.md#rendering-and-shaders), update 1.1.0: a 308.4 ms frame, 283.5 ms of it pipeline acquisition and 5.9 ms GPU work; with the tables the offline prebuild built all 7,495 bases. [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#pipelines-on-a-cold-start): frames of 100–546 ms before, none of its bases compiled on the first start after.
- **Check:** `test_nsp_mac_app`: `App.test_pipeline_pair_tables`, `App.test_split_pso_pairs` and `App.test_layout` fail without the tables in the packer; `Script.test_pack_command` checks the split's copy is passed only when the profile has no `pso_pairs.tsv`.

### Offline pipeline archive missing for the device GPU

- **Symptom:** `pipeline archive bundled with the app: none for this GPU (...)`: the device builds the bases itself although the app carries an offline archive.
- **Cause:** each archive targets one GPU architecture. The iOS packers defaulted to `g18p` for profiles with `pso_mtl4 on` (then an opt-in); another GPU family needed an explicit build override.
- **Fix:** both packers also read the optional profile key `pso_gpu`, which names the families to build for; explicit build overrides still win. Without the key a profile gets `g19p` (the A20 / A20 Pro; it was `g18p` before the Metal 4 path became the iOS default for every profile); a profile that set `pso_mtl4 off` got no archive until that key was retired, and every NVN profile gets one now. See [archive target selection](../development.md#the-pipeline-archive-made-offline).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md#offline-pipeline-archive-probe). Its note records the contributor's Mac build probe and the outstanding device checks.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_game_profile.py` validates the key; `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` exercises both packers' selection and override precedence with synthetic profiles.

### A bundled pipeline archive that crashes at boot

- **Symptom:** a game whose app carries the pipeline archive made offline for its device's GPU crashes at every start, in Metal's archive loader or in the first seconds (no first frame, or a crash while *Preparing pipelines* is shown), while the same game starts with the archive left out (`DM_PSO_BUNDLE=off` in `Documents/env.txt`) or built without it (`--gpu none`). With the crash guard, the start after one such crash logs `[RED] pipeline archive bundled with the app: a start with applegpu_… on this system build (…) did not get past its first frames … this start uses it again`, and the start after a second one in a row logs `[RED] pipeline archive bundled with the app: two starts in a row with applegpu_… did not get past their first frames` and builds the bases itself. Once a start without it gets past its first frames, later starts log `[RED] pipeline archive bundled with the app: … is left out on this system build`.
- **Cause:** **open.** The archive's container is checked before Metal reads it (`arch_file_ok`, [The pipeline archive made offline](../development.md#the-pipeline-archive-made-offline)): Mach-O load commands, slice bounds and library ranges. That catches a malformed file, not a well-formed archive whose compiled payload makes Metal crash: [LNPANDY/NX2Apple#640](https://github.com/LNPANDY/NX2Apple/issues/640) reported a bundled `g18p` archive crashing Metal at boot on an iPhone Air with iOS 27.0, cause unknown, and an archive built for another GPU family than its name says would be another. The archive only supplies pipeline bases the device would otherwise compile itself, looked up by content. Every NVN game's iOS app carries the archive of its device's GPU by default (`PSO_GPUS = 'g19p'` in the NSP script), and every game reads it, since the Metal 4 path is always on and the profile key `pso_mtl4` is retired. Without a guard, such a game crashed at every start until the player edited `env.txt` or the app was rebuilt.
- **Fix:** a crash guard (`dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_pso_guard.h`, used by `pso_bundle_open` in `nvn_mtl.m`), ported from the Nintanium line (its commits 479850fc and abd731cc, with the names `psg_`). It is on by default on iOS, where #640 was found and the app runs one process at a time. On the Mac it is off unless `DM_PSO_BUNDLE_GUARD=1`, so the launcher's, `bench.py`'s and `regress.py`'s runs keep their archive after a crash in bring-up, as before. `DM_PSO_BUNDLE_GUARD=0` turns it off on iOS, and so do `off` (the word `DM_PSO_BUNDLE` takes), `false` and `no`, in any case; any other value turns it on, the Mac included.
  - **The record.** After `arch_file_ok` passes the file and before Metal first reads it (`newArchiveWithURL`), a start writes `pso_bundle_guard.tsv` beside the device's own archive folder (`Documents/` on iOS). It holds the state, the system build (`kern.osversion`) and the archive's identity: its file name, its size, a SHA-256 of its first and last 64 KiB and the app executable's `LC_UUID`. A file `arch_file_ok` refuses never reaches Metal and writes no record; an archive Metal refuses to open (an error, not a crash) puts the record back as the start found it.
  - **The window.** The start takes the record back once it is past its window: 300 presented frames after the prepare step has ended and every compile of its prebuild has returned (or the background prebuild has ended, without a prepare step). A start without the archive builds every base on the device, so its window covers that whole build. With no such end (no prewarm list, or a compile that never returns, [GitHub issue #294](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/294)), the window ends after 1,800 frames.
  - **Starts that end while away do not count.** While the app is in the background (`dm_gfx_suspend`), inactive (`dm_gfx_inactive`, from the scene's resign-active: a swipe in the app switcher ends the app with no trip to the background) or in the graceful exit (`dm_gfx_exiting`, its first step, before the waits and the 1.5 s fallback), and at a clean exit, the record is as the start's end would leave it; it is written again when the app is back. So only a start that ends in the foreground inside its window counts: a crash, or iOS ending the app there (for memory). The same holds while an archive opened ahead of its first use is not used yet (`PSG_AWAY_IDLE`): a large archive opens at the game's start ([The bundled pipeline archive opened at the first submission](#the-bundled-pipeline-archive-opened-at-the-first-submission)), its record is written before Metal reads it and kept while Metal opens it, then put back until the first submission takes the archive and writes it again. An early end in the game's own start (its modules' init, `nnMain`, its file system and graphics init) so counts for nothing, as when the archive was opened at the first submission; a start that leaves the archive out is watched from the first submission too.
  - **The verdict: two in a row, for every game.** One such start is followed by another start with the archive, watched; two in a row leave the archive out at the next start, which builds the bases on the device (*Preparing pipelines*, once). The start without the archive is watched too. If it gets past its window, the archive stays out (`skip`) for that archive, app build and system build, start after start, so launches never alternate between crashing and not. If it does not either, the archive is not shown to be the cause, and the next start uses it again, watched as a first start. The Nintanium line took one early end for games that had no archive before it became the default there and two for a profile that set `pso_mtl4 on` (a game that had its archive at every start and may need it: 13 Sentinels: Aegis Rim was reported to crash on an A20 Pro without it). In NX 18 every game reads its archive, so every game takes two. The cost is a second crash when the archive is what crashes.
  - **A wrong verdict costs a long first start.** Two unrelated early ends in a row (a crash in the first seconds that has nothing to do with the archive, iOS ending the app for memory in the foreground) leave the archive out: that start, and every later one on the same archive, app build and system build, builds the bases on the device. The first such start waits on *Preparing pipelines* (minutes for a large game: 7–8 minutes for Breath of the Wild on an iPhone, [its note](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#pipelines-on-a-cold-start)); later starts take the bases from the device's own archive. Nothing a game sees changes otherwise: the bases are the same pipelines either way.
  - **Retrying.** Another archive or host build (new shaders, an NX 18 update of the app's code) or a system update is no record, so the archive is tried again. An app rebuilt from the same inputs keeps its identity, since the archive is reused and the linker's `LC_UUID` is a digest of its output, and so it keeps the record. Deleting `pso_bundle_guard.tsv` (the Files app, in the app's folder) tries the archive at the next start, and `DM_PSO_BUNDLE_GUARD=0` turns the guard off.
  - **A record that cannot be written** (a full disk): the start is not watched and the record stays as it was, so every start keeps the verdict it gives until the record can be written. The `[RED]` line says which.
  - **Open:**
    - The window counts presented frames, so a crash in an archive lookup after it (a scene first drawn minutes later) is not caught.
    - A start without the archive that crashes only after its window counts as past it. So a game that needs its archive and crashes late without it loses the archive (`skip`) after two unrelated early ends in a row with it, and then crashes at every start. Deleting the record, `DM_PSO_BUNDLE_GUARD=0`, or another archive, host or system build brings it back.
    - Whether iOS always sends resign-active before an app-switcher kill is not verified on a device.
    - With `DM_PSO_PREPARE=0`, the 1,800-frame bound can end the window before a long background prebuild does.
- **Found on:** no game in this repository: [LNPANDY/NX2Apple#640](https://github.com/LNPANDY/NX2Apple/issues/640)'s report. [13 Sentinels: Aegis Rim](../../dm/games/01003FC01670C000/README.md) is the case the two starts in a row and the watched start without the archive are for.
- **Check:** `t_pso_guard` (`make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> pso-guard`: plain, ThreadSanitizer and AddressSanitizer; in CI's tests workflow; on Linux it passes with clang and gcc and under gcc's sanitizers) takes the record through:
  - starts that crash, get past their window, go to the background, inactive or into the graceful exit, or exit cleanly, with one early end needed and two in a row;
  - records of another archive, app build or system build, and a record that cannot be written;
  - whole sequences of starts on devices where the archive, the device's own build (in its window or after it), both or neither crash. With two in a row, only the archive crashing gives two crashes, then none; a wrong verdict on a game that needs its archive gives one crash, then none; two wrong verdicts in a row on a game that crashes only after the window without its archive give a crash at every start (the limit above).

  `t_pso_bundle` (an Apple-silicon Mac; not run here) plants a record for a real archive. It checks that the Mac's default does not read it; that with `DM_PSO_BUNDLE_GUARD=1` one early end opens the archive again, two in a row leave it out, and the start without it, past its window, keeps it out; that `DM_PSO_BUNDLE_GUARD=0` and `=Off` open it; that the record is written before Metal reads the archive, taken back in the background and cleared after the window; that a file the container check refuses writes no record; and that another system build's record counts as none. `dm/ios/tests/test_pso_guard_wiring.py` checks the hooks' places and order in the sources (the scene, the graceful exit, the record between `arch_file_ok` and `newArchiveWithURL`, the window's end) and the defaults. Not run on a device.

### Offline pipeline archive reuse and fallback

- **Symptom:** a truncated `.mtl4a` reaches `MTL4ArchiveImpl::loadFromURL`; the bundle reports coverage with `0 of 0 probed bases found`; rebuilt shader libraries reuse an archive of their old binaries; or packing fails with `pso_offline: no base to build` when every eligible pair has function constants, or when the profile's `shader_pairs.tsv` holds only its header (`--platform mac-app`, [GitHub issue #338](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/338)).
- **Cause:** the archive guard checked only four magic bytes; coverage accepted `found == probed` even when every sampled pair was ineligible; the offline cache key included library paths and profile stamps but omitted library bytes; and a zero-base plan aborted before writing its device remainder list, also when there was no pair at all, so `mac_pso()` never reached its no-archive branch.
- **Fix:** `arch_file_ok` checks the GPU Mach-O load commands and payload ranges, universal slice bounds and the serializer's bundled Metal library ranges before opening the archive. It does not validate the compiler's private payload. `pso_bundle_covers` requires at least one actual lookup and no misses. `pso_offline.py` hashes every consumed library's bytes into the cache key; when all eligible pairs need device specialization, it removes old archives and writes `rest_pairs.tsv` without invoking `metal-tt`. A pair list with no pair takes the same path (old archives removed, stamp written, exit 0), and the app is packed without an archive; pairs that the host would leave out entirely (wrong or missing libraries) still fail.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#found-on-this-game), during review of its offline archive path with synthetic libraries and pair lists; no new gameplay or iPhone run. The empty pair list on [ANIMAL WELL](../../dm/games/010020D01AD24000/README.md#found-on-this-game).
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` changes vertex and fragment library bytes without changing entries or stamps, and exercises the all-constant plan, the header-only pair list and pairs all left out. `t_pso_bundle` rejects bare headers, truncated archives, malformed load commands and out-of-range universal slices, accepts real offline and serialized archives, and rejects zero-probe coverage.

### Bundled archive of cover pairs

- **Symptom:** the iPhone log says `pipeline archive bundled with the app: holds the bases of every shader pair (16 of 16 probed bases found …)`, yet the first run compiles about 110 pipeline bases in play, with 21 long frames of up to 340 ms that list `new pipelines`.
- **Cause:** the archive made offline holds its bases without a vertex layout (pulling bases) only for the pairs of `shader_pairs.tsv` (with `pso_pairs.tsv`). A game whose programs have one stage is listed through a cover: the shader split pairs each vertex shader with the same one or few pixel shaders ([Single-stage programs paired with a cover](#single-stage-programs-paired-with-a-cover)), and the real pairs exist only when a draw binds the two. 13 Sentinels lists 376 vertex shaders, all with the pixel shader `0ddfcfcdcc3ddf9e`, which no draw uses as its fragment; Luigi's Mansion 3 lists 963, each with the same 4. The probe (`pso_bundle_covers`) looks up 16 of those same listed pairs, so it found all 16, and the line claimed every pair. The bases the draws take, with a vertex layout, are in this archive only for the prewarm list's `R` rows (X1.0's layout bases, [Pipelines with a vertex layout compiled at a fresh install's prepare step](#pipelines-with-a-vertex-layout-compiled-at-a-fresh-installs-prepare-step); 13 Sentinels' list was empty then), and the pulling bases of the real pairs that the hybrid stand-ins need are never in it. Each hybrid miss (and the prediction scan's hybrid lookup of a predicted pair) then also kicks the background compile of a pulling base for the pair's next miss (`hybrid_get`), beside the layout base the draw compiles at once; the `pipelines up to frame` line did not count those kicks, so a log could not show how the compiles split.
- **Fix:** the probe line says what it checked: `holds the bases of the listed shader pairs (N of N probed pairs from shader_pairs.tsv found …)`, and, when the list is a full cross product of vertex shaders over the same pixel shaders (`pre_cover_only`: one pixel shader over at least two vertex shaders, or several with at least 4 vertex shaders for each, since three Unity games list 3 × 2 real BNSH variation pairs; about 50 profiles qualify), `; cover pairs only: the pairs the game draws with are not in this archive (it holds only the layout bases of the prewarm list's R rows, which the prepare line counts), their other bases are compiled as the draws meet them`; the prepare line's `layout bases from the archive bundled with the app` says how many of the draws' bases the archive did serve. The `pipelines up to frame` line counts `pulling base builds kicked` (`g_hy_kicked`), each distinct pulling base once, whether a draw's miss or the prediction scan's hybrid lookup kicked it, apart from the predictions' `background builds kicked`, and `scripts/devlog.py` totals them. `DM_PSO_HYBRID_KICK=0` (A/B only; the default keeps the kick) leaves those pulling bases unbuilt when the bundled archive has cover pairs only, and the line then counts them as `not kicked`, each distinct base once as well, so the two runs' counts compare; the draw waits for its layout base as before, so no draw changes. The flag is set by the probe, which runs in the prepare step or at the start of the background prebuild, and cleared by any probe that refuses the archive, so a miss before it kicks as before; a start that runs no prebuild (no prewarm list) has no probe and keeps the kick. The archive's pair bases still cannot serve such a game; seeding the profile's `pso_manifest.tsv` (its `R` rows also become layout bases in the bundled archive) is one remedy (13 Sentinels' list is seeded since, 858 rows, 107 `R`), and since 2026-11-15 the archive also holds stand-ins for the real pairs: `pso_offline.py` (`plan_standins`) adds, for each `R` row whose vertex shader has a pulling library, the pulling base `hybrid_get` asks for when a later draw of that pair misses its pipeline (another vertex layout or colour state): the `<key>_bp` function for the depth mode, no vertex descriptor, the fragment entry the row's targets call for, its samples and alpha to coverage (`pso_desc_pull`). The rows are read by `pso_offline.py` alone, so they feed neither `vs_pad`'s padding nor the libraries' inputs (`dmsh_chain.py` `INPUT_TABLES`) nor the prebuild stamp (`prebuilt.tsv` stays the pair lists'). A pair whose pixel shader reads a `[[user(locnN)]]` input the pulling library does not write is left out (GitHub issue #283: metal-tt refuses it; `user_locations` reads the locations from each library's AIR metadata strings, so a type mismatch is not seen there: `scripts/regress.py`'s interface check names one before a game build, [The regression harness](../development.md#the-regression-harness)), and the stand-ins are groups of their own (`batches.<arch>/s<index>/`), halved down to a base metal-tt refuses, as the layout groups are. `archive.tsv` counts them (`standin`, with the rows left without one by reason), `standins.tsv` beside the archive lists them, and the probe line adds `; stand-ins for the pairs the prewarm list's R rows draw with: N (looked up in the background)` (the cover note then says the archive holds their stand-ins). Up to 8 of them are looked up as `pre_desc_ex` describes them (`standin_probe_in`), at utility QoS beside the game's first frames (`standin_probe_bg`), on a line of their own, `pipeline archive bundled with the app: stand-ins for the pairs the prewarm list's R rows draw with: P of Q probed found (… ms, in the background)`: the probe's verdict does not depend on them, and in the prepare step they would add about 10 ms before the first frame at every start (two reflections, two library loads and a lookup each; the pair probe's 15 took 18.8 ms in Ocarina of Time's 55-minute iPhone run). The first version looked them up in the probe, before the first frame (found in review). `scripts/devlog.py` reads both lines, and the older form `N (P of Q probed found)`. `--no-standins` builds none. Estimated from the lists (pairs not in the pair lists, an upper bound before the libraries are read) and each game's layout bases' size: Persona 5 Royal ≤ 976 stand-ins, about 45 MB more archive; Luigi's Mansion 3 ≤ 630, about 28 MB; Smash Ultimate ≤ 502, about 24 MB; Catherine ≤ 286, about 11 MB; 13 Sentinels ≤ 94, about 3 MB; Star Fox ≤ 399 and NieR ≤ 487, about 30 MB each; Ocarina of Time 12, Bananza 23, Breath of the Wild none.
- **Found on:** [13 Sentinels: Aegis Rim](../../dm/games/01003FC01670C000/README.md#found-on-this-game), the first run of an iPhone 18 Pro Max build: the probe line beside 110 bases compiled in play (7.8 s of compiling on all threads, 1.4 s of it waiting on the submit thread). The stand-ins: [Persona 5 Royal](../../dm/games/01005CA01580E000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (an iPhone 18 Pro Max session of about 23 minutes, build `431d5d18`: 935 hybrid misses with no pulling base ready, 9.2 s of pipeline time on the submit thread, stalls of 0.57-0.95 s), [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (stalls of 0.95-1.0 s; its pair lists are no cover, but miss 490 of the pairs its rows draw with), [Catherine: Full Body](../../dm/games/0100BF00112C0000/README.md#found-on-this-game) and [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md#found-on-this-game), whose probe lines say cover pairs only.
- **Check:** `t_pso_bundle` (`make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_bundle`, needs a Mac): the test's own three-pixel-shader list is no cover list, lists of one pixel shader and an 8 × 2 cross product are, a 3 × 2 cross product, a single pair or a depth-only pair are not, the kick is skipped only with the switch at 0 and cover pairs, and a probe of a cover list sets the flag that `hybrid_kick_ok` reads while a later refused probe clears it. `dm/tools/tests/test_devlog.py` (`PipelineLines`) fills the host's two format strings from `nvn_mtl.m` and reads the kicks, the cover note and the stand-in note back with their lookups' own line (or without it, in a log that ends before it), and still reads the older lines. `t_pso_bundle` checks the stand-in count on the probe line and their lookups on a line of their own (3 of 3 found). `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (`StandIns`, `Build.test_standins_are_groups_of_their_own`, `Build.test_a_refused_standin_group_is_compiled_in_halves`): the base `hybrid_get` asks for (entry for the depth mode, the fragment entry of the row's targets, samples, alpha to coverage, depth only), a row of a listed one-sample pair left to the pair base, every reason a row has none, the AIR locations of synthetic and of real compiled libraries, `standins.tsv` and the `s` groups, the stamp unchanged, a refused group halved and its stand-in left out of `standins.tsv`, and that `dmsh_chain.py` and `dmsh_build.py` read no `R` rows. Not yet run: a game build with them (metal-tt), the probe on a device, and an A/B of the hybrid misses (`misses with no pulling base ready`) on Persona 5 Royal or Luigi's Mansion 3; nor an A/B of `DM_PSO_HYBRID_KICK` on a fresh install of 13 Sentinels.

### A bundled pipeline archive read a page at a time when it opens

- **Symptom:** a gray screen for several seconds at startup, before the pipeline progress screen, on a Mac whose GPU has an archive in the app: `pipeline archive bundled with the app: .../pso/applegpu_g16s.mtl4a, 4405.6 MB, opened in 7978.8 ms`. The same app starts fast on a Mac whose GPU has none, although that Mac's own `pso_archive` folder holds twice as much (38 files, 8,880 MB, about 1.4 s on an M1 Pro).
- **Cause:** Metal's open (`MTL4ArchiveImpl::loadFromURL`, macOS 27) makes a library of every Metal library in the file and hashes all of it with SHA-256 on the calling thread. It reads the whole `__AIR_DATA` segment and `__TEXT,__metallib` section of each GPU image, 2.9 GB of Ocarina of Time's 4.4 GB file (`mincore` after the open). It leaves the GPU code (`__vertex`, `__fragment`, `__compute`) for when a pipeline is taken. A file not in memory is mapped and read one page fault at a time, about 230 MB/s. The device's folder is opened file by file in parallel, so its faults overlap. The bundled archive is one file: 12.5 s cold and 1.5 s warm in a probe on an M4 Max. The bytes Metal reads, not an index, are the cost: the device files' `.keys` index only speeds up lookups after the open.
- **Fix:** `arch_open_read` (`nvn_mtl.m`) first asks for the ranges Metal will read, 8 MB at a time on every core, from the load commands `arch_file_state_rd` already checks: each piece is mapped on its own, given `MADV_WILLNEED` and unmapped, so the kernel reads it into the file cache that Metal's mapping shares. Metal then finds it in memory. It is used for every archive file the host opens: the bundled one, the bundle's prewarm files and the device's folder, inside the window that maps them (see [A pipeline archive on an external drive is read into memory](memory.md#a-pipeline-archive-on-an-external-drive-is-read-into-memory)). The first version read each piece into an 8 MB heap buffer: as fast, but `malloc` kept the freed buffers, +99 MB of footprint on a 12-core M6 after the open, and a warm open copied every byte again. One mapping of the whole file is ruled out because Metal maps the whole file too, and two of a 4.4 GB file do not fit an iPhone's address space; `F_RDADVISE` was erratic (0.34–12.9 s cold for the same file).
- **Found on:** (upstream) [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (v131072), its Mac app on an M4 Max, macOS 27. The host's `pso_bundle_open` on the bundled archive, each time a fresh APFS clone (a clone's pages are not in memory): 8.07 / 7.91 s → 1.61 / 1.52 s; warm 1.04–1.15 → 1.09–1.10 s. A device folder of 21 files (1,490 MB) through `pso4_init`: 452 → 220 ms cold, 88 → 100 ms warm. In the game, headless boots of host `f4b7e77c` with and without the change (the app's chain), each on fresh clones of the bundle and of a 23-file device folder: the bundled archive 10.57 / 8.07 s → 1.63 / 1.53 s, the device folder 673 / 460 → 258 / 221 ms. Both sides found the same pipelines: 15 of 15 probed bases, 324 compute pipelines from the bundle, 164 mesh pipelines from the archive and 6,061 indexed pipelines.
- **Measured again** (upstream) on an M6 (macOS 27, 12 cores) after the rebase onto the mapped reader, a harness opening through the host's code inside its window, each cold run on a fresh APFS clone on an external drive: Divinity: Original Sin 2's bundled archive (373 MB, 231 MB read ahead) 0.99–1.58 s → 0.34 s cold, warm 92–97 → 99–103 ms; 16 of Ocarina of Time's device files opened in parallel (1.8 GB, 746 MB read ahead) 0.93–1.26 → 0.64–0.77 s cold, warm 81–82 → 91–98 ms. Footprint after the open the same on both sides (20 and 26–27 MB).
- **Check:** `t_pso_bundle` checks that the reads of an offline archive and of a serialized one are their records and libraries, less than the file, and that both open through `arch_open_read`. Ported to NX 18 from upstream PR #2657; the measurements above are upstream's, not taken with this host. NX 18 uses it for the bundled archive and the device's folder (it has no bundled prewarm files), and the bundled archive's crash guard still runs between the check and the open.

### The bundled pipeline archive opened at the first submission

- **Symptom:** with the read-ahead above, the iPhone's open of the archive bundled with the app is still on the path to the first frame: `pipeline archive bundled with the app: …/applegpu_g19p.mtl4a, 4617.3 MB, opened in 2361.5 ms` (Ocarina of Time, iPhone 18 Pro Max, build `431d5d18`), 1.2 s for Super Mario RPG (2.5 GB), 1.1 s for Donkey Kong Bananza (1.8 GB), 0.9 s for Super Mario Bros. Wonder (2.2 GB) and 0.5-0.6 s for Mario Kart World, Smash Ultimate, Odyssey and Breath of the Wild (0.8-1.5 GB), at every start.
- **Cause:** `pso4_init` opens the archive when the game first needs a pipeline, at its first submission (`warm_start`, before the prepare step), on the submit thread. What is left of the open with the file read ahead is Metal's own work on the calling thread (`loadFromURL` hashes every library in the file), and nothing of the game runs beside it: the game's start (its modules' init, `nnMain`, its file system and graphics init, the shader map and libraries) had finished before the open began.
- **Fix:** general, every game whose bundled archive is 256 MB or more (`PSO_PREOPEN_MIN`; a smaller one opens in tens of ms). The open starts at the game's start, in the background on the system default device (the object `mtl_init` takes later): `main.c` sets `dm_mem_ready_hook` to `mtl_pso_bundle_preopen` (`nvn_mtl.m`), which `hle_svc.c` calls once the guest's address space is reserved (`mem_init_once`, the game's first memory call, at the start of `nnosInitialize`; the launcher's settings are in the environment by then). After that block and not before it, so the two largest mappings keep their order in the address space (Metal maps the whole file; a second mapping of a 4.4 GB file did not fit an iPhone's address space, upstream). The first submission's `pso_bundle_open` takes that archive (`pso_bundle_join`), waiting only for what is left of the open. The open runs on a serial queue of its own (`dm.nvn.pso-preopen`) at user-initiated QoS, below the game's threads (user-interactive, `QoS 0x21` on the iPhone), and the first submission waits for its block with `dispatch_block_wait`, which raises that queue and the thread running it to the waiting thread's QoS for what is left (Metal's hashing, CPU bound). The first version ran it on a global queue and waited with `dispatch_group_wait`, which raises nothing (nor does a wait for a block of a global queue: libdispatch overrides only a serial queue), so what was left of the open ran below the threads still busy with the game's start, a priority inversion on the path to the first frame (found in review; a small program at utility QoS under a default-QoS waiter read priority 20 → 31 for the serial queue's block wait, 20 → 20 for the other two). It is the same file, container check, crash guard, read-ahead and open (`pso_bundle_load`), so the archive every lookup asks is the same and nothing a game sees changes. The crash guard's record is written before Metal reads the file, as before, and kept while Metal opens it; once the open returns, nothing reads the archive until the first submission takes it, so the record is put back as the start's end would leave it (`pso_guard_idle`, `PSG_AWAY_IDLE` in `nvn_pso_guard.h`) and the first submission writes it again before its first lookup. An early end in the game's own start (an unrelated boot crash, iOS ending the app for memory while the game's files load) so counts for nothing, as before the early open. The first version kept the record from the game's start on: two such ends in a row left the archive out, and once the start without it got past its window, for good on that app and system build, each later start compiling every base on the device (found in review). The mapping window that keeps a `.mtl4a` read mapped (`arch_open_begin` / `arch_open_end`) is counted, since the two opens can overlap. The open line keeps its form and adds `; opened at the game's start, the first submission waited N ms for it`, which `scripts/devlog.py` reads (`bundled archive open waited for (ms)`, the whole open for a log without the early open). A game that asks for no memory before its first submission, and `DM_PSO_PREOPEN=0`, open it at the first submission as before.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (2.35 s on an iPhone 18 Pro Max), [Super Mario RPG](../../dm/games/0100BC0018138000/README.md#found-on-this-game), [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#found-on-this-game) and [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (the 2026-11-15 iPhone round, X2.0 plan item A13 widened).
- **Check:** `t_pso_bundle`: under the size floor the archive is left to the first submission, `DM_PSO_PREOPEN=0` starts nothing, an early open runs on the system default device and closes its mapping window, the first submission takes its archive (the same object, the bundle's folder, its bases found) once, the open's queue is its own serial one at user-initiated QoS and a block queued ahead of the open runs at the waiting user-interactive thread's priority once the first submission waits (not checked where the process's threads are clamped to the default priority, as under the agents' sandbox: both QoS read 31), a first submission that comes before the open has begun waits for it, a GPU without a file is said once and not looked for again, a file that is no archive is refused before Metal with no record, the crash guard begins with the early open (watched as a start with the archive), its record is as the start's end would leave it until the first submission writes it again (also after one start that ended early, the second in a row, and after two in a row, which leave the archive out and are watched from the first submission), and it is cleared past the window, and two overlapping windows restore `NSData`'s method once, after the last. `dm/ios/tests/test_pso_guard_wiring.py`: both opens go through `pso_bundle_load` (the container check, then the record, then Metal), the early open sets the archive aside after it and the join takes it back after its wait and before anything reads the archive, the join begins no guard of its own (the check read `pso_bundle_open`'s body before: the join's declaration comes first), and `main.c` sets the hook after the launcher's switchable mods and before `front_run`, which `hle_svc.c` calls at the end of `mem_init_once`. `dm/tools/tests/test_devlog.py` reads the line from `nvn_mtl.m`'s format. Not yet measured on a device: how much of the open the game's start hides (the open line's wait), and whether the read-ahead beside the game's own start slows its file reads.

### The device's pipeline list read in whole by the first new row

- **Symptom:** the first shader or pipeline a session meets that is not on the game's lists (a new area, a cold start after an update) makes one long frame on the replay thread; Instruments or a hang report shows it reading `pso_manifest.tsv` and `pso_user.tsv` (`NSString` splits, set inserts) under `pso_user_record`.
- **Cause:** `pso_user_record` writes each new row to this device's `pso_user.tsv` only when neither list holds it, and its first call read the profile's whole `pso_manifest.tsv` and the device's list into that filter set, on whichever thread recorded first, mostly the replay at a draw's first pipeline (`pso_get`, the hybrid stand-in's demand row). Ocarina of Time's seed is 20,054 rows (6 MB); MacNativeGames measured over 100 ms once in every session with its 27 MB list. The recording threads (the replay, the prefetch queues, the prewarm's workers) also shared the set and the file with no lock.
- **Fix:** in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every game (`DM_PSO_USER_QUEUE=0` restores the old path; a player build keeps `DM_PSO_*` lines). The row is formatted on the caller's thread and handed to a serial queue of its own at utility QoS, which builds the filter set, filters and writes; the set and the file then have one thread. Rows still queued are written at exit (`atexit`) and before an archive save, which going to the background makes (`mtl_arch_save` calls `pso_user_sync`). A process the system kills loses at most the rows still queued, as a kill mid-write already could (the list's cut last line is mended at the next start). Adapted from MacNativeGames 7cdf1b27 (MIT); that commit's main change (alias heaps that grow with room, migrations in the game's order) has no counterpart here: NX 18 shares a placement heap only between descriptions at one exact address and format (`image_texture`), with no region a texture past its end would migrate.
- **Measurement:** not measured on NX 18.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game), through MacNativeGames 7cdf1b27.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_user_queue`: with the queue held, the recording thread neither builds the filter set nor writes; after it, the new row is written once and a row the list holds not again; 64 rows from many threads, 32 distinct, are written once each; `--off` does it on the recording thread. `check_t_pso_demand_record` reads its rows after `pso_user_sync`.

### The device's pipeline list filter held every row as a string

- **Symptom:** none on screen: memory. The filter that keeps the device's `pso_user.tsv` free of rows it already has, or the profile's seed has, held every row of both lists as an `NSString` in an `NSMutableSet` for the whole run, from the first recorded row (in practice every run: the replay, the prefetch queues and the prewarm's workers all record).
- **Cause:** `pso_user_put` read both lists into strings, split them into arrays of lines and added each line to the set; nothing let it go. Ocarina of Time's seed is 32,500 rows (11.8 MB of text).
- **Fix:** general, `nvn_mtl.m` `pso_user_put` (`DM_PSO_USER_HASH`, on by default; `=0` keeps the string set, as before). The filter is a set of 64-bit MurmurHash64A hashes of each row's bytes (open addressing, under half load, about 8 bytes a row). The lists are read mapped (`NSDataReadingMappedIfSafe`) and scanned for newlines in place, with no strings and no split. The answers are the string filter's: a row is a line without its newline, empty lines and `#` lines are not rows, and a list with a byte of 0x80 or more goes through the old reader, so a list that is not valid UTF-8 adds nothing, as before; a recorded row that is not UTF-8 is not written, as before. Two different rows share a hash with odds of about rows² / 2⁶⁵ (about 1e-10 at 32,500 rows), and the cost of that would be one row not written: its pipeline built on the spot next time instead of prewarmed.
- **Measured (Mac, M4 Max, `./t_pso_user_hash <seed> <other> bench`, best of 5):** Ocarina of Time's seed with a 163-row device list: 15.2 MB kept for the run → 0.5 MB, the fill 39.2 → 4.9 ms. The largest seed in the tree (0100F2C0115B6000, 31,400 rows): 17.6 → 0.5 MB, 49.8 → 5.8 ms. Both largest lists together (63,900 rows, a device list as big as a seed): 32.4 → 1.0 MB, 87.5 → 10.6 ms. The fill runs on the list's own utility queue (`DM_PSO_USER_QUEUE`), so its time is off the replay; the memory is the gain.
- **Not done:** `warm_parse_h` still splits each list twice at the start (once to log its line count, once to parse it), a few ms per large list; left for after the start-up log lines' privacy change, which edits the line between the two splits.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_user_hash`: on this GAME's seed and the largest other list in the tree, with a device list carrying a comment, an empty line, a CR-ended row, a seed row again and a cut last line, 95,900 rows recorded (every seed row, every other row twice, rows one byte off a seed row or with a trailing space, an empty row) write exactly the file a reference string filter writes (27,580 rows for Ocarina of Time); `DM_PSO_USER_HASH=0` checks the reference against the old code. A hash that drops a row's last byte fails the file check (the negative control, tried by hand). `check_t_pso_user_queue` passes as before.
- **Phone check:** none needed for the picture; on the phone the resident memory after the first new pipeline is about 15 MB lower in Ocarina of Time (the memory line), and `pso_user.tsv` grows as before (no duplicate rows, new areas still recorded).

### Shader extraction reruns after translation-only updates

- **Symptom:** an unchanged NSP spends minutes in `shader split` after a shader-chain update, on both Mac and iOS builds, although the extracted package is identical.
- **Cause:** the split key included all graphics Python tools, every profile file and the whole decoder host executable. Translation tools, profile notes and the host's compiled `DMSH_CHAIN` label therefore invalidated extraction.
- **Fix:** the NSP script follows the extractor's transitive repository Python imports and fingerprints its profile settings, manual facts, checked/generated tables, modules, RomFS/DLC identities and capture directories. A managed decoder host records its build inputs with only the chain label normalized, bound to its executable's bytes; unknown or replaced binaries use their binary identity. All other host build inputs and rewritten module contents still invalidate conservatively. Package and supplemental-pair digests reject damaged cached outputs. The common split serves Mac and iOS, while translation keeps its own target-specific keys. Existing split caches require one warm-up.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md#build-performance).
- **Check:** `test_nsp_incremental.py` covers translation-only hits, transitive readers, extraction settings, auxiliary modules, explicit captures, decoder changes, corrupt outputs and the existing registered/derived, DLC and damaged-input paths. `test_host_msl.py` verifies executable-bound provenance, chain-only reuse and invalidation for decoder/profile/compiler/header changes. `test_nsp_mac.py` checks separate Mac/iOS translation outputs.

### Offline pipeline archive rebuilt after a chain-only update

- **Symptom:** rebuilding an unchanged NSP after a shader-chain update runs `metal-tt` for minutes even when all referenced Metal libraries and pipeline settings remain identical.
- **Cause:** the offline archive key included the chain label, profile stamp, whole builder source and absolute library paths inside `b-<chain>`. These can change without changing the compiler's inputs.
- **Fix:** `pso_offline.py` keys each archive by the complete generated pipeline configuration with library locations normalized to their labels, every referenced library's name and bytes, GPU architecture, platform, minimum OS and Metal translator version. It still validates reflection against the current chain before planning. After a cache hit it writes the current `prebuilt.tsv`, remainder list and archive metadata, so host validation remains current. macOS and iOS use the same builder with separate target keys. Legacy cache keys rebuild once; a changed shader, entry point, pipeline descriptor or compiler target still rebuilds the archive. The cache format has an explicit version for future changes not represented by these inputs.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md#build-performance), while measuring repeat Mac app builds. Timings and platform-validation limits are in the game note.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` exercises chain relocation and metadata refresh on both platforms, changed shader bytes and pipeline settings, target and compiler changes, missing archives, stale reflection and legacy keys.

### Offline pipeline archive and profile depth disagree

- **Symptom:** the host logs `pipeline archive bundled with the app: made for ... this run needs ...`, naming different depths, and pipeline bases compile during play despite a successful archive build. There need not be a `[RED]` line.
- **Cause:** offline builds use the profile's `depth_mode` (`gl` when omitted), while runtime vertex entries follow `nvnDeviceSetDepthMode`. An archive built with `main0` cannot supply a near-zero base using `main0_z`. The runtime stamp comparison correctly rejects the mismatch; the missing input is in the profile.
- **Fix:** declare the observed mode in the profile and rebuild the archive. Both vertex entries already exist, so this does not require a shader-chain change. `scripts/regress.py` records the runtime depth and compares it with the tested revision's profile on every NVN smoke run, including quick runs without an archive. It also records bundled archive stamp rejection for any chain, depth or pair-list mismatch. New mismatches fail the check; unchanged baseline mismatches are reported as known and prevent `--record` from marking the game validated. An absent depth log is unknown, not evidence for GL. This detects mismatches on exercised routes; it does not infer unobserved games' modes or edit profiles automatically. For a new game the NSP script does: the heap and address region checks of a provisional or derived profile set the key from the game's own `nvnDeviceSetDepthMode` call in their boot, before the shader steps ([Super Mario RPG](../../dm/games/0100BC0018138000/README.md) before this: a 24 minute prebuild at the default mode, then 0 of 34 archive lookups found). A game with no check boot, or one that stops before the call, keeps the default.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Echoes of Wisdom](../../dm/games/01008CF01BAAC000/README.md#rendering-and-shaders), update 1.1.0, [GitHub issue #758](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/758): its note already recorded near-zero depth, but its profile omitted the key. The game note records archive validation and its limits; castle-entry performance remains open.
- **Also confirmed:** missing depth keys on [SteamWorld Heist II](../../dm/games/010089C01E9CA000/README.md#shaders), [NieR:Automata](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md#files-and-shaders), [Portal](../../dm/games/01007BB017812000/README.md#rendering), [DRAGON QUEST III HD-2D Remake](../../dm/games/01003E601E324000/README.md#files-and-shaders), [Raging Loop](../../dm/games/010020400E1C2000/README.md#files-and-shaders), [Subnautica](../../dm/games/0100429011144000/README.md#files-and-shaders), [Subnautica: Below Zero](../../dm/games/010014C011146000/README.md#files-and-shaders), [Batman: Arkham Knight](../../dm/games/0100ACD0163D0000/README.md#what-it-needs), [Blasphemous 2](../../dm/games/010040801A4C8000/README.md#what-it-needs), [EA SPORTS FC 27](../../dm/games/0100C49025D3E000/README.md#what-it-needs), [Paper Mario: The Origami King](../../dm/games/0100A3900C3E2000/README.md#what-it-needs) [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md#what-it-needs) (13.0.5, a player's Mac app: its 1,316.9 MB archive refused, 31,148 pairs built before the first frame in 397 s) and [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (v131072, a Mac app: 1,483 pairs built before the first frame in 6.3 s; with the key the archive is taken and the first frame comes 5 s sooner). Recorded routes, short isolated boots and the NSP script's check boots select near-zero depth on their registered module builds; their notes record the evidence. The profile corrections have not been measured as rebuilt-archive performance fixes.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` runs the archive builder with the affected games' real profiles and synthetic libraries, checking the stamp and the vertex entry. `dm/tools/tests/test_regress.py` replays runtime depth and rejection logs through a smoke run, checking failure classification and reporting.

### Offline pipeline archive made for another major version

- **Symptom:** `pipeline archive bundled with the app: this system cannot open …/applegpu_<gpu>.mtl4a (deployment target for architecture … found in archive … is not compatible with current OS …): the bases are built on the device`. On macOS 26.6 with Xcode 27.1, `t_pso_bundle` failed 10 checks, from `bundle: opened` on, and a Mac app packed there compiled every base on its first start, as without the archive.
- **Cause:** a Metal 4 pipeline archive opens only on the major system version it was made for. Measured on macOS 27.2 (M4 Pro, `newArchiveWithURL:`): archives that `metal-tt` made with `-macos-version-min` 26.0 or 26.6 were refused, from libraries built for 26.0 or 26.6 alike; those made for 27.0 and 27.2 opened. `pso_offline.py` took the SDK's major version for every platform. That suits the iPhone, whose system is the SDK's, but the Mac app is built for the Mac that builds it, and Xcode 27.1's SDK is 27.0 on macOS 26.6. Also, the Mac host had no deployment target, so clang took the SDK's 27.0, and `pack_app.py` copied it into the app's `LSMinimumSystemVersion`. `metal-tt` also refuses libraries built for a later system than the archive's (`image AIR version (2.9) is bigger than the one of the target (2.8)`, macOS 27.0 libraries for a 26.6 archive); the chain's Mac libraries are built for the building Mac's system, so that Mac's major version is never below them.
- **Fix:** for macOS, `pso_offline.py`'s default `--os-min` is this Mac's major version (`sw_vers`); iOS keeps the SDK's. The Mac host's minimum is `MACOS_MIN` in `dm/mac/min_os.mk` (26.6, as the README asked; 27.3 since the Metal 4-only release), on the host's compile lines (`-mmacosx-version-min`, libvpx's too), which the NSP script and `pack_app.py` follow. The host builds with the same warnings at 26.6 as at 27.0 (no unguarded newer API). An archive is still for one major version: an iPhone on iOS 26 cannot open the IPA's 27.0 archive and builds its bases itself (open: one archive per major version would serve both). Since then the iOS default is the app's deployment target (`deploymentTarget` in `dm/ios/project.yml`, 27.3), which the app's libraries are built for as well; the SDK's major version is left only for a tree without that project.
- **Found on:** no game: [GitHub issue #673](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/673), `t_pso_bundle` on an M4 Pro with macOS 26.6.2 and Xcode 27.1.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (`test_macos_takes_this_macs_major_version`). `t_pso_bundle` builds its archive with the default and opens it on macOS 27.2: 52 checks pass with its libraries built for 27.0 and for 26.6 (`MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=26.6`), the mirror of the reporter's Mac; the same libraries in an archive made for 26.6 failed the 10 checks. Open: no run on macOS 26.x here.

### Pipeline archive files left behind by a major system update

- **Symptom:** after a major macOS or iOS update, every start logs `[RED] pipeline archive file …/pso_archive/p00042.mtl4a not usable (deployment target for architecture … is not compatible with current OS …): its bases are built again`, once per file written on the old system, but no `pipeline prebuild:` line; the game then stalls for one to several seconds whenever a scene shows bases it had before (frames of 1–7 s talking to a character or when bombs go off). The red lines and the missing prebuild come back on every later start; a base a draw built is captured again, so the stalls move on to the scenes not yet played since the update.
- **Cause:** the [same rule as the offline archive](#offline-pipeline-archive-made-for-another-major-version) for the files the host writes: Metal opens a pipeline archive only on the major version it was made for. `pso4_init` skipped a file it could not open but left it on disk. The prebuild's files (`p*.mtl4a`) carry its bases, and `prebuilt.tsv` still matched (the stamp does not hold the system version), so the prebuild was skipped as done for good and those bases were left to the draws, each built on the submit thread (about 0.3 s, M1 Pro) the first time a scene needed it. The refused files also stayed on disk for good.
- **Fix:** a file Metal refuses is removed with its index, as old indexes already were; a refused prebuild file also removes `prebuilt.tsv`, so the next start runs the prebuild again. The log says `… not usable (…): removed, its bases are built again`; in NX 18 the parentheses end with Metal's error domain and code (`…; <domain> <code>`), so a removal that does not follow an OS update shows in `log.txt`. NX 18 (`pso4_init`, `arch_file_state`): a file is removed only when Metal refused it, `arch_container_ok` rejected its container or it is empty or no regular file (upstream's rule), never when it could not be opened or its size read (`… not usable (could not be read): its bases are built again`, the file kept); a refused `p` or `pb` file removes `prebuilt.tsv` whichever program's prebuild wrote it last; a plain file kept because it could not be opened or read stays out of `compact_failed.tsv` (`g_arch_unusable`), as it is out of the start's own list, or a failed compaction would run again at every start while it stays. After a major iOS or macOS update the first start therefore shows **Preparing pipelines** for its long prebuild once, as a first start does ([Pipeline prebuild](../settings.md#pipeline-prebuild)).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md), M1 Pro with 16 GB, after updating macOS 26.6.2 to 27.2.1: 57 refused files (1.68 GB of 3.1 GB), 7 of them prebuild files, and no prebuild in a 24-minute session, which had 53 stalls of 100 ms or more on new pipelines (39 s in all). On a copy of that archive upstream's fixed host removed the 57 files and `prebuilt.tsv` and ran the prebuild; on upstream's main with an empty archive the whole prebuild took 13.4 minutes for 34,649 pairs (10 workers) and 2.7 more for the prewarm lists, once.
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive` (`test_unusable_file_removed`): a prebuild file Metal will not open, its index and `prebuilt.tsv` are removed on the next start, and the other files still give every base; in NX 18's form a prebuild file the start cannot open (no read permission) is left in place, and an empty one is removed. It fails without the fix.

### Python memory during offline pipeline archive preparation

- **Symptom:** `pso_offline.py` holds about 1.8 GB while `metal-tt` builds an archive of 140,036 bases, and the system's memory view shows the build far larger than that (180.5 GB) before `metal-tt` fails or is stopped.
- **Cause:** the builder made the pipeline script (503 MB at 140,036 bases) as one JSON string and as bytes, read the previous script whole to compare them, and serialized the whole key input once more; the descriptors stayed referenced while `metal-tt` ran. That is at most about 1.8 GB: the 180.5 GB were `metal-tt` and its compiler processes, not Python.
- **Fix:** `write_script` streams the script to `bases.mtl4-json.partial` and replaces the previous script only when the two differ; `archive_key` hashes the same JSON as it is encoded (the keys of earlier builds still match); the descriptors are released before `metal-tt` starts. Measured with 140,036 synthetic bases: peak 1.83 → 0.45 GB, 1.81 → 0.06 GB held while `metal-tt` runs, about 11 s more encoding (Python 3.15), byte-identical script and key.
- **Found on:** [Mario Party Superstars](../../dm/games/01006FE013472000/README.md) ([GitHub issue #1022](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1022), [PR #1024](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1024)). Open: why `metal-tt` failed after 76 minutes there, and how its memory grows with the number of bases.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (`test_large_script_memory_and_compatible_archive_key`).

### Bounded groups for the offline pipeline compiler

- **Symptom:** the offline archive stops with only `applegpu-nt command failed`, sometimes after `Max Unstruct ID` lines ([a note, not the failure](#front-end-at--o1-apples-control-flow-lowering)); the macOS kernel log reports `no paging space action` and kills `air-nt` as the largest compressed process.
- **Cause:** Apple's GPU compiler can exhaust memory while compiling the complete pipeline list. Lowering the Python builder's memory does not bound the compiler's. RSS excludes compressed and swapped allocations: use kernel resource-kill diagnostics and physical-footprint measurements to distinguish this failure from a compiler rejection or a full disk.
- **Fix:** with more bases than `--batch-size` (default 1,000; 0: one compiler invocation), `pso_offline.py` compiles groups of at most that many (`--base-jobs` at a time, by default a third of the logical CPUs and at most one per 6 GiB of memory over 4 GiB: one at a time on an 8 GB Mac, as before, five on a 16-core 48 GB M4 Max), each script referencing only its libraries, then merges all results with `metal-pack -nthreads 1`. The normal single-file output, full-input archive key and runtime stamp stay compatible. Successful groups are cached by their own descriptors, library bytes, GPU, OS and compiler. A failed group or merge publishes neither a new complete archive key nor coverage stamp, and keeps the groups already done. The groups stay in the work folder (`batches.<arch>/`, 1.2 GB for Odyssey), so a run after a failure or after a change to some libraries compiles only what changed. This bounds input count, not compiler memory; an individual pipeline or the final merge may still exhaust resources.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md#files-and-shaders), which records the resource-kill evidence, before/after measurements and complete archive validation. [Issue #1022](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1022) requests archive partitioning for another game's resource-heavy build; that workload remains unverified.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` covers the default, pilot reuse, compatible full-archive reuse, partial final batches, changed-group invalidation, compile failure and merge failure. `t_pso_bundle` compiles one base per group, opens the merged result, retrieves a base from each group, rejects an absent depth entry and compares the complete coverage stamp with the ordinary build.

### A failed pipeline archive stops the whole build

- **Symptom:** `--platform mac-app` or the iPhone build stops at its pipeline archive stage, after every earlier stage has finished: `[RED] pso_offline: metal-tt failed for applegpu_<gpu> (exit 1)`, then `pso_offline.py exit code 1`.
- **Cause:** the archive is an optimization (without one the device builds the bases on its first start, as with `--gpu none`), but the NSP script and `build_ipa.sh` treated its failure as fatal. Whatever made it fail, such as [memory](#bounded-groups-for-the-offline-pipeline-compiler) or a [full disk](#offline-pipeline-archive-on-a-full-disk), lost the whole build.
- **Fix:** both remove the unfinished archive folder, print a `[RED] pipeline archive: pso_offline.py failed` line and pack the app without one. The NSP script ends the stage with a warning (`⚠` in the console, `warn: true` in its JSON event). `pso_offline.py` run by itself still exits non-zero.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md#found-on-this-game) 1.4.1 on an M1 (`applegpu_g13g`), [LNPANDY/NX2Apple#98](https://github.com/LNPANDY/NX2Apple/issues/98), through [PR #494](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/494).
