- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_nsp_mac_app.py` (`test_pso_archive_failure_is_a_warning`, `test_ipa_build_says_its_archive_failed`), `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (`IpaFallback`), `dm/tools/tests/test_nsp_progress.py` (`test_warned_stage_end`).

### Offline pipeline archive on a full disk

- **Symptom:** `[RED] pso_offline: metal-tt failed for applegpu_<gpu> (exit 1)` with only `applegpu-nt command failed` in `metal-tt.<arch>.log`.
- **Cause:** `metal-tt` writes one `.airp` per base and its `translate-*` folders in `TMPDIR` (the work folder), and it does not say when the volume is full. Cruis'n Blast's 33,858 bases failed with 366 MB free. A failed run also left its temporary files in `pso/mac-work`.
- **Fix:** `pso_offline.py` clears those temporary files and stops before `metal-tt` when the work folder's volume has less than about 96 KB per base free (2 GB at least), and names the free space. When `metal-tt` still fails, it clears the temporary files and says when the volume is nearly full.
- **Found on:** Cruis'n Blast (0100B41013C82000, version 1.0.7; no profile in this repository), on a Mac with 366 MB free.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (`test_low_disk_space_stops_before_metal_tt`).

### Compute pipelines compiled at first use

- **Symptom:** a game without a recorded prewarm list (a provisional profile) stalls at its first dispatches while Metal compiles their compute pipelines. The Mac app's pipeline archive held render bases only (`archive.tsv`: `bases`), whatever the shader map listed.
- **Cause:** a compute pipeline was only ever built on the device, through the `MTLDevice` (`newComputePipelineStateWithFunction`), at its first dispatch or from the prewarm's `C` rows, and a provisional profile's `pso_manifest.tsv` has none. Yet such a pipeline depends on nothing but its function: the host passes no threadgroup size or other option (the dispatch gives the threadgroup size), so it can be compiled offline for every compute shader of the shader map.
- **Fix:** `pso_offline.py` adds a compute pipeline descriptor that names the function alone for the `<key>_b` of every compute shader: the prewarm list's `C` rows first, then the map's rows of stage `compute`; shaders with function constants are left out (the host specializes them). They compile in groups of their own (`batches.<arch>/c<index>/`, 16 a group, `--jobs` groups at a time), cached by their inputs and merged with the bases. The host looks `<key>_b` up in the bundled archive first (`cps_new`) and builds it on a miss; the prewarm's last lines count both.
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md) (v131072, with a provisional profile before this repository had one), chain `ae59da13e481`, on an M5 Pro, measured on a fork with other host changes: 233 compute shaders give 233 compute pipelines beside 3 bases, a 35 MB archive. Built in 16 groups, 7 at a time, in 268 s, against 453 s as one `metal-tt` run (842 and 829 s of CPU; both at background priority, with load averages of 19-26 from other jobs); unchanged inputs reuse it in 0.3 s. Two headless boots of 90 s to the title took all 7 of their compute pipelines from it and built none, with no watchdog stop. Also [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (v131072, chain `f9f01e4f9994`, `applegpu_g18g`): 4,125 compute pipelines (480 MB, 204 s), and all 103 compute pipelines of a route to the village came from the bundle with none built; PR #1522 measured what the archive saves with the Metal cache cold, 195 s of submit-thread pipelines (155 s in the title movie's twelve kernels) to 0.9 s.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (`Compute`, among them `test_pipelines_from_a_library_image` for one-file libraries, `test_compute_pipelines_are_groups_of_their_own`, `test_compute_pipelines_alone_make_an_archive`); `t_pso_bundle` builds an archive with compute shaders and checks that a map's shaders come from it and that a shader with constants or outside the map is built.
- **Open:** iOS archives get the compute pipelines too (`build_ipa.sh`), not measured on a device.

### Compute pipelines outside the bundled archive built at every start

- **Symptom:** a compute pipeline the archive bundled with the app does not hold (no bundle, as in a headless boot or a build without `pso_offline.py`; a shader outside the shader map; an OS update the bundle's binaries do not survive) was compiled again at its first dispatch of every start the system's shader cache had dropped it, and the device's own pipeline archive (`pso_archive`) never held one: `compute pipelines so far: 0 from the archive bundled with the app, N built` start after start.
- **Cause:** `cps_new` built such a pipeline through the `MTLDevice` (`newComputePipelineStateWithFunction`), which no pipeline serializer sees. Only the render bases went through the flush period's Metal 4 compiler, whose serializer captures what it compiles.
- **Fix:** a compute function with a Metal 4 library descriptor (`fn4_note`) is looked up in the bundle, then in the device's own archive files. On a miss it was built through the `MTLDevice`, for the dispatch, and compiled again through the Metal 4 compiler into the current flush period on a background queue, one at a time at utility QoS (`arch_harvest`, `arch_capture_cs`): the serializer captures compute pipelines as it captures bases, and the flush writes them. The dispatch does not wait for the capture, which compiles the pipeline whole where a plain compile gets it from the system's shader cache: a first version that built the dispatch's pipeline through the capture took 95 ms for Star Fox's first 5 compute pipelines, against 1.2 ms through the `MTLDevice`. Since the Metal 4-only release the dispatch's pipeline is compiled by the host's plain Metal 4 compiler (`g_comp4`), not the `MTLDevice`: with no serializer it costs the same as the device's compile, cold or from the system's shader cache (measured on an M4 Max, macOS 27.3), and the capture still runs in the background. A period with compute pipelines is a plain file, never a `p<seq>` one, so the lookups skip the prebuild's files. Compaction captures a compute pipeline the prewarm takes from an old file again, as it does bases. At first a function with constants and the embedded kernels, which had no descriptor, were still built through the `MTLDevice` and never looked up. Now the embedded kernels are noted (library `host/builtin`), and a function with constants is noted with its values, so both are looked up in, and captured into, the device's own archive; a function with constants is still never in the bundled one (`metal-tt` cannot specialize it). The log counts them: `compute pipelines so far: N from the archive bundled with the app, M from this device's archive, K built (… ms looking up, … ms building)`, and `pipeline archive: … and M compute pipelines written to …`.
- **Measurement:** headless boots of 90 s to the title (frame 5,100) without the bundle, each series with an archive and prewarm list of its own. Before: 5 compute pipelines built at the first start (1.2 ms in all, from the system's shader cache) and 9 at the second (2.2 ms), none kept. After: 5 built at the first start (0.6 ms), and the flushes wrote them (`2 new pipeline bases and 1 compute pipelines`, `50 … and 4 compute pipelines`); the second start took 8 from the device's archive (the prewarm's `C` rows and the first dispatches; 1.4 ms of lookups in all) and built none. Both reached the same frame with the same five `[RED]` lines. With a warm system cache the two cost about the same; the archive keeps the pipelines when that cache drops them.
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md) (v131072, with a provisional profile before this repository had one), chain `ae59da13e481`, M5 Pro, macOS 27.0, on a fork with other host changes. Also [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): without the bundle, the first start wrote 88 compute pipelines into the device's archive and the second took 88 from it.
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive` (`compute_pipelines`): a compute pipeline is built, compiled into the flush period in the background, written to a plain file, taken from it again without a build, captured again during a compaction, and a function without a descriptor is refused: it gets no pipeline and the error `function X has no Metal 4 descriptor (fn4_note)`; `t_pso_bundle`: a shader outside the map is captured, one with function constants is not.

### A learned mesh pipeline is built again after prewarm

- **Symptom:** a manifest contains native mesh rows, but the game still logs `task / mesh pipeline: … required at frame` for the same pipelines after preparation.
- **Cause:** a shader row could carry NVN2's task / mesh stage numbers (6 / 7), while the host uses slots 3 / 4. The prewarm workers cache a shader by name, so the first worker could publish the raw stage even for a later request from a valid mesh row. A draw using that shader could write stage 7 into its own mesh row; the next launch then rejected that row.
- **Fix:** `warm_parse` loads standalone shader rows (`S`, `C`) with the stage the game's shader map gives, as the live loader does, once per shader name. `pso_mesh_skey_parse` reads a stored stage 7 as 4 in a mesh row only; the map, task, fragment and render-state checks still apply. One pipeline is prepared per normalized mesh state and task shader. Ported from upstream NXNative `5ca161f26` (PR #2912).
- **Check:** `t_pso_skey` (a stage-7 mesh row parses as stage 4), `t_pso_warm` (raw and host stage numbers together: one library and one pipeline).
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md) upstream; not reproduced in NX 18.

### Native mesh pipelines compiled at their first draw

- **Symptom:** the first sight of a scene drawn with task and mesh shaders freezes for seconds on a cold Metal cache, on every start once the system's shader cache has dropped the pipelines, although the prewarm finished: `task / mesh pipeline: task … mesh … fragment …, N ms required at frame F`. Before ea0c98df the draw was left out instead, and the foliage stayed missing or white until the pipeline was ready.
- **Cause:** a native task / mesh pipeline (`mtk_pso` in `nvn_geometry.inc`) was built only at its first draw, through the `MTLDevice` (`newRenderPipelineStateWithMeshDescriptor`), and nothing kept it. No prewarm row named one, so the shader rows loaded its libraries and built nothing, and no pipeline serializer saw it, so the pipeline archive never held one. Since ea0c98df the draw waits for its pipeline, so each one met in play is a stall on the submit thread.
- **Fix:**
  - **Rows:** `mtk_pso` records each pipeline it builds as an `M` row in `pso_user.tsv` and the record list. The row holds the task shader's key or `-`, then the `pso_skey` hex of the render state with the mesh shader in the vertex place and the pixel shader, if any; `pso_mesh_skey_parse` refuses a row with a vertex layout, a specialized fragment or the wrong stages.
  - **Prewarm:** the prewarm builds each row on its workers (a task of its own, `kind 3`), so with the prepare step every listed pipeline is ready before the first frame.
  - **Claim:** the submit thread takes the pipeline under the live shader objects when they come from the same library and entry (`warm_mesh_shader_equal`). The program prefetch loads its own objects for the same keys, and claiming by object identity alone dropped prewarmed pipelines (`native mesh prewarm: live shader variant differs`, 0-29 a run on an M2 Pro, reported in PR #1368). A mesh shader loaded with its other entry after a depth mode change (`entry_z`) is not taken.
  - **Archive:** with the Metal 4 compiler, `mtk_build` keeps native mesh pipelines in the pipeline archive as `cps_new` keeps compute pipelines. It looks a pipeline up in the archive files first (a `p` file when a flush holds only such pipelines, as they have no vertex layout). On a miss the `MTLDevice` built it, which the system's shader cache serves when it holds it, and the Metal 4 compiler compiled it again into the flush period in the background (`arch_harvest`). Since the Metal 4-only release the host's plain Metal 4 compiler builds it for the draw instead, which the system's shader cache serves as well (a pipeline the device built before is compiled once more after the switch), and the background capture is unchanged. Compaction captures one the prewarm takes from an old plain file again. A pipeline is whole, with no base: a game has some hundred, each with functions of its own.
  - **Bundled archive:** `pso_offline.py` compiles a pipeline for each `M` row of the profile's `pso_manifest.tsv` into the archive bundled with the app, so a fresh install takes them from it instead of building them in the prepare step or at a draw. It describes each one as `mtk_desc4` does: the task shader's entry or, with no task, the mesh shader's `entry_object`; the mesh shader's entry (`entry_z` for near = 0); the pixel shader's entry; the threads and payload of `mesh_stage`; and the colour attachments `pso_desc` makes from the row's key. The integer, mixed and dual-source entry points, write masks cut to the written outputs and the output map, blending only on written float targets, an unwritten second source as zero and advanced blend modes all follow the host. Rows whose descriptor it cannot match are left to the device and counted: functions with function constants (`metal-tt` makes no specialized function from a library), no pixel shader (the host's own empty fragment is not among the game's libraries), the alpha test or a logic operation (side libraries), and a colour format the pipelines script cannot name. The pipelines are groups of their own (`m<index>`). A group `metal-tt` refuses is left out with a red line, kept as refused by its own inputs so later builds reuse the archive without compiling it again or merging every group, and the rest of the archive still ships ([issue #283](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/283): one refused pipeline failed the whole archive).
  - **Seed rows:** Ocarina of Time's `pso_manifest.tsv` carries `M` rows recorded in play; [its note](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) has the count, the areas and what each set measured.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game), v131072. Measured by contributors on hosts that carried the rows and prewarm of PR #1368, not the archive path:
  - **M5 Max, cold cache:** without rows, the Kokiri Forest entry built 63 pipelines one after another on the submit thread, 59.5 s in all, with one frame of 42.9 s. With 88 `M` rows and the prepare step, all 88 were built before the first frame (76.6 s on 18 workers), none at a draw, and the longest frame was 281 ms.
  - **M1, cold cache:** a 300 s walk built 47 pipelines at their draws, 103.5 s in all, the longest 12.76 s. With the rows and a fork's archive, 58 were prewarmed in 7.3 s on 8 workers.
  - **iPad Pro (M5):** 1.9-5 s per pipeline with a cold cache, 0.1-45 ms cached.
  - **M4 Pro:** with the rows, 0 built in play, against 56 without.
- **Check:**
  - `t_pso_skey`: mesh keys round-trip, and malformed or incompatible rows are refused.
  - `t_pso_warm` (`native_mesh`, on both compiler paths): only the canonical row loads, the warm task publishes a pipeline, duplicate live objects claim it without a build, a different depth entry is not claimed and builds its own, and the warmed and demanded pipelines draw the same pixels.
  - `t_pso_archive` (`mesh_pipelines`): a pipeline is built, captured in the background and written to a `p` file, with or without a task or pixel shader. A later start takes it from that file without a build, a compaction captures it again, and a function without a Metal 4 descriptor is refused (no pipeline, not captured).
  - `t_mesh_first_use`: no first draw is left out.
  - `t_pso_bundle`: `pso_offline.py` compiles the `M` rows the host's own keys make (`pso_skey_from`) for this Mac's GPU. `mtk_build` then takes three pipelines from the bundle, building and capturing nothing: one with a task shader, a blended one with its own object function and a write mask cut by the output map, and one with no colour target. A row whose pixel shader has function constants, a row with no pixel shader and other blend state are built. With the write mask written as `All`, the lookups miss, so the check depends on matching the descriptor exactly.
  - `test_pso_offline.py` (`Mesh`, and `Build`'s task / mesh tests): the key layout, rows left out, `pso_desc`'s entry points and colour state, the script, groups, and a refused group that leaves the rest of the archive, kept by its own inputs (the next build reuses the archive with no `metal-tt` and no merge; a change to the group's library compiles it again), while a killed compiler leaves no key.
- **Measured:** the 124 rows on Ocarina of Time's library image for `applegpu_g19p` (iOS 27.3, Xcode 27.3 beta's `metal-tt`, AIR-TT 32023.925, on a Mac with an `applegpu_g16s` GPU): 122 pipelines, 2 rows with the same descriptor and none left out, 35 MB in 10 s in 8 groups.
- **Open:** an iPhone run of an app with the bundled task / mesh pipelines has not been made: the prewarm line should then count them as `taken from the pipeline archive` on a fresh install, with no `pso_archive/` folder. Before that, the 60 fps run on an iPhone 18 Pro Max took its 123 from the device's own archive files, kept from earlier installs. Rows left to the device as above are still built there.

### The log had no start time

- **Symptom:** a start-time change cannot be measured from a device log. `scripts/devlog.py` dated everything from frame counts and power windows, and the log said nothing about when the player pressed Play or when the first frame showed. The prepare step's own line gave its time, but not the time on either side of it.
- **Cause:** nothing in the host stamped the start. The process start (`g_t0_ns`) is not the start on the iPhone, where the host's loading begins at the app's launch, long before Play, while the settings screen shows.
- **Fix:** the settings screens set `DM_PLAY_AT`, the wall clock at Play (`Launcher.swift` on the iPhone, before its cloud-save check; `dm/mac/Launcher.swift`). `front_ios.m` `startGame` and the Mac launcher set it for a start without the screen, and a program hand-over on the Mac sets it for the program it starts (`prog_plan_make`). When the host starts the game's code (`main.c` → `dm_start_game`, `hle_lifecycle.c`), it logs `start: Play at <local date and time, ms> local time (<from where>)`. When the first present completes on the GPU (`nvn_mtl.m`, the present feedback), it logs `start: first frame N s after Play: host load A s, prepare B s, first frame C s`. Host load runs from Play until the game's code starts: the front end's hand-over, the iPhone's cloud-save check, and the host's loading where it had not finished before Play. Prepare is the prepare step that held the first frame (`warm_start`: the prewarm lists and the pairs' prebuild, with the archive's open). First frame is the rest: the game's own start-up and loading and its first frame's work. Each part is at least 0, and they add up to the total (`start_line.h`). Without `DM_PLAY_AT` the lines count from the host's own start. `scripts/devlog.py` reads both lines into a `start` row and four rows of its comparison.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/host/tests GAME=<id> start-line` (`t_start_line`): `DM_PLAY_AT`'s values, the Play line to the millisecond, and the three parts. The parts must add up, a start before Play counts as no load, and a prepare longer than what is left after the load is cut to it. `dm/host/tests/t_oe.c`: a hand-over's plan carries `DM_PLAY_AT` once, at the hand-over's time, not the first program's. `dm/tools/tests/test_devlog.py` `StartLines` fills in `start_line.h`'s own formats and reads and compares them. Not yet seen on a device.
- **Found on:** no game; the X2.0 round's start-time items, whose gains could not be shown without it (the archive open on the path to the first frame, and S rows loaded after it).

### Pipelines with a vertex layout compiled at a fresh install's prepare step

- **Symptom:** a fresh install (or a start after the system dropped its shader cache) spends its prepare step compiling bases although the app carries a pipeline archive made offline: `pipeline prepare: … bases N compiled, M from the archive, …` with N about the number of the seed list's `R` rows (Breath of the Wild's prepare step on the Mac: 872 bases compiled, [its note](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#pipelines-on-a-cold-start)). Each takes 10-80 ms; on an iPhone the prepare screen stays up for them.
- **Cause:** the archive bundled with the app held only the pulling bases of the shader pairs (no vertex descriptor), the compute pipelines and the task / mesh pipelines. A draw's pipeline with a vertex layout is built from a base of its own (`pso_desc` / `pso_desc4`: the vertex function, the vertex descriptor of the key's attributes and streams, the fragment entry the colour targets call for, samples and alpha to coverage), which `pso_offline.py` never compiled, and `arch_lookup_in` skipped the bundle for such a lookup (`plain`): only the device's own files could hold them.
- **Fix:**
  - **Offline:** `pso_offline.py` compiles the base of every `R` row of the profile's `pso_manifest.tsv` (`plan_layout`, `LayoutBase`), described as `pso_desc` makes it, in Python: the vertex function, or its shadow-input one (`<key>_bv`, when `vin_on` would take it); the vertex descriptor of `vfmt` / `vfmt_for_ct` / `VB_SLOT`, with the zero buffer at slot 30 and the CPU conversion's slots from 1 (`vconv_wanted`); the fragment entry of `fragment_entry` (`entry_u`, `entry_m`, `entry_d`, else `entry`, split out of the task / mesh rows' `mesh_fragment`); samples and alpha to coverage; every colour attachment unspecialized; the host's empty fragment for a depth-only row. Rows that share a base give one, and a row whose vertex shader has no stage input and whose base the pairs already give adds none.
  - **Left to the device**, counted by reason in the output (`N render rows: M layout bases, left out: …`) and in `archive.tsv` (`layout_rows`): a key that does not parse; a merged pass (`merged`); a specialized fragment or a mark (`specialized`: `pso_skey.fz`, the merge's own function, transform feedback, geometry capture, rasterizer discard, view amplification); the alpha test or a logic operation (`side library`); a reflection of another chain version or none; a mesh shader; a vertex shader that writes the layer; a pixel shader that reads the primitive ID; a missing library or entry point; function constants (`constants`) other than `dm_border_off`, which the script specializes as `pso_build` does when the row's key has `border` 0, or any constant when it has `border` 1; a vertex format the pipelines script cannot name (`format`: `FloatRG11B10`); and an attribute beyond its buffer's stride (`stride`).
  - **Groups:** the layout bases are groups of their own (`batches.<arch>/l<index>/`, 64 each, `--jobs` at a time). A group `metal-tt` refuses is compiled again in halves (`l<index>/a`, `l<index>/b`, and so on down to one base), each half cached and its refusal kept by its own inputs as a task / mesh group's is, so a base `metal-tt` refuses costs only itself. `archive.tsv` names what an architecture's archive left out (`left_out`). `--no-layout` leaves the layout bases out.
  - **Host:** a plain lookup (a base with a vertex layout) asks the bundle after a miss in the file its index names, and before the device's own files (`arch_lookup_in`). A compute lookup does not ask it there, since `cps_new` asked it first. The prepare line ends with `; N layout bases from the archive bundled with the app` when the app has one, as does the prewarm's `pipeline bases so far` line (`g_pb_bundle_vd`); `scripts/devlog.py` reads it.
- **Why the stride rule:** `pso_desc` gives a constant stream (stride 0) a stride of 4 whatever its attributes. The device's compiler takes such a descriptor, `metal-tt` refuses it (`Attribute at index 1: the attribute offset (0) + attribute size (8) must be <= the stride of the buffer (4)`), and at first that one row cost its whole group of 64. The descriptors themselves match: the offline `vertex_descriptor` writes MTLVertexDescriptor's defaults (offset 0, `PerVertex`, step rate 1) for the slots it does not use, and the engine's own capture (`MTL4PipelineDataSetSerializer` with descriptor capture, `serializeAsPipelinesScript`) left them out and had an `options.shader_reflection` block, which the lookup ignores.
- **Measured:** `t_pso_bundle` (M4 Max, `applegpu_g16s`, macOS 27.3): 15 `R` rows written by the engine's own `pso_key_fill` / `pso_skey_from` (float, half, instanced, constant and I2F streams, 4 samples with alpha to coverage, an integer input fed its own class and through its shadow inputs of either sign, integer / mixed / dual-source fragments, depth only): the 13 expected bases came from the bundle with nothing compiled, each counted by `g_pb_bundle_vd`; the function-constant row and the beyond-stride row were built. With the `arch_lookup_in` change undone the 13 were compiled. On the game libraries this Mac holds (chain `ac67cb709fa0`, iOS libraries, `applegpu_g19p`, iOS 27.3, Xcode 27.3 beta's `metal-tt`, AIR-TT 32023.925; layout bases alone, `--jobs 4` at background priority on an M4 Max): Breath of the Wild's 960 rows gave 902 bases, none left out, 27 MB in 5.5 s (29 s of CPU); Ocarina of Time's 2,723 rows gave 2,479, one left out (`specialized`), 251 MB in 60 s (464 s of CPU); Persona 4 Golden's 23 rows gave 9, 9 left out (`stride`). `metal-tt` refused no group. Persona 4 Golden has no shader pair, so its app had no archive before; it now gets one of layout bases alone. Built with the pairs, as the app's archive is, a row whose vertex shader takes no stage input uses its pair's base, so Breath of the Wild's archive holds 872 layout bases (30 fewer) and Ocarina of Time's 2,131 (348 fewer). Breath of the Wild's whole `applegpu_g19p` archive: 711 MB in 162 s of `metal-tt` without them (0.5), 732 MB in 169 s with them (263 → 270 MB deflated). End to end on the Mac (M4 Max, `--platform mac-app` apps for `applegpu_g16s`, a clean install: no `pso_archive/`, no `pso_user.tsv`; two runs a release, alternating): Breath of the Wild's prepare step compiled 871 bases in 1.48–1.55 s behind the progress screen on 0.5, and on X1.0 took the 871 from the app, compiled none and took 0.04–0.06 s with no screen; Ocarina of Time's compiled 2,098 in 24.0–24.7 s on 0.5 (first frame at 31–32 s), and on X1.0 took 2,096 from the app and compiled 1 (the `specialized` row) in 0.40–0.74 s, no screen (first frame at 7.6–7.9 s). Play was the same on both: the same bases compiled in play (74 and 0), no long frame with pipeline work in the first minute. The descriptors match: the offline `vertex_descriptor` with MTLVertexDescriptor's defaults is found by the engine's own lookups.
- **Check:** `t_pso_bundle` (above), `t_pso_archive`, `t_pso_warm`; `test_pso_offline.py`: `Layout` (the key, the vertex descriptor and fragment entries as `pso_desc`, every reason a row is left out, the script) and `Build` (`test_layout_bases_are_groups_of_their_own`, `test_layout_bases_alone_make_an_archive`, `test_a_refused_layout_group_is_compiled_in_halves`, `test_a_layout_group_that_fails_otherwise_is_not_halved`); `test_devlog.py` (`PrepareLine`).
- **Open:** not measured on a device: on an iPhone the prepare line of a fresh install should count the seed list's layout bases as from the bundle, with almost none compiled. Bases built offline that no lookup asked for in those runs: 1 of Breath of the Wild's 872 and 35 of Ocarina of Time's 2,131 on the Mac (rows the device folds into one base that the offline copy keeps apart, not found yet); each costs only its size. The `stride` rows (9 of Persona 4 Golden's 23) are still compiled on the device: a constant stream's stride does not move its data, so `pso_desc` could give it the stride its attributes need, which `metal-tt` would take; that changes the descriptor of those bases, so the device's own archive would miss them once.

### Depth-only pipelines take an empty fragment

- **Symptom:** none seen in a game; a requirement of the Metal 4-only release. A depth-only draw (a program with no pixel shader: shadow maps, depth pre-passes) was the one render pipeline still built through the `MTLDevice`.
- **Cause:** Metal 4 asks for a fragment function whenever rasterization is on (`MTL4RenderPipeline.h`), so `pso_desc4` had no descriptor for such a draw and `pso_build` built it through the `MTLDevice`, with no base and no archive. With the `MTLDevice` path removed, it needs a Metal 4 descriptor of its own.
- **Fix:** `pso_desc4` gives a rasterizing pipeline with no fragment the host's empty fragment (`nop_fragment`: `dm_nop` of `dm/gfx/msl/nop_frag.metal`), and every colour attachment the write mask none, so it writes depth and stencil only, as before. It is a base like any other, archived and specialized. `pre_desc` (the prebuild) and `pso_offline.py` (the offline archive) describe the same base for a pair with an empty pixel key (a line `<vertex key><TAB>`), and `pso_offline.py` compiles `nop_frag.metal` per build into `work/host` with `host_msl.py`'s SDK and minimum. The archive stamp did not change, because no existing pair's key did: no pair list holds such a pair yet (`shader_split.py` writes none), so a depth-only draw builds its base itself at first and the device's archive keeps it. Transform-feedback pipelines, with rasterization off, take no fragment.
- **Measured:** an empty fragment against no fragment for depth-only draws (4096x4096 `Depth32Float`, 25 runs each on an Apple M4 Max, macOS 27.3): 2 million small triangles 7.672 ms either way, 1 million mid-sized 4.645 ms, 64 full-screen layers 0.424 ms in either order, with the same depth.
- **Found on:** no game: the Metal 4-only release (2026-11-10), which removed the `MTLDevice` pipeline path.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_depth_only` (under `MTL_DEBUG_LAYER=1`, with and without the GPU capture profile): the pipeline is a base specialized through the Metal 4 compiler, a triangle writes its depth where it covers and leaves the clear elsewhere, and a colour target in the pass keeps its clear colour. `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline_depth.py`: the offline base of an empty-pixel-key pair names `host_nop_frag` / `dm_nop`, and a list without such pairs compiles no host library.
- **Open:** deriving depth-only pairs from `pso_manifest.tsv`'s render rows with no fragment would let the prebuild and the offline archive cover them, but changes the pair lists and their stamps.

### Vertex shaders with layer entry points left out of the prebuild

- **Symptom:** a frame of 200 ms or more the first time an effect appears, although the bundled pipeline archive was trusted and the prewarm finished. With `DM_PSO_WAITLOG=1` the frame shows waits on render pipelines with no pulling entry, 34-74 ms each, for vertex shaders that have no stage inputs.
- **Cause:** whether a vertex shader writes the layer was not in its reflection. Three places guessed it from the library's bytes (`render_target_array_index` anywhere in the file), each in its own way: the shader loader (`shader_load`) excepted a library with layer entry points of its own (`entry_layer`, `main0_l`, which alone write the layer; its `main0` writes none), while the offline archive (`pso_offline.py`) and the device's prebuild (`pre_desc`) did not, and left every pair of such a shader out. A shader with no stage inputs has no stand-in pipeline, so each of those pairs was compiled at its first draw while the frame waited.
- **Fix:** the reflection holds the fact: `layer_out` when `main0` (and so `main0_z`) writes the layer (`dmsh_entry.py` `layer_out`, from `main0`'s output; the geometry output reader `<key>_bgv` too). The loader, the prebuild and `pso_offline.py` read it and nothing else, so they cannot part again, and no library is searched. Layer entry points do not count: the host asks for them by name (`entry_layer`) and their pipelines are layered by that. A chain change (d403eb0683df): the libraries are rebuilt, and with them the archive.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game), v131072, M6 (macOS 27.2): 2,675 pairs had been left out, so the archive grows from 30,690 bases to 33,365. On the `field-run` bench route from a Hyrule Field save (a camera turn at about 105 s), two runs each: the worst frame was 230-234 ms with 4 waits of over 20 ms per run before, and 39 ms with none after.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_dmsh_layer_out.py` (`main0` writing the layer sets it, layer entry points do not); `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (a library that names the layer without `layer_out` gets its `main0` base, one with it none); `t_pso_bundle` (the same for the prebuild).
- **Open:** a 39 ms frame at about 117 s on the same route, one pipeline taking 15-20 ms, under the wait log's threshold; not traced.

### The device's prewarm list unread without a seed list

- **Symptom:** `[RED] pipeline prewarm: seed list does not exist: …/pso_manifest.tsv` at every start, no `pipeline prewarm list …/pso_user.tsv` line, and the same compile stalls every session in places already visited, while `pso_user.tsv` keeps growing; the pipeline archive gains files every start and is never compacted (Donkey Kong Bananza: 88 files, 7 s to open them).
- **Cause:** `warm_start` (`dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_mtl.m`) returned as soon as the profile had no seed list, before it added the device list, so neither list was warmed, and the prebuild of every shader pair's pulling base and the archive's compaction, which run after the prewarm, never ran either. Draws then waited for their pipelines on the submit thread with no hybrid stand-in.
- **Fix:** a missing seed list is logged and left out; the device list is still warmed, and the prewarm returns only when neither list exists. Host only.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md): before, a 13,200-frame session had 154 frames over 100 ms (122 mostly compiling, the worst 7.1 s), 87 s of compiling on the submit thread and 95 draws with a hybrid pipeline; after, the start warmed the 2,484-line device list in 7.6 s (1,002 bases from the archive), compacted 98 archive files into one and ran the prebuild once (20,647 pairs, 116 s before the first frame), and a 17,700-frame session had 68 such frames (40 compiling, the worst 1.9 s), 12 s of compiling and 5,320 hybrid draws. The game has a seed list now.

### A pipeline a hybrid draw stood in for listed only once built

- **Symptom:** on an iPhone the next start still waits on, or draws with stand-ins for, the pipelines the last session asked for last. Ocarina of Time on an iPhone 18 Pro Max asked for about 400 pipelines in Castle Town and finished about 60 a minute; whatever was still unbuilt when the run ended was not in `pso_user.tsv`, so the next start's prepare step did not build it. HookLink's measurement; we have not reproduced it.
- **Cause:** `pso_user.tsv` took a pipeline's row from the submit thread only once its build finished. While a hybrid draw drew with its shader pair's pulling pipeline, the pipeline it wanted was queued in the background (`pso_pend_demand`), where a build took 5-50 s with the game running on two performance cores, against about 1 s in the prepare step before the first frame.
- **Fix:** `demand_record` (`dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_mtl.m`) writes the row when the hybrid draw first asks for the pipeline, once per key, including an already pending build or one the full build table could not admit. The later row from the built pipeline is the same line, which `pso_user_record` drops. Early recording remembers at most 65,536 keys per process; new keys beyond that retain completion-only recording. `DM_PSO_DEMAND_RECORD=0` records a pipeline only once built, as before. The added rows move work into the next preparation; they do not remove compilation.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game), found by [HookLink](https://github.com/dsi-hub/HookLink) (engine branch: https://github.com/dsi-hub/nxnative/tree/hooklink, commit 0ad4e1a9) on an iPhone 18 Pro Max.
- **Check:** `t_pso_demand_record` calls the real `pso_get` hybrid path with synthetic Metal functions and cached stand-ins while its production demand queues are suspended. New and independently prequeued demands persist canonical rows before compilation; an exec child reads them through `warm_parse`. After releasing the queues, completed pipelines replace the stand-ins and record identical rows without duplicates. Separate processes check recording disabled and the 65,536-entry early-record cap (completed pipelines still record beyond it). Removing call-site recording, or recording only newly queued demands, fails matched controls. API validation is enabled. This proves pipeline lookup and process-reload visibility, not power-loss durability, a game draw, fresh-process prewarm compilation, or native performance.
- **Preparation cost:** a matched, controlled backlog of recorded game pipelines was measured through the real prepare step on the Mac; [NieR:Automata's note](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md#preparation-cost-of-a-recorded-backlog) records its inputs, repeated timings and limitations. The Castle Town iPhone preparation increase remains unmeasured.

### Shader libraries loaded when the program is made

- **Symptom:** the first frame that draws with new programs loads their shader libraries on the submit thread (`[longframe] ... shader libraries N, X ms`): tens of milliseconds with the files cached, seconds when the drive is slow.
- **Cause:** a library was loaded when a draw first resolved its program (`prog_get`), or, from the first pipeline miss of a submission, for the whole rest of the submission at once and in parallel (`shader_prefetch_rest`), but still on the submit thread's time. The game names a program's shaders earlier, when it makes the program (`nvnProgramSetShaders`), often while it loads a scene.
- **Fix:** `mtl_prog_prefetch` (called from `nvnProgramSetShaders`) loads each stage's library on a background queue, with a vertex shader's pulling entry as the prewarm does, and the next submission takes it into the shader cache (`warm_claim`), writing it to the prewarm lists as `shader_get` would. A set of the libraries loaded or under way (`progpf_first`, which `shader_get` and `shader_prefetch_rest` also mark) loads each once. A draw that comes before its library has loaded loads it itself, as before. The shader map is read once from any thread (`shmap_read_once`); the prewarm still starts on the submit thread, once (`shmap_load`). A first version put the prewarm inside the same `dispatch_once`, and the pipeline prebuild's own call of `shmap_load` from inside it re-entered it: libdispatch aborted Tears of the Kingdom and Pokémon Scarlet at boot (the regression found it; Donkey Kong Bananza runs no prebuild). `DM_NVN_PREFETCH=0` turns it off.
- **Check:** Donkey Kong Bananza's scripted route, both runs with the drive idle: the first frame's 4 libraries (22 ms on the submit thread) no longer load there; the level opening's 3 still do (0.5-3.9 ms), its programs made just before their draws.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#found-on-this-game): 3 to 7 libraries a new area, which took up to 4 s a frame in a session with the drive slowed by other work.

### The prepare step inside the shader map's once

- **Symptom:** at boot, `CRASH sig=5 … pc=_dispatch_once_wait.cold.1` right after the `pipeline bases so far` line, with `bt: pso_prebuild __shmap_load_block_invoke … mtl_prog_prefetch h_nvnProgramSetShaders`.
- **Cause:** making `shmap_load` safe from any thread for the [prefetch](#shader-libraries-loaded-when-the-program-is-made) put its whole body in a `dispatch_once`, and that body ended in `warm_start`. With a prepare step (a window, `DM_PSO_PREPARE=1`, or the regression harness's runs), `warm_start` runs the prebuild in place, and `pso_prebuild` calls `shmap_load` again on the same thread inside the once, which libdispatch traps. The prefetch also moved the start of the prepare step from the first submission to the game's first `nvnProgramSetShaders`, on the game's thread. Separately, `warm_start` created the list that finished items go on, so a library the prefetch finished before it, or in a run with no prewarm list, was dropped, and its first draw loaded it again.
- **Fix:** `shmap_read_once` is the `dispatch_once` around reading the map; `shmap_load` reads through it and then starts the prewarm once, outside it. The prefetch only reads the map, so the step runs at the first submission again ([the prepare step](../development.md#the-prepare-step)). `warm_enqueue` makes its own list. Host only.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/t_shmap_warm.m` needs no game: the map's read leaves the prewarm unstarted, an item finished earlier is kept, and the first submission gets through the prebuild's second call. Before the fix the same sequence trapped with exit 133.
- **Found on:** general to games that run a prebuild: [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md) and [Pokémon Scarlet](../../dm/games/0100A3D008C5C000/README.md) at boot, [Blasphemous](../../dm/games/0100698009C6E000/README.md) under `regress.py`, [Hollow Knight](../../dm/games/0100633007D48000/README.md#found-on-this-game) with a window. [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md), where the prefetch came in, runs no prebuild and never showed it.

### A prebuild stamp written without the pulling libraries

- **Symptom:** after an IPA packed without `_bp.metallib` ran once with Metal 4 pipelines (then `DM_PSO_MTL4=1`, now always on), an app with those libraries still stalls on new pipelines: `pipelines up to frame` lines show `draws with a hybrid pipeline 0` and `draws that waited: pulling base not made yet`, and the log has no `pipeline prebuild:` line. The run before it logged `[RED] pulling library of … not usable` and `pipeline prebuild done: … 19838 left to the draws`.
- **Cause:** `build_ipa.sh` left the pulling libraries out when the profile did not set `pso_mtl4 on` (until [Pulling vertex inputs](rendering.md#pulling-vertex-inputs) kept them in every iOS build; `IOS_PULL=0` still leaves them out), while the launcher can still turn the Metal 4 path on. `pre_desc` then returned no descriptor for every pair with a pulling vertex entry, and `pso_prebuild` wrote `prebuilt.tsv` as for a finished run. The stamp covers the chain, the depth mode and the pair lists, none of which change when the app gains the libraries, so the next start took the prebuild as done and no pulling base was ever made.
- **Fix:** the device's `prebuilt.tsv` now ends with what the run found (`pre_device_stamp` in `nvn_mtl.m`): `libs all`, or `libs missing <file>` naming one library file a pair needed and the app lacked (`pre_lib_absent`): a pulling library `<key>_bp.metallib`, or a shader's `<key>_b.json` or `<key>_b.metallib`, which a partial shader build can add later. `pre_stamp_done` treats the second as done only while the library still lacks that file, so a package with it builds the pairs. A file counts as there when it is in the library's folder or in its image `b-<chain>.img` (`msl_has`, the lookup `msl_data` and `lib_from_file` make): the Mac app and the IPA carry only the image, so a check of the folder alone would find every library absent. A stamp without the line was written before this check and may hide such pairs: it runs once more, taking the bases already archived. The run also logs `[RED] pipeline prebuild: N pairs need a library file this app does not carry (first: …)`. The stamp of the archive made offline, which `pso_bundle_covers` compares, is unchanged.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_stall` covers a missing pulling library, a later start of the same app, the library arriving, a stamp without the line, and the same with the library packed as an image: its files found there, an absent shader named by its file, and that shader added in a folder beside the image (the first version of this fix checked the folder only and, against an image, built none of the pulling bases and stamped that as done). No device run with the fix yet: the iPad's recovery below was made by overwriting its stamp by hand.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md): iPad Pro 11 (M5), 1.4.3. 20,016 pairs: 178 bases built and 19,838 left to the draws without the libraries; with them and the stamp overwritten, 19,779 built in 718 s, and the archive grew to about 1.9 GB. That build raised the thermal state to serious, so the frame step held 30 fps for the first start after it.

### Metal's build folders left by pipeline archive flushes

- **Symptom:** the user cache folder (`getconf DARWIN_USER_CACHE_DIR`) fills with `gpuarchiver-*` folders, one per pipeline archive flush: in `com.apple.gpuarchiver/` for a host from a build, in `org.nxnative.<title ID>/com.apple.gpuarchiver/` for a built Mac app. Each holds an LMDB store (`PersistentState`) and captured binaries, 0.3–5 MB at first (456 folders, 2.5 GB after two days), later up to about 127 MB (an M4 Pro, 2026-10-29: 19 GB in 423 folders after a day of Mac runs and regression checks); why they grew is **open**. A built app's folders were never removed: Ocarina of Time's app had 4,016 of them, about 285 GB, and one Mac's apps 345.8 GiB in all; each start of Ocarina's Castle Town route left 6 more, 560 MB ([issue #2680](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2680)). A later compile can fail with `cannot open '…/gpuarchiver-…/PersistentState': No such file or directory`.
- **Cause:** the Metal 4 serializer (`MTL4PipelineDataSetSerializer`) creates a private working folder for each serializer, and the host makes one serializer per flush period (`ep_new`). Metal puts them under the main bundle's ID: `<user cache folder>/<bundle ID>/com.apple.gpuarchiver`, or `<user cache folder>/com.apple.gpuarchiver` for a process without one (`getCacheMainFolder` in Metal; its exported `MTLGetGPUArchiverCachePath` returns the folder, measured on macOS 27.2: a test program without a bundle wrote to the shared folder, with an `Info.plist` beside it to `org.example.gatest/`). In an iOS 27 simulator app the folder was `<container>/Library/Caches/<bundle ID>/com.apple.gpuarchiver`, and `confstr(_CS_DARWIN_USER_CACHE_DIR)` returned nothing there. The host took the folder as `<user cache folder>/com.apple.gpuarchiver` in both cases, so its iOS cold-start cleanup most likely never found a folder, and a built Mac app's were outside both it and `scripts/clean_metal_cache.py`. A former cleanup inferred ownership from the sole new directory between two snapshots of the shared folder; when another compiler created one during the capture or flush, the foreign folder was claimed and deleted. Serialization success concerns the requested output archive, not the lifetime of Metal's private files; the public API exposes neither a scratch-directory selector nor an ownership token. A later start never reads the folders: with them emptied against kept, a Castle Town start's first frame was 2.22 s against 2.32 s and its prewarm 18.2 s against 19.3 s (n=3 each, issue #2680).
- **Fix:** `nvn_mtl.m` asks Metal for the folder (`ga_dir`; the same rule computed when Metal lacks the function). A host with a bundle ID (the Mac app, the iOS app, one rule on both) owns its folder together with the other copies of the same game's app: from before its first serializer to its exit, each holds a shared `flock` on `gpuarchiver.lock` beside the folder (`ga_claim`). At start it first tries an exclusive one; with it, no other copy runs, so it lists the `gpuarchiver-*` folders there, takes the shared lock, and removes them in the background (`pipeline archive: N of M of Metal's build folders left by earlier runs removed from …`). Otherwise it waits for the shared lock, at most a starting copy's listing, and removes nothing (`another copy of this app is running`). A lock held exclusively for the run would not do: a second copy that could not take it would hold nothing, and a third start after the first's exit would delete the second's live folders. Folders are never removed at a flush or exit, and a host without a bundle ID removes none from the shared folder. A run still keeps its own folders until the next start (Ocarina: about 560 MB a start, more in a long session). `scripts/clean_metal_cache.py` removes the shared folder's and the built apps' (`org.nxnative.*`) folders from outside, for apps not started again: a folder only when nothing in it changed for an hour (`--idle-min`) and its last change is older than the start of every running `dmhost`, and an app's only while no copy of it holds its lock (listed under the exclusive lock, removed after it). `scripts/regress.py` runs it at its start and when the disk falls under its limit. A folder of another program's live compiler that stayed idle for an hour in the shared folder would still go; none is known.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): a built app's folders, 285 GB ([issue #2680](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2680)). [Pokémon Scarlet](../../dm/games/0100A3D008C5C000/README.md#pipeline-cache-ownership): the ownership defect is reproduced with an isolated foreign-folder fixture. Its longer house run had 20 missing-`PersistentState` failures, but the deleting process was not observed, so that run's attribution remains open. Earlier cache-growth report: MacNativeGames (`1eff989`, `110e6c6`, Metal 3's `MTLBinaryArchive`, 25 GB overnight on Tears of the Kingdom).
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive` (`make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_archive`) preserves a foreign compiler fixture created during a compiler call through the subsequent flush, and checks the app folder's lock: a first copy lists the earlier runs' folders, a second lists none, and a third start after the first's exit lists none while the second runs. `dm/tools/tests/test_clean_metal_cache.py` keeps folders changed within the idle time or since a running host started, keeps an app's folders while its lock is held, removes the rest, and looks in the shared folder and the `org.nxnative.*` folders only. The iOS folder is from a simulator app (Metal's function; the simulator has no Metal 4 serializer); no device run yet.

### A compaction flush that crashes, then crashes every start

- **Symptom:** a previously working game exits with 139 on every start after pipeline preparation; `dispatch_retain(NULL)` is called by `-[_MTLBinaryArchive materializeAirNTFromOnlineForSlice:entryList:]` under `airntSerializeToURL` and `arch_flush` ([issue #342](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/342)).
- **Cause:** more than eight run archives enables compaction. The prewarm used `MTL4CompilerTaskOptions.lookupArchives` to recapture their bases into the serializer that also captures newly compiled bases. Metal can crash while materializing that dataset for serialization. It crashes before `arch_compact_finish` removes the old files, so every restart retries it. Framework analysis recorded in issue #342 identifies a nil `MTLBinaryEntry.bitcode`, read from the captured function's library by `bitcodeDataInternal` when the bytes at its bitcode offset are not an AIR wrapper. Which function lacks AIR and why remain **open**. A lead, not a cause: a probe that reused one serializer after each write, with no compile during the write, crashed once in 111 runs with this signature, and a compile under way across a write comes back with its entry's AIR script and bitcode nil ([A compile that does not return stalls every flush and draw](#a-compile-that-does-not-return-stalls-every-flush-and-draw)); the host now never uses a serializer again after its write, and a serializer per period crashed in none of 118 runs; simple synthetic recaptures and a replay with no new bases did not reproduce it. A mixed Tears of the Kingdom replay did, with the reported null call and backtrace, on an M4 Pro with macOS 27.0.1 (26A434).
- **Fix:** work around the framework defect by capturing each compacted base from its original shader functions, with no lookup archives. Metal's ordinary compiler cache still applies. The archive threshold, ordinary archive lookups, deferred flush during prewarm and removal of old files only after a successful write remain in place. A compaction start can do more compiler work; normal starts keep their archive hits. `arch_capture` passes no task options, so captured bases cannot accidentally import archive entries. The recovery guard from the earlier workaround remains: `pso_archive/compacting` records the system build and the capture method before a compaction flush, and is removed when the flush returns. A marker for this build and method suppresses another attempt after a crash. Legacy OS-only markers from archive imports are cleared so the safer capture method can run; a later OS or capture-method change also allows another attempt.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#found-on-this-game), reported on an M5 Max and reproduced with the local shader libraries on an M4 Pro. This is shared NVN archive code, not a game profile rule.
- **Check:** the same 19-file snapshot, 1,490 archived bases and 44 new bases failed in two of three runs with lookup imports and passed all three when only that option was removed. The implemented fix passed three further compactions and their following restarts: each compacted file supplied all 1,534 bases with zero new base compilations on restart. These are library-only manifest replays using the local `e7245308050d` libraries and a matching diagnostic chain header, not a new gameplay run or an iOS test. `t_pso_archive` rejects lookup imports at the real compiler seam; `test_pso_archive.py` exercises disk-built libraries, concurrent recaptures mixed with a fresh function, function constants, compaction and restart in separate processes (`make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_archive`). The marker tests retain the recovery guard and check migration from the old OS-only marker, including a fresh-process restart with that marker present.

### Stale prebuild files kept in the pipeline archive

- **Symptom:** `pso_archive/` keeps files no lookup needs, and only grows: an iPhone 18 Pro Max run of Persona 5 Royal with Metal 4 pipelines (then `DM_PSO_MTL4=1`, now always on; a fork of this tree) logged `pipeline archive …: 21 files (20 of bases without a vertex layout), 259.6 MB` beside `pipeline archive bundled with the app: holds the bases of every shader pair`. No `p*.mtl4a` file was ever removed.
- **Cause:** the prebuild's compiles went into the draws' flush periods, so a prebuild flush became a `pNNNNN.mtl4a` file beside the draws' bases without a vertex layout (pulling bases built at a draw, the device-built bases of `rest_pairs.tsv`, task / mesh pipelines). Such a file cannot be told from one that holds draws' bases, so compaction leaves every `p` file alone, and nothing else removed them: not when the bundled archive came to hold every pair's base, and not when the prebuild's stamp changed (another pair list, chain version or depth mode), which leaves old bases unused.
- **Fix:** the prebuild has a flush period of its own (`arch_pre_begin` / `arch_pre_end` in `nvn_mtl.m`), written as `pbNNNNN_<program ID>.mtl4a`, so these files hold its bases alone. A prebuild that runs looks every pair up in the files first; each `pb` file opened at start that gave no pair's base is removed once every pair is done, before the new `prebuilt.tsv` is written (`pre_trim`). Only the running program's `pb` files are candidates (`arch_pre_mine`): the programs of one application (Super Mario 3D All-Stars' launcher and games) share `pso_archive/` (the parent of `DM_SAVE`, which is the same for each) and the bundled archive, but each has its own pair list, so a trim that took every `pb` file for the running program's removed the other program's at each switch, and each start of that one built its whole pair list again. When the bundled archive holds every pair's base and leaves none to the device, every `pb` file of the program is removed, and `prebuilt.tsv` with them when it holds this program's stamp, so that a later start the bundle no longer covers (an OS update that changed the GPU compiler) builds them again. A file that gave one base stays whole. `p` files, also those an earlier prebuild wrote, are never removed this way, since they may hold draws' bases: a folder from before the change keeps them until it is cleared by hand (the draws' bases in them are then built once more). `DM_PSO_PREBUILD_TRIM=0` keeps every file. The log says `pipeline prebuild: N of its files removed, … MB (…)`.
- **Found on:** [Persona 5 Royal](../../dm/games/01005CA01580E000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (the iPhone log above, read in an audit). Shared NVN archive code, not a game rule.
- **Check:** `t_pso_stall` (`make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_stall`): the prebuild's bases go into a `pb` file and a draw's into a `p` file; a file a later prebuild found a base in stays, one it found none in is removed and the draw's `p` file stays; `DM_PSO_PREBUILD_TRIM=0` keeps it; a bundle that holds every pair removes the `pb` files and `prebuilt.tsv`; two programs whose pair lists alternate in one folder keep each other's `pb` files, and a covered program leaves another program's `pb` files and stamp.

### Prebuilt pipeline archive files not opened in the run that built them

- **Symptom:** a first start (or one whose pair list changed) spends its prepare step on `pipeline prebuild: N shader pairs …`, yet the same session's `pipelines up to frame` lines still show `draws that waited: pulling base not made yet` and `misses with no pulling base ready`, and pulling bases are built again in the background; the next start has none of it.
- **Cause:** `pso4_init` opens `pso_archive/` once, before the prebuild, and the lookup tables (`g_arch_ix`, `g_arch_ixf`, `g_arch4p`) stay as it left them for the process. The prebuild only captures its bases into the archive (`pre_pair` keeps no pipeline), so the `pb<seq>_<program>.mtl4a` files it wrote were never looked in until the next start, and the misses recorded meanwhile (`g_pbase_miss`) kept every lookup of those bases from searching again.
- **Fix:** ported from upstream NXNative `4add5fbb4`. After every pair is handed out, `prepare_prebuild` waits for the compiles still under way for `g_pre_tail_ms` (10 s) at most, and for the last period's write once every worker has returned (`g_pre_flushing`); none after the idle limit for pairs put back. With no worker left, `arch_adopt_prebuilt` opens the folder's `p` and `pb` files that `pso4_init` did not find (`g_arch_opened`), oldest first so the newest file's key wins in `g_arch_ix`, puts a file without a complete index after the bundled archive in `g_arch4p`, and forgets `g_pbase_miss`. It runs on the prepare step's thread before the first frame, so the lookups stay lock-free. The files are not probed (they carry this chain's stamp) and not noted for this run's prebuild trim. They open only when the task's largest free address-space gap keeps 2 GiB free beside them (upstream's runtime reserve; NX 18 applies it here alone, not to `pso4_init`'s opens); otherwise the log says `files the prebuild just wrote … not opened in this run` and the next start takes them. A compile that does not return (issue #294) holds the start `g_pre_tail_ms` at most; its prebuild finishes in the background and its files open at the next start. The log says `pipeline archive …: N files the prebuild just wrote (…), … opened in … ms before the first frame`.
- **Found on:** upstream's Resident Evil 4 report ([GitHub issue #3030](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/3030)): an M5 Pro whose first run prebuilt 15,764 pulling bases and still waited 4.4 s on `pulling base not made yet` draws in that session. Not yet seen on an NX 18 device run. Shared NVN archive code, not a game rule.
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive --adopt-prebuilt` (`make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_archive`): a base captured into a `p` file after `pso4_init` is found by a compile 0 lookup in the same run from its index, with the earlier miss forgotten and nothing compiled; the file is neither probed nor the trim's; a second call opens nothing; with the reserve raised above the address space the file stays closed. `t_pso_stall`: `prepare_prebuild` opens its `pb` files before it returns and a pair's base is found through their index; with a held compile it returns after `g_pre_tail_ms` and opens nothing.

### A compaction that fails is tried again every start

- **Symptom:** `[RED] pipeline archive: compaction left the old files (a flush failed, or a base was not captured again)` at every start, and `pso_archive/` gains a set of copies each time.
- **Cause:** a compaction that does not finish keeps the old files beside the new ones that hold their bases again (`arch_compact_finish`). The next start has more plain files still, so it compacts again, and a failure that repeats adds another set.
- **Fix:** `pso_archive/compact_failed.tsv` names the system build and the plain files there were when it failed, leaving out any the start could not open, which `arch_compact_retry` never counts (`arch_compact_fail_note`). A start of that system build with all of them still there does not compact until more than `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT` (8) files were added since (`arch_compact_retry`, which logs `pipeline archive: a compaction of … did not finish on this system build: not tried again …`); a compaction that finishes removes the file. This bounds the copies; why a compaction failed stays as the red line says, and is **open** where it happens. The line counts the bases left out because another capture of their shader function did not return in time (`fn_claim`, [Two captures of one shader function crash in Metal's binary cache](rendering.md#two-captures-of-one-shader-function-crash-in-metals-binary-cache)).
- **Found on:** no game yet; found by reading `arch_compact_finish` in the same audit.
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive` writes the file after a compaction and checks that the same files, or up to 8 more, skip compaction, and that more files, a missing one or another system build try again.

### Pipeline keys omit unused colour state

- **Symptom:** identical render pipelines compile repeatedly under different keys, lengthening startup prewarm and adding pipeline misses during play.
- **Cause:** NVN blend and channel-mask state persists between draws, including state for colour targets absent from the current pass. `pso_desc` skips targets without a format and reads blend factors only when blending is enabled, but the old keys retained unused state. Deduplicating manifest lines alone also misses old rows whose unused state differs.
- **Fix:** `pso_key_norm` in `nvn_pso_skey.h` clears blend factors when blending is disabled and, for normal passes, the blend-enable bit and write-mask nibble of every target without a format. Merged passes retain their logical blend-enable and mask state because `lmap` maps physical attachments to logical targets; a missing physical format does not imply the same logical target is unused. `pso_key_fill` normalizes live keys, `pso_skey_parse` normalizes historical prewarm rows, and `warm_worker` deduplicates the parsed keys across all input lists before compiling. Depth and stencil formats remain in the key because the `MTLDevice` path needs them, even though Metal 4 bases omit them.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#files-and-shaders) (disabled blend factors), [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#files-and-shaders) (absent-target state, reported by MacNativeGames).
- **Check:** `t_pso_skey` covers normalized round trips, historical rows differing only in unused state, and preservation of merged-pass logical masks and blend bits; `t_pso_archive` checks disabled blend factors with all targets present. `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_warm` loads two synthetic manifests through `warm_worker`, checks compilation counts and draw-time cache hits, and compares GPU pixels with the original descriptors through both MTLDevice and Metal 4. It includes a merged pass whose physical attachment 0 uses logical target 7's blend and mask state.

### Shader cache key covering the pairs

- **Symptom:** a vertex shader whose pairs changed was restored unpadded from `cas/`.
- **Cause:** `dmsh_build.py`'s shader cache was keyed without the pairs.
- **Fix:** the per-shader key covers the pairs, and a paired vertex shader's key also covers its pixel shaders' keys (`shader_key`, `pair_keyed`), so a change to a profile's pairs rebuilds what it affects ([Pixel shader inputs the vertex shader does not write](#pixel-shader-inputs-the-vertex-shader-does-not-write)).
- **Found on:** [13 Sentinels: Aegis Rim](../../dm/games/01003FC01670C000/README.md).

### Shader cache key missing helper modules

- **Symptom:** an edit to a shader build helper reaches no library: `shader cache: hit` for every shader, and the old MSL is filed under the new chain version. An edit to `dmsh_meshstage.py` did not change the chain version either.
- **Cause:** a cache hit restores a shader's outputs and skips `job_for` and `finish`, so everything they run has to be in the shader's key. `CODE_FP` left out `dmsh_wide.py`, `dmsh_ampere.py`, `dmsh_meshstage.py` and `dmsh_fetch.screen_varyings` (through `raster_facts`), and `dmsh_chain.py`'s `FILES` left out `dmsh_meshstage.py`.
- **Fix:** `FILES` and `CODE_FP` cover them. The modules that stay out of `CODE_FP` are made again from the restored MSL on every build (`dmsh_vin.py`, `dmsh_pull.py`, `dmsh_side.py` with `dmsh_alpha.py` and `dmsh_logic.py`, `dmsh_mesh.py`, the rest of `dmsh_fetch.py`), or are covered otherwise (`dmsh_facts.py`: its row is in the key; `dmsh_mtlcache.py` changes no output). The change bumps the chain with the libraries byte for byte as before (Animal Well's 46), so Metal compiles nothing again.
- **Found on:** no game: found reading the code after Mario Kart World added `dmsh_meshstage.py`, then reproduced on [ANIMAL WELL](../../dm/games/010020D01AD24000/README.md) (an edit to `wide_loads` reached 0 of 46 libraries before the fix, all 46 after it).
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_code_fp.py` `Coverage` fails when `dmsh_build.py` imports a `dmsh_*` module that neither list accounts for.

### Partial vertex builds missing paired pixel sources

- **Symptom:** a full build reports successful shaders, but `metal-tt` rejects its pipeline archive with fragment inputs not written by the vertex shader. Fifteen cached vertices lacked default outputs in DRAGON QUEST I & II.
- **Cause:** a partial build requested only its vertex keys. `vs_pad` skipped absent paired pixel MSL, then cached the incomplete vertex under a key that already included those pixel shaders' input digests. A later full build restored it before pixel-first finishing and never padded that cache hit again.
- **Fix:** include available paired pixel shaders in partial build keys and output cleanup, then finish pixels before vertices. Do not publish a paired vertex cache entry while any paired pixel MSL is missing. The code fingerprint excludes the old incomplete cache entries; the Metal source cache still reuses unchanged complete libraries.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST I & II HD-2D Remake](../../dm/games/0100D7C01F254000/README.md#found-on-this-game), while building its Mac pipeline archive.
- **Check:** `test_shader_toolchain_cache.py` requests a vertex alone, exercises dependency cleanup and pixel-first finishing, and checks that padding survives publication and a later full-cache restore. A missing pixel source prevents publication.

### Target selection keeps the shader analysis files in the cache

- **Symptom:** a repeated iOS-only shader build crashes with `FileNotFoundError` for `<key>_b.json` in the Mac output directory; requesting only Mac libraries with `IOSOUT` set still compiles iOS side libraries.
- **Cause:** target selection excluded the Mac directory from the per-shader cache entirely, but cache-hit processing, paired vertex padding and the profile tables still read its MSL and reflection. Side-library paths tested `IOSOUT` rather than the selected targets. Prefilling the Metal cache also compiled validation samples for unrequested targets.
- **Fix:** cache and restore the Mac MSL and reflection for every target; require and copy metallibs only for selected targets. A cache entry without its analysis source is a miss. Clear `IOSOUT` when iOS is not selected and restrict prefill validation to selected targets. The NSP script writes only the requested target's reuse key.
- **Found on:** [Celeste](../../dm/games/01002B30028F6000/README.md#shaders), while reviewing PR #275 (resolved).
- **Check:** real Metal output and cache comparisons are recorded in Celeste's shader note above. `test_shader_toolchain_cache.py` covers geometry analysis files, incomplete entries and prefill selection; `test_nsp_mac.py` checks target-specific reuse keys.

### An iOS build after a Mac-only build kept no Mac libraries

- **Symptom:** after `scripts/build_ipa_from_nsp.py <NSP folder> --platform mac`, a plain (iOS) run of the script on the same folder succeeds, but the Mac run command the first build printed then logs hundreds of `[RED] failed to load shader <key>_b: cannot open …/shader/cache/b-<chain>/<key>_b.metallib` lines, until `--platform mac` is run again.
- **Cause:** both builds write the Mac sources and reflection into `<id>_build/shader/cache/b-<chain>/`, which also holds the Mac libraries. A full `dmsh_build.py` run first removes every `<key>_b*` output there, so that a key that left the map or a shader that no longer builds keeps no old library, and then restores or compiles only the targets in `DMSH_TARGETS`. Since target selection ([Target selection keeps the shader analysis files in the cache](#target-selection-keeps-the-shader-analysis-files-in-the-cache)) the NSP script asked an iOS build for `ios` alone, so the Mac `.metallib` files were removed and only their `.metal` and `.json` came back.
- **Fix:** the NSP script (`build_shaders`) asks an iOS build for `mac,ios` when the folder holds the current chain's Mac libraries, and then writes both reuse keys, so a later `--platform mac` run is reused. The Mac libraries are rebuilt, not kept as they were, so none is stale: the Metal compile cache gives back every one whose MSL was compiled before, and only a changed MSL is compiled. The first such build of a folder may translate again, because the per-shader cache entries an iOS-only build left hold no Mac library. A folder without Mac libraries (only ever packed as an IPA) still compiles only the iPhone's. `dmsh_build.py`'s rule, that a full build leaves only what it built, is unchanged; it is part of the chain version, so the fix stays in its caller.
- **Found on:** [Team Sonic Racing](../../dm/games/010092B0091D0000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (resolved).
- **Check:** `test_nsp_mac.py` (`test_ios_after_mac_keeps_mac_libraries`, `test_ios_after_mac_of_another_chain`).

### Old shader library folders grew a game's build folder with each chain change

- **Symptom:** the build folder of a game (`<id>_build/shader/`) gains about 2 GB for Breath of the Wild with every upstream update that changes the shader chain: after two updates `shader/cache` held `b-157e1b96c8da` (2.1 GB) and `b-a78f966a7b38` (2.3 GB) besides the current one, and the next build added a third.
- **Cause:** the libraries are written to `b-<chain>/` (`shader/cache` for the Mac, `shader/ios` for iOS, with the reuse keys `shader/ios/b-<chain>.key` and `shader/mac-b-<chain>.key`) and a chain change starts a new folder. Nothing removed the old ones. The host loads only the current chain's folder, `dm/mac/pack_app.py` and `build_ipa.sh` pack only it, and `regress.py` reads the folder of its own run, so an old folder is dead weight. `dmsh_build.py` took libraries of older folders into the Metal compile cache (`dmsh_mtlcache.py`) before it translated, and `shader_regressions.py` compared the new build with the newest older folder's `_b_build.json`.
- **Fix:** the NSP script (`prune_stale_chains`, called last in `build_shaders`) removes the folders named `b-<12 hex digits>` of other chain versions in `shader/cache` and `shader/ios`, and their keys, once the libraries, their tables and the key of the current chain are in place (a reused build prunes too). It logs one line, `removed old shader libraries b-… (N GB)`. A build that stops or fails earlier keeps the old libraries. The regression check has run by then, and the next chain change compares with the folder this build keeps. The compile cache already holds what the old libraries compiled, since `dmsh_build.py` took them in at the first build of the new chain. The only loss is for a chain that comes back (a checkout of an older commit): its shaders compile again where the cache does not hold their MSL. The compile cache itself (`shader/cache/cas/metallib`) is keyed by the MSL and the compiler, not by the chain, so entries are shared across chains and it is not pruned. It grows only with MSL that is new, and no code removes an entry.
- **Found on:** [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (resolved).
- **Check:** `test_nsp_mac.py` (`test_prune_stale_chains_after_success`, `test_failed_build_keeps_stale_chains`, `test_prune_ignores_other_names`).

### Old per-shader cache entries grew a game's build folder with each chain change

- **Symptom:** `<id>_build/shader/cache/cas/shader/` holds several entries for each shader, and grows by a full set with every upstream update that changes the shader chain. Tears of the Kingdom ([seedds/nxnative#1033](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1033)): 172,014 entries after six builds for 28,669 shaders, 11.85 GB of `.metal` and `.json` that no build read; the test Mac's copy held 934,955, of which the prune (dry run) found 906,286 unused: 70.2 GB of sources and reflection, scanned in 18 minutes on an external SSD.
- **Cause:** each shader's cache digest (`dmsh_build.py` `shader_key`) covers the chain's code and tools, so a chain change misses every entry and publishes a new one. [Old shader library folders](#old-shader-library-folders-grew-a-games-build-folder-with-each-chain-change) removed the old `b-<chain>/` folders, not these entries. `cas/index/<key>.json` cannot tell which entry is in use: a cache hit does not rewrite it, so after a checkout of an older commit it can name a newer digest than the one the build restores. The digests also cover the iOS compile convention, so a Mac-only and an iOS build of one chain use different entries, and `regress.py` builds other chains into the same cache.
- **Fix:** a full `dmsh_build.py` run lists the digests it used, `b-<chain>/_b_cas.txt` beside the Mac outputs (every full build writes those; the iOS folder goes whole into the IPA). The NSP script (`prune_shader_cache`, called with `prune_stale_chains` after a successful or reused build) removes the `shader/<64 hex digits>` entries of the compile cache that the list does not name and that are older than it, and logs `removed old shader cache entries: N (X GB)`, the space of their sources and reflection (an entry's libraries are clones of the compile cache's). No list (libraries built before it), a failed or stopped build, or a regress run: nothing goes. The Metal compiles are keyed by the MSL; since [Old chains' compiles stayed in the one compile cache file](packing.md#old-chains-compiles-stayed-in-the-one-compile-cache-file) each chain has its own file, and the files of other chains go whole, so this prune now covers only the chain's own file, where an edit of the shader map leaves old entries. A partial `dmsh_build.py` run (named keys) does not rewrite the list, so the next prune can remove the entries it published; the next full build translates those shaders again.
- **Found on:** [Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (resolved).
- **Check:** `test_nsp_mac.py` (`test_prune_shader_cache`, `test_prune_shader_cache_needs_success_and_the_list`).

### A shader build that keeps its packages in memory runs one worker at a time

- **Symptom:** the NSP script's Mac or iOS shader step for a game with very large shader packages runs at one or two shaders a second whatever `J` is. Its Python process sits at one full core, mostly system time, while few `metal` compiles run. Pokémon Pokopia (4.4 GB of packages, 131,426 shaders): players reported ETAs of 20 to 200 hours ([seedds/nxnative#1036](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1036)).
- **Cause:** `dmsh_build.py` read each package whole and kept its records as separate copies for the whole build: 105,118 of them for Pokopia's 4.25 GB `ps_rdb.bin`, leaving the process at 8.7 GB. Every child process (spirv-cross, `metal`, `metallib`, several per shader) starts with a fork of the builder, which then took 75 ms instead of 3, and Python forks with the GIL held, so the `J` workers queued behind each other. The same bytes in one buffer fork in 3 ms: the cost comes from the many separate allocations.
- **Fix:** `record_index` scans a package for its record markers through a read-only mapping it closes again, and keeps only the offsets; `rec_of` reads one record with `pread` when the build needs it and keeps none. The outputs are byte-identical.
- **Measurement:** a cold build of 1,001 Pokopia shaders on an M4 Pro (`J=10`): 528 s before, 240 s after (1.9 to 4.2 shaders a second); the builder's Python went from 100% to about 46% of a core, and 8 to 10 Metal compiles ran at once instead of about 3. At that rate the whole map would take about 9 hours on that Mac (not run). The largest packages of the other local games are 241 MB (Splatoon Raiders) and less, where a fork costs a few milliseconds.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (resolved).
- **Check:** `test_shader_toolchain_cache.py` (`test_package_records_are_read_from_the_file_at_their_offsets`).

### Each Metal compile instantiates Metal's texture classes again

- **Symptom:** the Mac or iOS shader step of a game with a large map still takes many hours with every worker busy, and a `metal -c` of a translated shader takes about 0.7 s of CPU, nearly all in the front end. Optimising adds about 0.1 s, and an empty shader takes 0.025 s. Pokémon Pokopia's 131,426 shaders: about 9 hours on an M4 Pro ([seedds/nxnative#1036](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1036)).
- **Cause:** a translated shader's argument buffer names several texture types, and Metal's texture classes are large templates.
