The diagnostic overrides (`DM_NVN_HILITE`, `DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`, `DM_NVN_TEXDUMP`) compiled theirs from source until the Metal 4-only release; they are now `hilite.metal`, `magscan.metal` and `texdump.metal`, with Metal 4 descriptors like the rest. MetalFX's temporal and spatial scalers share the same `g_comp4`, which `mtl_init` makes in every run, capture runs included, and the GPU deswizzle pipelines are built through it too.
- **Found on:** [Animal Crossing: New Horizons](../../dm/games/01006F8002326000/README.md#found-on-this-game), iPhone 17 Pro Max: the HUD's compile warning in play, from clear-triangle libraries compiled when a new room first issued a scissored clear.
- **Check:** `t_host_msl` loads every library, with a swizzled `newLibraryWithSource:` that counts 0 calls, and finds all 24 clear functions. `t_builtin`, `t_geometry` and `t_deswz_gpu` use the built libraries.
- **Build inputs:** Mac libraries use `MACOS_MIN` from `dm/mac/min_os.mk`, matching the host's deployment target; otherwise a newer SDK emits libraries older supported Macs cannot load. They are MSL 4.1 (`-std=metal4.1`), which needs an OS minimum of 27 or later. Every build rechecks the compiler, SDK, deployment target and source contents, reusing the header only when all match.
- **Build callers:** the NSP packer and regression harness generate the Mac header before checking their cached host executables, and include it in the host fingerprint. The standalone iOS compile check generates the iPhone header before its dry run, as the app build does. A dry run alone does not generate either header.

### Raw pool table words that predate the pools

- **Symptom:** a raw global memory read of a memory pool made after the shader's arguments were encoded gets 0, though the pool exists when the GPU runs it; a task shader that reads both ends of a range this way loops `(0 - 1) >> 5` times and hangs the GPU. The memory report's `raw pool table words at commit` line counts the words that were out of date.
- **Cause:** an argument block holds the address of the pool table that was current when it was encoded (`gmem_map_gpu`). A pool made later in the same submission, or on another thread while it is encoded, is not in that table, and the table is rebuilt only for the next block that is encoded. A save load makes more than 4,000 pools in a burst, so many blocks are encoded with a table that lags.
- **Fix:** the words are noted as they are encoded (`gmem_slot_note`) and, at each commit of the command buffer, whole or segment, pointed at a table of the pools as they are then (`gmem_commit_patch`). A new table is built when the pools changed, not refilled in place, as earlier segments may be reading the old one; the replaced one goes through the deferred release. The write log of a block is a bitmap of the entries of the table its shader searches, so the log follows its table (`NvGmlLog`): `gml_read` decodes its bits against the table the block holds at the commit, not the one it was encoded with. The idea is #1407's, which rewrites the words at commit in its own stack.
- **Note:** the commit of the part encoded so far before an indirect-count multi-draw reads its count on the CPU (`mdi_wait`: quads, fans, 8-bit indices, records or counts in no Metal buffer, geometry draws) is such a segment too, and it went without the patch upstream: the GPU ran blocks whose words could still name an older table, so a raw read of a pool made earlier in the submission got 0 there (wrong rendering, or a hang as above). It now calls `gmem_commit_patch` before `res_commit`, like `mtl_sub_commit`, `sub_cut` and `lp_finish`; NX 18 release 1 took it from the earlier Nintanium line, whose merge of NXNative b4318c7a..b2d46b86 had added it. Since upstream 1fc17bbcf all four end the command buffer through `cb_commit` (`cb_seal`: the blit encoder, the upload batch and these words; then the residency set), so none can miss a step again.
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time (`0400486024DEC000`, profile in PR #1299): on the author's stack, loading the outdoor save, 5,729 of 154,107 words held an older table than the current one by frame 5,100 and 2,582 held the current one behind the pools. Whether this is what is left of the hang in issue #1326, minutes after a clean table growth, is open.
- **Check:** `t_gmem_words` (words noted, a pool made after them, an older table, a table that lags, a commit with no words, the segment `mdi_wait` commits).

### Extended products behind spirv-cross's optnone helper

- **Symptom:** integer divisions by a constant cost GPU time that the compiler should have folded away. The MSL holds calls such as `(spvMulExtended<_2, uint>(uint(_5), 16u))._m1`, and spirv-cross defines `spvMulExtended` as `[[clang::optnone]]`, so each product stays a call to an unoptimized function.
- **Cause:** spirv-cross writes `OpUMulExtended` and `OpSMulExtended` through one template that it keeps unoptimized, because Metal's compiler may fail on an inlined `mulhi` (its comment at `SPVFuncImplMulExtended` in `spirv_msl.cpp`). Switch 2 shaders divide by constants through the high word of such a product: Ocarina of Time's MSL has 348,587 of these calls, all scalar `uint` by a literal and 98% of them by a power of two. Four Switch 1 games checked (Fire Emblem: Three Houses, Minecraft Dungeons II, Octopath Traveler II, Sonic Superstars) have none.
- **Fix:** `mul_extended` in `dmsh_build.py` adds overloads for scalar `uint` and `int` operands beside spirv-cross's helper. They are always inline and take the halves of the 64-bit product, with no `mulhi`. Overload resolution prefers them to the template, so every scalar call leaves the barrier and the compiler folds its constant; a power of two becomes a shift. Vector calls keep the template. A helper that is not exactly as spirv-cross writes it leaves the shader unchanged. [PR #1741](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1741) first took out the calls with a literal other than a power of two (5,679 of them); that left the gameplay route below unchanged.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#frame-rate). On an M4 Pro, the Kokiri Forest deck route's GPU time fell from 15.55 to 14.76 ms a frame and its frame rate rose from 54.0 to 59.1 fps. The title movie's GPU time fell from 12.96 to 12.44 ms. On an M1 Pro, PR #1741's partial version took the title movie from 26.57 to 28.40 fps.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_extended_multiply.py`. On the Mac GPU, it compares unsigned and signed products by literals (power of two or not) and by variables, over 65,536 input pairs including the edge values, with 64-bit products on the CPU. It compiles the kernel for macOS and iOS and checks that only the vector call is left calling the template.

### NoContraction helpers behind spirv-cross's optnone

- **Symptom:** vertex shaders cost GPU time in their position math that the arithmetic does not explain. The MSL computes `gl_Position` through calls such as `spvFAdd(spvFMul(x, y), z)` and `spvFMulMatrixVector(m, v)`, which spirv-cross defines as `[[clang::optnone]]` templates, so every use is a call to an unoptimized function (optnone implies noinline).
- **Cause:** a float operation the translator marks NoContraction must keep its own rounding, so spirv-cross writes it as one `fma` in an optnone helper (`spirv_msl.cpp` `SPVFuncImplFAdd`, `FSub`, `FMul`: `spvFAdd`, `spvFSub`, `spvFMul`, `spvFMulVectorMatrix`, `spvFMulMatrixVector`, `spvFMulMatrixMatrix`) that the compiler can neither fold nor fuse. With relaxed precision (`dmsh_xlate`'s `reduced`, the default) the translator marks only what must be exact: the dependency chain of `gl_Position` (Ryujinx's `ForcePrecise`), which keeps one geometry's depth the same in a depth pre-pass and an equal-depth pass ([Position invariance across shaders](#position-invariance-across-shaders)). The S2Arm project counted 61 such calls in one main entry of a sports game's foliage vertex shader.
- **Fix:** `nocontraction_inline` in `dmsh_rewrite.py` (from `dmsh_build.py`, after `mul_extended`) replaces each of the six helpers, as this spirv-cross writes it, with an always-inline one doing the same `fma`s with their operands in the same order, whose 0 or 1 operand is a load of a `thread volatile float`. A literal 0 or 1 would let the front end turn `fma(l, r, 0)` into a multiply (legal under `nsz`), which the GPU compiler may then contract into a neighbouring add, and the two shaders of a pre-pass could round differently; a volatile load is not a constant, so each step stays one `fma`: `fma(1, l, r)` rounds `l + r` once and `fma(l, r, +0)` rounds `l * r` once with the same signed zero, as the optnone helper did. A helper not exactly as written is left as it is, and the others are still replaced. Ported from S2Arm `7cfd7be8` (Apache-2.0), which changes spirv-cross's own source; NX 18 runs the pinned spirv-cross build and rewrites its text.
- **Found on:** not measured in a game here. A microbenchmark on an M4 Max (2026-11-15; 2^20 threads, 64 rounds of `spvFAdd(spvFMul(p, y), z)` and `spvFSub(spvFMulMatrixVector(m, p), y)`, the iPhone's flags): 2.64 ms with spirv-cross's helpers, 0.91 ms inline with the volatile operands, 0.81 ms with plain inline arithmetic (which does not keep the rounding).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_nocontraction.py`: the text (every helper replaced, the calls and any other text unchanged); the compile for macOS and iOS with both production flag sets (no helper call left, no `fma` with a literal 0 addend or 1 multiplier, while the helpers inlined with their literals show both); and on this Mac's GPU, random and edge inputs (signed zeros, denormals, the largest floats, half-ulp neighbours, infinities, NaN) through the six helpers: 1,310,720 output words identical to spirv-cross's optnone helpers under the Mac's and the iPhone's flags, and 107,387 different with plain inline arithmetic, so the comparison sees a fused step. The iPhone's GPU compiler has not run it: a depth pre-pass game (Ocarina of Time, Mario Kart World) on the iPhone should show no new depth fighting.

### Signed integer overflow in translated shaders

- **Symptom:** integer results in a shader come out wrong where the game's code relies on signed arithmetic wrapping: a packed field unpacked by hand, an overflow test, a sign extension. In Ocarina of Time, one section of an otherwise dark wall in the Deku Tree (Gohma's room) was lit brightly and shimmered while the camera moved. Only models drawn with task / mesh shaders were affected; the vertex-shader models beside them were lit correctly.
- **Cause:** SPIR-V, GLSL and the Switch's GPUs wrap on signed overflow. spirv-cross writes SPIR-V's `OpIMul` / `OpIAdd` on signed values as plain `int` arithmetic in MSL, where overflow is undefined, so Metal's front end optimizes as if it cannot happen. Ocarina's mesh shader `35785cddc971290c` (task `a5288d0a6c4971fd`, pixel `f4f7e6955d37f40c`) sign-extends the low half of a word for the lightmap / light-probe UV: `a = as_type<int>(w) * 65536`, then `(a >> 31) << 16 | uint(a) >> 16`. The compiler, assuming the multiply did not overflow, took the sign bits from bits 15-30 of `w` (`(w << 1) & 0xffff0000`) instead of from bit 15. The UV's x then came out far too large (13184 / 18432 / 31744 where y gave 0.20 / 0.28 / 0.48), the probe texture was sampled at the wrong texels, and the pixel shader's spherical-harmonics ambient term read about 8 instead of 0.001. On an older chain the translator wrote the extraction without the sign extension, and the fold did not happen.
- **Fix:** every Metal compile of translated shaders passes `-fwrapv`: `dmsh_build.py` (Mac) and `ios_metal.py` (iOS) (and the Vulkan and GL shader tools, until 0.5 removed those layers). The flag also drops `inbounds` from pointer arithmetic, because Metal's clang has no separate `-fwrapv-pointer`. It also makes the compiler keep loop bounds that may wrap (an extra `smax` before a loop). In a sample of 1,000 of Ocarina's shaders, compiled to LLVM IR on an older chain, 12 came out identical, 746 differed only in their `nsw` / `nuw` / `inbounds` flags, and 242 had other changes. None of it shows on the GPU. On Ocarina's `kokiri-deck` route ([`bench.tsv`](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/bench.tsv)) on an M6 (12-core GPU, 2026-11-09), both sides built from the same main with their own libraries and warmed pipeline archives, the medians of three runs a side were 9.94 / 10.51 ms GPU p50 / p95 and 59.4 fps without the flag, and 9.91 / 10.43 ms and 59.6 fps with it. Runs of one side differ by about 0.1 ms.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game), found by nawangwe ([PR #1844](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1844)) on an M4 Pro.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_wrapv.py` compiles the sign extension, an overflow test (`x + 0x7fffffff < x`) and a doubling and halving with `dmsh_build.py`'s command and checks the results on the Mac's GPU over 65,536 inputs. It also checks that the iPhone command keeps the shift and that all four tools pass the flag. Without `-fwrapv`, the GPU check and the iPhone check both fail.
